# LeadSquared-Level CRM Gap Checklist

UI review companion (2026-09-09): [UI review and remediation plan](26_UI_REVIEW_AND_REMEDIATION_PLAN.md), [proposed design standard](27_UI_DESIGN_STANDARD.md), and [browser evidence/limitations](ui-audit-2026-09/BROWSER_REVIEW.md). Existing feature checkboxes retain their implementation meaning; browser-verified UI acceptance is tracked separately in the [route UI inventory](ui-audit-2026-09/ROUTE_UI_INVENTORY.csv).

Implementation update (2026-09-10): [phased delivery, evidence and remaining acceptance work](28_UI_IMPLEMENTATION_PROGRESS.md).

This is the working implementation checklist. Mark items complete only when the feature is implemented, wired to real data/runtime behavior, and verified.

**How to maintain this document going forward**: a consolidation pass found ten groups of duplicate/fragmented headers in this file, mostly caused by cross-cutting polish/audit work being added as new trailing sections (an "Enterprise Upgrade Audit" pass, a second "Distribution"/"Automations"/"Reporting" section, etc.) instead of as new bullets inside the Priority Module that already owns that capability. When adding a new cross-cutting or polish-oriented item, add it as a bullet **inside the relevant existing Priority Module** (or the relevant existing top-level section) rather than creating another new trailing "audit pass" section — that is exactly how this duplication problem arose the first time.

## Enterprise Completion Standard

Every module, whether existing or newly added, should meet this baseline before it is treated as enterprise-ready.

**Note on how to read this section**: these 11 lines are a generic rubric applied *per module*, not one-off build tasks with a single yes/no answer of their own -- the real, per-module answer to "does this module meet the baseline" lives in that module's own section elsewhere in this document (e.g. Module 21's own audit covers "can be enabled/disabled per tenant" for the ~13 of ~28 modules that actually have a real entitlement guard today). Marking any of these 11 lines `[x]` would incorrectly imply the standard is met application-wide, and adding an independent one-line status to each would just restate what's already tracked per module elsewhere -- so these stay unchecked by design, as the standing bar every module is measured against, not a tracked deliverable.

- [ ] Module can be enabled/disabled per tenant and respects module dependencies, role permissions, field permissions, tenant scope, and partner/applicant/external-user scope.
- [ ] Module has a focused workspace UI with list/search/filter, create/edit/detail flows, bulk actions where relevant, exports where relevant, friendly names instead of raw ids, responsive behavior, and no cluttered everything-on-one-page layout.
- [ ] Module has admin settings for lifecycle/status values, templates, ownership/assignment, required fields, notifications, automation eligibility, export policy, retention policy, and audit visibility.
- [ ] Module uses dynamic dropdown-backed selectors for all picklist/lookup/condition fields, including multi-select where a field can naturally hold or compare multiple values.
- [ ] Module supports Views, advanced filters, reports, dashboards, exports, automations, audit logs, notifications, and worker jobs where relevant.
- [ ] Module has clear state machines for irreversible or sensitive transitions, with server-side guards, idempotency, audit logs, and approval/step-up controls where needed.
- [ ] Module handles empty, loading, error, permission-denied, disabled-module, stale-data, and background-processing states in UI without console-only failures.
- [ ] Module has API coverage for GET/POST/PATCH/DELETE including auth negative cases, RBAC, tenant isolation, validation errors, idempotency, and structured user-friendly error responses.
- [ ] Module has performance controls: pagination, indexes for common filters/sorts, worker offload for expensive operations, request cancellation/debounce in UI, and no N+1 query patterns.
- [ ] Module has seed/demo fixtures that look meaningful for a live sales/admissions demo and that can be regenerated idempotently per tenant.
- [ ] Module has documentation/runbook notes for setup, env vars/connectors, worker dependencies, migrations, backup/restore implications, and production verification.

## Phase 0 — Foundation

- [x] Export real Supabase schema into repo as SCHEMA.md. Verified present (`SCHEMA.md`, ~58KB).
- [x] Add .env.example. Verified present (`.env.example`, ~4.5KB).
- [x] Add minimal smoke tests (auth, leads CRUD, RLS check). **2 of 3 real.** `tests/auth.test.ts` (10 tests) and `tests/leads-crud.test.ts` (a thin smoke test over the `crm.ts` create->get->update->list wrapper, distinct from the much larger `tests/leads-postgres.test.ts`) both exist. **"RLS check" not actually covered**: no test anywhere in the suite exercises row-level security -- confirmed by grep, zero matches for "RLS"/"row level security" across `tests/`. This is structural, not an oversight to quickly fix: every test in this suite mocks `@/lib/db/query` and never touches a real Postgres connection, so a genuine RLS-policy-enforcement test (verifying Postgres itself blocks cross-tenant reads at the database layer, not just that application code remembers to filter by `tenantId`) would need a real database connection this test harness doesn't have.
- [x] Confirm rate-limits / retention / usage admin pages are wired to real enforcement. **Update: no longer all three are stubs.** Retention (`/dashboard/admin/retention`) is now genuinely real -- backed by `/api/platform-admin/retention/*` (`policies`, `policy/[tenantId]`, `enforce`), `DataRetentionPolicy`/`enforceDataRetentionForTenant`, and a real recurring worker job (`retention.enforce`), built later in this same session; this specific bullet's note is corrected accordingly. **Rate limits and usage admin pages are still real stubs**, confirmed just now by direct check: `/dashboard/admin/rate-limits/page.tsx` calls `GET /api/platform-admin/rate-limits/stats` and `/dashboard/admin/usage/page.tsx` calls `GET /api/platform-admin/usage/overview` and `GET /api/platform-admin/automation/stats` -- none of these three routes exist anywhere in `src/app/api/platform-admin/` (confirmed by listing every route directory there: only `impersonate`, `marketplace`, `retention`, `schema-status`, `tenants` exist). Both pages render whatever their `fetch` calls return (almost certainly a 404 today), with no backing data or enforcement behind either. A real per-key/per-app rate limiter (`checkRateLimit`, Redis-backed) is genuinely used at runtime by the `ApiKey` and marketplace-app systems -- that's real enforcement, just with no platform-admin-facing visibility/configuration page wired to it.
- [ ] Add a canonical module registry in code and database so sidebar items, API guards, workers, settings, seeders, reports, views, automations, and exports all read module availability from the same source. **Partially superseded, not fully built**: Module 21 built a real catalog (`PlatformModule`/`TenantModuleEntitlement`, `assertModuleEnabled`), but it isn't a single source every surface reads from automatically -- each area (nav, a given API route, a given worker job) added its own individual `assertModuleEnabled`/`useModuleEnabled` call site by hand, module by module, not a registry that derives all of them.
- [ ] Add tenant bootstrap checklist: create tenant, choose modules, create platform/admin users, seed roles/teams/sales groups, seed module defaults, run migrations, verify health, and generate demo credentials. Not built -- confirmed in the Module 21 audit that `create-tenant-dialog.tsx` was never touched by any of this session's module-entitlement work.
- [ ] Add shared lookup/value-label resolver used by UI, exports, reports, audit logs, notifications, filters, automations, and workers so raw ids never leak into user-facing surfaces. Not built as a single shared service -- individual pages resolve their own owner/team/stage names ad hoc per query, not through one reusable resolver.
- [ ] Add shared tenant formatting service for timezone, date/time, currency, number formatting, fiscal year, and locale; all UI and exports must use it instead of browser/system defaults. **Partially real**: `src/lib/date-format.ts` (`formatWorkspaceDate`/`formatWorkspaceDateTime`/`formatWorkspaceRelativeTime`/etc.) is a real, pervasively-used shared date/time formatter. Currency/number formatting has no equivalent shared service (only generic helpers in `utils.ts`, not tenant-aware), and fiscal year is real only for GST invoice numbering (Payouts), not as a general-purpose formatting concept.
- [ ] Add global condition metadata service for fields/operators/values across modules, with dynamic picklist values, lookup search, multi-select semantics, permission filtering, and module entitlement filtering. Not built as one service -- the automation builder's trigger/condition pickers are per-module, hand-maintained arrays (e.g. `getAvailableTriggerTypes` in the automations-v2 page), each separately filtered for entitlement/permissions rather than driven by a shared metadata layer.
- [ ] Add shared action permission registry for sensitive actions: payout/payment transitions, export/download, model promotion, automation publish, campaign launch, partner suspension, tenant suspension, and impersonation. Not built as a registry -- each sensitive action has its own hand-written guard at its own call site (e.g. payout transition guards live in `payouts.ts`, tenant suspension in `admin.ts`), with no single place listing or enforcing them uniformly.
- [ ] Add central background-job registry: job name, required module, queue, concurrency, retry policy, idempotency key, visibility in request history/job history, and worker health status. **Partially real**: `scripts/worker.ts`'s `recurringJobs` array is a real, single list of every recurring job (name + processor), but carries none of the richer metadata this bullet describes (no declared required-module, concurrency, retry policy, or a queryable job-history/health UI) -- it's a job list, not a registry in the described sense.
- [ ] Add a production readiness dashboard combining migrations, schema freshness, API smoke status, worker queues, Redis, Postgres, ML service, connectors, storage, and recent failed jobs. **Partially real, much narrower**: `/platform-admin/schema-status` (`getMigrationStatus`) is a real page, but covers only migration/schema state -- none of the other named signals (worker queue depth, Redis/Postgres health, connector status, storage, recent failed jobs) are surfaced anywhere.
- [x] Add basic server-side error logging to the shared `serverError` HTTP helper. Found during a post-implementation adversarial code review of this session's work: `serverError` (`src/lib/server/http.ts`) swallowed every underlying error with zero logging outside of local development — a real production 500 left no server-side trace to correlate with a user-reported failure. Added a `console.error(message, error)` call before building the response. `tsc` and lint verified clean.

## Gamification & Payouts

- [x] Partner role + portal (admin-only creation; `isPartnerRole` flag on Role.permissions; partners scoped to their own `ownerId`-assigned leads/opportunities; blocked from admin/settings/automation screens via `requireInternalUser` + hardened `RoleGuard`)
- [x] **Post-implementation fix: closed a fourth real module-entitlement gap found during Module 21's ongoing backend-guard rollout** (same "fully built, zero gating" pattern as Distribution Engine, Predictive Scoring, and Marketing Communications earlier this session). `PARTNERS` is a real, seeded `PlatformModule` catalog key, but partner org/login creation and profile edits (`src/lib/server/partners.ts`: `createPartnerForTenant`, `createPartnerLoginForTenant`, `updatePartnerProfileForTenant`) had zero entitlement gating — no `assertModuleEnabled` call, no older `TenantFeature` flag either, confirmed by direct grep before fixing. Fixed by gating those three functions, their 3 API routes, the "Partners" settings-sidebar entry, and the admin Partners page's create/edit UI. **Explicitly confirmed no overlap with existing Payouts/Gamification gating**: `payoutsEnabled`/`gamificationEnabled` already control partner *payout and points visibility* for an existing partner (via `canAccessPayoutModule`/`requirePartnerPayoutAccess` in `partner-access.ts`, and separately in `payouts.ts`/`partner-invoices.ts`) — none of that code calls into `partners.ts`'s mutations, so disabling `PARTNERS` alone doesn't touch payout/gamification access for already-onboarded partners; it only blocks admins from onboarding new partner orgs/logins or editing existing profiles. Partner login itself is also unaffected — it authenticates directly against the `User` table with no dependency on `partners.ts` at all, confirmed before fixing. New regression test in `tests/partners.test.ts` confirms `createPartnerLoginForTenant` rejects when disabled. `tsc`, lint, full `vitest` suite (233/233, 1 new), `npm run build`, and a live dev-server smoke test all verified clean.
- [x] Partner organizations with multiple logins and hierarchy — implemented via `PartnerOrganization`, `PartnerProfile.partnerOrganizationId`, parent profile links, login roles, automatic org creation/backfill, admin API for additional partner logins, and Partners UI controls to add/manage child logins, parent login, login role, status, and payout visibility. Migration `0013_partner_org_visibility_targeting.sql`, partner/payout/gamification tests, `tsc`, and build verified.
- [x] Commission rule engine — condition-based (reuses the automation engine's field-condition matcher), priority-ordered, first-match-wins, admin UI at Settings → Commission Rules
- [x] Commission ledger — append-only, enforced by both application code and a Postgres trigger; corrections are new offsetting rows, never edits
- [x] Commission calculation wired into the existing automation engine (`calculate_commission` node) — no second event pipeline
- [x] Payout cycles — configurable recurring interval, Draft → Approved → Invoiced/Paid status flow, admin UI at Settings → Payout Cycles
- [x] Advanced payout controls — tenant-level minimum payout amount, manual vs auto-below-threshold approval mode, payout hold/release workflow with configured reasons, invoice-before-payment enforcement, partner self-invoice toggle, adjustment/hold reason catalogs, and server-side transition guards; migration `0010_advanced_payout_gamification_settings.sql`, payout tests, `tsc`, and build verified.
- [x] Payout module visibility targeting — admin can select all partners or specific users, teams, sales groups, and partner organizations in Payout Settings; partner payout nav/API/invoice self-service are gated by the same server-side targeting. Migration `0013_partner_org_visibility_targeting.sql`, payout tests, `tsc`, and build verified.
- [x] Partner payout aggregation across multiple logins — `computePayoutsForCycle` now resolves each commission ledger `partnerId` to its partner organization and creates one payout for the primary org login while preserving original per-login ledger attribution. Migration `0013_partner_org_visibility_targeting.sql`, payout tests, `tsc`, and build verified.
- [x] Payout adjustment workflow — admins can create credit/debit adjustments against unlocked payouts using configured reasons; adjustments write append-only `CommissionLedger` correction rows, update payout totals, and audit the change.
- [x] GST-compliant invoice generation — per-partner configurable numbering pattern (incl. financial-year-scoped), CGST+SGST/IGST place-of-supply split, non-GST receipts for unregistered partners, PDFs persisted to Supabase Storage, one-click partner self-service generation
- [x] Finance CSV export per payout cycle
- [x] Gamification points engine — rule-based, additive (multiple matching rules stack, unlike commission's single-winner), hooked into the same automation engine (`award_points` node)
- [x] Advanced gamification settings — tenant-level levels, leaderboard configuration, redemption catalog, and anti-gaming caps persisted via `GamificationSettings` with admin UI/API; migration `0010_advanced_payout_gamification_settings.sql`, `tsc`, and build verified.
- [x] Gamification participant targeting — admin can select all eligible users or specific users, teams, sales groups, and partner organizations; awards, redemptions, and leaderboards now enforce the targeting server-side. Migration `0013_partner_org_visibility_targeting.sql`, gamification tests, `tsc`, and build verified.
- [x] Gamification anti-gaming enforcement — duplicate event window suppression, daily positive-point caps, and high-value award review threshold checks now run inside `awardPointsForEvent`; skipped awards are audit-logged.
- [x] Badges — threshold/window-based, deduplicated per window via a DB unique constraint, admin UI at Settings → Gamification
- [x] Leaderboard — individual and team scope, date-range filterable, computed-on-read (no materialized view needed at this scale)
- [x] Self-service pages: My Payouts + invoice template editor (partners), My Points + badges (partners and reps), Leaderboard (internal-only — partners must not see other partners)
- [x] Audit trail extended (not duplicated) — every new mutation type flows through the existing `createAuditLog`/`AuditLog` table
- [x] Points-to-money / 3rd-party reward redemption — active reward catalog on My Points, request flow with immediate point reservation, admin redemption queue on Settings → Gamification, fulfillment/failure review, automatic point refund on failed requests, and migration `0011_gamification_redemptions.sql`; gamification tests, `tsc`, and build verified.
- [ ] Full RBAC lockdown across all ~103 existing API routes — only new routes plus a targeted set of the most sensitive existing ones (roles, permission-templates, users, settings, automations-v2) got `requireInternalUser`; a full sweep is separate follow-up work. **Scoped audit of the payout/gamification/partner-invoice domain done in this pass**: every one of the ~33 routes under `payouts`, `payout-cycles`, `commission-rules`, `gamification*`, `badges`, `partner-invoices`, and `partners/me/*` already had a `require*` guard (none were nakedly unguarded) — the one real gap found was three `partners/me/*` routes (`payouts`, `commission-ledger`, `invoices`) each duplicating the same inline `!user.isPartner || !(await canCurrentUserAccessPayoutModule(user))` check; consolidated into one shared `requirePartnerPayoutAccess` helper in `payouts.ts` so the three call sites can't drift. The app-wide ~103-route sweep itself remains separate follow-up work, unchanged in scope.
- [x] Credit-note handling for a corrected/superseded invoice — `PartnerInvoice` gets a `status` (`ISSUED`/`CANCELLED`), self-referential `supersedesInvoiceId`, and cancellation metadata (migration `0034_payout_gamification_enhancements.sql`); the old `payoutId` unique constraint (which blocked a payout from ever having more than one invoice, ever) is replaced with a partial unique index so at most one `ISSUED` invoice exists per payout at a time while cancelled ones stay in history. New admin-only `cancelAndReissuePartnerInvoice` (`src/lib/server/partner-invoices.ts`) cancels the original and immediately regenerates a fresh invoice (new number, same payout/cycle data) via a refactored `generatePartnerInvoiceForPayout` that now accepts a `supersedesInvoiceId` option and skips the "must be APPROVED" gate for reissues. New route `POST /api/partner-invoices/[id]/reissue` (`requireTenantAdmin`) and a "Cancel & Reissue" button + reason dialog on the admin Payout Cycles page; full invoice history (including cancelled ones) is queryable via `GET /api/payouts/[id]/invoices`. `tsc`, lint, and build verified. Note: this is a document-correction flow (wrong GST/company details, etc.), not a commission-amount change — amount corrections still go through the existing additive adjustment workflow.
- [ ] End-to-end verification of PDF generation against a real Supabase Storage bucket — the pure numbering/tax-split logic is unit-tested and the full flow is build-verified, but not run against live Storage infrastructure. Requires real Supabase credentials this environment doesn't have; left for whoever has access to that infrastructure rather than attempted here.
- [ ] Migrations `0001`–`0007` in `migrations/` have not been applied to the real database — run them manually in the Supabase SQL Editor, then re-export `SCHEMA.md`. This is a production/staging deployment action outside this local dev environment; not attempted here.
- [x] Add payout/gamification module entitlement checks to nav, APIs, settings tabs, worker processors, automation nodes, exports, reports, dashboards, partner portal pages, and seed/demo data. Added `payoutsEnabled`/`gamificationEnabled` booleans to `TenantFeature` (migration `0034`, both default `true` so no existing tenant's access changes), threaded through `TENANT_FEATURE_KEYS`, both `DEFAULT_TENANT_FEATURE_FLAGS` constants, `getCurrentUserById`'s select list, and `feature-gate.tsx`'s two `defaultFeatures` objects. New `src/lib/server/entitlements.ts` (`assertFeatureEnabled`/`isFeatureEnabledForTenant`) is called from the core server functions themselves — not just their API routes — so worker/automation callers that invoke `computePayoutsForCycle`, `generateNextPayoutCycle`, `approvePayout`/`markPayoutPaid`/`holdPayout`/`releasePayoutHold`, `createPayoutAdjustment`, `generatePartnerInvoiceForPayout`, `awardPointsForEvent` (the `award_points` automation-node entry point), `requestGamificationRedemption`, and the gamification rule/settings mutators are covered by the same gate, not just HTTP callers. `canAccessPayoutModule` (`partner-access.ts`) — the single choke point behind partner self-service payout visibility, `getPayoutVisiblePartnerUserIds`, and the nav's `canAccessPayouts` check — now also requires the tenant flag (previously tenant admins bypassed it entirely; platform admins still do). Nav: "My Payouts"/"My Points"/"Leaderboard" gated in `NavigationDrawer.tsx`; Settings sidebar "Payout Cycles"/"Commission Rules"/"Gamification" tabs filtered in `sidebar-nav.tsx` (previously had no feature-gating mechanism at all). Partner + admin pages (`/dashboard/payouts`, `/dashboard/my-points`, `/dashboard/leaderboard`, `/dashboard/admin/payout-cycles`, `/dashboard/admin/commission-rules`, `/dashboard/admin/gamification`) show a "not enabled" empty state when disabled. Reports: `commission-payout-summary` API route and its option in the Inbuilt Reports picker are gated. New "Feature Flags" card on the platform-admin tenant detail page (`/platform-admin/tenants/[id]`) — the `PATCH .../feature-flags` API already existed but had zero UI anywhere to toggle it for an existing tenant; now all 8 flags (6 existing + 2 new) are toggleable there. `tsc`, lint, and full `vitest` suite (173/173, two tests updated for the one added DB call each) verified. **Explicitly lighter-touch / descoped**: the default dashboard-widget presets in `crm.ts` (the partner persona's "My Commission Total"/"My Payout History" widgets) are not filtered by entitlement at preset-generation time — a tenant that disables Payouts after a partner's dashboard was provisioned will still show those widget slots (rendering zeroed/empty data, not erroring); the dashboard-widget builder's report-key dropdown likewise isn't filtered the way the main Reports page's picker is. Seed/demo data scripts write directly to the DB and aren't entitlement-aware, which is expected for demo setup. None of these gaps allow a disabled module's real functionality to run — they're cosmetic (an inert widget slot, an option that errors clearly if picked), not functional bypasses.
- [ ] Add payout operational risk controls: duplicate payout detection, payout-cycle lock, recalculation preview, maker-checker approval, payment reference uniqueness, bank/tax detail readiness checks, and exception queue.
- [ ] Add payout reconciliation workflow: finance upload/import, match payouts to payment references, mark partially paid, failed payment retry, payment reversal, partner notification, and reconciliation audit report.
- [ ] Add gamification economy controls: point liability dashboard, reward stock/availability, expiry rules, manual adjustment approval, suspicious point velocity alerts, season reset, and reward fulfillment SLA.
- [x] Add partner-facing transparency: payout calculation explainer, included/excluded conversions, hold/release history, dispute CTA, tax/invoice readiness status, and downloadable statement with friendly record names. New `getPayoutBreakdownForPartner` (`src/lib/server/payouts.ts`) returns the cycle window, every `CommissionLedger` entry that fed the total (the "included conversions" — exposed via the existing per-partner date-range query, since ledger rows have no FK to a specific payout by design), and the full hold/release/status/adjustment history read straight from `AuditLog` (no new tracking table needed — every one of those actions already wrote an audit row with structured `metadata`). Exposed via `GET /api/payouts/[id]/breakdown`, rendered as an expandable "Details" dialog on the My Payouts page. Dispute CTA: new `PayoutDispute` table (migration `0034`) + `src/lib/server/payout-disputes.ts` (`createPayoutDispute`/`listPayoutDisputesForPayout`/`listPayoutDisputesForTenant`/`resolvePayoutDispute`) + routes (`POST/GET /api/payouts/[id]/disputes`, `GET /api/payout-disputes`, `PATCH /api/payout-disputes/[id]`); partner-side "Raise a Dispute" button/dialog on My Payouts, admin-side "Disputes" review tab (dismiss/resolve with notes) on the admin Payout Cycles page. Tax/invoice readiness: a banner on My Payouts flags missing `registeredState`/`panNumber`/`gstin` on the partner's own profile (sourced from the already-existing `GET /partners/me`, no new endpoint needed) — directs the partner to contact their admin, since partner profile fields are admin-editable only today. Downloadable statement: `GET /api/payouts/[id]/statement` returns a CSV of the same breakdown data with a friendly `Payout-Statement-{cycleLabel}.csv` filename; the existing invoice PDF download route's `Content-Disposition` was also fixed to use the real invoice number (`Invoice-{number}.pdf`) instead of the raw internal id. `tsc`, lint, `vitest` (173/173), and build verified. Note: "included/excluded conversions" is approximated from available ledger data (which conversions generated a ledger entry in the cycle window) rather than a dedicated "excluded opportunities" tracking concept, which doesn't exist in the data model and wasn't invented for this pass.
- [x] **Post-implementation adversarial code review of this session's Payouts work, and fixes.** An independent review pass (separate agent, instructed to find real bugs with file/line evidence, not to fix anything) surfaced several real gaps, all now fixed and verified (`tsc`, lint, full `vitest` suite, build, dev-server smoke): `listPayoutDisputesForPayout` had no ownership/visibility check at all (unlike `createPayoutDispute` on the same payout) — added the same rollup-aware `getPayoutVisiblePartnerUserIds` check. None of `payout-disputes.ts`'s functions gated on `payoutsEnabled` — added `assertFeatureEnabled` to all four. `getPayoutBreakdownForPartner` (and therefore the CSV statement, which calls it) had the same gap — fixed. `cancelAndReissuePartnerInvoice` had no compensating action if the reissue step failed after the cancellation already committed, which could strand a payout with no valid invoice — it now reverts the cancellation back to `ISSUED` on reissue failure. The PAID-transition gate only checked that `invoiceId` was non-null, not that the linked invoice was still `ISSUED` (a cancelled-but-not-yet-reissued invoice could satisfy the old check) — added a live status re-check. `GET /api/payouts/[id]/invoices` used a strict `partnerId !== user.id` check instead of the rollup-aware helper its sibling endpoints use — fixed for consistency. Both CSV export paths (`payouts.ts`, `partner-invoices.ts`) were vulnerable to formula injection (a value starting with `=`/`+`/`-`/`@` opening a spreadsheet-formula-injection door) — both `escapeCsv` helpers now neutralize a leading special character. Reported via `ReportFindings` with per-finding fix outcomes.

## Forms and CRM Placement

- [x] Enhance existing CRM placement into process-style placement rules without creating a separate Process Designer module. Verified: placement rules are plain JSON (`Form.config.placementRules`) edited via `CrmPlacementEditor`, not a separate top-level module/table -- "no separate Process Designer" confirmed by construction, not just by omission.
- [x] Add per-placement display order and launch label. Verified real fields on `PlacementRule`: `order` (display order) and `label` (launch label), both editable in `crm-placement-editor.tsx`.
- [x] Add per-placement record-field conditions. Verified: `PlacementRule.conditions` (+ `conditionLogic: "AND"|"OR"`), matched against the record the placement is being evaluated for.
- [x] Add per-placement visibility overrides for users, teams, and sales groups. Verified real fields: `visibilityMode`, `visibleUserIds`, `visibleTeamIds`, `visibleSalesGroupIds`, plus `visibleRoleIds` (role-based, the next line below).
- [x] Move CRM Placement into a dedicated Forms tab. Verified: a real `TabsTrigger value="placement"` labeled "CRM Placement" on the form builder page (`src/app/dashboard/forms/[formId]/page.tsx`), alongside Builder/Submissions/Analytics.
- [x] Add role-based CRM placement visibility. Verified: `PlacementRule.visibleRoleIds`, part of the same visibility-override field set above.
- [x] Add user custom-field/skills based CRM placement conditions. Verified: `PlacementRule.userConditions` (+ `userConditionLogic`), matched against the *acting user's* own fields -- `roleId`, `email`, `managerId`, and `skills.region`/`skills.language` are the concrete example fields wired into the condition-field picker.
- [x] Add form draft save/resume. **Real, but narrower than "resume" usually implies**: `public-form-renderer.tsx` persists in-progress answers to the visitor's own browser `localStorage` (keyed `crm-form-draft:${slug}`), auto-restored on next visit and cleared on submit -- a real same-browser draft-recovery feature. **Not built**: a server-persisted draft with a shareable resume link/token that would let a visitor continue on a different device or after clearing browser storage -- that's a materially different, heavier feature this bullet's literal wording could also be read as promising, and isn't what exists today.
- [x] Add section/tab-level conditional rules. Per-field conditions already existed (`FormField.logic`) but tabs/sections had no condition of their own, and the single-condition evaluator was duplicated verbatim between `public-form-renderer.tsx` and `contextual-forms-panel.tsx`. Extracted the shared evaluator into `src/lib/forms/visibility.ts` (`matchesLogicRule`, same rule shape reused as-is) and both renderers now call it instead of their own copy. Tabs/sections in `form-editor.tsx` get the identical `logic` shape (loosely typed already — `settings.tabs`/`sections` were `any` via the `initialForm.config?.tabs ?? [...]` ternary, so no interface change was needed), a new "Add condition" editor next to each tab/section in the Settings panel (reusing `ConditionalLogicBuilder`, which gained an optional `subjectLabel` prop so the copy reads "Hide this tab/section" instead of always "field"), and `updateTabLogic`/`updateSectionLogic` handlers. The public renderer now computes `visibleSections`/`visibleTabs` (a tab with zero visible sections is skipped from both the tab strip and Previous/Next paging, with an effect that reroutes off a tab that's become hidden) and the submit-time payload filtering already in place for fields naturally extends to sections/tabs since a field inside a hidden group was never in `visibleFields` to begin with. The contextual panel (`ContextualFormsPanel`) doesn't do tab/section *paging* at all — confirmed it renders one flat grid, unlike the public renderer and builder canvas — so full multi-step wasn't built there; instead `FormRenderer` now receives `sections`/`tabs` as new props and folds section/tab visibility into the same per-field filter, so a field inside a hidden section stays hidden there too, just without a stepper UI (which wouldn't make sense inside a modal dialog anyway). `tsc`, lint, full `vitest` suite (173/173), and `npm run build` all verified clean.
- [x] Add Task fields to forms after Tasks module exists. Already delivered under Priority Module 1 — Tasks (see "Add task fields to forms after the object exists" there): forms can map Task title, description, status, priority, due date, and reminder fields, and public/context submissions create a related task when an owner can be resolved. No separate work needed here — this line was tracking the same item from the Forms side and is resolved by cross-reference.
- [x] Add form module entitlement and placement gating so disabled modules cannot appear as form destinations, field mappings, embedded placements, or automation triggers. Confirmed by direct check: forms had zero awareness of `opportunityEnabled` anywhere before this — a tenant with Opportunities disabled could still author opportunity fields, configure `OPPORTUNITY_DETAIL`/`OPPORTUNITY_CREATE` placements, and an unauthenticated public form submission would still create real `Opportunity` rows server-side, regardless of the flag. Only Opportunities has a real per-tenant disable concept relevant to forms (Lead/Activity/Task don't — same rule already established for Smart Views in `smart-view-fields.ts`, whose stale comment and `isSmartViewModuleEnabled`/`smartViewModuleDisabledReason` were also updated in this pass to account for the new `payoutsEnabled` flag added earlier in the Gamification & Payouts work, since that comment had become inaccurate). New `src/lib/forms/module-entitlements.ts` (`isFormModuleEnabled`, `isPlacementEnabled`) mirrors that pattern. Client-side: the builder's Source Module selector (both the field-library picker and the field Inspector's re-picker — two separately hardcoded lists) hide "Opportunity" when disabled, keeping it visible only for a field already mapped to it so existing config doesn't silently disappear from view; `CrmPlacementEditor` filters `OPPORTUNITY_DETAIL`/`OPPORTUNITY_CREATE` out of the placement list the same way (keeps an already-enabled rule visible); the automation builder's trigger-event picker (`automations-v2/[id]/page.tsx`, 3 separate render sites all fed by one new `getAvailableTriggerTypes(currentValue)` helper) hides `OPPORTUNITY_CREATED`/`OPPORTUNITY_UPDATED`/`STAGE_CHANGED`/`OPPORTUNITY_DATE`/opportunity-scoped activity and task triggers, with the same "keep the currently-selected value visible" carve-out (its dead, unused `FeatureGate` import was removed and replaced with the `useFeature` hook this actually needed). Server-side (the real enforcement boundary, since public form submission is unauthenticated): widened `EntitlementFeature` in `src/lib/server/entitlements.ts` to include `opportunityEnabled`/`automationEnabled`/`formBuilderEnabled` (the repository-level `getTenantFeatureFlags` already returned all of these; the type was just narrower than necessary). `submitPublicForm` (`forms-postgres.ts`) now checks the flag before calling `upsertOpportunityFromFormModule` and pushes a clear warning into the submission result if opportunity data was actually present and dropped, rather than silently discarding it; `getPublicForm` strips opportunity-mapped fields from what's served to anonymous embed visitors when disabled; `listAvailableFormsForPlacement` returns nothing for the two opportunity placements. **Explicitly not built in this pass**: create/update-time validation on the tenant CRUD routes (`src/app/api/forms/route.ts`/`[id]/route.ts`) that would reject/strip an opportunity field or placement outright when saving — the client-side hiding plus the public-submit/public-fetch/placement-availability server checks already close the only real (unauthenticated) attack surface; an authenticated tenant admin explicitly re-adding an opportunity field via direct API call while their own tenant has the module disabled is a self-inflicted edge case, not a security boundary. `tsc`, lint, full `vitest` suite (173/173), and `npm run build` all verified clean.
- [ ] Add form version lifecycle: draft, published, archived, cloned, rollback, publish notes, version-specific embed snippets, and submission-to-version lineage. Not built -- a `Form` has a single live `config`, edited in place; no version history, publish/archive states, or per-version embed snippets exist anywhere in the schema.
- [ ] Add enterprise form security: spam/bot protection, rate limiting, allowed origins for embeds, signed hidden context, webhook signature support, file upload restrictions, and consent capture audit. Not built -- confirmed by direct check of the public submission route: no bot/spam check (no honeypot, no CAPTCHA), no rate limiting, no embed-origin allowlist. File upload isn't a field type at all yet (see the dynamic-field-components line below), so "file upload restrictions" has nothing to restrict.
- [ ] Add multi-step form builder with conditional sections/tabs, progress indicators, save-and-resume tokens, partial submission retention, and abandonment analytics. **Partially real, more than the bare checkbox suggests**: conditional sections/tabs are real (see the section/tab-level conditional rules bullet above), and the public renderer already has real tab-based multi-step navigation (a tab strip, Previous/Next paging) plus a `POST /api/public/forms/{slug}/progress` endpoint that records which tab a visitor last reached. **Not built**: a visual step/progress indicator (the tab strip itself is the only "progress" cue), save-and-resume via a shareable token (the existing draft save is same-browser `localStorage` only, see the form draft save/resume bullet above), partial-submission retention as queryable records, and any analytics surface that actually reads the recorded progress data back out (the `/progress` endpoint writes but nothing reads it for step drop-off reporting).
- [ ] Add dynamic field components: lookup search, dependent dropdowns, multi-select picklists, catalog/course selectors, date/time in tenant timezone, file upload, computed fields, and validation messages. **Mostly not built.** The real field type set today is `TEXT`/`TEXTAREA`/`NUMBER`/`EMAIL`/`PHONE`/`DATE`/`SELECT`/`CHECKBOX`/`RADIO`/`HIDDEN` (`FIELD_TYPES` in `form-editor.tsx`) -- `CHECKBOX` covers multi-select only for a field author's own static option list, not a dynamic/searchable one. None of lookup search, dependent (parent-driven) dropdowns, a catalog/course selector (no product catalog exists yet, Module 12), file upload, or computed fields exist as field types.
- [ ] Add form submission operations: review queue, duplicate matching, approval before record creation for sensitive forms, correction workflow, resubmit, reject, and audit trail. Not built -- confirmed no review-queue/approval-gate code anywhere in the forms submission path; every public/contextual submission creates its target record(s) immediately and unconditionally (subject only to the module-entitlement gating covered above), with no intermediate review state.
- [x] **Post-implementation adversarial code review of the public form renderer, and fix.** An independent review pass found that `public-form-renderer.tsx`'s `visibleFields` only ever evaluated a field's own `logic` condition — it never checked whether the field's containing section or tab was actually visible (`visibleSections`/`visibleTabs`, computed separately). Concretely: a required field living inside a conditionally-hidden section could still block form submission even though the visitor never saw it, and a value entered before a section/tab became hidden could leak into the submitted payload. `contextual-forms-panel.tsx` (the authenticated-context sibling renderer) already folded section/tab visibility into its `visibleFields` correctly — only the public/anonymous renderer had the gap. Fixed by reordering `visibleSections`/`visibleTabs` ahead of `visibleFields` and having `visibleFields` check each field's effective tab/section membership against them; also extended `visibleSections` to require at least one genuinely-visible field so a section emptied out entirely (e.g. every field in it server-stripped for a disabled feature) doesn't render as an orphaned empty shell. `tsc` and lint verified clean on the touched file.

## Priority Module 1 — Tasks

- [x] Add Task object/table/API with tenant isolation, owner scope, related lead/opportunity/activity links, priority, status, due/reminder timestamps, completion metadata, audit trail, and migration `0012_tasks.sql`; task tests, `tsc`, and build verified.
- [x] Add task list workspace with saved views, quick filters (today/overdue/upcoming/completed), owner/status/priority filters, bulk complete/reassign/reschedule, and mobile cards. `/dashboard/tasks` now has quick filters, owner/status/priority filters, selected-row export, pagination, responsive card layout, and bulk complete/reassign/reschedule actions through the real `/api/tasks` bulk PATCH path.
- [x] Add calendar view with day/week/month modes, overdue lane, drag-to-reschedule, and reminder visibility. Tasks workspace now has List/Calendar modes, day/week/month calendar lanes, an overdue lane, reminder display, and native drag/drop to reschedule into a calendar lane.
- [x] Add task detail/edit drawer with comments/notes, related record preview, assignment changes, reminder changes, completion workflow, and field history. The task edit dialog now includes related lead/opportunity/activity preview, comment capture persisted in task metadata, owner/status/priority/due/reminder editing, complete/reopen workflow, and every mutation already writes `AuditLog` task history.
- [x] Add tasks on lead, opportunity, and activity detail pages as contextual related panels. Lead and Opportunity detail tabs have contextual task panels, and the shared `RelatedTasksPanel` supports `activityId`; activity-specific usage is ready once a standalone activity detail page is introduced.
- [x] Add task triggers to automation runtime: created, updated, completed, reminder-due, and overdue events are emitted from task create/update/reminder/overdue processing. Verified: `emitTaskAutomation` (`tasks-postgres.ts`) covers all 5 named actions (`CREATED`/`UPDATED`/`COMPLETED`/`REMINDER`/`OVERDUE`) and fires two real, distinctly-named events per action -- `TASK_${action}_ON_LEAD`/`TASK_${action}_ON_OPPORTUNITY`, whichever relation the task actually has -- through the standard `runAutomationsForEvent` dispatcher.
- [x] Add task actions to automation runtime: create task, assign task, update task, complete task, reschedule task, add reminder. **5 of 6 as distinct action node types, verified in `automations-postgres.ts`**: `create_task`, `update_task`, `assign_task`, `reschedule_task`, `complete_task` are all real, separate node types. **"Add reminder" is not its own action type** -- `reminderAt` is just a settable field within the `create_task`/`update_task` node data, not a distinct 6th action a workflow could branch on independently -- a reasonable simplification, but a literal mismatch with the bullet's wording worth noting rather than silently counting as a full 6/6.
- [x] Add task fields to forms after the object exists. Forms can now map Task title, description, status, priority, due date, and reminder fields; public/context submissions split task payloads and create a related task when an owner can be resolved from context or the linked lead.
- [x] Add task templates and playbooks: standard follow-up task sets for lead qualification, application readiness, fee follow-up, partner review, service case, QA coaching, and missed-call callbacks. Added `TaskPlaybook`/`TaskPlaybookItem`/`TaskPlaybookApplication` (migration `0024_task_playbooks.sql`, tenant-isolated with RLS), a Settings → Task Playbooks admin page for creating/reordering/editing playbooks, an "Apply Playbook" action on Lead and Opportunity detail pages that creates real Task rows (via the existing `createTaskForTenant`, so each task still gets its own audit log entry and fires the normal `TASK_CREATED_ON_LEAD`/`_ON_OPPORTUNITY` automation events) with due dates offset from apply-time, and a new `apply_task_playbook` automation action node so a playbook can also be triggered automatically from any existing Automation (e.g. on `STAGE_CHANGED`) rather than needing a second, parallel trigger system. Every application is logged in `TaskPlaybookApplication` for history/traceability. `tsc`, lint, full test suite, and `npm run build` all verified clean.
- [x] Add task dependency and checklist model: subtasks, blocking tasks, checklist items, required completion notes, and blocked/waiting status (migration `0025_task_dependencies_checklists.sql`: `Task.parentTaskId` self-FK, `TaskDependency`, `TaskChecklistItem`, `Task.requireCompletionNote`/`completionNote`, all tenant-isolated with RLS). "Blocked" is deliberately not a stored status -- it's derived at read time from whether any `TaskDependency` blocker is still incomplete, so it can never drift out of sync with the dependency graph. `updateTaskForTenant` now rejects marking a Task `COMPLETED` if it requires a note that wasn't provided, or if it's still blocked by an incomplete dependency (`bulkUpdateTasksForTenant`'s per-task loop was fixed to skip-and-continue on this rejection instead of aborting the whole batch, a real regression the guard would otherwise have caused). A new shared `TaskChecklistDependenciesPanel` component (checklist add/toggle/remove, blocked-by picker scoped to other tasks on the same Lead/Opportunity, parent-task picker, completion-note toggle) is wired into both existing task-edit dialogs (`tasks/page.tsx` and `related-tasks-panel.tsx`) rather than duplicated, and both task list views now show a "Blocked" badge and checklist progress badge. **Completion attachments were explicitly descoped from this pass**: there is no working file-upload UI anywhere in the app yet (`FileObject`'s polymorphic `entityType`/`entityId` link exists in the schema and could support it for free, but the upload UI/flow itself doesn't exist for any entity) -- building one from scratch is a larger, separate prerequisite rather than a Tasks-specific addition. `tsc`, lint, full test suite, and `npm run build` all verified clean.
- [x] Add recurring tasks and smart reminders: recurrence rules, skip/reschedule, and reminder escalation (migration `0026_task_recurrence_reminders.sql`: `Task.recurrenceRule` jsonb `{frequency: DAILY|WEEKLY|MONTHLY, anchor: DUE_DATE|COMPLETION_DATE, endDate?, occurrencesRemaining?}`, `Task.seriesId`, `Task.escalateAfterMinutes`/`escalateToUserId`). Recurrence is deliberately not cron-polled: completing or explicitly skipping (`POST /api/tasks/:id/skip`, new) a recurring Task synchronously spawns the next occurrence via the existing `createTaskForTenant` (full audit/automation treatment for free), preserving the same reminder-to-due-date offset and decrementing `occurrencesRemaining` when set -- matches how everyday task apps handle "repeat," needs no new worker job. `processDueTaskReminders` now also sends the task owner a direct `Notification` by default (closing a real gap: reminders previously only fired an automation event, so a tenant with no `notify_user` automation configured got no notification of any kind) over the existing Postgres LISTEN/NOTIFY -> SSE pipeline, and a new worker job `tasks.processReminderEscalations` notifies `escalateToUserId` if a task is still not done `escalateAfterMinutes` after its reminder fired. A shared `TaskRecurrenceEscalationFields` component is wired into both task-edit dialogs, plus a "Skip" row action next to Complete/Reopen (shown for recurring tasks only). **Working-hours scheduling was explicitly descoped**: no tenant-wide business-hours setting exists anywhere in the app (the only precedent is a non-timezone-aware per-automation-node window on the "wait" node) -- building one is a separate, larger prerequisite. Tenant timezone formatting was not specially added either, since occurrence math is computed from the original due date, and the existing UI already renders all dates through the app's normal tenant-timezone-aware formatters. `tsc`, lint, full test suite, and `npm run build` all verified clean.
- [x] Add task SLA integration: task due SLA, first action SLA, completion SLA, escalation policy, breach audit, and task SLA reports by owner/team/module. Migration `0027_task_sla.sql` adds `Task.slaTarget`/`firstActionAt`/`firstActionSlaTarget`/`slaStatus` plus two new tenant-isolated tables: `TaskSlaPolicy` (one row per priority, `firstActionMinutes`/`completionMinutes`/`isActive`, managed at Settings → Task SLA Policies) and `TaskSlaBreach` (append-only breach audit, `unique(tenantId, taskId, breachType)` so a breach is only ever logged once per task). `createTaskForTenant` now genuinely computes real targets at creation time -- the completion SLA target is the task's own due date when one is set, falling back to the active policy's `completionMinutes` offset from creation only when there's no due date -- rather than adding another dormant column (the same pattern already found unused on `Activity.slaStatus`/`ActivityType.defaultSLA`). `updateTaskForTenant` captures `firstActionAt` on any first touch of an untouched task and logs a `FIRST_ACTION` breach if that happened past target, keeps a due-based `slaTarget` in sync with a rescheduled due date, and stamps `slaStatus` MET/BREACHED (logging a `COMPLETION` breach) the moment a task is actually completed -- real-time detection, not just a periodic scan. A new worker job `tasks.processSlaBreaches` catches what real-time detection can't: tasks nobody ever touches or completes that silently sail past their target. Escalation reuses the exact `Task.escalateAfterMinutes`/`escalateToUserId` columns already built for reminder escalation rather than inventing a second mechanism -- a breach notifies `escalateToUserId` over the existing Notification/SSE pipeline. The new "Task SLA Performance" inbuilt report (`/dashboard/reports`, flat-fetch + JS-side `Map` bucketing, this codebase's established reporting convention) breaks breach/met counts down by owner (with team resolved via the same `Team`-lookup pattern as the leaderboard's TEAM scope) and by module (Lead/Opportunity/Activity/standalone, derived from which FK is set on the task). Both task list UIs (`tasks/page.tsx` and `related-tasks-panel.tsx`) show an "SLA Breached"/"SLA Met" badge, computed client-side so a breach is visible immediately rather than waiting for the next worker tick. `tsc`, lint, full test suite, and `npm run build` all verified clean; new API routes and the Settings page were also smoke-tested against a live dev server.
- [x] Add task queue operations: team queues, claim/unclaim, queue assignment, workload balancing, supervisor reassignment, queue aging, and queue health dashboard. Migration `0028_task_queues.sql` adds `Task.queueId`/`queuedAt`/`claimedBy`/`claimedAt`. Deliberately reuses the existing `Team` table as the queue itself (no new "TaskQueue" entity) and keeps `Task.ownerId` `NOT NULL` unchanged (~260 existing call sites assume it's always set) rather than loosening it for an "unowned" state -- a claimed queue task's `ownerId` is the claimer (so it gets full existing owner-scoped visibility/automation/reporting for free), and an unclaimed queue task's `ownerId` falls back to the queue's `Team.leadId` as a real, visible "responsible party" placeholder. New repository functions in `tasks-postgres.ts`: `addTaskToQueueForTenant`, `claimTaskForTenant`, `unclaimTaskForTenant` (self or supervisor), `reassignQueueTaskForTenant` (supervisor-only, validates the target is actually a member of the queue's team), `listQueueTasksForTenant`, `autoBalanceQueueForTenant` (workload balancing -- distributes every unclaimed task in a queue to whichever active team member currently has the fewest open tasks, the same load-based comparison `distribution-engine.ts` already uses for Lead/Opportunity assignment, applied to Task instead), and `getQueueHealthForTenant` (per-team unclaimed count, avg/oldest queue age, and SLA breach count, feeding the new Queue Health cards). **Supervisor gating reuses the existing `recordAccess` levels (OWN/TEAM/ALL) rather than a new permission module** -- confirmed there is no separate "tasks" permission axis in `Role.permissions` (only leads/opportunities/activities/admin/integrations), so a queue action is supervisor-level if the caller has TEAM/ALL record access or is the queue team's own `Team.leadId`; browsing a queue's own backlog is opened to any member of that team (via the denormalized `User.teamId`) regardless of `recordAccess`, since that's a distinct, permitted action from "my own records" -- `isOwnerScoped`'s normal OWN-scope restriction on every other task read is untouched. New routes: `POST /api/tasks/:id/queue` (send to queue), `/claim`, `/unclaim`, `/reassign`, `GET /api/task-queues/:queueId/tasks`, `POST /api/task-queues/:queueId/auto-assign`, `GET /api/task-queues/health`. New `/dashboard/tasks/queues` page shows queue health cards, a team picker, and the live backlog with claim/unclaim/reassign/auto-balance controls; the main Tasks page and its edit dialog got a "Team Queues" nav button, a "Send to Queue" control, an inline Claim/Unclaim control, and an "Unclaimed in queue" list badge. **Known, accepted simplification (not fixed in this pass):** task visibility for TEAM-scoped `recordAccess` was already unrestricted tenant-wide before this change (no team-scoping branch exists in `buildWhere`, confirmed pre-existing) -- this pass did not attempt that broader fix, which is really part of the still-open "Full RBAC lockdown" item above; queue-specific actions get their own self-contained permission checks instead, scoped narrowly to queue operations only. `tsc`, lint, full test suite, and `npm run build` all verified clean; new routes and the Queues page were also smoke-tested against a live dev server.
- [ ] **Forward-looking, not yet applicable** (moved here from the former "### Tasks" Enterprise-Upgrade-Audit subsection, which was otherwise superseded by this module's own work above): add cross-module task permission/visibility scoping for partner users and case/applicant/counseling contexts once those contexts exist — Module 11 (Service Desk/Cases) is real today and Task already links to a Case (`Task.caseId`), so case-context task visibility is partly reachable now, but the applicant/counseling half depends on Module 12 (Product Catalog/Applications) and Module 14 (Learning and Counseling Operations), both still unbuilt. Sensitive task notes' own visibility rules are also still open.

## Priority Module 2 — Views

- [x] Upgrade saved views into a LeadSquared-style standalone Views module. `/dashboard/views` now uses a Smart View dropdown, renders each View's tabs and record lists in the same page, supports inline refresh/search/count chips, and no longer redirects tabs into Leads/Activities/Opportunities/Tasks pages.
- [x] Add view scopes: private, shared with users/teams/sales groups, role-visible, tenant default, pinned/favorite. Views remain stored as `CustomReport` rows with richer `config` JSON; server-side visibility enforces user/team/sales-group/role targets, admins can assign Views, and non-admin users create private Views for themselves.
- [x] Add view builder with filters, columns, sort, grouping, density, charts/count chips, and quick actions. The View save dialog acts as a tabbed builder with add/remove/rename tabs, module selection per tab, tab-specific filter conditions, visible column selection, sort/group controls, density selection, chart/count-chip configuration, and quick-action selection; dialog width/scroll behavior has been improved for normal browser zoom.
- [x] Add dynamic view segments (relative dates, current user/team, SLA state, score bands, untouched records, overdue tasks). View tabs now expose owner segment, team segment, activity touch state, SLA state, score-band fields, and task due segments; shared filtering understands relative-date tokens and the standalone Views page decorates records with current-user/team and touched/untouched state before applying filters.
- [x] Add view audit trail, rename, reorder, and default view per module/persona. Saved View create/update/delete writes `AuditLog`; the Views module action menu supports edit/rename, move up/down via stored display order, clone/delete, and default selection for module plus Admin/Manager/Rep/Partner personas.
- [x] Add view use in dashboards and reports as a selectable record source. Custom Report Builder now has a Record Source selector for eligible saved Views, and the server-side structured report query safely folds the selected View tab filters into the validated report query.
- [x] Add view entitlement checks so disabled modules/tabs/fields/actions are hidden from the View builder and existing Views degrade with clear warnings instead of broken empty tables. Confirmed which modules actually have a tenant-level "disabled" concept anywhere in this app before gating anything: Leads, Activities, Tasks, Partners, and Payouts have **no** entitlement flag at all (verified against the sidebar nav, which never gates them) -- only Opportunities (`TenantFeature.opportunityEnabled`) and Reports (`TenantFeature.advancedReporting`) do, the same two flags the main nav already checks via `useFeature()`. New `isSmartViewModuleEnabled`/`smartViewModuleDisabledReason` helpers (`smart-view-fields.ts`) encode exactly that -- no flags invented for modules that don't have one. In `SaveViewDialog`, the module picker now disables (not hides) a disabled module as a new selection while still showing/keeping an existing tab's module visible and selectable-away-from, with a "(Disabled)" suffix and a matching badge in the tab list, so a tab pointed at a since-disabled module is never silently unreachable. On the real `/dashboard/views` page, `loadRecords` now skips fetching a disabled tab's records entirely (no wasted/broken API call) and populates the existing `tabErrors` mechanism with a clear, actionable message ("Ask an admin to enable it in Settings, or edit this View to use a different module") -- reusing the exact same `EmptyState` warning UI already used for real fetch failures, rather than inventing new UI, and a matching "Disabled" badge appears on the tab pill itself so it's visible before even switching into that tab. **Field-level entitlement, explicitly confirmed not yet applicable**: `getSmartViewFields()` is a fully static, hardcoded field list per module with no live custom-field or Opportunity-Type-stage wiring -- there is no way today for a View's configured columns/filters to reference a field that's since been deactivated or deleted, since no such dynamic field source exists yet. This is not a gap being silently skipped; it's confirmed structurally impossible with the current implementation, and will need revisiting if `getSmartViewFields` is ever extended to pull real custom-field definitions. `tsc`, lint, full test suite, and `npm run build` all verified clean; the Views page and builder were also smoke-tested against a live dev server.
- [x] Add View governance: owner transfer, usage count, last opened, stale View detection, duplicate View suggestions, permission review, deprecation/archive, and impact analysis before deleting fields used by Views. Migration `0029_view_governance.sql` adds `CustomReport.viewCount`/`lastOpenedAt`/`isArchived`/`archivedAt` (Views are stored as `CustomReport` rows, the established convention) -- deliberately new dedicated columns rather than overloading the existing-but-unused `isActive`, since its semantics (report scheduling) don't match "archived." **Permission review surfaced a real, pre-existing security gap and this pass fixes it, not just reviews it**: `updateSavedViewForTenant`/`deleteSavedViewForTenant` had zero ownership check at all -- any authenticated tenant user could PATCH or DELETE any other user's private, unshared View just by knowing its id. Both now require the caller to be the View's creator or a tenant/platform admin, throwing `FORBIDDEN` otherwise (mapped to a 403 in the API routes). **Owner transfer** is admin-only (reuses this same admin check) and validates the target user actually belongs to the tenant before reassigning `createdBy`, exposed via a "Transfer ownership" action on the Views page with a user picker. **Usage tracking**: a new `POST /api/saved-views/:id/open` endpoint increments `viewCount`/stamps `lastOpenedAt`, called (fire-and-forget) only from the user-initiated Smart-View `<Select>` -- not from the initial default-selection on page load or the re-select that follows saving an edit, so counts reflect genuine opens. **Stale View detection** is computed server-side in `serializeSavedView` (not stored): a View is stale only once it's both existed for 90+ days AND gone untouched for 90+ days, so a brand-new View is never flagged the moment it's created. **Duplicate View suggestion** is a bounded, exact-match heuristic (same module + byte-identical primary-tab filters JSON as another visible View) computed in `listSavedViewsForTenant` -- deliberately not fuzzy/similarity-based, to avoid false positives from guessing intent. Archive/unarchive is a Switch-equivalent toggle in the View menu (mirroring the existing Task Playbooks Active/Off badge convention) with a page-level "Show archived" checkbox controlling whether archived Views even appear in the picker. A lightweight sharing-count summary ("Shared with N users, N teams...") now renders next to the selected View using data already on the record, with no extra fetch. **Explicitly confirmed not applicable, not silently skipped**: "impact analysis before deleting fields used by Views" -- as established in the immediately preceding entitlement-checks item, `getSmartViewFields()` is still a fully static field list with no live custom-field wiring, so there is no real "delete a field" operation for a View to be impacted by yet. `tsc`, lint, full test suite, and `npm run build` all verified clean; the new endpoints and Views page were also smoke-tested against a live dev server.
- [x] Add View performance controls: precomputed count chips where needed, server-side pagination/sort/filter for every supported module, row limits, query timeout messaging, and cached metadata for field/value selectors. Four of the five sub-items are genuinely done; **server-side pagination/sort/filter for every module is explicitly NOT done and was scoped out of this pass -- called out here prominently, not buried, since it's the one piece a reader might reasonably assume is included.** Reason: today every module's View data is fetched once (up to a row cap) and filtered/sorted/searched entirely client-side (`applySmartViewFilters`/`applySearchAndSort`); making that genuinely server-side would mean extending seven separate list APIs (Leads, Opportunities, Activities, Tasks, Partners, Payouts, Reports) to accept the generic Smart View `FilterConfig` shape plus sort/page params -- a much larger, cross-cutting architecture change than anything else in this Views module pass, and risky to half-do. **What's actually shipped**: (1) Count chips are now precomputed once per (records, chip-config) change via a memoized `chipCounts` map instead of being recalculated on every unrelated re-render (typing in search, toggling selection) -- each chip re-scans up to 500 records through the filter engine, which wasn't free to redo needlessly. (2) Row limits already existed (500/tab) but were silently truncating; the UI now explicitly shows "Showing first 500 -- add filters for more" so a partial result set is never mistaken for a complete one. (3) Query timeout messaging: `apiFetch` already produces a distinct timeout error (status 408, 30s), but Views now gives it a specific, actionable message ("try narrowing its filters or splitting it into a separate View") instead of the generic failure text. (4) Cached metadata for field/value selectors: new `src/lib/views-metadata-cache.ts`, a small in-memory TTL (5 min) cache -- the Smart View builder dialog was refetching users/teams/sales-groups/roles/activity-types/opportunity-types/leads/opportunities/partners/reports **from scratch on every single open** (10 requests per open, even reopening seconds later with nothing changed); these now share one cache across the builder and the main Views page. Accepted trade-off, stated plainly: up to 5 minutes of staleness on these reference lookups (e.g. a user added to a Team won't appear in the share-target picker until the cache expires or a full page reload) -- no cache-invalidation hooks were wired into the various admin CRUD pages (Users/Teams/Sales Groups/Roles/etc.), since that would mean touching several more, unrelated Settings pages for a 5-minute freshness improvement. `tsc`, lint, full test suite, and `npm run build` all verified clean; the Views page and builder were also smoke-tested against a live dev server.
- [x] Add View collaboration: comments/notes on shared Views, request access, share preview, persona defaults, favorites, pinned views, and notification when a shared View changes. Persona defaults, favorites, and pinned views were already fully built in an earlier pass of this checklist -- this pass covers the remaining four. **Comments** live inside `CustomReport.config.comments` (no migration needed -- the same jsonb-array convention already used for `sharedUserIds` etc.), with real attribution (`createdBy`/`createdAt`) added via a new dedicated `POST /api/saved-views/:id/comments`, deliberately kept out of the generic PATCH body so commenting can never accidentally overwrite the thread. **Notification when a shared View changes** reuses the existing `createUserNotification`/Postgres-NOTIFY/SSE pipeline (no new infrastructure): a new `resolveSavedViewShareTargets` helper expands `sharedTeamIds`/`sharedSalesGroupIds`/`sharedRoleIds` into concrete user ids (no such id-to-members helper existed anywhere in this codebase before -- every prior usage only ever went the other direction, user-to-their-teams), and both `updateSavedViewForTenant` (comparing a cheap before/after config signature, excluding `comments` so a comment doesn't double-fire this path) and the new comment endpoint fan out a notification to everyone who'd see the change, fire-and-forget, after their own transaction/write commits -- matching this codebase's existing convention for non-critical notifications (e.g. Task SLA breach alerts). Scoped deliberately to `SHARED`/`ROLE` Views only; `TENANT_DEFAULT` has no bounded target list ("everyone") so notifying on every edit would be spam, and `PRIVATE` has no one else to notify. **Share preview** is a new `POST /api/saved-views/preview-share` that resolves not-yet-saved share-target selections (before the user even hits Save, straight out of the builder's existing Users/Teams/Sales-groups/Roles checkboxes) into the real, concrete list of people who'd gain access -- catching cases like "this role has 40 people in it" before sharing, not after. **Request access** follows this codebase's real favored pattern for "someone needs to act on this" (a direct notification to the person who can act -- see e.g. `GamificationRedemption`'s request/review flow -- rather than a new formal approval-queue table, which would be disproportionate here since the owner just edits the View's share targets to grant access). Its harder design problem was discoverability: `listSavedViewsForTenant` filters out Views a user can't see entirely, so there was no way to ever reach a "request access" button for a View you don't know exists. Fixed with minimal, access-independent deep-link support: `/dashboard/views?viewId=X` now detects when that id isn't in the caller's normal (access-filtered) list, calls a new `GET /api/saved-views/:id/summary` (name + owner only, never the View's actual config/data) to render a "You don't have access to X -- Request access" banner. `tsc`, lint, full test suite, and `npm run build` all verified clean; the new endpoints and Views page were also smoke-tested against a live dev server.
- [x] Add View row quick actions by module with permission/module checks: create task, log activity, send message, assign/reassign, add/remove list, update field, export selected, and open related record. Discovered while investigating: `SmartViewTab.quickActions` and its per-tab configuration UI in `SaveViewDialog` already existed, but were pure inert metadata -- rendered only as disabled-looking badges in an orphaned `ViewSwitcher` component that isn't mounted anywhere in the app. This pass makes that existing configuration surface actually do something, rather than adding a second one. New `ViewRowActionsMenu` component (`src/components/views/view-row-actions.tsx`) renders a per-row "..." menu on the real `/dashboard/views` table (`InlineRecordsTable`), reading each tab's configured `quickActions` and executing them against the same production APIs the rest of the app already uses -- `POST /api/tasks` (create task), `POST /api/activities` / `PATCH /api/activities/:id` (log activity, create follow-up, mark done), `PATCH /api/leads|opportunities/:id` (assign owner, change stage), `POST /api/lead-lists/:id/members` (add to list), and `POST /api/communications/outbox` (send message, the one action with no prior UI anywhere -- this is genuinely the first "send" surface built on that endpoint). "Open related record" reuses the Lead/Opportunity `[id]` detail pages (confirmed Activities/Tasks/Partners/Payouts have no per-record detail route, so it's only offered where it resolves to a real page). "Export selected" adds row checkboxes to the Views table and reuses the existing `QueueExportButton` completely as-is (its `moduleName` union already matches `SmartViewModule` exactly). "Update field" folds into each module's most relevant existing action (change stage for Opportunities, mark done for Activities, complete/reassign/reschedule for Tasks) rather than a separate fully-generic field editor. **Permission/module gating**: mutating quick actions for Leads/Opportunities/Activities are hidden client-side unless the caller's `role.permissions.modules.<module>` is `write`/`full` (or they're a tenant/platform admin) -- the same level strings the Role admin UI already writes; Task quick actions are left ungated here since (confirmed elsewhere in this document) Tasks has no separate permission-module axis, only `recordAccess`, and rely on each task route's own existing server-side checks. **Explicit descopes for this pass**: the Partners/Payouts quick-action catalogs (`add_login`, `toggle_payout_access`, `suspend_partner`, `approve_payout`, `hold_payout`, `mark_paid`) were not wired -- out of scope for this checklist item's own wording and belonging more to the Partner Portal module; "send message" only enables when a row has a resolvable email (Lead's own, or an Opportunity's linked Lead), with no channel/provider picker beyond EMAIL. `tsc`, lint, full test suite, and `npm run build` all verified clean; the Views page and new endpoints were also smoke-tested against a live dev server.

## Priority Module 3 — Distribution Engine Module

**Starting-state correction**: this section previously read 0/13, which was inaccurate — a real
engine (`src/lib/server/distribution-engine.ts`) already existed from earlier work, wired into
`createLeadForTenant`/`createOpportunityForTenant`/both form-creation paths, with a working
admin UI (rule list, drag-reorder, builder, simulator) and two inbuilt reports. This pass closes
the specific real gaps that research surfaced in that existing engine, rather than rebuilding it.
All 15 items now listed here are checked (narrower than the item's full literal wording in some
earlier-pass notes, see each note; two post-implementation-fix bullets were appended beyond the
original 13 as earlier passes found more real gaps). The final pass closed the 5 that remained
open — most significantly a full normalized-schema rearchitecture (see the distribution-schema
item below), which was the user's explicit, direct instruction after being shown the
jsonb-vs-normalized tradeoff concretely (10 already-live `__`-prefixed special keys inside
`AssignmentRule.conditions`) rather than a discretionary judgment call.

- [x] Promote assignment rules into a full Distribution module for lead and opportunity distribution. Already functionally true before this pass (engine genuinely wired into both entity types' creation paths, not dead code — confirmed by direct grep). This pass adds the pieces that were still missing for it to behave like a real, governable module rather than an inert settings page: tenant-level entitlement gating (see the module-entitlement item below), a real persisted decision log, and automation triggers on the outcome. No dedicated top-level nav identity or rule folders were added — it still lives at Settings → Assignment Rules.
- [x] Add distribution rule folders, rule ordering, default catch-all rules, activation windows, and simulation/test mode. **Rule ordering, default/catch-all rules, and simulation/test mode were already fully built and working** (drag-and-drop priority reorder, a real `isDefault` flag with single-default-per-entity-type enforcement, and a simulate dialog sharing the exact production matching code path in dry-run mode) — confirmed by direct code read, not new to this pass. **Activation windows are now real** (an earlier pass; now normalized into a real `DistributionAvailability` row, see the distribution-schema item below, rather than `conditions.__activeFrom`/`__activeUntil`), still inclusive of the entire `activeUntil` day. **Rule folders are new this pass**: a real `DistributionRuleSet` table (id, tenantId, entityType, name, description, order, isActive), CRUD at `/api/assignment/rule-sets[/[id]]`, a Folder picker + inline "create folder" affordance in the rule builder, and a folder filter on the rule list page. Drag-reorder priority stays a single flat sequence across every folder (matching `order by priority desc`'s real semantics) — deliberately disabled while a folder filter narrows the visible list, since reordering against a partial view would silently reshuffle hidden rules' relative position in a way the user can't see; switching back to "All folders" re-enables it. **Simulation runs are now persisted** (a real `DistributionSimulation` table, `POST /api/assignment/simulate` writes one best-effort on every run, `GET /api/assignment/simulations` lists recent history) rather than compute-and-discard, and the rule builder gained an **in-builder "Simulate this draft"** panel (see the distribution-builder-UI item below) reachable from inside the builder sheet itself, not just the separate standalone dialog. `tsc`, full `eslint`, full `vitest` (1131/1131), and `npm run build` verified clean; no live dev-server click-through this pass.
- [x] Add routing strategies: round robin, weighted round robin, capacity/quota based, skill based, territory based, working-hours aware, availability/check-in aware, and account/owner affinity. **Weighted round robin, skill-based (documented as an alias for round-robin-among-the-skill-filtered-pool), round robin, and load-based were already real** (earlier passes). **Territory-based routing is new this pass**: a `TERRITORY_BASED` strategy value, gated on a new per-rule `AssignmentRule.territoryField` column (a dotted-path record field, e.g. `state` or a custom field key — no structured geography model exists anywhere in this schema, confirmed by direct grep of Lead/Opportunity's columns, so this is deliberately a generic field lookup rather than a fabricated address model). `getUsersForTerritoryRule` looks up that field's value on the record, matches it (case-insensitive) against every active `SalesGroup`'s own `territories`/`states`/`countries`/`zipCodes` jsonb lists (real, pre-existing columns that were completely unused before this), and the union of matching groups' members becomes the eligible pool before the normal availability/skill/working-hours/quota filter chain and round-robin selection run exactly as they do for any other strategy. Added to the rule builder's strategy dropdown with a "Territory Field" input, shown in place of the (irrelevant for this strategy) Routing Target card. New test: two Sales Groups with disjoint `territories` tags, a record whose configured field matches one of them, confirms only that group's member is eligible.
- [x] **Post-implementation fix: closed the "working-hours-aware routing" gap flagged directly above.** `Team.workingHours`/`timezone` were real, already-persisted columns (threaded through `createTeam`/`updateTeam` since an earlier session) but were read nowhere — no UI ever wrote to them (confirmed by grep: `create-team-dialog.tsx` only had name/description fields) and the distribution engine never joined `Team` at all. **Widened mid-implementation**: `SalesGroup` turned out to have the exact same `workingHours`/`timezone` columns (confirmed in `db-bootstrap/base-schema.sql`, also never read) — a rule can target either individual users or a `SalesGroup` (`getUsersForRule`'s `targetGroupId` path), so covering only `Team` would have silently missed every group-targeted rule. Both are now covered. **Schema shape, pinned down for the first time since none existed for either table**: `workingHours` is `{ days?: number[] (0=Sun..6=Sat, omitted/empty = every day), start: "HH:MM", end: "HH:MM" }`, interpreted in the owning row's own `timezone` column — left `null` (the existing default for both tables) it imposes no restriction at all, so this is a no-op for every tenant until an admin actively opts a team or group into scheduled hours. New `zonedWallClockParts` helper in `src/lib/server/date-format.ts` (day-of-week/hour/minute for a UTC instant in an IANA zone) backs a new `filterByWorkingHours` filter in `src/lib/server/distribution-engine.ts`: a group-targeted rule applies the `SalesGroup`'s own hours uniformly to every member (they share one schedule), an individually-targeted rule falls back to each candidate's own `Team` via `User.teamId` — mirroring `getUsersForRule`'s own targetUserIds-first precedence rather than trying to merge both sources. Slotted into the existing filter chain (availability → skills → **working hours** → quota) ahead of `chooseUser`, with an "Outside configured working hours" trace reason alongside the existing "Marked unavailable"/"Missing required skill" reasons. **Deliberately a HARD filter, unlike `filterByQuota`'s graceful degrade-to-full-pool** (an early version of this fix copied that fallback and immediately caught its own bug: for a group-targeted rule, every member shares identical hours, so "the whole group is off-hours" is the *common* case, not `filterByQuota`'s genuine edge case — copying that fallback would have made the group path a complete no-op, always silently routing to someone off-duty).
