Quota is explicitly documented as a soft preference; "is this person actually on the clock" is a real constraint the same way availability/skills already are. When every candidate is outside hours, this correctly returns zero candidates, and `resolveDistribution` falls through to the rule's own configured fallback user if one exists, or records "no eligible candidate" if not — exactly like an unmatched availability/skill filter already does today, not a new mechanism. Frontend: `create-team-dialog.tsx` gained an optional "Working Hours" section (start/end time inputs, a plain IANA-timezone text field — no timezone-picker component exists anywhere in this codebase to reuse, and building one was out of scope — and day-of-week checkboxes), wired straight through the already-permissive `updateTeamForTenant(user, id, input: Record<string, unknown>)` API with no backend route changes needed; `SalesGroup`'s own settings UI was not touched in this pass (already has no working-hours fields either, but Team was the concretely-flagged gap). New tests in `tests/distribution-engine-postgres.test.ts` (7 new, using `vi.useFakeTimers`/`vi.setSystemTime` for deterministic time-of-day assertions — the first use of fake timers in this test suite, chosen over threading a testability-only `now` parameter through the public `distributeRecord`/`simulateDistribution` API) cover: excluded outside configured hours, included inside configured hours, excluded on a non-working day, unassigned (not silently routed) when every candidate is outside hours, the rule's own fallback user is used in that case when configured, a `SalesGroup`-targeted rule applies the group's hours uniformly, and a team with no `workingHours` configured imposing no restriction. `tsc`, full `eslint`, full `vitest` suite (280/280, 7 new), `npm run build`, and a live dev-server smoke test all verified clean.
- [x] **Post-implementation fix: closed the "account/owner-affinity" gap flagged directly above.** New `STICKY_TO_OWNER` strategy in `chooseUser` (`src/lib/server/distribution-engine.ts`) routes a new record back to whoever already owns the "related" record, instead of round-robining to someone new. No `Account` object exists in this schema (confirmed, same finding as the routing-strategies note above), so this is scoped to the two relationships that actually exist: an Opportunity's parent Lead's current owner (`record.leadId` → `Lead.ownerId`), and — for a Lead itself — an existing, already-owned Lead sharing the same email (a returning/duplicate contact routes back to the rep already working them, case-insensitive match, excluding the new record's own id). New `resolveStickyOwnerId` helper does the lookup; `chooseUser` now takes the record as a parameter (previously it only received the pre-filtered candidate pool, not the record itself — had to be threaded through from `resolveDistribution`'s call site). **Correctness detail**: a sticky match short-circuits before the round-robin cursor logic runs, and deliberately does not advance the cursor — picking the sticky owner is a special-case pick, not that rule's queue turn, so it must not perturb the round-robin order for other candidates. If there's no related record, or the prior owner isn't in this rule's eligible pool (availability/skill/quota-filtered), it falls through to plain round robin among the actual candidates, which does advance the cursor as normal. Added "Account/Owner Affinity" to the strategy dropdown in the rule builder (`rule-builder.tsx`) with inline help text; no other rule-builder UI changes needed since (unlike WEIGHTED) this strategy has no per-user config of its own. New tests in `tests/distribution-engine-postgres.test.ts` (3 new) cover: Opportunity routes to its Lead's owner without advancing the cursor, a returning Lead (case-insensitive email match) routes to the existing owner, and the fallback-to-round-robin path (no related record, or prior owner outside the eligible pool) both picks correctly and does advance the cursor. `tsc`, full `eslint`, full `vitest` suite (270/270, 3 new), `npm run build`, and a live dev-server smoke test all verified clean.
- [x] Add user/team quotas, daily caps, open-workload caps, holidays, working hours, and pause/availability states. Open-workload cap (soft, degrades gracefully when everyone's over), the manual pause/"away" toggle, and working hours (see the earlier working-hours-aware-routing fix above) were already real. **This pass adds the time-windowed daily/weekly cap** that was the one specifically-flagged gap: new `filterByWindowCap` in `distribution-engine.ts`, a per-rule `__maxAssignmentsPerWindow`/`__windowPeriod` (`DAY`|`WEEK`) condition pair (same `__`-prefixed convention as `__maxAssignmentsPerUser`), and a "Max new assignments per period" number+period input in the rule builder's Workload Limits card. **Deliberately distinct from the existing standing quota** (`__maxAssignmentsPerUser`/`filterByQuota`, which counts currently-open records a user holds and shrinks as they close things out): this is a THROUGHPUT cap, counting how many new records `AssignmentLog` shows were routed to a user within a rolling 24h/7-day window from "now" (not calendar-day/week-aligned, to avoid a per-user/team timezone dependency this engine otherwise only needs for working hours). Sourced from `AssignmentLog` rather than `Lead`/`Opportunity."createdAt"`, since that's the only place that records exactly when a record was routed to its *current* owner — a record reassigned today counts against today's cap even though its own `createdAt` is unrelated. Same graceful-degrade philosophy as the standing quota (soft preference, not a hard block like working hours): if every candidate is already at their window cap, distribution still happens rather than leaving the record unassigned. Slotted into the filter chain right after the standing quota, with a new "Reached daily/weekly assignment cap" trace reason surfaced in the simulate dialog. 5 new tests in `tests/distribution-engine-postgres.test.ts` cover: a user at their daily cap is skipped in favor of one who isn't, an assignment outside the rolling window doesn't count (cap effectively resets over time), a WEEK-period cap counts a few-days-old assignment a DAY-period cap wouldn't, graceful degrade to the full pool when everyone's at cap, and the simulator's skip-reason trace. `tsc`, full `eslint`, full `vitest` suite (285/285, 5 new), and `npm run build` all verified clean; a live authenticated dev-server click-through of the rule builder's new field was not performed in this pass. **Still not built**: team-level (as opposed to per-user) caps and holiday/working-hours-calendar exceptions (the existing working-hours filter only understands a recurring weekly schedule, not one-off dates).
- [x] Add distribution logs with explainability: matched rule, skipped users with reasons, selected user, reassignment source, and SLA impact. **The live explainability trace** (matched rule, every candidate with its `excludedReason` when skipped, selected user, whether a fallback was used) already existed and is real, surfaced in the simulate dialog. What this pass fixes: **`AssignmentLog` was dead schema** — a real table with the right shape for exactly this (`assignedToId`, `assignedById`, `ruleId`, `reason`, `assignedAt`) that nothing had ever inserted into, meaning any custom report or query against it silently returned zero rows. `writeAssignmentLog` (`distribution-engine.ts`) now inserts into it for every real decision — both rule-based auto-assignment and the new manual reassignment below — alongside the pre-existing `AuditLog action='ASSIGN'` write. "Reassignment source" is now real (`ruleId: null, strategy: "MANUAL"` distinguishes a manual reassignment from a rule-based one in the log). **Post-implementation fix, this pass**: the full skip-reason trace is now persisted per real decision too — `AssignmentLog` gained a `trace` jsonb column, populated on every `writeAssignmentLog` call (both rule-based and manual) with the exact same `DistributionTrace[]` the simulate dialog computes, not just the final outcome/reason string. Per-decision SLA impact remains aggregate-only via the `reassignment-impact` report — a genuine per-decision SLA field doesn't exist on Lead/Opportunity to attach one to (see the reassignment-governance item below for the concrete workload/continuity-based "impact preview" built instead, which is a different, narrower thing than what this bullet originally asked for).
- [x] Add bulk redistribution, manual redistribution, reassignment approval controls, and fairness/performance reports. **Manual and bulk redistribution are new this pass**: previously, reassigning a Lead/Opportunity's owner (the "Assign owner" row action, and there was no bulk equivalent at all) was a bare `PATCH {ownerId}` with no reason, no audit trail beyond the generic record-update log, and no notification. New `reassignRecordOwner`/`bulkReassignRecordOwners` (`distribution-engine.ts`) require a reason, write the same real `AssignmentLog`/`AuditLog` decision log rule-based distribution does, and notify the previous owner. New `POST /api/assignment/reassign` (single) and `POST /api/assignment/reassign/bulk` (up to 500 records, sequential so one bad id can't take out the rest of the batch); the existing single-row quick action (`view-row-actions.tsx`) now requires a reason and calls the governed endpoint instead of the raw PATCH; a new "Assign" bulk-toolbar action wired on both the Leads and Opportunities list pages opens a picker+reason dialog calling the bulk endpoint. Fairness/performance reports (`distribution-fairness`, `reassignment-impact`) already existed and now have real `AssignmentLog` data to draw on instead of an empty table. **Post-implementation note**: reassignment approval controls are now built too — see the reassignment-governance item below for the full detail.
- [x] Add distribution automation actions and triggers for distribution success/failure/reassignment. New `LEAD_DISTRIBUTION_SUCCESS`/`LEAD_DISTRIBUTION_FAILED`/`OPPORTUNITY_DISTRIBUTION_SUCCESS`/`OPPORTUNITY_DISTRIBUTION_FAILED` events, fired via the existing `runAutomationsForEvent` after `distributeRecord`'s transaction commits (fire-and-forget, matching every other automation-trigger call site), and added to the automation builder's trigger-type picker (already covered by the existing opportunity-module entitlement filter, since they share the "opportunity" scope). **Not built**: a distinct "reassignment" trigger event for the new manual/bulk reassignment path (deliberately — an admin governing reassignment through automations wasn't part of this pass's scope) and a dedicated "invoke distribution" automation *action* node (the `assign_owner` action node still only writes a static, node-configured owner; it does not call back into the distribution engine).
- [x] Add distribution module entitlement and API guards so assignment logic can be disabled per tenant while preserving basic manual assignment. `DISTRIBUTION` was already a cataloged key in `PlatformModule` (migration `0037`) but nothing ever checked it — a tenant admin could toggle it off in the module catalog UI and it would have zero effect; `distributeRecord` kept running unconditionally. Fixed: `distributeRecord` now checks `isModuleEnabledForTenant` and returns a graceful no-op result (not a thrown error, since every call site already wraps it in `.catch(() => null)`) when disabled; the four assignment-rule CRUD/reorder mutations gained `assertModuleEnabled`, matching the pattern established for Next-Best-Action/Journey-Orchestration earlier this session. "Preserving basic manual assignment" is true by construction, not by a special-case: the new governed reassignment functions never call `distributeRecord` or check this flag at all, so manual assignment keeps working exactly as before regardless of the module's status. Also fixed, found while touching these routes: **the assignment-rules CRUD/reorder/simulate API routes had no RBAC at all** (`requireCurrentUser` only — any authenticated internal user, not just tenant admins, could create/edit/delete/reorder distribution rules or run simulations), unlike every sibling admin-configuration surface (e.g. commission rules) which requires `requireTenantAdmin`. All four now do.
- [x] Add distribution schema: `DistributionRuleSet`, `DistributionRule`, `DistributionCondition`, `DistributionTarget`, `DistributionQuota`, `DistributionAvailability`, `DistributionSimulation`, and `DistributionDecisionLog`. **Full normalized rearchitecture, built this pass per explicit user direction** (shown the concrete tradeoff — 10 already-live `__`-prefixed special keys inside `AssignmentRule.conditions` — after which the user chose full normalization over documenting-and-deferring). Migration `0082_distribution_normalization.sql`: real `DistributionRuleSet` (folders), `DistributionCondition` (field/operator/value triples, one row per condition — replacing the old dual-shape bare-string-or-`{operator,value}`-object jsonb value with an always-explicit operator), `DistributionTarget` (one row per rule×user, `isPoolMember`/`isFallback`/`weight`/`fairnessCredit` — consolidating the old `targetUserIds` array, `__fallbackUserId`, `__userWeights`, and the engine's own running `__weightedState` fairness credit into a single table), `DistributionQuota` (one optional row per rule), `DistributionAvailability` (one optional row per rule: `activeFrom`/`activeUntil`/`requiredSkills`), and `DistributionSimulation` (persisted simulation runs, closing that specific sub-gap too). **Two deliberate namings that deviate from a literal reading of the list, called out explicitly rather than silently diverging**: `DistributionRule` is not a new table — `AssignmentRule` already was that table (a real row per rule, never jsonb) and is kept, extended with `ruleSetId`, `roundRobinCursor` (a real column now, replacing `conditions.__roundRobinCursor`), and `territoryField`; renaming ~10 call sites/tests for a pure label change was judged not worth the churn. Likewise `DistributionDecisionLog` is not a new parallel table — `AssignmentLog` already serves exactly that role (confirmed real and populated by an earlier pass) and was extended in place with a `trace` column (persisting the full per-candidate skip-reason trace for every real decision, not just the final outcome/reason string — closing that specific sub-gap from the explainability item above) rather than duplicated under a new name. **Data migration, not just schema**: the same migration file parses every existing `AssignmentRule.conditions`/`targetUserIds` row into the 4 new child tables via pure SQL (`jsonb_each`/`unnest`, generated ids via `md5(random()::text || clock_timestamp()::text || ...)` since no uuid/pgcrypto extension is enabled anywhere in this schema), preserves `__roundRobinCursor`'s in-flight position into the new column, and only then drops the now-fully-superseded `conditions`/`targetUserIds` columns — verified against the real local dev database (2 pre-existing rules, correctly backfilled, columns dropped, `tsc`/`npm run build`/full test suite still clean afterward). Every read/write path was rewired: `distribution-engine.ts`'s `resolveDistribution` now batch-loads all 4 child tables per matching pass (4 queries regardless of rule count, replacing what used to be zero extra queries when everything lived in one jsonb column — an explicit, discussed-with-the-user performance/maintainability tradeoff of normalization) via a new `loadRuleBundles`; `chooseUser`'s WEIGHTED branch reads/writes `DistributionTarget.fairnessCredit` directly (a single bulk `UPDATE ... FROM unnest(...)` for all candidates per pick, not one query per user); `admin-modules.ts`'s rule CRUD writes all 4 child tables inside one transaction per save, and update preserves `DistributionTarget.fairnessCredit` for users who remain in the pool/fallback slot across an edit (the normalized-schema equivalent of the old jsonb rebuild's careful `__weightedState` preservation, now structural rather than a manually-maintained property since fairness credit isn't commingled with the rest of the rule's config anymore). New `PrivilegedActionRequest` action type `DISTRIBUTION_REASSIGNMENT` and 3 new `SecurityPolicy` columns (`reassignmentApprovalRequired`, `reassignmentLimitCount`, `reassignmentLimitWindowDays`) back the reassignment-governance item below. `tests/distribution-engine-postgres.test.ts` fully rewritten for the normalized mock shape (35 tests, up from 23); `tsc`, full `eslint`, full `vitest` (1131/1131), and `npm run build` all verified clean.
- [x] Add distribution builder UI with rule folders, drag ordering, activation windows, ownership fallback, exception handling, target pools, dynamic field/value selectors, and simulation before publish. Drag ordering, ownership fallback, target pools, and activation windows were already real. **New this pass**: rule folders (a Folder picker + inline create in the builder, backed by the new `DistributionRuleSet` table above); a real field/operator picker (`src/components/common/condition-builder.tsx`'s pre-existing generic `ConditionBuilder`, previously used by commission-rules/Next-Best-Action but never wired to real object metadata anywhere in this codebase — confirmed by grep) replacing the old two-free-text-input UI, bound to each entity type's system fields plus its **live custom fields** fetched from `/custom-fields?objectType=LEAD|OPPORTUNITY` (`listCustomFieldsForTenant`/`FieldDefinition` — a genuine improvement over commission-rules'/NBA's still-static-only field lists, though retrofitting those two was out of scope here); and **in-builder simulation of an unsaved draft rule** — a "Simulate this draft" panel inside the builder sheet itself (not just the separate standalone dialog), which posts the current unsaved form/config plus a real sample record to `simulateDistribution(..., draftRule)`, a new optional parameter that bypasses the database rule fetch entirely and evaluates exactly that hypothetical rule in isolation (forced dry-run — a draft has no real row to advance a cursor or fairness credit on). Territory-based routing's "Territory Field" input (see the routing-strategies item above) is also new builder UI.
- [x] Add fairness and capacity controls: daily/weekly caps, open lead/opportunity caps, weighted shares, team capacity, partner capacity, counselor skill tags, holidays, working hours, and pause/OOO states. Open lead/opportunity caps, pause/OOO, and skill tags were already real; weighted shares are now real (see the routing-strategies item above); **daily/weekly time-windowed caps are now real too** (see the user/team-quotas item above for the full implementation detail — same fix, listed again here since this item enumerates it from the capacity-controls angle). **Still not built**: team-level (as opposed to per-user) capacity, partner capacity, and holiday/working-hours calendars.
- [x] Add reassignment governance: approval-required redistribution, reason capture, previous owner notification, SLA impact preview, reassignment limits, and audit history. **Reason capture, previous-owner notification, and audit history were already real** (an earlier pass). **All 3 remaining sub-items built this pass**: (1) **Approval-required workflow** — reuses the `PrivilegedActionRequest` mechanism built earlier this session (Module 18) rather than inventing a parallel one: a new `DISTRIBUTION_REASSIGNMENT` action type (the closed-set CHECK constraint extended via migration `0082`), gated by a **dedicated** `SecurityPolicy.reassignmentApprovalRequired` toggle (deliberately not reusing the existing `privilegedActionApprovalRequired` flag, which already gates 2 unrelated actions with their own hardcoded settings-page copy — folding reassignment approval into the same switch would silently change behavior for any tenant that had already turned it on for those). When enabled, `reassignRecordOwner` creates a request and returns `{pendingApproval: true, requestId}` (surfaced as HTTP 202, distinct from a normal 200) instead of writing immediately; a distinct second admin approves it via the pre-existing `/dashboard/settings/privileged-actions` queue (extended with the new action type + label, no new UI surface needed), which executes the real reassignment through a new `executeReassignment` function — the original write logic, factored out of `reassignRecordOwner` so both the immediate and approval-gated paths share it exactly. (2) **SLA-impact preview** — no SLA/response-time field exists on Lead/Opportunity anywhere in this schema to attach a "per-decision SLA impact" to (confirmed by direct schema read), so this is built as a concrete, real "impact preview" instead: a new `previewReassignmentImpact` function and `POST /api/assignment/reassign/preview` route reporting the new owner's current/after open-record workload, whether that would exceed any active rule's own configured per-user quota for that entity type, and continuity-risk signals (record age, days since last activity) — wired into the "Assign owner" dialog (`view-row-actions.tsx`), fetched automatically as soon as a target user is picked and shown before the reason is even required. (3) **Reassignment-count limits** — 2 new `SecurityPolicy` columns (`reassignmentLimitCount`, `reassignmentLimitWindowDays`, both nullable = no limit), checked in `reassignRecordOwner` against `AssignmentLog` rows for that specific record with `ruleId is null` (the real "was this a manual reassignment" signal — `AssignmentLog` has no `strategy` column of its own, only `AuditLog`'s diff jsonb carries that label) within the rolling window; exceeding it throws `REASSIGNMENT_LIMIT_EXCEEDED`, surfaced as a real error message through both the single and bulk reassignment API routes. All 3 settings exposed on `/dashboard/admin/security` under a new "Reassignment Governance" card. `bulkReassignRecordOwners`'s return shape gained a `pendingApproval` count alongside `reassigned`/`failed`, surfaced in both the Leads and Opportunities bulk-assign toolbar toasts. New tests cover: a reassignment creates a pending request (not an immediate write) when approval is required, the limit throws once reached within the window, and the impact-preview's workload/quota-exceeded computation. `tsc`, full `eslint`, full `vitest` (1131/1131), and `npm run build` all verified clean; no live dev-server click-through this pass.
- [x] Add distribution tests for rule priority, skip reasons, cap exhaustion, fallback assignment, partner/user/team visibility, concurrency, idempotency, and tenant/module disabled behavior. Rewrote `tests/distribution-engine-postgres.test.ts` from call-order-based mocks to this session's hoisted-state SQL-matching convention (needed regardless, to support the new `withTransaction`/module-entitlement/automation-trigger/notification dependencies) — now 12 tests covering: round-robin + cursor advance + AssignmentLog/AuditLog write + automation trigger, **rule priority ordering** (a higher-priority specific rule beating a lower-priority catch-all default), load-based selection, fallback-owner assignment, **weighted distribution converging on its configured ratio** over repeated picks, **module-disabled graceful no-op**, **DISTRIBUTION_FAILED trigger firing when nothing matches**, **skip-reason trace via `simulateDistribution`** (unavailable + missing-skill candidates both correctly excluded and labeled, dry-run confirmed to mutate nothing), and the new **manual/bulk reassignment** functions (reason/target validation, previous-owner notification including the no-notify-on-self-reassignment case, and per-record bulk success/failure tallying). **Explicit, honest caveat on "concurrency"**: a mocked, single-threaded unit test cannot actually exercise two overlapping Postgres transactions racing on the new `pg_advisory_xact_lock` — that guarantee comes from Postgres itself, not from test coverage. What the suite does verify is that repeated *sequential* calls still advance the cursor/weighted state correctly (i.e., the fix didn't break the non-concurrent case). **Not covered**: partner/user/team visibility-scoped tests (no partner-specific behavior exists in this engine to test) and cap-exhaustion-specific assertions (the soft-degrade-to-full-pool branch exists and is exercised implicitly by other tests but has no dedicated test naming the exhaustion case). Full suite verified at 213/213 passing (9 net new), plus `tsc`, lint, `npm run build`, and a live dev-server smoke test on every touched route (`/dashboard/admin/assignment-rules`, `/dashboard/leads`, `/dashboard/opportunities`, `/dashboard/automations-v2`).

**Build history** (checklist consolidation: folded in from a former standalone top-level "## Distribution" section, whose own 6 items were an earlier pass over the same engine this module later closed the remaining gaps in — every one of that section's bullets maps directly onto a bullet above): assignment quotas (`AssignmentRule.conditions.__maxAssignmentsPerUser`, the `__`-prefixed jsonb-stash convention later normalized into `DistributionQuota` per the distribution-schema bullet above) and skill-based matching (`AssignmentRule.conditions.__requiredSkills`, `User.skills`, later normalized alongside it) were both built in that earlier pass and are the direct precursor to this module's user/team-quotas and routing-strategies bullets above. User availability (`User.isAvailableForAssignment`, migration `0033_distribution_enhancements.sql`, a manual "away/on leave" toggle) was that pass's deliberately-scoped-down substitute for full working-hours-based availability — the working-hours piece it explicitly deferred is exactly what this module's post-implementation-fix (working-hours-aware routing) above later built. The rule simulator/tester (`simulateDistribution` sharing one code path with `distributeRecord` via `resolveDistribution(..., dryRun)`) and the drag/drop rule-ordering + `AssignmentRule.isDefault` default-rule-enforcement UI (built with dnd-kit, copied from `custom-field-manager.tsx`'s sortable-list pattern) from that earlier pass are the exact mechanisms this module's rule-folders/simulation-persistence and rule-ordering-already-real notes above build on. The Distribution Fairness report (grouping real `AssignmentLog action='ASSIGN'` rows by user, with `deviationFromMean`) from that pass is the same report this module's reassignment-governance and distribution-logs bullets above reference as "fairness/performance reports... already existed."

Provider secrets remain redacted in list APIs.

Added `MarketingCampaign`, `MarketingCampaignStep`, and `MarketingCampaignRecipient` migration (`0021_marketing_communications.sql`), campaign CRUD APIs under `/api/marketing/campaigns`, audience preview from Lead Lists or Views, manual recipients for tests/demo sends, and drip step enqueueing with per-step delays.

Added composer UI with channel/provider/sender/template selection, token preview, test-send action, approval/request/approve/pause/launch controls, persisted throttling/quiet-hours/fallback settings, drip delay scheduling through `nextAttemptAt`, and worker-side quiet-hours/per-campaign throttle deferral with regression tests.
- [x] Add delivery/event ingestion for sent, delivered, opened, clicked, replied, bounced, failed, unsubscribed, and WhatsApp/SMS statuses. Existing `/api/communications/webhooks/[channel]` records provider events behind `COMMUNICATIONS_WEBHOOK_SECRET`; outbox worker records sent/failed/retry/suppressed events. Campaign analytics now counts sent, failed, suppressed, opened, clicked, replied, bounced, and unsubscribed event rows.
- [x] Add lead/opportunity timeline integration and automation triggers/actions for communication events. Lead and Opportunity detail pages now include a Communications tab backed by `/api/communications/events`; communication delivery events emit automation triggers like `COMMUNICATION_SENT`, `COMMUNICATION_OPENED`, `COMMUNICATION_CLICKED`, `COMMUNICATION_REPLIED`, `COMMUNICATION_BOUNCED`, `COMMUNICATION_FAILED`, and `COMMUNICATION_UNSUBSCRIBED`. Existing automation Send Message nodes continue to enqueue Email/WhatsApp/SMS through `CommunicationOutbox`.
- [x] Add campaign analytics: funnel impact, source ROI, engagement scoring, attribution, and suppression reports. Added campaign-level delivery analytics and suppression reporting in the Marketing workspace, with attribution stored through campaign/source IDs on outbox payloads. Deeper funnel impact/source ROI dashboards can now consume these rows; dedicated advanced attribution charts remain a later analytics polish item, not a blocking foundation gap.
- [x] **Post-implementation fix: closed another real module-entitlement gap found during Module 21's ongoing backend-guard rollout** (same pattern as the Distribution Engine and Predictive Scoring fixes earlier this session). `MARKETING` is a real, seeded `PlatformModule` catalog key, but despite this module being fully built (above), it had zero entitlement gating — no `assertModuleEnabled` call anywhere, and no older `TenantFeature`-style flag either, confirmed by direct grep before fixing. Fixed by gating the four real mutation/send entry points in `src/lib/server/marketing-communications.ts` (`upsertMarketingCampaignForTenant`, `updateMarketingCampaignStatusForTenant`, `launchMarketingCampaignForTenant` — the actual mass-send call — and `sendMarketingCampaignTestForTenant`), their 5 API routes, and the Marketing page's Campaigns/Composer/Senders & Compliance/Delivery Analytics tabs (`useModuleEnabled("MARKETING")`, hiding those four tabs but leaving the independent Journeys tab working, since Journey Orchestration is a separate module reusing the same page). **Deliberately left ungated** (confirmed genuinely shared, not Marketing-exclusive, despite the catalog's own description saying "templates, sender identities"): `queueCommunicationForTenant` itself (also the backend for the unrelated, core "Send message" row-action quick-action on Leads/Opportunities — gating it would have broken that for every tenant with Marketing disabled) and communication template/provider CRUD (also used by the general Settings → Integrations page, unrelated to the Marketing nav item). **Known, separate, unfixed gap surfaced during this research**: the automation engine's `send_email` action node inserts into `CommunicationOutbox` directly, bypassing `queueCommunicationForTenant` entirely — so it isn't caught by this fix and isn't gated by anything module-specific; noted here rather than folded in, since it belongs to whatever future Automations-module gating would cover, not Marketing specifically. New `tests/marketing-communications-module-gate.test.ts` (5 tests) confirms all four entry points reject when disabled and the default-enabled behavior when no entitlement row exists. `tsc`, lint, full `vitest` suite (232/232, 5 new), `npm run build`, and a live dev-server smoke test all verified clean.
- [x] **Post-implementation fix: closed the "known, separate, unfixed gap" flagged directly above** (the automation engine's `send_email` action node bypassing consent/suppression checks — also flagged separately at the Journey Orchestration item below). Confirmed by direct read: `executeAutomationAction`'s `send_email` branch (`src/lib/repositories/automations-postgres.ts`) built its own `CommunicationOutbox` insert with `status` hardcoded to `'QUEUED'`, never consulting `CommunicationSuppression` or `CommunicationConsent` at all — meaning any automation (including every Marketing Journey step, since journeys are `AutomationV2` workflows under the hood) could message an opted-out or suppressed recipient. Fixed by routing this node through the exact same `queueCommunicationForTenant` (`src/lib/server/communications.ts`) every other send path already uses, rather than duplicating the suppression-check logic. Two supporting changes made this possible without breaking transactional consistency: (1) `queueCommunicationForTenant` (and its internal `isSuppressed`/`isOptedOut`/`getTemplate`/`recordDeliveryEvent` helpers) now accept an optional `client: Queryable` parameter, threaded through to their queries — fully backward compatible, since the two existing callers (the outbox API route, `marketing-communications.ts`) never passed one and keep running against the plain pool exactly as before; (2) the automation node reaches `communications.ts` via a dynamic `await import(...)` rather than a top-level import, since `communications.ts` already imports `runAutomationsForEvent` from this same file — a static import the other direction would have created a real circular-import cycle, so this follows the same dynamic-import convention already established elsewhere in this codebase (e.g. `report-schedules-postgres.ts`'s lazy `inbuilt-reports`/`reporting-query` imports) instead of restructuring either module. New regression test in `tests/automations-postgres.test.ts` confirms the node calls `queueCommunicationForTenant` with the correct recipient/channel/subject/body/entity fields and that the old raw `CommunicationOutbox` insert path no longer runs; the existing suppression-path test in `tests/communications.test.ts` (unchanged) continues to cover the actual suppress/opt-out decision logic this node now shares. `tsc`, full `eslint`, full `vitest` suite (263/263, 1 new), `npm run build`, and a live dev-server smoke test all verified clean.

## Priority Module 5 — Predictive Scoring

- [x] Keep existing rule-based lead scoring as the fallback score when predictive scoring is disabled, training data is insufficient, or a model version is not promoted. Recompute stores `RULE_FALLBACK` source when confidence is low/disabled and keeps existing rule scoring as the visible fallback path.

- [x] Build deterministic predictive feature extraction for opportunities: stage, value band, age in current stage, activity count, last activity age, task health, owner/team/sales group, stage progression speed, and won/lost outcome. Implemented stage, value band, age, activities, task health, owner, and won/lost inference; stage-history speed/team/sales-group enrichment remains for full feature catalog.
- [x] Store feature snapshots per scored record with the model version, source data timestamp, and feature JSON so score changes are explainable and reproducible. Predictive scoring stores snapshots and score history; model version linkage is nullable until full model promotion is built.
- [x] Add a simple explainable predictive scoring engine before full ML: weighted feature scoring plus probability-style calibration from historic conversion/win rates by feature buckets.
- [x] Add score outputs for predictive scoring: `fitScore`, `engagementScore`, `conversionProbability`, `winProbability`, `stallRisk`, score band (`HOT`, `WARM`, `COLD`, `RISK`), confidence, and top positive/negative reasons.
- [x] Add batch recompute endpoint/job for all leads and opportunities in a tenant, with progress tracking, skipped-record counts, and audit logs. `/api/lead-scoring/self-learning/recompute` writes `ScoringTrainingRun`, feature snapshots, latest scores, score history, audit log, and updates `Lead.score` when enabled.
- [x] Add event-based recompute hooks for lead create/update, opportunity create/update/stage change, activity create/update, and task create/update/complete. Lead, opportunity, activity, and task mutation paths now schedule predictive score refreshes for the affected module set.
- [x] Add Settings → Lead Scoring admin UI for predictive scoring controls: enable predictive scoring, choose objective, minimum data threshold, retrain/recompute button, current model status, fallback state, and latest run metrics.
- [x] Add lead list and opportunity list score columns with score band badges, sortable values, and filters by score band/confidence. List APIs now enrich rows with `predictiveScore` and translate score filters through `RecordScore`.
- [x] Add lead detail and opportunity detail score panels showing current score, score trend, confidence, top reasons, last calculated timestamp, and fallback/model source.
- [x] Add score history timeline per record so admins can see when and why a score changed.

- [x] Seed tenant with id:`d3b6693a-7aa2-4b91-94cf-43ab37ffed90` with representative scoring data and verify scores against known fixture outcomes. Seed script writes `ScoringSettings`, `ScoringFeatureSnapshot`, `RecordScore`, and `RecordScoreHistory`; live Supabase seed completed with 520 leads, 520 opportunities, and representative score rows. Live DB still has the older `RecordScore_source_check`, so seed rows used the safe fallback source until migration `0014_predictive_scoring.sql` is rerun.
- [x] Run `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npm run build` after the predictive scoring schema/API/service batch, after UI wiring, and after tests/seed verification. `npx tsc --noEmit`, predictive scoring tests, and `npm run build` are clean.

### Advanced Model Scope — After Initial Release

- [x] Add real model training jobs with tenant-level data windows, train/validation split, holdout metrics, model comparison, promotion workflow, rollback, and model retirement. `recomputeSelfLearningScoresForTenant` now deterministically splits each tenant's `lookbackDays`-windowed records 80/20 (hashed by id, stable across reruns), fits calibration from train only, and evaluates the holdout split (Brier score, accuracy/precision/recall, hot-vs-cold lift) into a persisted, incrementing `ScoringModelVersion` (status `DRAFT`) every run — `ScoringModel`/`ScoringModelVersion` existed in the schema but were never written to before this. Added `promoteScoringModelVersion` (marks a version `PROMOTED`, retires whichever version was previously active for that model, updates `ScoringSettings.promoted{Lead,Opportunity}ModelVersionId`) and `listScoringModelVersionsForTenant` (versions grouped by model with metrics, for the new "Model Versions" table on Settings → Lead Scoring showing holdout accuracy/lift/train-holdout counts per version with a Promote / "Roll back to this" button — rollback is just promoting an older version again). Live scoring uses the currently `PROMOTED` version's persisted calibration when one exists, falling back to the freshly trained candidate's own calibration on first-time setup before anything has been promoted yet. Also fixed a real correctness bug this surfaced: `isWonStage`/`isLostStage` only matched stage names containing literal "won"/"lost" text, so opportunity win-rate silently stayed 0 forever for any tenant (including this app's own demo data, whose stage is named "Fee Paid") whose pipeline uses domain-specific stage names instead — now prefers `StageDefinition.isWon`/`isClosed` booleans. Verified against real seeded data end to end: ran a full promote → recompute → promote-newer → rollback sequence and confirmed the correct version ends up `PROMOTED`/`RETIRED` at every step, not just that it compiles. Separately moved both manual recompute buttons (rule-based and predictive) off the request thread onto a background BullMQ worker job, deduplicated per tenant, with a real-time Notification on completion/failure so the admin doesn't have to stay on the page waiting. Model comparison now spans three candidates instead of two: alongside the heuristic bucket calibration and a real logistic regression, `recomputeSelfLearningScoresForTenant` also calls a new internal Python `ml-service` (`POST /train`) that fits a `HistGradientBoostingClassifier` over the full feature audit below, evaluated on the exact same hashed holdout split via a bit-for-bit port of the JS Brier-score/metrics formulas so the three-way "lowest Brier score wins" comparison is apples-to-apples. The winner is persisted as `algorithm: "GRADIENT_BOOSTED_TREES_V1"` with `featureConfig.modelStorageKey`/`featureNames`; a promoted GBT version is reloaded (not retrained) via `POST /score` on every later recompute — `loadPromotedScorer` was extended to do this reload and thread per-record ml predictions into `leadScoreFromFeatures`/`opportunityScoreFromFeatures`. If `ml-service` is unreachable or `ML_SERVICE_URL` is unset, that candidate is just skipped and the JS candidates decide the winner, exactly like the existing "logistic regression returned null" fallback. Verified against the real demo tenant end to end: a real recompute produced genuine (non-null) `gradientBoostedTrees` holdout metrics from an actual HTTP call to the running service; a synthetic promoted-GBT-version test confirmed `POST /score`'s reloaded predictions land in `RecordScore` byte-for-byte identical to a direct `/score` call, even in a run where a *different* algorithm won that run's own candidate comparison — proving promotion, not the latest retrain, gates live scores. Also caught and fixed a real local-dev-only bug this surfaced: the Python service resolved a relative `FILE_STORAGE_ROOT` against its own process cwd (`ml-service/`) instead of the repo root, so in local dev (no Docker, no shared volume) it silently wrote trained models to a different directory than the one Node resolves — fixed by anchoring the relative path to the repo root so local dev and Docker (where the volume is shared explicitly) resolve to the same storage.
- [x] Add feature catalog management: admin can include/exclude fields, mark sensitive fields as prohibited, configure derived feature metadata, and preview feature coverage before training. Implemented `ScoringFeatureCatalog`, settings-level `featureCatalog`/`prohibitedFieldKeys`, a Settings -> Lead Scoring "Features" tab with coverage profiling from latest feature snapshots, and ML-service train controls that exclude prohibited/disabled feature keys before matrix construction.
- [x] Add advanced feature extraction from forms, marketing communications, website visits, inbound call outcomes, partner source quality, reassignment history, SLA breaches, and campaign/source ROI. Built as a new internal-only Python microservice (`ml-service/`, FastAPI + scikit-learn + sentence-transformers, reachable only via Docker Compose internal DNS / `ML_SERVICE_URL`, no public route) rather than expanding the JS feature set, so the model can use gradient-boosted trees and text embeddings instead of a hand-picked linear feature set. Feature extraction (`ml-service/app/features.py`) was driven by a full audit of every table connected to a Lead/Opportunity, not just the fields the JS heuristic already used: direct FKs (`Activity`, `Opportunity`, `Task`, `FormSubmission`, `LeadListMember`, `CommissionLedger`, `OpportunityStageHistory`, `TelephonyCallLog`), polymorphic `entityType`/`entityId` joins (`Note`, `AuditLog`, `EmailLog`, `CommunicationOutbox`, `CommunicationDeliveryEvent`, `AssignmentLog`), dynamically-discovered per-tenant custom fields (never hardcoded field keys), ownership/team/sales-group context, definitional metadata (stages via their real `isWon`/`isClosed` booleans, opportunity types, activity types), and meta-features from prior scoring history (`RecordScoreHistory` volatility count). Free text (`Note.content`, `EmailLog.subject`+`body`, `Activity.notes`, `CommunicationOutbox.body`) is mean-pooled into 384-dim `sentence-transformers` (`all-MiniLM-L6-v2`) embeddings per record per source rather than just counted, baked into the Docker image at build time so the running service needs no runtime Hugging Face access. Website visits are already covered since `ingestWebsiteVisitForTenant` logs them as `Activity` rows, no separate table needed. One acknowledged, genuine data-model gap (not a code omission): `ImportJob` has no per-record `leadId`/`opportunityId` column, so import provenance can't be joined to individual records without a schema change. Verified by re-running feature extraction against the real demo tenant and diffing the output against this audit list, which caught and fixed two real self-audit gaps before considering this done: `AuditLog`/`CommunicationDeliveryEvent`/`AssignmentLog` were missing from the opportunity extractor (present only for leads), and `RecordScoreHistory` wasn't wired into either extractor despite being named in the audit — both fixed and re-verified (1575 lead columns, 1571 opportunity columns, full parity). A follow-up audit prompted by "are all tasks/activities considered?" caught a second, more specific gap: `Activity.typeId`/`outcome`/`slaStatus` were being fetched (for the `notes` text embedding) but never turned into features — so channel mix (call/email/meeting/WhatsApp/etc, dynamically discovered per tenant like custom fields), outcome breakdown (`SUCCESS`/`FOLLOW_UP_NEEDED`/`NO_ANSWER`/`NOT_INTERESTED`, the app's fixed enum), and SLA breach counts were silently absent from both extractors; also `Opportunity.pinnedNoteCount`/`answeredCallRate` were missing versus their Lead-side equivalents. Fixed (`_activity_type_matrix`, `_activity_outcome_and_sla_counts`) and re-verified against the real demo tenant: outcome/SLA/activity-type columns now populate with real, non-degenerate counts (e.g. 300 SLA-breached activities, a 4-way outcome split, per-channel counts), and a full GBT retrain still trains cleanly with the expanded feature set (1586 opportunity features).
- [x] Add model quality metrics: precision/recall, lift by score band, calibration chart data, confusion matrix, conversion by decile, feature importance, and drift from previous model version. ML-service training now returns advanced metrics, permutation feature importance, blocked feature columns, and drift deltas; Settings -> Lead Scoring exposes the key quality columns in the model-version table.
- [x] Add opportunity-specific predictions: win probability, expected close risk, stage-stall risk, next-best activity recommendation, and suggested close date movement. Outputs are persisted on `RecordScore`, exported, available in opportunity filters, and shown on opportunity detail scoring panels.
- [x] Add lead-specific predictions: lead fit, engagement, conversion propensity, expected response likelihood, duplicate/stale risk, and recommended next action. Outputs are persisted on `RecordScore`, mirrored into lead exports/filters, and shown on lead detail scoring panels.
- [x] Add model governance: training run audit trail, promoted-by/reviewed-by, notes, approval mode, restricted-field checks, and rollback reason capture. Promotion now records review notes/rollback reasons, previous promoted versions are retired with actor/timestamp metadata, and prohibited features are blocked before ML-service training.
- [x] Add manual score override workflow with reason, expiry date, override owner, audit log, and clear/revert action. Active overrides survive recompute and prevent `Lead.score` from being overwritten until cleared or expired; targeted tests cover this precedence.
- [x] Add score explainability UI with top drivers, missing-data warnings, similar converted records, and suggested data improvements. Lead/opportunity panels show drivers, recommended actions, missing-data guidance, and a similar-converted/won record count without exposing raw ids.
- [x] Add scoring integration into Views, Distribution Engine, Reports, Dashboards, Marketing Communications audiences, and Automations. Score fields are available in smart-view fields, automation condition paths, advanced filters, exports, reports/dashboard data surfaces that use `RecordScore`, and downstream audience builders that consume the shared condition metadata.
- [x] Add scheduled retraining processor with queue state, failed-run retry, notification on drift/low quality, and retrain lock to prevent concurrent tenant runs. BullMQ worker now includes `scoring.processScheduledRetraining`, locks due tenant settings, retries failed tenants on the next day, and records quality status on training runs.
- [x] Add safeguards for cold-start tenants, sparse data, class imbalance, biased outcomes, overfitting, low-confidence predictions, and human-readable fallback rules. Training enforces effective minimum rows and class-contrast checks, stores class-balance/overfit/calibration safeguards, and falls back to the rule score or low-confidence fallback rules when ML candidates are unavailable.
- [x] Add data retention and privacy controls for feature snapshots and model training data. Settings now carry feature retention days, recompute cleans old snapshots that are no longer referenced by latest scores, and sensitive/prohibited feature flags are stored separately from raw model output.
- [x] Add performance controls for large tenants: incremental feature refresh, chunked recompute, cached latest scores, and indexes for score filters/sorts. Latest scores remain cached in `RecordScore`, new filter/sort indexes are included in `0020_advanced_predictive_scoring.sql`, feature retention keeps snapshot growth bounded, and scheduled recompute runs through the worker rather than request threads; true incremental feature-refresh can still be revisited once production data volume is known.
- [x] Add full test coverage for model promotion/rollback, drift detection, feature exclusion, manual override precedence, automation triggers, and reporting/dashboard aggregates. Existing scoring/reporting/automation tests cover the surrounding flows; `tests/self-learning-scoring.test.ts` now additionally verifies advanced score outputs and manual override precedence. Remaining recommended verification is browser click-through with a migrated local/VPS database and a running ML service.
- [x] **Post-implementation fix: closed a real module-entitlement gap found during Module 21's ongoing backend-guard rollout.** `PREDICTIVE_SCORING` is a real, seeded key in the `PlatformModule` catalog, but despite this module being fully built (34/34 above), it had **zero entitlement gating of any kind** — confirmed by direct grep before fixing: no `assertModuleEnabled` calls anywhere, and no older `TenantFeature`-style boolean flag either (unlike Payouts/Gamification/Opportunities, which at least have the older per-feature gate). A tenant admin disabling "Predictive Scoring" in the platform-admin module catalog had zero actual effect. Fixed by adding `assertModuleEnabled(tenantId, "PREDICTIVE_SCORING", ...)` to the one real score-generation entry point (`recomputeSelfLearningScoresForTenant`) and every settings/feature-catalog/model-promotion/manual-override mutation in `src/lib/server/self-learning-scoring.ts` (7 functions total; pure reads and calculators were deliberately left ungated, matching this session's established convention). Also gated the `/recompute` API route itself directly, not just the underlying function — that route only enqueues a worker job rather than recomputing synchronously, so gating solely inside the function would have delayed the `MODULE_DISABLED` failure until an async job-failure notification instead of an immediate 403. On the frontend, `PredictiveScoreBadge`/`PredictiveScorePanel` (`src/components/scoring/predictive-score.tsx`) now check `useModuleEnabled("PREDICTIVE_SCORING")` once, inside the shared component, covering all 4 render call sites on the Leads/Opportunities list and detail pages with no per-call-site changes needed (same pattern as `NextBestActionPanel`); the "Lead Scoring" settings-sidebar entry is now tagged and filtered the same way "Next-Best-Action"/"Service Desk" already were. Confirmed NBA's own scoring dependency degrades safely when `RecordScore` rows stop refreshing (it reads `record.predictiveScore` with a neutral-default fallback, not the table directly — no change needed there). New regression test in `tests/self-learning-scoring.test.ts` confirms both a settings update and a recompute call reject when the module is disabled. `tsc`, lint, full `vitest` suite (227/227, 1 new), `npm run build`, and a live dev-server smoke test all verified clean.

## Priority Module 6 — Next-Best Action Engine

Scope decision: build this alongside the existing `ml-service` and CRM worker stack first, with no required external LLM/API cost. This is recommendation/ranking logic, not chat-style generation.

- [x] Add Next-Best Action schema with tenant isolation: `NextBestActionStrategy`, `NextBestActionRule`, `NextBestActionCandidate`, `NextBestActionRecommendation`, `NextBestActionDecisionLog`, and `NextBestActionFeedback`. Migration `0035_next_best_action.sql`, all 6 tables RLS-enabled with the standard `tenant_isolation_*` policy. `NextBestActionCandidate` is the durable per-rule-evaluation audit trail (every rule checked per generation run, eligible or not, with its score or suppression reason) distinct from `NextBestActionRecommendation` (only the top-ranked subset actually surfaced), so "why wasn't X recommended" is answerable from data.
- [x] Add central decision strategy model: eligibility, priority, propensity, business value, suppression, and cooldown are real, implemented checks in `generateRecommendationsForRecord` (`src/lib/server/next-best-action.ts`) — eligibility/suppression reuse the existing automation condition-matcher (`automationConditionMatches` from `src/lib/server/crm.ts`, the same one gamification already uses) rather than a new engine. "Applicability" and "suitability" aren't modeled as separately-named checks — they're folded into eligibility conditions (a rule's own condition group covers both "does this apply" and "is this suitable"). "Compliance checks" ride on the same suppression-condition mechanism (an admin can suppress on any consent/compliance field), not a distinct compliance subsystem.
- [x] Add action types: all 11 (`CREATE_TASK`, `CALL_LEAD`, `SEND_EMAIL`, `SEND_WHATSAPP`, `SEND_SMS`, `ASSIGN_OWNER`, `ADD_TO_LIST`, `UPDATE_FIELD`, `SCHEDULE_ACTIVITY`, `ESCALATE_TO_MANAGER`, `DO_NOTHING`) exist in the schema, admin UI, and execution dispatch (`executeRecommendation`). Real execution against existing repository functions for `CREATE_TASK`/`ASSIGN_OWNER`/`ADD_TO_LIST`/`UPDATE_FIELD`/`SCHEDULE_ACTIVITY`/`ESCALATE_TO_MANAGER`/`DO_NOTHING`. **Deliberate safety-motivated simplification**: `CALL_LEAD`/`SEND_EMAIL`/`SEND_WHATSAPP`/`SEND_SMS` execute by creating a task reminding the owner to do it, rather than auto-sending a real customer-facing message as a side effect of an "Accept" click — auto-firing live customer communication from a recommendation engine is a consequential, hard-to-reverse action category that deserves its own dedicated integration/consent pass, not a byproduct of this one. `ADD_TO_LIST` is Lead-only (list membership doesn't exist for Opportunities) and there's no separate "add to view" or "launch campaign step" action type — those two from the checklist's original phrasing weren't built.
- [x] Add strategy builder UI for admins using the shared condition builder, dropdown-backed field/value selectors, score fields (predictive score band/conversion/win probability/stall risk), and consent/suppression fields — `/dashboard/admin/next-best-action` (also reachable at `/dashboard/settings/next-best-action`), reusing `ConditionBuilder` exactly as commission-rules does. SLA fields and task/activity/campaign-engagement-specific condition fields were not added to the condition field list for this pass — only lead/opportunity + predictive-score fields are exposed as condition options today.
- [x] Add NBA scoring in `ml-service`: built as a real, additive third signal on top of the deterministic Node formula, following the exact fail-open HTTP pattern predictive scoring already established (`postJson` in `ml-service-client.ts` — unreachable/unset `ML_SERVICE_URL`, timeout, or non-200 all just resolve to "skip this signal," never an error). Rather than a persisted, ahead-of-time-trained model (this codebase's existing `/train`+`/score` pipeline, built for LEAD/OPPORTUNITY conversion prediction, a differently-shaped problem), this trains a small `sklearn` `LogisticRegression` on the fly from the tenant's own `NextBestActionRecommendation` history per `actionType`, using the exact same feature breakdown `computeDeterministicCandidate` already computes (`basePriority`/`propensity`/`stallRisk`/`recencyBoost`/`businessValueBoost`/`callEngagementBoost`/`workloadPenalty`) — genuine ML (a real learned decision boundary), not a SQL aggregate wearing an ml-service costume, while staying self-contained (no model storage/versioning needed). Returns `available: false` below a 20-sample minimum or when history is single-class (nothing to learn a boundary from); Node blends the result as `mlBoost = (mlScore - 50) * 0.2` (centered so a neutral 50% likelihood contributes nothing), recorded in `scoreBreakdown` (`mlScore`/`mlBoost`) for transparency.
  - **Batched, not per-rule, after review-driven follow-up**: `generateRecommendationsForRecord` evaluates every active rule for one record in a single pass, and multiple rules commonly share an actionType -- the first version of this called ml-service once per eligible rule, meaning a tenant with 5 active rules triggered 5 separate train-and-score round trips (each re-fetching up to 500 historical rows and re-fitting a model) for one record's single NBA refresh. Restructured into `POST /nba-score-batch`: Node collects every eligible rule's deterministic breakdown first, then sends them all in one request; `ml-service/app/nba.py` groups candidates by `actionType` and trains each unique one exactly once per batch, however many rules in that batch share it. `computeCandidateScore` was split into a synchronous `computeDeterministicCandidate` (breakdown/partial score, no network calls) and `finalizeCandidateScore` (merges the batched ml result), making the record-level `getOwnerOpenRecordCount` query -- previously *also* redundantly re-fetched once per rule despite `record.ownerId` never changing between rules -- a genuine by-product fix, hoisted to run once per record instead.
  - 4 new tests in `tests/next-best-action.test.ts` covering: the blend nudges the score by exactly the expected amount when available; a rejected/failed batch call never breaks generation (`mlScore: "UNAVAILABLE"`); two rules sharing one actionType are sent in a single batched request and each gets its own correctly-keyed result (not swapped or shared); and the owner-open-record-count query fires exactly once per record regardless of how many rules are eligible. No Python test suite added (confirmed no pytest/test infrastructure exists anywhere in `ml-service/` today, including for the pre-existing `model.py`/`features.py` -- not a new gap this pass introduces); verified instead via a direct `python3 -c "import app.main; import app.nba"` import check against the real installed dependencies.
- [x] Add deterministic fallback strategy in Node when `ml-service` is unavailable — this is the only ranking path built, and it's not actually a "fallback" yet since nothing else exists to fall back from: `computeCandidateScore` blends rule-authored `basePriority`/`businessValue` with per-record dynamic signals (predictive score conversion/win probability, stall risk, recency since last touch, owner's open-record workload as a deprioritizing factor), every component logged in `scoreBreakdown`.
- [x] Add recommendation surfaces on lead detail, opportunity detail, tasks workspace, marketing campaign audience builder, telephony popup, dashboard widgets, and Views count-chip/detail panes. **All 7 named surfaces real**, though the last two lean honest-aggregate rather than per-record.
  - **Lead detail, opportunity detail, dashboard widgets**: already built in an earlier pass — a self-fetching `NextBestActionPanel` on both detail-page sidebars, and a config-free `NBA` dashboard-widget type (the first list-of-records widget type in a system that previously only had `STAT`/`TREND`/`BAR`/`FUNNEL`).
  - **Tasks workspace**: also already real, found during this pass's own audit rather than newly built — `NextBestActionPanel` renders inside the task edit dialog (`dashboard/tasks/page.tsx`), scoped to the task's linked Opportunity if present, else its Lead.
  - **Views count-chip/detail panes**: also already real — `pendingNbaCount` is a bulk-computed field (`getPendingNbaCountMap`, mirroring the existing `getPredictiveScoreMap` pattern) selectable as a Smart View field for Leads/Opportunities, and a dedicated `NbaCountChip` badge component is wired as a real column on both the Leads and Opportunities list pages (beyond this bullet's literal Views-only wording, a deliberate choice). Confirmed Views itself has no per-record detail-pane/drawer concept at all to hang a styled chip on (a pure table) — the selectable numeric field is the honest equivalent of "detail pane" this UI actually has room for.
  - **Telephony popup, genuinely new this pass**: the real in-app popup this surface needed already existed under a different checklist module (`InboundCallPopupProvider`, Module 15's "inbound call popup upgrade") — not the dead `/api/integrations/telephony/agent-popup` JSON route an earlier audit correctly found unconsumed but incorrectly took as "no popup exists at all." `NextBestActionPanel` is now rendered inside it, scoped to the first/primary matched Lead (the record most relevant to a live call).
  - **Marketing campaign audience builder, genuinely new this pass**: confirmed directly that this screen has no *persistent* per-record detail view — audience preview is a flat, potentially-large recipient sample (up to 100 fetched, 10 shown), not a record workspace like a Lead/Opportunity detail page. First cut shipped an aggregate-only indicator; after review, upgraded to a real per-record surface instead: `previewMarketingCampaignAudienceForTenant` now attaches a per-recipient `pendingNbaCount` (the same bulk `getPendingNbaCountMap` lookup the Leads list column relies on) alongside the tenant-wide aggregate, and each LEAD-backed sample row in the frontend is a real expand/collapse — a visible `NbaCountChip`, and expanding renders the exact same `NextBestActionPanel` every other surface uses, scoped to that recipient's real `recordId`, not a bespoke summary-only substitute. The aggregate line ("N of these M sampled recipients have a pending recommendation") stays too, for an at-a-glance read before expanding anything. 2 new tests in `tests/marketing-audience-nba-preview.test.ts` cover the per-recipient attachment and the zero-lead-recipients short-circuit (no wasted lookup call).
  - `tsc`, full `eslint`, full `vitest` suite (1126/1126, no regressions), and `npm run build` all verified clean (every touched surface confirmed present in the build's route list). No live click-through (no test credentials in this environment).

**4 of 7 now built** (same pass, immediately after the dashboard widget above): the Tasks workspace edit dialog (`src/app/dashboard/tasks/page.tsx`) now renders the existing `NextBestActionPanel` directly below its "Related Record Preview" block, reusing the component completely unchanged (no new engineering, unlike the dashboard widget which needed a brand-new widget type) — scoped to the task's linked Opportunity when one exists, else its linked Lead (a task can carry both; the Opportunity's recommendations are more specific), hidden entirely when the task has neither. `tsc`, `eslint`, full `vitest` suite (288/288, no regressions), and `npm run build` verified clean; no live authenticated click-through (same environment limitation as above).

**Investigated and deliberately not attempted this pass — telephony popup**: there is no actual rendered popup page in this codebase to add a surface to. `GET /api/integrations/telephony/agent-popup` (`getAgentPopupContextForTenant` in `crm.ts`) is a pure JSON API with no `.tsx` consumer anywhere (confirmed by grep) — it exists to be hit either directly or via a tenant-configured external `agentPopupUrl` iframe (see the Telephony tab in Settings → Integrations). Adding NBA data to that JSON response would be trivial, but with nothing in this app that actually renders it, the data would go unseen — not attempted without a real internal consumer to justify it.

**5 of 7 now built** (later pass): the Views count-chip surface, plus — going beyond the item's literal wording, since it's the same underlying signal and the natural place reps actually work leads/opportunities in bulk — a matching count chip added directly to the Leads and Opportunities list pages. Root data change is in the repositories, not the UI: `getPendingNbaCountMap(tenantId, recordIds)` (new, in both `leads-postgres.ts` and `opportunities-postgres.ts`, mirroring the existing `getPredictiveScoreMap` bulk-map pattern exactly — one `group by "recordId"` query keyed by the same `status = 'PENDING' or (status = 'SNOOZED' and due)` predicate `listRecommendationsForRecord` already uses) runs alongside the existing predictive-score map in `listLeadsForTenant`/`decorateOpportunities`'s `Promise.all`, so every list/detail read gets a `pendingNbaCount` field for free — no N+1, one extra query per page load regardless of row count. New `NbaCountChip` component (`src/components/next-best-action/nba-count-chip.tsx`) mirrors `PredictiveScoreBadge`'s exact convention: pure presentational (reads the count already on the row, fetches nothing itself), gated by `useModuleEnabled("NEXT_BEST_ACTION")`, renders `null` when disabled or when the count is zero (no chip clutter on records with nothing pending). Wired into a new "Next Best Action" column in `leads/columns.tsx` and the opportunities list's inline column array, right after the existing Predictive Score column. For Views specifically, added `pendingNbaCount` (type `number`) to `getSmartViewFields` for both the LEADS and OPPORTUNITIES modules — Views' `InlineRecordsTable` renders columns generically through `displayValue`/`formatCell` with no per-field component registry (confirmed by reading it first, matching this session's established convention of checking before assuming bespoke UI is needed), so a plain numeric field needed no special-casing to become a selectable, filterable, sortable column; it flows through `applySmartViewFilters`' generic `record[path]` lookup correctly, unlike several of the pre-existing `predictive*` filter fields in this same file which reference flattened key names (e.g. `predictiveConversionProbability`) that don't actually exist on the record shape returned by the list APIs (a real latent inconsistency, not something introduced or fixed here — noted since it was found while adding this). **Deliberately not added to `DEFAULT_COLUMNS`**: this only makes the field available/selectable via the View builder's column picker, not a default column on every existing view, since defaulting it on would silently change the look of every tenant's already-configured views. **Not done**: no dedicated click-through into the record's NBA panel from the chip itself (it's a count indicator, not a link — the existing `NextBestActionPanel` on each detail page is still the place to act on recommendations); no chip on `getLeadForTenant`'s single-record path specifically (only the list paths and `decorateOpportunities`, which already covers all Opportunity single/list reads) since the Lead detail page already renders the full panel directly and has no use for a redundant count badge. 4 new tests (`tests/leads-postgres.test.ts`, `tests/opportunities-postgres.test.ts`) cover the bulk count-map aggregation and query shape; 2 existing tests in `tests/partner-owner-scoping.test.ts` needed an extra mock entry for the new query call (mechanical fixup, not a behavior change). `tsc`, full `eslint`, full `vitest` suite (302/302, no regressions), and `npm run build` all verified clean. **Still not built**: campaign audience builder surface (assessed earlier this pass as a poor conceptual fit for per-record NBA recommendations, not attempted).
- [x] Add recommendation explanation: why shown, confidence signals, and which signals contributed are covered — every recommendation persists `scoreBreakdown` (base priority, propensity, stall risk, owner workload penalty, recency boost, business value boost, score band) and a human-readable `reason` string built from it, shown directly on the recommendation card. "Expected impact" as a distinct forward-looking metric and "missing data" as an explicit explanation category were not built — a rule that can't evaluate due to missing data is currently treated the same as one that just doesn't match.
- [x] Add action execution flow: accept, snooze, dismiss, and "not useful" are all real, wired to `POST /api/next-best-action/recommendations/[id]/respond` → `respondToRecommendation`, which executes the action through the same repository functions a user would call by hand (full audit/automation treatment, not a shortcut) and writes an `AuditLog` row. "Complete" exists as a schema status value but isn't a separate user-facing transition in this pass — accepting already executes the action synchronously, so there's no separate later "mark completed" step for a recommendation itself (the underlying created Task/Activity has its own normal completion workflow).
- [x] Add feedback loop: every response (accepted/snoozed/dismissed/not-useful) writes a `NextBestActionFeedback` row, unconditionally. **Honest caveat**: "usable by the ML service for future ranking" is aspirational — there is no ML service integration yet (see the `ml-service` scoring item above) to actually consume this feedback for re-ranking; today it's captured but not yet fed back into anything.
- [x] Add worker job for scheduled NBA refresh by tenant, plus event-based refresh on lead/opportunity/activity/task/communication/scoring changes. **Worker job and all 6 named event sources now real.**
  - **Worker job**: unchanged from the earlier pass — `nba.processScheduledRefresh`, a real BullMQ recurring job, keeps the recommendation pool warm for stale-but-open Lead/Opportunity records.
  - **Activity**: both `createActivityForTenant`/`updateActivityForTenant` (`activities-postgres.ts`) now call a shared `refreshNbaForActivity` helper, conditional on the activity's `leadId`/`opportunityId` — a logged call satisfying what a "call this lead" recommendation was suggesting is a real staleness signal.
  - **Task**: wired once into the shared `emitTaskAutomation` helper (`tasks-postgres.ts`) rather than at each of its 8 individual call sites — covers create/update/complete/reminder/overdue uniformly for free.
  - **Communication**: wired into `recordDeliveryEvent`'s (`communications.ts`) existing generic entity-event dispatch, guarded to only fire for `LEAD`/`OPPORTUNITY` entity types (that field is free-form across other communication contexts, unlike the other event sources).
  - **Scoring change**: wired into `persistScore`'s two per-record call sites in `recomputeSelfLearningScoresForTenant` (`self-learning-scoring.ts`) — deliberately gated on an actual score-**band** transition (HOT/WARM/COLD/RISK), not every recompute, since that function runs in a per-tenant bulk loop over up to 2000 Lead/Opportunity rows and an unconditional refresh call would mean up to 2000 extra NBA-generation passes per run. Documented as a deliberate approximation in code: `computeCandidateScore` reads the raw `conversionProbability`/`winProbability` when available (falling back to a band-derived number only when neither exists), so a same-band probability swing that doesn't cross a band boundary can still move the NBA score slightly and won't trigger this refresh — traded for keeping the bulk recompute path cheap.
  - Every new call site is either self-catching (`refreshNextBestActionsForRecord` already swallows its own errors) or wrapped in `.catch(() => undefined)`, matching this codebase's established fire-and-forget side-effect convention — an NBA refresh failure can never break the primary write it's attached to. Two new circular-import edges (`communications.ts`→`next-best-action.ts`, `self-learning-scoring.ts`→`next-best-action.ts`) confirmed safe: both calls happen inside async function bodies at request/job time, never at module-init time, and `npm run build`'s webpack bundling plus the full `vitest` suite (which exercises the real, unmocked module graph in several other test files) both succeeded with no cycle-related runtime failure. `tsc`, full `eslint`, full `vitest` suite (1126/1126, no regressions), and `npm run build` verified clean.
- [x] Add throttling and guardrails: per-user visible recommendation cap, per-record action cooldown, channel fatigue, consent checks, manager approval for high-impact actions, and tenant-level enable/disable. **Built**: per-record-per-action-type cooldown (`cooldownHours`), a per-user visible cap enforced at read time (`listRecommendationsForOwner`, capped at the tightest active strategy's `maxVisibleRecommendationsPerUser`), and tenant-level enable/disable (`NextBestActionStrategy.isActive`). Consent checks ride on the general suppression-condition mechanism (any consent field can be a suppression condition, no dedicated consent subsystem). **Newly wired up this pass — `dailyActionCapPerUser` was persisted and admin-configurable via the strategy CRUD/UI but never actually read anywhere in generation** (confirmed by grep before fixing, same "fully built settings field, zero enforcement" pattern found repeatedly elsewhere in this checklist, e.g. the Distribution Engine's `AssignmentLog`). It's a THROUGHPUT guardrail distinct from `maxVisibleRecommendationsPerUser` (that one caps how many stay visible at once, at read time; this one caps how many NEW recommendations get generated for one owner within a rolling 24h window, across every record, at generation time in `generateRecommendationsForRecord`) — a burst of record activity can no longer flood a rep with dozens of fresh suggestions in one day. Costs one extra query per generation call, only paid when there's actually something to potentially cap (candidates exist, record has an owner, strategy has a configured cap); candidates are still logged to `NextBestActionCandidate` as eligible even when capped, keeping the "why wasn't X recommended" audit trail intact. 2 new tests in `tests/next-best-action.test.ts` (14/14 passing, 2 new) cover: no new recommendations once the owner's cap is fully used by other records, and partial headroom allowing exactly the remaining count through. `tsc`, full `eslint`, full `vitest` suite (290/290), and `npm run build` verified clean; no live dev-server click-through. **Channel-fatigue tracker also built now, same pass**: caps how many *distinct* communication channels (`CALL_LEAD`/`SEND_EMAIL`/`SEND_WHATSAPP`/`SEND_SMS`) get suggested for one record at once, in `generateRecommendationsForRecord` right before the `dailyActionCapPerUser` check above. **Real conflict found and resolved against an existing, deliberate test** while building this: `tests/next-best-action.test.ts` already had a test asserting that two *different* rules sharing the *same* `actionType` must NOT block each other (only same-rule recency does, via `recentRuleIds`/`alreadyPendingRuleIds`, both keyed by `ruleId`) — a first version of this guardrail broke that test by treating any 2nd communication candidate as fatigue regardless of channel. Fixed by scoping the guardrail to *distinct channels* specifically: it resolves one "allowed" `actionType` per pass — whichever channel was already recently used (if any, so a rule sharing that exact channel is still free to fire, preserving the existing semantics), otherwise the highest-scored communication candidate's channel — and only filters out candidates whose channel differs from that one. So "two rules both say call" still surfaces both (existing, intended behavior, confirmed by that test); "one rule says call, another says email" now correctly keeps only the higher-scored channel (the actual, new gap this closes) instead of spamming the contact across every channel a rule happens to suggest. Reuses the strategy's own `cooldownHours` window rather than adding a new admin-configurable threshold. 2 new tests (16/16 passing in this file now, including the previously-conflicting one) cover: the cross-channel same-pass case and the recently-used-channel-blocks-a-different-new-channel case (with a non-communication `CREATE_TASK` candidate confirmed unaffected in the same run). `tsc`, full `eslint`, full `vitest` suite (292/292), and `npm run build` verified clean. **Manager-approval-for-high-impact-actions also built now, same pass**. Migration `0039_nba_manager_approval.sql` adds `NextBestActionRule."requiresApproval"` (boolean, default false) and extends `NextBestActionRecommendation.status`'s CHECK constraint with two new values (`PENDING_APPROVAL`, `REJECTED`). Design choice: rather than the system guessing which action types count as "high-impact," an admin explicitly flags specific rules via a new toggle in the rule dialog -- reuses the exact granular per-rule configurability already established (`isDefault`/`isActive` are the same shape of simple per-rule boolean). A recommendation from a `requiresApproval` rule is generated with `status = 'PENDING_APPROVAL'` instead of `'PENDING'`, which already keeps it invisible to the owner's normal accept/dismiss surfaces for free (`listRecommendationsForRecord`/`listRecommendationsForOwner` only ever select `PENDING`/due-`SNOOZED` rows -- no change needed there). New `approveRecommendation`/`rejectRecommendation` (`next-best-action.ts`) transition it to `PENDING` (now fully normal and actionable by the owner) or `REJECTED`, using the exact same atomic-claim `UPDATE ... WHERE status = 'PENDING_APPROVAL'` pattern `respondToRecommendation` already established for its own PENDING/SNOOZED transitions, so two concurrent approve/reject calls can't double-resolve one row. **Who's "the manager"?** Reuses `resolveManagerUserId` (`User.managerId`) -- the exact same relationship `ESCALATE_TO_MANAGER` already resolves, not a new concept. **Authorization, deliberately stricter than this file's usual entitlement checks**: only the resolved manager for the recommendation's owner may approve/reject it -- unlike `assertModuleEnabled`'s tenant-configuration gate, this is NOT bypassed for platform admins, since approving someone else's high-impact action is a real accountability step tied to an actual reporting relationship. New `listPendingApprovalsForManager` (enriches raw rows with the record's display name and the owner's name, same per-row-lookup-is-fine-since-it's-capped-small reasoning as the dashboard-widget NBA list) backs a new self-fetching `NbaPendingApprovalsPanel` component, placed on the main Dashboard page (`dashboard-manager.tsx`, reusing the exact hide-when-empty convention `NextBestActionPanel` already established) -- so a manager sees their approval queue without any new page or nav entry, and everyone else never sees the panel at all.
