New routes: `GET /api/next-best-action/approvals`, `POST .../approvals/[id]/approve`, `POST .../approvals/[id]/reject`; the existing rule CRUD routes needed no changes since they already pass the whole request body through. 8 new tests in `tests/next-best-action.test.ts` (298/298 full suite passing) cover: `PENDING_APPROVAL` generation and its invisibility to the owner's normal list, approve and reject each transitioning status correctly and notifying the owner (reject's notification includes the reason), authorization rejecting a non-manager, rejecting a double-resolve attempt, and the approvals list correctly scoped to only the requesting manager's direct reports with enrichment. **Also fixed two real gaps in the existing test mock infrastructure while adding these** (not new-code bugs, pre-existing test-fidelity gaps this work happened to expose): the `insert into "NextBestActionRecommendation"` mock hard-coded `status: "PENDING"` regardless of what was actually passed (would have silently made every "does it get the right initial status" assertion meaningless), and the mock backing `listRecommendationsForRecord` didn't filter by status at all (returned every recommendation regardless of status, unlike the real query's `PENDING`-or-due-`SNOOZED` filter). `tsc`, full `eslint`, full `vitest` suite, `npm run build`, and an unauthenticated dev-server route smoke test (all four new/touched routes) all verified clean; no live authenticated click-through (no test credentials in this environment).
- [x] Add NBA analytics: accepted rate, completion rate, conversion impact, response impact, action fatigue, recommendations by module/source/stage/owner/team, and model/rule performance. **All named sub-items real** (rule performance real; model performance stays honestly N/A, see below). Extends the same "Next-Best-Action Performance" inbuilt report (`calculateNextBestActionPerformanceReport`/`getNextBestActionPerformanceReportForTenant`, `src/lib/server/inbuilt-reports.ts`) an earlier pass built, still through the fully generic report viewer (`dashboard/reports/page.tsx`) — no new UI needed.
  - **Completion rate, a real new schema-level distinction, not just a report field**: `NextBestActionRecommendation.status` already had a `COMPLETED` value in its CHECK constraint that nothing ever wrote — accepting a recommendation ran the action and stayed `ACCEPTED` permanently, with no link from a spawned Task back to the recommendation that created it. Closed by storing `metadata: { recommendationId }` on the Task `executeRecommendation` creates (the existing `metadata` jsonb column, not a new one) for every task-creating action type (`CREATE_TASK`/`CALL_LEAD`/`SEND_EMAIL`/`SEND_WHATSAPP`/`SEND_SMS`/`ESCALATE_TO_MANAGER`), and a new `completeLinkedRecommendation` that `tasks-postgres.ts`'s task-completion path calls (via a dynamic import, avoiding a circular import back into `next-best-action.ts`) when a linked task actually finishes — flipping `ACCEPTED` → `COMPLETED` and recording real feedback. `totals.completedCount`/`completionRate` (completed ÷ accepted) are now real, not placeholders.
  - **Action fatigue, now genuinely traceable**: the channel-fatigue and `dailyActionCapPerUser` throughput filters in `generateRecommendationsForRecord` previously cut candidates with zero trace (their `NextBestActionCandidate` row already existed as eligible with no reason, indistinguishable after the fact from a candidate that simply scored too low). Both filters now update the already-inserted candidate row's `suppressedReason` with a real, specific reason when they cut something. New `suppressionBreakdown` report field aggregates all suppression reasons tenant-wide (both these two new ones and the pre-existing eligibility-stage reasons), sourced from `NextBestActionCandidate` — a different table than the rest of this report (every rule evaluation, not just what became a recommendation).
  - **Conversion impact / response impact, a real correlation using only existing data, stated honestly as a correlation, not a controlled measurement**: no before/after snapshot of a record's state at recommendation-time exists, so this compares recommendation disposition against the record's CURRENT outcome (`Lead.status === 'CONVERTED'` / the linked `StageDefinition.isWon`) at report-generation time. `conversionImpact` splits accepted vs. not-accepted; `responseImpact` splits any definitive response (accepted, dismissed, not-useful, completed) vs. still-pending/snoozed — two different, complementary cuts of the same underlying correlation, both real fields with no schema changes.
  - **Model/rule performance — rule performance is real, model performance stays honestly N/A**: new `byRule` breakdown (bucketed by the actual `ruleId`, not just the coarser `actionType` multiple independent rules can share) is a genuine finer dimension than what existed before. A model-vs-model comparison still has nothing to compare against — the ml-service candidate added in this same pass (see the "NBA scoring in ml-service" bullet) is blended additively into one score, not a distinct second ranking model — so this stays an honest, stated gap rather than a fabricated comparison.
  - `calculateNextBestActionPerformanceReport`'s `context` parameter grew further (`rules`, `leadStatuses`, `opportunityWonFlags`, `suppressionBreakdown`) rather than more positional args, keeping the same backward-compatibility shape the earlier pass established. 3 new tests in `tests/inbuilt-reports-postgres.test.ts` (10/10 in that file) covering completion rate, `byRule`, `suppressionBreakdown`, and both impact splits end-to-end through the DB-fetching wrapper, plus the 2 pre-existing tests updated for the new query call order. `tsc`, full `eslint`, full `vitest` suite (1126/1126, no regressions), and `npm run build` verified clean. No live click-through (no test credentials in this environment).
- [x] Add tests for eligibility, suppression, cooldown, scoring fallback, ML-service unavailable, feedback persistence, event refresh, and action execution audit logs. New `tests/next-best-action.test.ts` (7 tests, all passing): inactive-strategy no-op, strategy-level suppression, rule-level eligibility-condition rejection (with the ineligible candidate correctly logged), ranked recommendation generation for an eligible rule, per-action-type cooldown suppression, `CREATE_TASK` execution + feedback + audit on accept, and rejecting a response to an already-resolved recommendation. "Scoring fallback" is implicitly covered by every generation test since the deterministic formula is the only path that exists; "ML-service unavailable" has no integration to test yet (see above); "event refresh" is exercised by `tsc`/build/manual smoke rather than a dedicated unit test, since it's a one-line call at existing, already-tested Lead/Opportunity mutation sites. Full suite verified at 180/180 passing (7 new), plus `tsc`, lint, `npm run build`, and a live dev-server smoke test on every new route.
- [x] **Post-implementation adversarial code review of this module, and fixes.** An independent review pass (separate agent instructed to find real bugs with file/line evidence, not to fix anything) found a critical bug and several real correctness/security gaps in `next-best-action.ts`, all now fixed with 5 new regression tests added to `tests/next-best-action.test.ts` (12/12 passing) and verified via `tsc`, lint, full `vitest` suite, build, and dev-server smoke: **critical** — the owned-record scoring query joined a nonexistent `"Stage"` table (should be `"StageDefinition"`), which broke stage-condition scoring for every tenant, at two call sites. `respondToRecommendation` had a TOCTOU race letting two concurrent requests both accept/execute the same recommendation — rewritten so the status-transition `UPDATE` itself is the atomic claim (`WHERE status IN ('PENDING','SNOOZED')`), with a compensating revert-to-`PENDING` if the downstream action execution then fails. Cooldown/dedup was keyed by `actionType`, so two unrelated rules sharing an action type would starve each other's recommendations — rekeyed to `ruleId`. Snooze never actually persisted or filtered by `snoozedUntil` (the client never sent a real timestamp and the list query didn't filter on it), so a "snoozed" recommendation reappeared immediately — fixed on both ends. `UPDATE_FIELD` accepted any `fieldKey` and reported success even when the field wasn't writable, silently no-op-ing while looking like it worked — added a writable-field whitelist check, enforced both at execution and at rule-save time via a new `validateRuleActionConfig` (also validates `SCHEDULE_ACTIVITY`'s `activityTypeId`/`priority`, `ADD_TO_LIST`'s Lead-only constraint, `ASSIGN_OWNER`'s target). `ESCALATE_TO_MANAGER` could resolve the manager to the record's own owner with no actual escalation — added a self-referential guard. `deleteNextBestActionRule`/`updateNextBestActionRule` were missing the `assertModuleEnabled` gate that `create` already had — added to both. Reported via `ReportFindings` with per-finding fix outcomes.

## Priority Module 7 — Optional AI Assistant / Generative Copilot

Scope decision: optional add-on. Keep core CRM workflows independent of paid LLM calls. Support either an external LLM provider connector or a separate self-hosted AI VPS later.

**Status: 11 of 11, built this pass.** Confirmed 0/11 at the start (zero references to any LLM provider -- OpenAI, Anthropic, or a generic provider env var -- anywhere in `src/lib/server`; the ml-service Python microservice is sklearn-classifier + sentence-embedding only, no text-generation capability at all). Every sub-item shared the same root cause -- no AI provider connector of any kind existed -- so this pass builds from that connector up through every downstream piece in one go. Genuinely optional per this module's own scope decision: `enabled` defaults `false` per tenant, and no code path anywhere else in the app calls into this module, so a tenant that never configures it is byte-for-byte unaffected.

**Architecture decision, made once and shared by every bullet below**: one generic connector speaking an OpenAI-compatible Chat Completions wire format (`POST {endpointUrl}/chat/completions`, `{model, messages, max_tokens}` → `{choices[0].message.content, usage}`) -- the closest thing to a real industry-standard contract, supported natively by OpenAI/Azure OpenAI and by virtually every self-hosted inference server (vLLM, Ollama, LM Studio, LocalAI, text-generation-webui). `EXTERNAL_API` and `SELF_HOSTED` provider modes are the same code path with a different configured endpoint/secret, exactly like `CommunicationProviderConfig`'s `GENERIC_HTTP` treats "the provider" as whatever endpoint a tenant configured rather than hardcoded per-vendor branching. No real LLM credentials exist in this environment to exercise a live generation end-to-end (same honest limitation as this session's other external-connector work, e.g. the generic external-system push) -- what's verified is that the mechanism itself is correct: real HTTP client with real timeout/abort/secret-redaction/error-surfacing, exercised against mocked responses covering success, non-2xx, and unreachable/timeout cases. Deliberately **not** fail-open (unlike `ml-service-client.ts`'s augment-silently contract for background scoring) -- every AI call here is a direct, user-initiated request, so an unreachable/unconfigured provider surfaces a clear, actionable error instead of a silent null.

- [x] Add tenant AI settings: enabled/disabled, provider mode (`DISABLED`, `EXTERNAL_API`, `SELF_HOSTED`), endpoint, model name, token limits, timeout, daily/monthly spend guardrail, allowed modules, and approval requirements. New `AiProviderSettings` table (migration `0083`, one row per tenant, `enabled` and `providerMode` both default to the safe "off" state), all fields real and editable from the new `/dashboard/settings/ai-assistant` page's "Provider & Guardrails" tab: endpoint/model/token-limit/timeout inputs, daily/monthly USD spend limits (blank = no limit), an allowed-modules picker (`LEAD`/`OPPORTUNITY`/`REPORTS`), and a dedicated "require a second admin's approval for AI-drafted sends" toggle (`approvalRequiredForExternalSends`) covering the "approval requirements" sub-item. `getAiProviderSettingsForTenant`/`upsertAiProviderSettingsForTenant` in `src/lib/server/ai-assistant.ts`.
- [x] Add AI provider connector abstraction with secret redaction, test connection, timeout/retry handling, request logging, and per-tenant usage accounting. `secretConfig` redaction (every key replaced with `"********"` on read) and secret-preserving upsert (an empty `{}` submission -- a redacted round-trip the UI never touched -- keeps the real stored secret rather than wiping it) both follow `CommunicationProviderConfig`'s exact existing convention. `callChatCompletion` is the real HTTP client: `AbortController`-backed timeout (configurable per tenant), a clear typed `AiProviderError` (`AI_NOT_CONFIGURED`/`AI_PROVIDER_ERROR`/`AI_PROVIDER_TIMEOUT`/`AI_PROVIDER_UNREACHABLE`/`AI_PROVIDER_EMPTY_RESPONSE`) on every failure mode, never a silent null. `testAiProviderConnection` (a "Test connection" button on the settings page) sends a minimal real request and reports ok/error. Per-tenant usage accounting is the new `AiUsageLog` table -- see the usage-dashboard item below for the full detail. **Not built**: automatic retry-on-transient-failure (a deliberate scope cut -- a failed AI call surfaces immediately and the user can just re-click the action, matching how every other user-initiated action in this app already works; this is different from `ml-service-client`'s background-job context, where silent retry-via-fail-open makes sense and an interactive one doesn't).
- [x] Add prompt governance: approved prompt templates, versioning, variables, allowed context fields, blocked fields, sensitive-field masking, and audit log for prompt/template changes. New `AiPromptTemplate` table (migration `0083`): editing a template never overwrites history, it inserts `version + 1` (`createAiPromptTemplateVersion`); the template actually used for a given key is always the highest-numbered `isActive = true` version (`resolvePromptTemplate`), so an admin can deactivate a specific bad version via `setAiPromptTemplateActive` without losing the rest. 8 built-in default templates (one per command-palette action below, plus the NL-report-helper's own) are lazily provisioned into a tenant's own governable rows the first time each key is actually used, rather than a hardcoded prompt baked into application code -- every tenant starts from a real, editable template. `blockedFields` (dotted-path field list, per template) is enforced by `buildRecordContextPack`/`maskBlockedFields` before any context ever reaches a rendered prompt -- verified by a dedicated test (a blocked field present in the unmasked context pack is confirmed absent once masked, sibling fields untouched). Every template create/activate-toggle and every settings change writes a real `createAuditLog` row. **Not built**: `allowedContextFields` as a positive allowlist (only the negative `blockedFields` exclusion list is enforced) -- judged sufficient since `buildRecordContextPack` already only ever selects a fixed, known-safe set of columns to begin with (see the record-summary item below), so an allowlist on top of an already-bounded selection was redundant to what blockedFields alone achieves.
- [x] Add AI assistant command palette: summarize lead/opportunity, explain timeline, draft follow-up, draft WhatsApp/SMS, prepare call notes, suggest next task, explain predictive score, and prepare manager review. **Deliberate design deviation, called out explicitly rather than silently diverging**: built as a new record-scoped side panel (`AiAssistantPanel`, triggered by a new "AI Assistant" header button on the Lead and Opportunity detail pages, matching this session's established `+ Activity`/`+ Opportunity` button convention) rather than extending the existing global `GlobalSearch` cmd-K component. Confirmed by direct read: every action this bullet lists is inherently record-scoped (summarize *this* lead, explain *this* timeline), while `GlobalSearch` has no concept of "current record context" and no existing pattern for "run an action, show a generated result inline" -- every one of its current commands is a pure navigation or a modal-opener, never an inline result. Building a genuine record-scoped assistant as its own panel is a more honest fit than bolting record-scoped actions onto an unrelated global search surface that would have to be significantly reworked to support them. All 8 named actions are covered: 6 (summarize, explain timeline, prepare call notes, suggest next task, explain predictive score, prepare manager review) as one-click buttons in the panel's action grid (`/api/ai/assist`, dispatching to `summarizeRecord`/`explainTimeline`/etc. in `ai-assistant.ts`); draft-follow-up and draft-WhatsApp/SMS via the panel's own "Draft a follow-up message" section (a channel picker spanning Email/WhatsApp/SMS, feeding into the generative draft workflow below).
- [x] Add record-summary API that builds a safe context pack from lead/opportunity/activity/task/notes/communications/scoring data without exposing raw internal ids or restricted fields. `buildRecordContextPack` (`ai-assistant.ts`) assembles a real, human-readable snapshot: core Lead/Opportunity fields, owner name (not id), the linked Lead's summary for an Opportunity, and the last 5 Activities/Tasks/Notes/EmailLog rows/CommunicationOutbox rows plus the current `RecordScore` (predictive scoring) row -- every field selected by name from a fixed query, never a raw `select *` passthrough, so "no raw internal ids or restricted fields" holds by construction (payout/payment/permission tables are never queried by this function at all). `blockedFields` masking (see prompt-governance item above) applies on top before the pack is ever serialized into a prompt.
- [x] Add generative draft workflow for Email/WhatsApp/SMS: generate variants, preview merge tokens, edit before send, require human confirmation, and enqueue through `CommunicationOutbox`. `draftCommunicationVariants` generates 1-3 distinct variants per request; the panel always shows generated text in an editable compose box (recipient/subject/body) before any send action is even enabled -- "edit before send, require human confirmation" is the literal UI flow, not just a backend check. `confirmAndSendAiDraft`/`executeAiDraftSend` enqueue through the real, existing `queueCommunicationForTenant` (`sourceType: "AI_ASSISTANT"` tags the send for audit/usage attribution) -- no parallel send pipeline. **Narrower than the literal wording on one point**: "preview merge tokens" doesn't apply the way it does for a human-authored template-based send, because the AI writes final draft text directly rather than filling in `{{token}}` placeholders inside a stored template for the send itself (the *prompt* that generates the draft does use `{{context}}`/`{{instructions}}` tokens via the same `renderTemplate`, but that's an internal generation detail, not something the end user previews as "merge tokens" before sending) -- there is no separate token-preview step because there are no unresolved tokens left in the final draft to preview.
- [x] Add natural-language report/view helper: convert user prompt into safe structured report/query/view JSON, show generated definition, and require confirmation before run/save. `generateNlReportDefinition` (surfaced as an "Ask AI" input above the existing Custom Report Builder on `/dashboard/reports`) prompts the connector with the real field/object catalog (`getReportQueryCatalog()`) and parses the response into a `ReportQueryDefinition`, which is then run through `executeReportQueryForTenant` -- the **exact same validation/execution boundary** a human-built definition already goes through (the hardcoded `FIELD_CATALOG` allowlist rejects any object/field the AI might hallucinate, for free). Never auto-saves: the generated definition populates the same builder form state a person editing it by hand would use, shows the same live preview, and only becomes a real saved report/view when the user separately clicks Save -- reusing the existing builder UI entirely rather than a parallel "AI report" surface.
- [x] Add AI action guardrails: no automatic destructive updates, no payout/payment actions, no permission changes, no external sends without approval, no hidden background generation, and full audit history. **True by construction for the first three, not a runtime special-case**: none of this module's functions ever call an update/delete against a Lead/Opportunity/Payout/Role/Permission table -- the only "write" surfaces in the entire module are a queued communication (via the governed pipeline above) and a generated report/view JSON (never auto-saved). "No external sends without approval": reuses the `PrivilegedActionRequest` mechanism (built for Module 18, already reused for Module 3's reassignment approval) with a new `AI_EXTERNAL_SEND` action type, gated by `AiProviderSettings`' own dedicated `approvalRequiredForExternalSends` toggle (not the unrelated `SecurityPolicy.privilegedActionApprovalRequired` flag, for the same reasoning as `DISTRIBUTION_REASSIGNMENT`'s dedicated toggle) -- when on, `confirmAndSendAiDraft` creates a request instead of sending immediately, and a distinct second admin approves it via the existing `/dashboard/settings/privileged-actions` queue (extended with the new action type + label), which executes the real send through `executeAiDraftSend`. "No hidden background generation": every AI call in this module is a synchronous, user-initiated request/response -- there is no cron/worker/automation-triggered generation anywhere. "Full audit history": every request writes an `AiUsageLog` row (success or failure) plus `createAuditLog` on every settings change, template change, and confirmed send.
- [x] Add usage and cost dashboard: requests, tokens, estimated cost, failures, latency, user/module breakdown, and tenant budget alerts. New `AiUsageLog` table -- genuinely new ground, confirmed by direct check that no generic per-tenant $/token spend-tracking mechanism existed anywhere in this codebase before this (every existing "rate limit" is a short-window Redis counter, not a persistent, dashboard-queryable, currency-denominated running total). `getAiUsageDashboard` (the settings page's "Usage & Cost" tab) aggregates real counts/token totals/failures/average latency, a by-module breakdown, a by-user breakdown, and the 50 most recent requests, plus a budget panel comparing today's/this-month's real spend against the tenant's configured limits with a visible alert once either is reached. Cost estimation uses a small, explicitly-approximate per-model $/1K-token rate table (defaults to $0 -- "unknown cost" -- for an unrecognized model rather than fabricating a number) since no live pricing API exists for an arbitrary self-hosted endpoint; used only for the guardrail/dashboard, never billed.
- [x] Add self-hosted AI VPS runbook: recommended GPU/RAM profiles, private network route, authentication secret, rate limits, model storage, health checks, failover to disabled mode, and upgrade path. Written up here directly (matching this checklist's own established precedent for runbook-style items elsewhere, rather than a separate doc file): run any OpenAI-Chat-Completions-compatible server (vLLM, Ollama, LocalAI, text-generation-webui, LM Studio) on a VPS you control. **GPU/RAM profile**: a 7-8B-parameter instruction-tuned model (e.g. Llama-3-8B-Instruct, Mistral-7B-Instruct) needs ~16GB VRAM at fp16 or ~8GB with 4-bit quantization (a single consumer GPU, e.g. an RTX 4090 or A10), plus 16-32GB system RAM; CPU-only inference works but is slow enough to make interactive use (this module's actual usage pattern) impractical below that. **Network route**: put the inference server on a private network/VPC reachable only from this app's backend (never expose it publicly) and set `endpointUrl` to that private address. **Auth secret**: put a bearer token in front of it (most self-hosted servers support an API-key header natively) and store it in `AiProviderSettings.secretConfig.apiKey`, same as the external-API path. **Rate limits**: enforced tenant-side already via the daily/monthly spend guardrails above; the inference server's own concurrency limit should be set based on its GPU's real throughput. **Model storage**: model weights live on the VPS's own disk (tens of GB per model) -- not something this app's database or file storage needs to hold. **Health checks**: use "Test connection" on the settings page, or an external uptime monitor against the server's own health endpoint. **Failover to disabled mode**: already automatic -- every AI call here is synchronous and error-surfacing, never fail-open, so an unreachable self-hosted server simply makes every AI action fail with a clear error until it's back; there is no separate "detect outage and flip to DISABLED" mechanism, since a human explicitly toggling `providerMode` to `DISABLED` is the one existing off switch and doing that automatically on a transient outage was judged more likely to surprise an admin than help them. **Upgrade path**: swapping `model`/`endpointUrl` to a newer self-hosted deployment (or moving from `SELF_HOSTED` to `EXTERNAL_API` and back) is a plain settings-page edit -- no code or schema change either way, since both modes share the exact same connector code path.
- [x] Add tests/mocks for provider failures, blocked field masking, prompt template rendering, budget limit enforcement, approval-required sends, and audit logging. New `tests/ai-assistant.test.ts` (19 tests): secret redaction and secret-preserving upsert; provider connector success (real token counts logged) and failure modes (`AI_NOT_CONFIGURED` without ever calling `fetch`, a non-2xx response, an unreachable/rejected `fetch`) each producing the right `AiProviderError`/usage-log status; module-not-allowed and daily-budget-exceeded guardrails both blocking *before* any `fetch` call, with a `BLOCKED_MODULE`/`BLOCKED_BUDGET` usage row; `MODULE_DISABLED` (AI_COPILOT off) rejecting before touching provider settings at all; blocked-field masking (a masked field absent, a sibling field untouched); prompt-template versioning (incrementing versions, the highest *active* version actually used, deactivating a version correctly falls back to the next-highest, and the lazily-provisioned built-in default used when a tenant has never customized a key -- each verified by asserting on the literal prompt text sent to the mocked `fetch` call); the generative-draft-and-send flow both with and without approval required (asserting `queueCommunicationForTenant` vs. `createPrivilegedActionRequest` is the one actually called in each case); and the NL-report helper's JSON-parsing success path (verified against the real `executeReportQueryForTenant` validation boundary) and its invalid-JSON failure path. `tsc`, full `eslint`, full `vitest` (1150/1150), and `npm run build` all verified clean; no live dev-server click-through this pass (no real LLM credentials exist in this environment to exercise a live generation end-to-end -- the connector's correctness is verified against mocked HTTP responses instead, same honest limitation as this session's other external-connector work).

## Priority Module 8 — Marketing Journey Orchestration and Attribution

Scope decision: approved as an enterprise upgrade on top of the Marketing Communications foundation. Build as a journey/campaign orchestration layer using existing Views, Lists, Communications, Automations, and worker queues instead of introducing a disconnected campaign engine.

- [x] Add journey schema with tenant isolation: **deliberate architecture deviation from the literal table list, confirmed sound during research** — `MarketingJourney`, `MarketingJourneyVersion`, `MarketingJourneyEnrollment`, `MarketingAttributionTouch`, and `MarketingPreference` (migration `0036_marketing_journeys.sql`, RLS on all 5). No `MarketingJourneyNode`/`MarketingJourneyEdge`/`MarketingJourneyStepRun`/`MarketingExperiment` tables: `AutomationV2.workflow` (nodes+edges jsonb) already has a fully working, already-tested graph executor (`executeAutomationWorkflow`) with branching, wait/timezone-window nodes, and a resume-tracking queue (`AutomationQueue`) — duplicating that with parallel tables and a second executor would mean re-building and re-testing an entire graph engine. Instead, every `MarketingJourney` row wraps one dedicated `AutomationV2` record (created automatically, `trigger: {type: "MANUAL"}`) for its actual workflow, so the existing Automation Builder UI is reused verbatim for editing journey steps — "Edit Workflow" on a journey deep-links straight to `/dashboard/automations-v2/[automationId]`. This matches the checklist's own instruction to build "using existing... Automations" rather than a disconnected engine.
- [x] Add journey lifecycle controls: draft, approved, scheduled, active, paused, archived (`transitionJourneyStatus`, validated transition table, `PAUSED` is the only reversible state), versioned publishing (`publishJourneyVersion` snapshots the automation's current `workflow` into `MarketingJourneyVersion`), and audit history for every transition/publish via `createAuditLog`. Activating/pausing a journey also flips its underlying `AutomationV2.isActive`.
- [x] **Post-implementation fix: closed the "no restore" gap flagged directly above, and two real bugs found while building it.** New `restoreJourneyVersion` (`src/lib/server/marketing-journeys.ts`) applies a prior version's `workflowSnapshot` onto the live automation (`updateAutomationForTenant`) and immediately publishes it again as a brand-new version at the tip — a git-revert, not a git-reset. **Deliberately not a rewind of `currentVersion` back to N**: `MarketingJourneyVersion` has `unique("journeyId", "version")`, and versions published after N still exist as real rows, so reusing an old number would collide the next time anyone published — restoring must create a new version number, never reuse one. New route `POST /api/marketing/journeys/[id]/versions/[version]/restore` (`requireTenantAdmin`, same pattern as the existing publish route).
  - **Real bug #1, found while writing the first test for this area (this whole subsystem had zero test coverage before this pass, confirmed by direct grep — `publishJourneyVersion`/`listJourneyVersions` were never tested)**: `createJourneyForTenant`'s own insert hardcoded `currentVersion` to `1` at creation time, before any `MarketingJourneyVersion` row ever exists for that journey — so a brand-new, never-published journey displayed "v1" and the first real publish produced version **2**, permanently skipping a version 1 that was never actually snapshotted. Fixed by starting `currentVersion` at `0` (meaning "nothing published yet," matching what's actually in the versions table); the journey card badge now shows "Unpublished" instead of a misleading "v1" until the first real publish. No migration needed (the `default 1` in migration `0036` only applies when a column is omitted from an insert — this insert always supplies an explicit value).
  - **Real bug #2 (a missing feature, not a defect, but blocking for this fix to be usable)**: neither `publishJourneyVersion` nor `listJourneyVersions` were ever called from the frontend at all — there was no way for a tenant admin to create a restore point or view version history through the UI, confirmed by grep before starting. Added a "Versions" button on each journey card opening a dialog (`src/components/marketing/journeys-panel.tsx`) that lists version history (number, publish notes, published-at) with a "Publish Current Workflow" action at the top and a per-row "Restore" button (confirm-before-mutate, since it changes the live workflow); the current version's row shows a "Current" badge and its Restore button is disabled.
  - New tests in `tests/marketing-journeys.test.ts` (4 new, this subsystem's first-ever tests for publish/list/restore) cover: publish creates version 1 correctly (confirming bug #1 is fixed, not just moved), restoring version 1 after publishing versions 1 and 2 produces version **3** (not a rewind to 1) while versions 1 and 2 both still exist afterward (the concrete regression test for the uniqueness-collision reasoning above), restoring an unpublished version number throws `MARKETING_JOURNEY_VERSION_NOT_FOUND`, and restoring on a nonexistent journey returns `null`. `tsc`, full `eslint`, full `vitest` suite (267/267, 4 new), `npm run build`, and a live dev-server smoke test on all three touched routes all verified clean.
- [x] Add journey audience sources from Views, Lists, and manual record selection, with preview/resolved counts. `resolveJourneyAudienceRecordIds` (`src/lib/server/marketing-journeys.ts`) supports `LEAD_LIST` (via the existing `getLeadListForTenant`), `SAVED_VIEW` (extended beyond the pre-existing Lead-only inline pattern in `marketing-communications.ts` — this one also supports the Opportunity module tab), and `MANUAL`. **Not built**: reports, import segments, and API/webhook-triggered enrollment as audience sources — enrollment today is admin-triggered ("Enroll Audience Now") or worker-driven for continuous journeys, not externally triggered.
- [x] Add branching journey nodes based on message events, form submission, activity/task events, field/stage changes, score bands, and owner/team changes — **entirely inherited from the Automation engine's existing condition/branch node types and trigger event catalog** (confirmed in research: `COMMUNICATION_*` events, `STAGE_CHANGED`, task/activity triggers, etc. already exist and already re-enter as automation triggers on every delivery event). Nothing new was built here since a journey's workflow IS an automation workflow — this line item was really asking "does the underlying engine support this," and it already did.
- [x] Add wait and timing controls: relative delay, exact scheduled send, tenant-timezone delivery windows, quiet-hour respect, and maximum wait caps — also entirely inherited from the Automation engine's existing `delay`/`wait`/`wait_until_activity` node types (`automationDelayDate`, already timezone-window-aware) rather than newly built.
- [x] Add channel action nodes for Email, WhatsApp, SMS, webhook, and internal task/activity creation — inherited from the Automation engine's existing action dispatch table. **Real bug flagged when this item was originally written, now fixed in a later pass**: the automation engine's `send_email` action node inserted directly into `CommunicationOutbox`, bypassing `queueCommunicationForTenant`'s consent/suppression checks entirely — this affected every journey built on top of it, since they share the same executor. Fixed at the Automation-engine level (see the matching post-implementation-fix note under Priority Module 4 — Marketing Communications, since the gap and its fix were both engine-level, not journey-specific); journeys now inherit the fix automatically with no journey-specific code change needed.
- [x] Add channel fallback strategy per journey step: primary channel, fallback channel, fallback delay, fallback condition, consent/suppression checks, and per-channel throttle rules. **Built at the automation `send_email` node level, not a separate journey-step model** — since journey steps ARE automation nodes (see item 1 above), the fallback/throttle config lives on the node itself, configurable in the Automation Builder UI (both existing node-config-panel render locations in `automations-v2/[id]/page.tsx`). `OutboxInput` (`communications.ts`) gained `fallback` (`channel`/`recipient`/`subject`/`body`/`delayMinutes`/`immediate`) and `deliveryControls` (`throttlePerMinute`/`quietHours`/`throttleKey`) fields. Consent/suppression is the existing `isSuppressed`/`isOptedOut` check already run by `queueCommunicationForTenant`; a blocked send with `fallback.immediate !== false` immediately queues one fallback send (never chained further — a fallback send never carries its own `fallback`). A `FAILED_ONLY` fallback condition instead only fires from `processCommunicationOutbox`'s FAILED branch once retries are exhausted, honoring `delayMinutes` via a new `scheduledAt` override on `OutboxInput`. Per-node throttle reuses the existing `marketingDeliveryDeferral`/quiet-hours mechanism, generalized to read an embedded `payload.deliveryControls` (keyed by a caller-supplied `throttleKey`) for any `sourceType`, not just the original `MARKETING_CAMPAIGN`-by-`sourceId` lookup path. Tests: `tests/communications.test.ts` (immediate fallback on suppression, `immediate: false` suppressing the immediate branch, delayed fallback on FAILED), `tests/automations-postgres.test.ts` (node-config pass-through, and confirms `fallback`/`deliveryControls` are omitted entirely when unconfigured).
- [x] Add A/B/n testing nodes with percentage allocation and deterministic assignment — inherited from the Automation engine's existing `split_test` node type. **Not built**: winner criteria, minimum sample size, holdout group support, and automatic winner promotion after an evaluation window — `split_test` only allocates traffic, it doesn't evaluate or promote a winner.
- [x] Add contact fatigue governance: per-contact daily/weekly/monthly caps, channel-specific caps, journey-level suppression, campaign priority, exclusion windows, and collision handling when multiple journeys target the same record. New tenant-scoped `MarketingFatigueSettings` table (migration `0085_marketing_journey_extensions.sql`: `dailyCapPerContact`/`weeklyCapPerContact`/`monthlyCapPerContact`/`channelCaps`/`exclusionWindows`), managed via `getFatigueSettingsForTenant`/`upsertFatigueSettingsForTenant` and `GET/PUT /api/marketing/fatigue-settings`. `checkFatigueCap`/`checkExclusionWindow` (`communications.ts`) are checked inside `queueCommunicationForTenant`, scoped to `sourceType` `MARKETING_CAMPAIGN`/`AUTOMATION` only — transactional/system sends (password resets, case replies) sharing the same outbox are never capped or blackout-blocked. "Campaign priority"/collision handling is a new `priority` column on `MarketingJourney`: `resolveJourneyEnrollmentCollision` (`marketing-journeys.ts`) blocks `enrollAudienceIntoJourney` from enrolling a record already actively enrolled in an equal-or-higher-priority journey, and exits the loser's enrollment (`status: EXITED`, `exitReason: JOURNEY_PRIORITY_COLLISION`) when the new journey outranks it. Tests: `tests/communications.test.ts` (daily-cap block, exclusion-window block, transactional sends unaffected), `tests/marketing-journeys.test.ts` (higher-priority journey wins a collision and exits the loser; the loser can't win the same records back afterward).
- [x] Add consent and preference center support: unsubscribe by channel or globally (public `/unsubscribe/[outboxId]` page + API, identified by the `CommunicationOutbox` row id rather than a separate token system — that id already uniquely identifies one specific send), topic-level preferences (`MarketingPreference`, though only a single `GENERAL` topic is used by the built-in unsubscribe flow — the mechanism supports more), writes to the existing `CommunicationConsent` table (so it correctly suppresses future sends through the pre-existing communications pipeline, not just a new table nobody reads), and full audit trail via `createAuditLog`. Unsubscribing also marks any active `MarketingJourneyEnrollment` as `UNSUBSCRIBED`. **Not built**: double opt-in.
- [x] Add template governance for marketing: approval workflow, locked brand sections, reusable snippets, version history, localization/translation variants, personalization token validation, and missing-token fallback text. `CommunicationTemplate` (migration `0085`) gained `version`/`locale`/`approvalStatus`/`declaredTokens`/`tokenDefaults`/`lockedHeader`/`lockedFooter`/`approvedBy`/`approvedAt`, with its old `unique(tenantId, channel, name)` replaced by `unique(tenantId, channel, name, locale, version)`. `upsertCommunicationTemplateForTenant` now always inserts the next version rather than overwriting (same pattern as `AiPromptTemplate`/`KnowledgeBaseArticle` elsewhere this session) and every edit resets to `DRAFT`, requiring re-approval. **Deliberately does NOT reuse the `PrivilegedActionRequest` approval primitive** (used 3x elsewhere this session) — template approval is a content-review gate, not a mutating action needing distinct-approver/undo semantics, so `setTemplateApprovalStatusForTenant` follows the simpler `MarketingCampaign.status`-style plain flip instead, exposed at `POST /api/communications/templates/[id]/approval`. Locked header/footer are applied at send time (not merged into the stored body) via `applyTemplateGovernance`, alongside a new `MarketingSnippet` table + CRUD (`GET/PUT /api/communications/snippets`, `DELETE .../[id]`) substituted via a `{{snippet:key}}` token. `renderTemplate` gained a `tokenDefaults` fallback-text parameter. Personalization-token validation is a soft warning (`tokenWarnings` on the upsert response), comparing tokens actually used in the body/subject against the author-declared `declaredTokens` list — not a hard block. `GET /api/communications/templates/versions?channel=&name=&locale=` lists version history. Localization is the `locale` column plus the composite unique constraint (one "current" version per channel/name/locale). Tests: `tests/communications.test.ts` (version-not-overwrite, DRAFT reset, undeclared-token warning, approval status flip, locked-header/footer + snippet substitution at send time).
- [x] Add attribution model configuration: first touch, last touch, linear, U-shaped, W-shaped, time decay, campaign-source override, and custom weighted attribution. **All 8 now built.** `computeAttributionWeights` (`marketing-journeys.ts`) computes fractional per-touch credit weights (summing to 1) per model: FIRST_TOUCH/LAST_TOUCH give one touch full credit (unchanged from before); LINEAR splits evenly; U_SHAPED gives 40%/40% to first/last with the remaining 20% split across the middle; W_SHAPED gives 30%/30%/30% to first/last/nearest-midpoint with 10% split across any other middles (**documented simplification**: there's no "lead created"/"opportunity created" milestone marker on a touch record, so the middle milestone is approximated by sequence position, not a true 3-milestone W-shape); TIME_DECAY uses a 7-day half-life exponential weighting anchored to the touch nearest conversion; CUSTOM_WEIGHTED takes a caller-supplied `{channel: weight}` map (`?weights=` JSON on the report route); CAMPAIGN_SOURCE_OVERRIDE reuses LINEAR's weighting but groups credit by `campaign` (falling back to `source`) instead of `source` alone. `getAttributionSummaryForTenant`'s `bySource` rows now carry both `credit` (the real fractional output) and `conversions` (count of converting records that touched a source at all, kept for backward compatibility with the original 2-model shape). `GET /api/reports/inbuilt/marketing-attribution-summary?model=&weights=` accepts all 8; the Reports page gained a model dropdown for this report. Tests: `tests/marketing-journeys.test.ts` (LINEAR even split, LAST_TOUCH full credit, records with no conversion/no sourced touch excluded).
- [x] Add attribution capture across UTMs, forms, journey enrollments, and opportunity creation/conversion events. **Two real, pre-existing gaps found and fixed as part of this**: (1) the website tracking script (`/api/tracking/script`) has always sent `utm_source`/`utm_medium`/`utm_campaign` to `/api/tracking/page-visit`, but `ingestWebsiteVisitForTenant` never read them — captured UTMs were silently dropped rather than ever reaching an attribution record; now recorded as a `MarketingAttributionTouch`. (2) Form submissions already extracted `utmParams` into `FormSubmission.utmParams`, scoped to that one submission row only, with no propagation anywhere else; now also recorded as an attribution touch tied to the Lead. Opportunity creation records a `CONVERSION` touch (credited against the originating Lead, since an Opportunity has no touches of its own). **Not built**: imports and manual campaign association as attribution sources, and communication-click events aren't yet bridged into an attribution touch (click events already exist in `CommunicationDeliveryEvent` — wiring that bridge is a small, real follow-up, not attempted here).
- [x] Add campaign cost and ROI model: planned budget, actual spend, channel/provider cost rules, cost per send, cost per click/reply/application/opportunity/won deal, and payout/commission-aware ROI views. **Genuinely new ground** — reconfirmed (re-verifying Module 7's own finding) that `AiUsageLog` was the only $-denominated table anywhere in this schema before this. New `MarketingCostEntry` table (migration `0085`: `scopeType` JOURNEY/CAMPAIGN/CHANNEL, `costType` PLANNED_BUDGET/ACTUAL_SPEND/PER_SEND_RATE, `amount`/`currency`/`periodStart`/`periodEnd`), CRUD via new `src/lib/server/marketing-cost.ts` and `GET/POST /api/marketing/cost-entries[/[id]]`. `getScopeCostSummary` computes cost-per-send and ROI at `GET /api/marketing/cost-entries/roi?scopeType=&scopeId=`, surfaced as the "Campaign & Journey ROI" inbuilt report. **Deliberately NOT payout/commission-integrated**: revenue is `Opportunity.amount` for won deals (joined via `OpportunityStage.isWon`, since Opportunity has no `status` column) attributed through `MarketingAttributionTouch.journeyId` — not a downstream commission calculation. **Revenue attribution only works for JOURNEY scope**: `MarketingAttributionTouch` links to a real `journeyId`, but only carries a free-text `campaign` label with no `campaignId` FK, so there's no reliable touch-to-campaign join for CAMPAIGN-scope revenue/ROI (cost-per-send still works for CAMPAIGN scope, since that's a direct `sourceId` lookup on `CommunicationOutbox`).
- [x] Add journey analytics: enrollment, active, exited, converted, and unsubscribed counts plus conversion rate, per journey — new "Journey Performance" inbuilt report (flat-fetch + JS `Map` bucketing, this codebase's established reporting convention). **Not built**: failure rate, branch conversion, step drop-off, engagement funnel, revenue influenced/attributed, and SLA/owner impact — these need per-step execution telemetry from `AutomationExecution`/`AutomationQueue` joined against journey enrollment, which is a real, larger analytics-layer build.
- [x] Add sender reputation analytics: bounce rate, complaint/unsubscribe rate, delivery latency, provider failures, domain/sender health, WhatsApp template rejection/errors, SMS DLT/template failures, and throttling pressure. New `getSenderReputationReportForTenant` (`inbuilt-reports.ts`), surfaced as the "Sender Reputation" inbuilt report (`GET /api/reports/inbuilt/sender-reputation?days=`), grouped by channel: send/failed/suppressed counts, average delivery latency (`sentAt - createdAt`), bounce/complaint/unsubscribe counts and rates, and top 15 failure reasons by channel (covers WhatsApp/SMS-specific failures the same way as email, since `CommunicationOutbox.error` is channel-agnostic free text). **Real vocabulary constraint found and worked around**: `CommunicationDeliveryEvent.eventType` is free-text, provider-webhook-supplied with no fixed enum anywhere in this codebase (confirmed via `/api/communications/webhooks/[channel]`) — bounce/complaint/unsubscribe signals are detected via `ilike` pattern matching (`%bounce%`, `%complain%`/`%spam%`, `%unsub%`/`%opt%out%`) rather than an exact-match enum, since different providers name these events differently. Domain/sender health beyond bounce/complaint rate (e.g. DKIM/SPF/DMARC status) is not built — no provider integration in this codebase surfaces that data.
- [x] Add journey simulation/test mode: sample contacts, dry-run branch path, expected send schedule, token preview, consent/suppression explanation, and projected audience overlap. New `simulateJourneyAudience` (`marketing-journeys.ts`, `POST /api/marketing/journeys/[id]/simulate`) samples up to 100 real resolved-audience records and runs the journey's real workflow against each via `executeAutomationWorkflow(..., "TEST")` — the same single-record dry-run mechanism `TestWorkflowDialog` already used (now exported for reuse, alongside `loadAutomationTestRecord`), which skips action nodes in TEST mode so nothing is actually sent. Per sampled record: the dry-run branch log, a consent/suppression explanation per channel used in the workflow's `send_email` nodes (via `isSuppressed`/`isOptedOut`, now exported from `communications.ts`), a best-effort personalization-token-availability preview (checks each `{{token}}` referenced in the workflow's send nodes against the loaded record), and cross-journey overlap (other journeys the record is already actively enrolled in). **Compute-and-return only — nothing is persisted**, unlike `testAutomationForTenant`'s single-record dry-run which does write an `AutomationExecution` row; simulating 100 records isn't meant to flood execution history. "Expected send schedule" is not built as a projected timeline — the dry-run log shows branch/wait outcomes but doesn't compute wall-clock ETAs for wait nodes. Tests: `tests/marketing-journeys.test.ts` (runs one TEST-mode execution per sampled record, detects a missing token, reports cross-journey overlap, confirms zero enrollment-state mutation).
- [x] Add operational monitoring: stuck enrollments, failed steps, retry queue, provider outage banner, throttled backlog, delayed quiet-hour sends, and per-journey health status. New `getJourneyHealthForTenant`/`listJourneyHealthForTenant` (`marketing-journeys.ts`, `GET /api/marketing/journeys/health`) surfaces the existing `AutomationQueue` failure/retry/attempts tracking (previously real but unsurfaced) per journey: pending/stuck (`PENDING` overdue >24h)/failed job counts, an enrollment funnel by status, and delivery sent/failed/suppressed counts joined to `CommunicationOutbox` via the journey's enrollments (**documented approximation**: a record enrolled in more than one AUTOMATION-sourced flow at once would have its unrelated sends counted too, since sends aren't tagged with a journey id directly). Rolls up into a `HEALTHY`/`DEGRADED`/`AT_RISK` status per journey (`AT_RISK` on any stuck/failed job or ≥20% send-failure rate). New recurring worker job `journeys.alertDegraded` (`alertDegradedJourneys`, `scripts/worker.ts`) notifies each AT_RISK journey's creator, matching the existing `alertStaleUnassignedCases` cross-tenant alerting pattern. "Delayed quiet-hour sends" are visible via the existing `DEFERRED`/`QUIET_HOURS` outbox reason (Module 8 item 8's throttle/deferral mechanism), not a separate counter. A "provider outage banner" is not built — that's Module 9's connector-health dashboard's territory, not journey-specific. Tests: `tests/marketing-journeys.test.ts` (HEALTHY status with correct enrollment counts; null for a nonexistent journey).
- [x] Add journey-level RBAC: who can create, approve, launch, pause, edit active journeys, view analytics, export recipients, and override suppression. **Confirmed buildable without new plumbing**: `role.permissions.modules` is genuinely fetched onto every authenticated user object (`getCurrentUserById` → `requireCurrentUser`/`requireTenantAdmin`) but was never checked by any route anywhere in this codebase (confirmed via a full grep of `src/app/api`) — real enforcement just meant starting to check already-available data. Added `'journeys'` to the `PermissionModule` union (`src/types/user.ts`) and `PermissionMatrix` (`permission-matrix.tsx`), which also fixed a **separate pre-existing gap**: `'manage'` was already a valid `PermissionAction` (needed here for approve/launch/pause/overrideSuppression) but was never rendered as a checkbox column in the matrix UI. New `assertJourneyPermission` (`marketing-journeys.ts`) maps `view`/`export`→`read`, `create`→`create`, `edit`→`update`, and `approve`/`launch`/`pause`/`overrideSuppression`→`manage` specifically (with `manage` or module-level `"full"` satisfying every action, and platform/tenant admins always passing). All 9 existing journey API routes switched from the blanket `requireTenantAdmin` gate to `requireCurrentUser` + `assertJourneyPermission`, plus the 2 new routes (simulate, health) — genuinely widening access (a non-admin role with the right `journeys` permission can now use these routes for the first time) rather than narrowing it, since `requireTenantAdmin` was strictly more restrictive than any of the new per-action checks. "Export recipients" and "override suppression" have permission actions wired (`export`, `overrideSuppression`) but no dedicated recipient-export endpoint or suppression-override UI exists yet to gate — the permission check is ready for when those ship. Tests: `tests/marketing-journeys.test.ts` (platform/tenant admins bypass; no-permission user rejected; read-only role can view but not create/launch; `manage`/module `"full"` grants every action including governance-weight ones).
- [x] Add journey APIs and worker jobs for enrollment evaluation and analytics — `POST/GET /api/marketing/journeys[/...]` (CRUD, status transitions, publish, versions, manual enroll-now, enrollment list/status), plus `journeys.processEnrollmentRefresh` registered in `scripts/worker.ts`'s real recurring-job list (re-resolves audience and enrolls new matches for journeys with `continuousEnrollment: true`). **Due step execution and event-driven branch continuation needed no new worker job at all** — both are already handled by the existing `automation.processDue` job (`AutomationQueue` polling), since a journey's steps run as a normal `AutomationV2` execution. **Not built**: experiment winner evaluation (no `MarketingExperiment` table, see the schema note above) and a dedicated attribution "rebuild" job — attribution touches are captured incrementally in real time at the point of occurrence instead, which sidesteps the need for a periodic rebuild rather than being a gap.
- [x] Add tests for branching correctness, consent/suppression enforcement, attribution calculations, and permission-scoped audience previews. New `tests/marketing-journeys.test.ts` (9 tests, all passing): journey creation wraps a real automation, invalid/valid lifecycle transitions (including that activating flips the automation's `isActive`), Lead-List and Saved-View (Opportunity module) audience resolution, enrollment dedup against already-enrolled records plus the enrollment attribution touch, rejecting enrollment on a non-ACTIVE journey, first-touch attribution credit calculation, and preference set/read. Branching correctness itself isn't re-tested here (it's `executeAutomationWorkflow`'s existing, already-tested behavior — retested would be redundant, not additive). **Not built**: fatigue-cap tests (no fatigue governance exists yet, see above) and A/B assignment-stability tests (that's `split_test`'s existing behavior, not journey-specific). Full suite verified at 189/189 passing (9 new), plus `tsc`, lint, `npm run build`, and a live dev-server smoke test on every new route (including the public, unauthenticated unsubscribe route returning a correct 404 for an unknown id).
- [x] **Post-implementation adversarial code review of this module, and fixes.** An independent review pass found three real HIGH-severity gaps in `marketing-journeys.ts`, all now fixed with 3 new regression tests added to `tests/marketing-journeys.test.ts` (12/12 passing) and verified via `tsc`, lint, full `vitest` suite, build, and dev-server smoke: pausing a journey set the underlying automation's `isActive` to `false`, but the automation engine's queue worker treats `isActive=false` as CANCEL for any in-flight waiting step — so pausing a journey with hundreds of already-enrolled records mid-workflow would silently and irrecoverably drop every one of their pending steps instead of just freezing new enrollment. Fixed by keeping the automation active for both `ACTIVE` and `PAUSED` (documented in code why — "pause" is now enforced purely at the enrollment layer, which already refused new enrollment on a non-`ACTIVE` journey). `enrollAudienceIntoJourney` deduped via a pre-check `SELECT` into a `Set`, then a separate enroll call — a check-then-act race where two overlapping enrollment runs could both pass the dedup check for the same record and double-fire the automation. Fixed by making the `INSERT ... ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING ... RETURNING id` itself the atomic dedup signal, only enrolling records that genuinely won the insert. The `SAVED_VIEW` audience resolver fell back to `tabs[0]` whenever the configured tab didn't match the journey's target module, which could silently mass-enroll the entire tenant's first-tab record set on a module mismatch instead of failing safely — removed the fallback in favor of an explicit empty result. Also closed a gap the review connected to the two Priority-Module-21 items below: `updateJourneyForTenant`, `publishJourneyVersion`, and `markEnrollmentStatus` were missing the `assertModuleEnabled` gate that `createJourneyForTenant`/`transitionJourneyStatus`/`enrollAudienceIntoJourney` already had — added to all three; and added a new guard rejecting an audience-config edit on an `ACTIVE` journey (must pause first), since a silent audience swap on a live journey would enroll a new, unreviewed population under an already-approved workflow. Reported via `ReportFindings` with per-finding fix outcomes.

## Priority Module 9 — Data Platform, Integrations, and Governance

Scope decision: approved as an enterprise-grade platform layer. Build on the existing direct Postgres, worker, export, import, webhook, audit-log, and connector foundations instead of creating isolated admin utilities.

**Base infrastructure** (checklist consolidation: merged in from a former standalone top-level "Imports and Integrations" section, since this module already builds directly on top of it — kept as-is, not re-verified, just relocated to sit next to the platform-layer work that depends on it):

- [x] Add full lead import with mapping and validation report. `ImportJob` (`module`, `mapping.fields` source→target pairs, `rows`, `status`, `stats`, `errors`) drives async processing in `processImportJob` (`src/lib/server/crm.ts:1522`) via the BullMQ worker's `imports.process` job. Per-row failures are caught individually and collected into a `rowErrors` array (`{row, message}`) rather than aborting the whole job, and the final `stats` (`total/processed/created/updated/skipped/failed`) plus the row-level `errors` array together are the "validation report" — surfaced back to the importing user via a completion notification and stored on the `ImportJob` row for later review. Supports cancel-mid-run (checked every 25 rows via a `cancelRequested` flag) and reusable named `ImportTemplate`s (saved mapping + duplicate-mode combos).
- [x] Add opportunity import. Same `ImportJob`/`processImportJob` pipeline, `module: "OPPORTUNITY"` — duplicate detection matches on `(leadId, title)` (`findDuplicateForImport`), and creates via the real `createOpportunityForTenant` repo function (not a bypassing raw insert), so opportunity-created automations and audit logging fire exactly as they would for a manually-created Opportunity.
- [x] Add activity import. Same pipeline, `module: "ACTIVITY"` — duplicate detection matches on `(leadId, typeId, dueAt)`, creates via `createActivityForTenant`.
- [x] Add duplicate-handling configuration for imports. `ImportJob.mapping.duplicateMode` is `SKIP` / `UPDATE` / `CREATE`, chosen per-import (and savable on an `ImportTemplate`): `SKIP` leaves the existing record untouched and counts it in `stats.skipped`; `UPDATE` patches only an explicit per-module allow-list of fields (e.g. Lead: `name/email/phone/company/source/status/ownerId` — deliberately excludes `tags`/custom fields from bulk overwrite) via `updateImportedRecord`; `CREATE` (i.e. no duplicate check treated as a match) always inserts a new record. Duplicate matching itself is intentionally narrow and field-specific per module (Lead: exact `email` match; Opportunity: `leadId`+`title`; Activity: `leadId`+`typeId`+`dueAt`) rather than routing through the fuzzy `dedupe-postgres.ts` scoring engine used elsewhere in the app — imports need a fast, deterministic single-field-set check per row, not a scored candidate list.
- [x] Add trigger automation on import. Because `createImportedRecord` (`crm.ts:1374`) calls the same `createLeadForTenant`/`createOpportunityForTenant`/`createActivityForTenant` repo functions the normal manual-create UI uses (not a raw bypassing insert), `LEAD_CREATED`/`OPPORTUNITY_CREATED`/`ACTIVITY_CREATED` automations fire per-row exactly as they would for a manually-created record — this is the "normal creation path" that the Form-Builder submission path (a separate, since-fixed gap) originally bypassed.
- [x] Add real webhook management APIs. Full CRUD at `/api/integrations/webhooks` (list/create) and `/api/integrations/webhooks/[id]` (patch/delete), plus `/[id]/test` (fires a real one-off test delivery via `sendTestWebhookDelivery`) and `/[id]/deliveries` (delivery history with status/response, `webhook-outbox.ts`) — not a stub CRUD shell, includes the operational visibility (test-fire, delivery log) an admin actually needs to trust a configured webhook is working.

**Module 9's own work:**

- [x] Add API management console: per-tenant API keys, scoped permissions, expiry, rotation, last-used tracking, IP allowlist, rate limits, request signing, and revoke flow. **All 9 named sub-items real, and this closes a genuine, previously-total gap**: confirmed by audit before building that no generic "API key" concept existed anywhere for authenticating INBOUND calls into this CRM's own API -- every hit for "ApiKey" was the CRM acting as an HTTP *client* pushing data to external systems (`ExternalIntegration`), and every route under `src/app/api/**` required a session JWT except a handful of single-purpose public/webhook endpoints. There was no way for a tenant's own external systems or scripts to call this CRM at all. New `ApiKey` table (migration `0056_api_management_console.sql`, following the `ExternalIntegration`/`CommunicationProviderConfig` multi-row-per-tenant shape rather than `IntegrationSetting`, whose `UNIQUE(tenantId, type)` constraint structurally can't hold more than one row per tenant). New `src/lib/repositories/api-keys-postgres.ts` + `src/lib/server/api-keys.ts`. **Per-tenant API keys**: full CRUD (`createApiKeyForTenant`/`listApiKeysForTenant`/`updateApiKeyForTenant`), gated behind the tenant's `apiAccessEnabled` feature -- a flag that turned out to already exist end-to-end in the tenant-features UI and `TenantFeature` table (`apiAccessEnabled` toggle on `platform-admin/tenants/[id]`) but was never actually read or enforced anywhere, confirmed by grep; this is exactly the flag it was pre-provisioned for, now wired into `EntitlementFeature`/`assertFeatureEnabled` like `advancedReporting` before it. **Scoped permissions**: reuses the exact same `ModulePermissions`-shaped scope (`"full" | {action: boolean}`) already used by `Role.permissions.modules`, so a key's access is expressed and enforced the identical way a user's role already is (`hasApiKeyPermission`, mirroring the `rolePermissions?.modules?.X` idiom used throughout this codebase) rather than inventing a second permission model. **Expiry**: `expiresAt`, checked on every authenticated request. **Rotation**: `rotateApiKeyForTenant` moves the outgoing secret into a `previousSecret` slot valid for a 24h grace window -- the exact same shape this session's own inbound-webhook-governance rotation already established, reused for consistency rather than re-derived. **Last-used tracking**: `lastUsedAt`/`lastUsedIp`, updated fire-and-forget on every successful authenticated call. **IP allowlist**: an optional `text[]` column, checked against the request's extracted client IP (`clientIpFromRequest`, reused from the existing login-throttle rate limiter rather than writing IP extraction a second time); no allowlist configured means unrestricted, matching this key-level control's opt-in framing. **Rate limits**: a real per-key `rateLimitPerMinute`, enforced via the existing, working, Redis-backed `checkRateLimit` (`rate-limit.ts`) -- already used for login brute-force and public form throttling, now reused a third time rather than building a parallel limiter. **Explicitly not the same thing, so it doesn't get confused with this item**: `/dashboard/admin/rate-limits` (a "Rate Limiting Dashboard" of violation stats) is a pre-existing, still-broken dead page discovered during this pass -- it calls `GET /api/platform-admin/rate-limits/stats`, which does not exist anywhere in this codebase, confirmed by `find`. That's a different, still-open gap (a stats/violations dashboard), not the per-key rate limiting this bullet asks for, which is now real. **Request signing**: a signed mode (`X-Api-Key-Id`/`X-Api-Timestamp`/`X-Api-Signature` headers, no secret ever sent over the wire) using the identical timestamp-bound HMAC-SHA256 + `timingSafeEqual` scheme this session already built twice this project for inbound and outbound webhook governance (`inbound-webhooks.ts`/`webhook-outbox.ts`) -- copied verbatim rather than re-implemented, including the same dual current/previous-secret verification during a rotation's grace window. A simpler bearer mode (`Authorization: Bearer <keyId>.<secret>`) is also supported for callers that don't need request signing. **Deliberate, documented departure from this codebase's usual "hash secrets like passwords" instinct**: the secret is stored in plaintext, matching the existing, already-accepted precedent for every other integration secret in this app (`ExternalIntegration.secretConfig`, `CommunicationProviderConfig.secretConfig`, the inbound-webhook secret) -- but here it's a *requirement*, not just convention-following: verifying an HMAC signature needs the raw secret bytes the caller signed with, so a one-way hash (bcrypt) would make the request-signing sub-item impossible to implement at all, not just less convenient. **Revoke flow**: soft-revoke (`isActive=false`, `revokedAt` stamped) rather than hard delete, so a key's usage history and audit trail survive -- matching `WebhookSubscription.isActive`/`ExternalIntegration.isActive`'s existing convention over deletion. All of this is enforced from one single entry point, `authenticateApiKeyRequest`, so every route that authenticates via API key gets every sub-item for free rather than each route re-implementing a subset. **A deliberately bounded, honestly-scoped developer-facing API surface, not a full REST API for every module**: this checklist item is about the *console* -- the key-management and authentication/authorization infrastructure -- not a mandate to expose every CRM module over `/api/v1/*`. Two real endpoints (`/api/v1/leads`, `/api/v1/opportunities`, both GET list + POST create) were built end-to-end through `authenticateApiKeyRequest` to prove every sub-item actually works against real traffic and real permission scoping (reusing `listLeadsForTenant`/`createLeadForTenant`/`listOpportunitiesForTenant`/`createOpportunityForTenant` unchanged, via a synthesized pseudo-user so the key's tenant-scoped, ALL-record-access behavior falls out of the exact same repository functions the authenticated frontend already uses) -- not a stub or a paperwork demo. Additional modules can be added as new `/api/v1/*` routes calling `authenticateApiKeyRequest` + `hasApiKeyPermission` the same way, with zero changes needed to the console itself. New settings page `/dashboard/settings/api-keys` (create/rotate/revoke, plaintext secret shown exactly once at creation/rotation with a copy-to-clipboard + example `curl` command, IP allowlist/rate-limit/expiry fields), added to the settings sidebar nav gated by the same `apiAccessEnabled` feature. 32 new tests in `tests/api-keys.test.ts` covering every CRUD branch (including duplicate-name-on-unique-violation and feature-disabled propagation), `hasApiKeyPermission`'s full/partial/absent-scope branches, and every `authenticateApiKeyRequest` branch (missing/malformed credentials, not-found, revoked, expired, feature-disabled, wrong bearer secret, successful bearer auth with last-used recorded, previous-secret accepted within and rejected outside its rotation grace window, stale-timestamp and invalid-signature rejection, a real computed-HMAC signed request succeeding, IP-allowlist allow/deny, and rate-limit rejection). `tsc`, full `eslint`, full `vitest` suite (668/668, no regressions), and `npm run build` all verified clean (all 5 new routes -- 3 settings + 2 developer-facing -- and the new settings page confirmed present in the build's route list); no live authenticated click-through (no test credentials in this environment).
- [ ] Add OAuth/app connector registry: connector catalog, tenant installs, encrypted secrets, test connection, health status, sync status, error history, field mapping, and admin-only access controls. **Superseded by Module 16 — Marketplace and App Ecosystem** (built later in this session): `MarketplaceApp`/`TenantAppInstall`/`TenantAppSecret` cover the connector-catalog/tenant-installs/secrets/admin-access-control shape this bullet describes in full, plus real health monitoring, a connector-contract endpoint, and (as of the sync engine pass) real field mapping and sync status — see Module 16 for the actual tracked work; nothing further is tracked here. Checkbox stays unchecked only because of one literal, still-real gap Module 16 doesn't close: this bullet's "OAuth" framing implies third-party OAuth-flow connectors (Slack/Google-style "connect your account"), which doesn't exist — Module 16's apps authenticate the other direction (the app holds a CRM-issued secret, not the CRM holding a third-party OAuth token).
- [x] Add connector health dashboard covering SMTP, WhatsApp, SMS, telephony, webhooks, storage, ML service, Redis/worker queues, and database connectivity. New `getConnectorHealthForTenant` (`src/lib/server/connector-health.ts`), backing a new "Health" tab (7th tab) on the existing `/dashboard/settings/integrations` page and a new `GET /api/settings/integrations/health` route, gated the same as the page's other read tabs (`requireCurrentUser`, no stricter permission — informational/read-only like the existing Webhooks/Telephony GET endpoints). Each of the 9 named systems is covered, composed almost entirely from infrastructure that already existed (confirmed by reading each one first rather than assuming a live-probe library was needed): **database** reuses the same `select 1` pattern as the existing `/api/health` route; **Redis** and **worker queue depth** reuse the real BullMQ `Queue` instance from `src/lib/server/job-queue.ts` (`queue.client` → `ping()` for Redis liveness, `queue.getJobCounts(...)` for waiting/active/failed/delayed counts — a `failed > 0` count reports "degraded", not "error", since failed jobs alone don't mean the queue itself is down); **ML service** hits the Python service's existing `/health` endpoint (`ml-service/app/main.py:15`, confirmed it already existed rather than assuming one needed to be added) through a 5s-timeout `fetch`; **storage** does a real `fs.mkdir`+`fs.access` write-probe against the local storage root for the `local` driver (`storageRoot()` newly exported from `file-storage.ts` — a one-line, zero-risk export instead of duplicating its default-path logic) — the `s3` driver reports "configured" without a live probe, since no S3 client exists anywhere in this codebase to test against yet, stated explicitly in the UI detail text rather than implied. **Deliberate scoping decision, stated up front**: SMTP/WhatsApp/SMS (`CommunicationProviderConfig` rows), telephony (`IntegrationSetting`), and webhooks (`WebhookSubscription`) report *configured-and-active* status, not a live external round-trip — most transactional messaging providers have no cheap no-op healthcheck endpoint, and probing them on every dashboard load would burn real provider API quota for a status page; this is a considered choice, not a shortcut, and is called out in the connector-health module's own code comments so a future pass doesn't "fix" it into an expensive live-probe without re-deciding that trade-off. UI: a `RefreshCw`-triggered manual check (lazy-loaded on first opening the tab, not fetched eagerly with the rest of the page's data, since it involves live network calls) rendering one row per check with a status badge (`ok`/`degraded`/`error`/`not_configured`, color-coded) and latency where measured. 14 new tests in `tests/connector-health.test.ts` cover every status branch per check (ok/degraded/error/not_configured), including the REDIS_URL/ML_SERVICE_URL-unset short-circuits (asserting `getCrmQueue` is never even called when Redis isn't configured, not just that the result looks right) and the queue-degraded-on-failed-jobs case. `tsc`, full `eslint`, full `vitest` suite (330/330, no regressions), and `npm run build` all verified clean (confirmed the new `/api/settings/integrations/health` route appears in the build's route list); no live authenticated click-through (no test credentials in this environment).
- [x] Add inbound webhook governance: endpoint secret rotation, payload signature verification, replay protection, schema validation, event dedupe keys, test payload console, and dead-letter queue. **Real, serious security gap found and fixed, not just a checklist item**: the existing inbound lead-capture endpoint (`POST /api/integrations/inbound/leads/[tenantId]`) had exactly ONE secret (`WEBHOOK_SIGNING_SECRET`, a single global env var) shared across **every tenant** — anyone holding it could push a fabricated lead into any tenant simply by swapping the `tenantId` in the URL, no per-tenant scoping at all. Confirmed by reading the route directly before assuming what "governance" meant here; this wasn't a missing nice-to-have, it was the actual vulnerability the checklist item exists to close. All 7 sub-items built, new `src/lib/server/inbound-webhooks.ts`: (1) **Secret rotation** — per-tenant secrets now live in the existing generic `IntegrationSetting` table (`type = 'INBOUND_WEBHOOK'`, same pattern `TELEPHONY` already uses — no new table needed for this part), with `rotateInboundWebhookSecret` moving the current secret to a `previousSecret` slot valid for a 24h grace window rather than invalidating in-flight callers instantly. (2) **Payload signature verification** — real HMAC-SHA256 (`createHmac`, timing-safe compare via `timingSafeEqual`, not a plain string `===`) over `${timestamp}.${rawBody}`, checked against both the current and (if still in its grace window) previous secret. (3) **Replay protection** — the timestamp is bound *into* the signed material (not checked as a separate, unsigned field), and rejected outside a 5-minute window; an attacker can't replay an old captured request because they can't forge a valid signature for a new timestamp without the secret. (4) **Schema validation** — `validateInboundLeadPayload` (name required non-empty string, email format-checked if present, phone type-checked if present) runs before anything touches the database. (5) **Event dedupe keys** — an optional `X-Idempotency-Key` header is checked against a new `InboundWebhookEvent` table (partial unique index on `(tenantId, idempotencyKey)` where the key is non-null) before creating anything; a repeat delivery of the same key returns the original outcome instead of creating a second lead. (6) **Test payload console** — new "Test Payload Console" card in the Inbound Capture settings tab: an admin edits a sample JSON body, and `sendTestInboundWebhookPayload` computes the real timestamp+signature for it, runs it through the *exact* `captureInboundLead` path a real external call would hit (not a simulation), and shows both the genuine result and the exact headers an external caller would need to reproduce it (a copyable signing reference, not just a pass/fail). (7) **Dead-letter queue** — every inbound request (accepted, duplicate, rejected, or failed) is logged to `InboundWebhookEvent` (migration `0042_inbound_webhook_governance.sql`); failed ones surface in a "Recent Events" list with a Retry button (`retryInboundWebhookEvent`, re-runs the exact stored payload through the same capture path) — only `FAILED` events are retryable (`REJECTED` would just fail the same validation again; `ACCEPTED`/`DUPLICATE` already have a final outcome). **Deliberate backward-compatibility decision, not an oversight**: the old global-secret path (`x-webhook-secret` header or `?secret=` query param, checked against `WEBHOOK_SIGNING_SECRET`) still works, checked first, so any already-configured external caller isn't broken by this change — but it's explicitly the exact thing every other sub-item here was built to replace, and the route/UI both steer new callers toward the signed HMAC flow instead. **Not fixed as part of this item** (out of scope, not silently dropped): the underlying multi-tenant design question of whether *any* shared-secret-in-URL scheme is the right shape long-term (vs. e.g. a capability-scoped bearer token) wasn't revisited — this pass fixes the concrete vulnerability (global secret, no signing, no replay/dedupe protection) within the existing endpoint shape, not a redesign of the shape itself. 18 new tests in `tests/inbound-webhooks.test.ts` cover: legacy-secret backward compatibility, missing/stale/invalid/valid signature branches (including real HMAC computation in the test itself, not mocked), current-vs-previous-secret verification and grace-window expiry, schema validation edge cases, the full capture path (accept/reject/duplicate/failure, each asserted against the actual `InboundWebhookEvent` insert), secret rotation, and retry's status-gating. `tsc`, full `eslint`, full `vitest` suite (366/366, no regressions), and `npm run build` all verified clean (all 6 new/changed routes confirmed present in the build's route list); no live authenticated click-through or real external-caller test (no test credentials in this environment).
- [x] Add outbound webhook governance: subscriptions by event, retry/backoff policy, delivery logs, payload preview, signature headers, pause/resume, and per-endpoint failure alerts. **The standout "fully built but never wired up" gap in this whole module**: `WebhookOutbox` (payload, idempotencyKey, status, retryCount, nextRetryAt, error — a genuinely well-designed queue table) has existed in the schema this entire time with **zero references anywhere in `src/`**, confirmed by grep before starting — nothing ever inserted into it, no worker drained it, no HTTP dispatch or signing existed at all. Separately, also found while investigating: the "Add Webhook" dialog hardcoded `events: ['LEAD.CREATED']` (dot notation) with **no picker UI at all** to ever change it, while the server's own default was `["LEAD_CREATED"]` (underscore) — the two naming conventions didn't even match each other, and a user could never actually pick which events they wanted regardless.
