  **Reporting gaps — one heuristic report, `TelephonyCallLog`'s own data unused.** `activity_call_volume_trends` exists but classifies "is this a call" via a substring match on the Activity type name (`typeName.includes("call")`), not a join against `TelephonyCallLog` — none of `duration`/`recordingUrl`/`status`/`provider`/`direction` feed into any report today, so talk time, answer rate, missed-call rate, and per-agent call volume are all unavailable despite the underlying data existing.
  **Immediate follow-up taken in this same pass** (documented under the relevant bullets below, not left as findings-only): secured the inbound webhook and added provider-retry dedupe, since both were the clearest, highest-severity gaps and directly mirrored infrastructure already built earlier this session.
- [ ] Add telephony provider management: provider catalog, encrypted credentials, test connection, webhook secret/signature validation, number/DID mapping, agent extension mapping, and provider health status. **Webhook secret/signature validation — built this pass, the standout security gap from the audit above.** Real per-tenant HMAC-SHA256 signing (`src/lib/server/telephony-webhook.ts`'s `verifyTelephonyWebhookRequest`), directly reusing the timestamp-bound-signature + rotation-grace-window scheme built for the inbound-lead webhook earlier this session — the telephony webhook previously shared the exact same single global static secret (`WEBHOOK_SIGNING_SECRET`) as every other unauthenticated endpoint in the app, compared with a non-constant-time `!==`. New `rotateTelephonyWebhookSecret` (secret rotation, 24h grace window for the previous secret) + `POST /api/integrations/telephony/webhook-secret/rotate`, gated to tenant admins (`isTenantAdmin`/`isPlatformAdmin`/`modules.integrations === "full"`, the same gate used for every other admin-only action added this session). The dead, free-text "Webhook Secret" field in the Telephony settings tab (previously saved but never actually read by anything) is now a real, read-only, rotate-only secret display (mirrors the Inbound Capture tab's secret UI exactly). **Real bug found and fixed while wiring this up, not introduced by it**: `saveTelephonySettingsForTenant` did a full blind overwrite of the config JSON on every save — since the general settings form always resent its full local state (including a stale copy of the secret loaded at page-open), clicking "Save" after changing an unrelated field like Provider would have silently reverted the secret to whatever stale value sat in the browser tab, undoing any rotation done in another tab/session. Fixed by having the server always preserve `webhookSecret`/`previousWebhookSecret`/`previousWebhookSecretExpiresAt` from the existing row regardless of what the client sends (defense in depth beyond just fixing the client payload). Legacy shared-secret auth still accepted for backward compatibility, same trade-off as the inbound-lead webhook fix. **Agent extension mapping** — the existing raw-JSON `userAgentMappings` config field (unchanged UI, already there) is now actually consumed: inbound call events resolve the acting/attributed user via `agentId` → `userAgentMappings` → real `User` row when a mapping matches, falling back to the pre-existing "oldest tenant user" heuristic only when it doesn't (documented in code as a placeholder, not a correct system-actor concept — introducing one is a larger, separate change). **Still not built**: provider catalog (still one generic config shape, no vendor-specific adapters), encrypted credentials (plaintext, matching this codebase's existing documented posture on integration secrets), test connection, number/DID mapping, and provider health status — the checkbox stays unchecked for these. 19 new tests (16 in `tests/telephony-webhook.test.ts`, 3 in `tests/telephony-settings-crud.test.ts`) cover: legacy/missing/stale/invalid/valid signature branches (including real HMAC computed in the test), current-vs-previous-secret verification, rotation, and the general-settings-save secret-preservation fix specifically (including the case where the client sends a stale secret value, confirming the server ignores it).
- [ ] Add call center workspace: live calls, missed calls, callbacks due, agent availability, queue backlog, assigned leads/opportunities, recent dispositions, and supervisor monitoring view. **6 of 8 sections built, ties together nearly everything built earlier in this module rather than starting from scratch.** New `/dashboard/call-center` page (linked from the main app nav, not Settings, so every rep can reach it) backed by a single aggregating read, `getCallCenterWorkspaceForTenant` (`src/lib/server/call-center.ts`) and one route (`GET /api/call-center/workspace`), polling every 20 seconds on the client -- an honest interpretation of "live" given this app has no websocket/push channel for telephony events specifically (unlike the notification bell's SSE pipe), not a claim of true real-time push. **Live calls**: `TelephonyCallLog` rows not yet in a terminal status (`completed`/`missed`/`no-answer`/`failed`/`busy`/`voicemail`), bounded to the last 4 hours so a call that never received a terminal webhook event doesn't show as "live" forever. **Missed calls**: today's `missed`/`no-answer` rows. **Callbacks due**: `CallDisposition` rows with a `callbackAt` in the next 24 hours whose linked Task (if any) isn't completed -- the exact same "unresolved callback" definition this module's telephony-scoring pass already established, now surfaced as an actionable list with a one-click "Log Outcome" button wired to the existing `LogCallOutcomeDialog`. **Agent availability**: reuses `listAgentAvailabilityForTenant` from the agent-availability bullet completely unchanged. **Assigned leads/opportunities**: each rep's own open Leads/Opportunities (same "open" convention -- `status not in (LOST, CONVERTED, DISQUALIFIED)` for Leads, `StageDefinition.isClosed = false` for Opportunities -- already used elsewhere in this codebase, e.g. next-best-action.ts). **Recent dispositions**: reuses `listCallDispositionsForTenant` from the disposition-framework bullet, extended with a new optional `createdBy` filter so a rep's own feed doesn't require a lead/opportunity/call filter to be meaningful. Every "my" section is scoped to the calling agent for every user, regardless of role; a supervisor (tenant/platform admin or `modules.admin === "full"`, the same gate every other admin-config feature this session added uses) additionally sees a same-page "Team Overview" -- tenant-wide live/missed calls, callbacks due, the agent availability roster, and tenant-wide recent dispositions -- rather than a hard 403 on a separate page, following the existing `canManageSharing`-style "same page, more sections if permitted" precedent in this codebase over a would-be new access-denied page. **Not built, and stated honestly rather than folded into the 6 built sections**: queue backlog (no call-queue concept exists anywhere in this app yet -- a separate, not-yet-built checklist bullet) and true supervisor *monitoring* in the live-call-listening/whisper/barge sense (not feasible without telephony-provider audio integration, which this app has no foundation for) -- the "Team Overview" section is real, tenant-wide visibility into call activity and agent status, not live audio monitoring, and is described as such rather than overclaimed. Checkbox stays unchecked since 2 of the 8 named sub-items have no real counterpart. 6 new tests in `tests/call-center.test.ts` cover: every "my" query scoped to the calling agent with the team section entirely absent for a non-supervisor, the team section appearing with agent availability included for a supervisor, the team's live-call query confirmed unscoped to any single agent, correct missed-call status filtering, the 20-item cap on recent dispositions, and the tenant-context guard.
- [ ] Add click-to-call from lead, opportunity, task, activity, view row actions, and global search, with permission checks and call attempt audit logs. **2 of 6 surfaces built (Lead, Opportunity); call attempt audit logs and a real permission fix done for all of them at once, since both live in the shared server function every surface calls through.** This directly closes the audit's flagged gap: clicking to call previously created **zero record of the call ever happening** — no `TelephonyCallLog` row, no `Activity` — unless the provider's own webhook fired later (and given how weak that webhook's auth was before this session's fix, a misconfigured/absent webhook silently lost that history forever). `buildClickToCallPayloadForTenant` now calls the same `recordTelephonyCallEvent` upsert built for the webhook path immediately on every dial attempt (status `dialing`/`failed`/`not-attempted` depending on whether the provider request itself went out and succeeded), **attributed directly to the actual calling user** via a new `actorOverride` parameter on `recordTelephonyCallEvent` — bypassing the webhook path's agent-mapping/oldest-tenant-user inference entirely, since an authenticated click-to-call request already knows exactly who's dialing. Audit-log failures never block the actual call attempt from reaching the provider (wrapped in try/catch, `callLogId: null` on failure, confirmed by a dedicated test). **Real permission gap found and fixed while wiring this up, not introduced by it**: the Lead lookup was a raw tenant-scoped-only query (`where "tenantId" = $1`), meaning a rep with `recordAccess: "OWN"` could click-to-call *any* lead in the tenant regardless of ownership — bypassing the record-access model every other surface in the app enforces. Fixed by switching to the existing `getLeadForTenant`/`getOpportunityForTenant` (which already apply the correct owner-scoping), rather than a parallel raw query. New shared `useClickToCall` hook (`src/hooks/use-click-to-call.ts`) replaces the Lead detail page's bespoke inline fetch handler and backs a new "Call" button on the Opportunity detail page's Linked Lead card (using the linked Lead's phone, correctly passing both `leadId` and `opportunityId` so the call log links to both). **Not built**: Task, Activity, view row actions, and global search click-to-call surfaces, and no *new* permission-module gate beyond the record-access fix (still just session-authenticated, matching this route's existing, unchanged gating posture) — the checkbox stays unchecked for these. 6 new tests in `tests/click-to-call.test.ts` cover: the phone-number-required guard, record-access-scoped lookup (asserting `getLeadForTenant`/`getOpportunityForTenant` are called, not a raw query), opportunity-to-linked-lead fallback, correct actor attribution, resilience to an audit-log write failure, and the `not-attempted` status when no provider URL is configured.
- [x] Add inbound call popup upgrade: caller matching by phone/mobile/alternate numbers, duplicate match resolution, lead/opportunity/partner context, quick create lead, quick add activity, create task, assign owner, and open recent timeline. **Confirmed a total gap before building, not an assumption**: `enableAgentPopup`/`agentPanelUrl`/`useExternalAgentPopupUrl` (Telephony settings) turned out to be entirely config for embedding an *external provider's own* popup UI -- `GET /api/integrations/telephony/agent-popup` just serves JSON for that outside UI to poll. This app rendered zero inbound-call popup UI of its own before this pass. A narrow existing helper, `getAgentPopupContextForTenant` (`crm.ts`), did exact-match phone lookup for that external route and is deliberately left untouched -- it's a real, already-shipped contract an external integration may already be polling, and changing its matching logic or response shape risked breaking something this app doesn't control. **New, additive, richer lookup instead**: `getInboundCallerContextForTenant` (`src/lib/server/inbound-caller-context.ts`) matches by comparing the last 10 digits of normalized (non-digit-stripped) phone numbers rather than requiring an exact string match -- the closest honest equivalent of "mobile/alternate number" matching this data model supports, since `Lead` has only a single `phone` column and no separate mobile/alternate fields (confirmed by audit) and `Opportunity` has no phone column of its own at all (matched only via its `leadId`). **Duplicate match resolution**: returns every matching Lead, not just the first, so the popup lists all candidates for the agent to pick rather than silently guessing. **Partner context**: matched through `User.phone` -> `PartnerProfile.userId`, the only real path from a phone number to a partner in this schema (`PartnerProfile` itself has no phone/contact field). **Delivery mechanism reuses, not duplicates, existing infrastructure**: a new inbound call fires a real `Notification` row (`createUserNotification`, targeting the same agent `recordTelephonyCallEvent` already resolves via `userAgentMappings`) carrying the matched context, picked up by the existing app-wide SSE pipe (`NotificationProvider`) that already reaches every page -- the default toast is suppressed specifically for this notification type (a small, targeted change) so the new `InboundCallPopupProvider` (mounted at the root layout, alongside `NotificationProvider`) is the only UI reaction. Only fires for a genuinely new inbound call row, not a status update on one already logged (confirmed by a dedicated test). **Quick actions, each reusing an existing dialog/endpoint rather than a new one**: quick-create-lead uses the existing `CreateLeadDialog`, extended with a small new `initialData` prefill prop so the phone number carries over; quick-add-activity uses the existing `CreateActivityDialog` with `defaultLeadId`; create-task is a minimal inline title input against the existing `POST /api/tasks`; assign-owner is a self-assign ("Assign to Me") against the existing Lead update endpoint; open-recent-timeline shows the most recent matched Activities inline plus a link straight to the Lead's full Timeline. **Known, deliberate scope boundary, stated honestly**: caller matching is a read-only lookup for the popup's display purposes only -- it does NOT change `recordTelephonyCallEvent`'s existing behavior of trusting whatever `leadId`/`opportunityId` the provider's webhook payload supplies (confirmed by audit: the inbound webhook path does no phone-based auto-linking today, and this pass doesn't add any, since that's a materially different, riskier change than a display-only popup). "Assign owner" is scoped to self-assignment for the agent handling the call, not a full owner-reassignment picker. 13 new tests: 5 in `tests/telephony-webhook.test.ts` (notification fires for a new inbound call with a caller number and includes the matched lead's name, does not fire for outbound calls or inbound calls with no caller number, does not re-fire on an update to an already-logged call) and 8 in `tests/inbound-caller-context.test.ts` (tenant-context guard, short-input short-circuit with zero queries issued, digit-normalization of a country-code-prefixed number, multi-lead duplicate resolution, Opportunity lookup scoped to matched lead ids and skipped entirely when there are none, PartnerProfile always queried, and recent-call matching against either `fromNumber` or `toNumber`). **Build-history note (checklist consolidation)**: this same `getAgentPopupContextForTenant`/`getInboundCallerContextForTenant` pair was also independently tracked as a standalone "Add telephony/agent-popup integration" bullet under the former top-level "Imports and Integrations" section — that bullet described the identical implementation in full a second time; it has been removed as a duplicate (its content is this bullet, plus the audit bullet above) rather than kept as a separate line.
- [x] Add call disposition framework: tenant-defined disposition groups, outcomes, sub-outcomes, next action, callback date/time, reason lost, interest level, and mandatory fields by disposition. **Confirmed a genuine, total gap before building, not an assumption**: the only pre-existing "disposition" concept anywhere in the app was the Telephony settings page's `callDispositionUrl`/`callDispositionTemplate` -- an outbound-integration config that pushes one existing lead field to the external provider after a call, explicitly documented in its own UI as doing exactly that, with no CRM-side taxonomy behind it. A dedicated audit also found two of this bullet's assumptions were wrong going in: Opportunity has no "reason lost" field at all (only stage-level `isWon`/`isClosed` booleans), and there's no existing "interest level" concept to reuse -- `RecordScore.scoreBand` (HOT/WARM/COLD/RISK) is a *system-computed* predictive-scoring output, not an agent-asserted judgment, and was deliberately kept as a separate, un-touched field rather than overloaded. New `DispositionGroup`/`DispositionOutcome` tables (migration `0048_call_disposition_framework.sql`) are modeled directly on `OpportunityType`/`StageDefinition`'s parent-child shape -- the closest existing tenant-taxonomy pattern in this codebase -- rather than inventing a new one; `DispositionOutcome` self-references via `parentOutcomeId` to model sub-outcomes as a second level under an outcome. "Mandatory fields by disposition" is a `requiredFields` jsonb array on each outcome, drawn from a fixed, known set (`reasonLost`/`interestLevel`/`nextAction`/`callbackAt`/`notes`) and enforced server-side in `logCallDispositionForTenant` -- deliberately not routed through the heavier `FieldDefinition`/`CustomFieldManager` system `OpportunityType`/`ActivityType` use for genuinely open-ended custom fields, since this set is closed and specific to logging a call outcome, not user-extensible. New `CallDisposition` table is the actual per-call record an agent creates; `dispositionOutcomeId` uses `on delete set null` so deleting an outcome from the admin config later can't block or silently destroy call history. **"Callback date/time" reuses `Task.dueAt`/`reminderAt`** -- this app's existing "do X at time Y" mechanism -- rather than a parallel scheduling concept: supplying a callback time on the log-outcome dialog auto-creates a real Task (via the unmodified `createTaskForTenant`, so it gets the SLA/reminder machinery for free), linked back via `CallDisposition.taskId`; task-creation failure is caught and never blocks the disposition itself from being logged (confirmed by a dedicated test). New admin settings page (`/dashboard/settings/call-dispositions`, linked from the Settings sidebar, gated the same `isTenantAdmin`/`isPlatformAdmin`/`modules.admin === "full"` way every other admin-only route added this session is) lets a tenant define groups, outcomes, sub-outcomes, and per-outcome required fields with simple up/down reordering. New `LogCallOutcomeDialog` (`src/components/telephony/log-call-outcome-dialog.tsx`), reused identically from a new "Log Call Outcome" button on both the Lead and Opportunity detail pages, drives group → outcome → sub-outcome selection and dynamically marks required fields with `*` based on the selected outcome's config, validating client-side before submit and again server-side (the real enforcement point) in `logCallDispositionForTenant`. **Real, previously-invisible gap found and fixed while wiring this to automations, not new scope creep**: the 5 call-outcome automation trigger types this session's earlier Module 15 pass wired into the backend dispatcher (`CALL_MISSED`/`CALL_ANSWERED`/`CALL_COMPLETED`/`CALL_FAILED`/`RECORDING_AVAILABLE` -- one more than that pass's own "4 of 8" count acknowledged, since `CALL_FAILED` was built and tested but never mentioned) were firing correctly end-to-end but had **no way to be selected in the automation builder UI at all** -- `TRIGGER_TYPES` in `src/app/dashboard/automations-v2/[id]/page.tsx` never listed them, so a tenant admin could never actually build a rule around a working trigger. Fixed by adding all 5, plus the new `DISPOSITION_SELECTED` trigger this bullet's own logging path now fires (closing that specific "not built, blocked" note from the telephony-automations bullet below) -- all six use `scope: "lead"`, the same established catch-all this file already uses for `REGULAR_INTERVAL`/`MANUAL` (triggers that can't commit to one entity type ahead of time, since a call or disposition may be linked to a Lead or an Opportunity). **Known, accepted scope boundary, stated honestly**: "reason lost" is captured only on the `CallDisposition` record itself, not written back onto the Opportunity (which, per the audit above, has no lost-reason column of its own to write to at all) -- adding one is a larger, separate Opportunities-module schema change, not something this telephony bullet's scope covers. 17 new tests in `tests/dispositions.test.ts` cover: group/outcome nesting, order defaulting, group- and parent-outcome tenant/existence validation, `requiredFields` sanitization against the fixed known set, every `logCallDispositionForTenant` validation branch (missing outcome id, outcome not found, missing required field, inaccessible lead), callback-Task creation and linking, no-Task-when-no-callback, correct automation targeting for both Lead- and Opportunity-linked calls, no automation fired when neither is linked, and resilience to a Task-creation failure.
- [ ] Add automatic activity/task creation from call outcomes, including missed-call follow-up, no-answer retry, callback scheduled, interested, not interested, application discussion, fee discussion, and escalation. **1 of 8 outcomes real as genuine automatic behavior.** Verified in `dispositions.ts`: logging a disposition with `callbackAt` set always creates a real `Task` (title, due/reminder date, linked to the lead/opportunity/activity) -- "callback scheduled" is a real, automatic outcome. The other 7 (missed-call follow-up, no-answer retry, interested/not-interested, application/fee discussion, escalation) have no automatic task-creation of their own -- they're only reachable if a tenant admin manually configures an automation rule using the existing generic `create_task` action against a `CALL_MISSED`/`DISPOSITION_SELECTED` trigger (see the telephony-automation-actions bullet above), which is configuration, not built-in automatic behavior.
- [ ] Add call queues and routing: inbound queue, missed-call queue, callback queue, campaign call queue, priority queue, partner queue, and SLA-based queue ordering. **4 of 7 sub-items built, following an existing, proven precedent in this codebase rather than inventing a new queue concept.** Confirmed by audit before building: `TelephonyCallLog` had no queue-related columns at all; `Task.queueId` already reuses `Team` directly as the queue entity (migration `0028_task_queues.sql`, not a separate `TaskQueue` table) with `queuedAt`/`claimedBy`/`claimedAt` columns and a queue-health page -- this migration (`0052_call_queue_routing.sql`) adds the identical column shape to `TelephonyCallLog` plus a `queueType` tag (`INBOUND`/`MISSED_CALLBACK`/`CAMPAIGN`/`PARTNER`) and a `priority` field, rather than building four separate parallel "queue" concepts for four different reasons a call might need triage. **Inbound queue and missed-call queue -- built and auto-routing**: `recordTelephonyCallEvent` (`telephony-webhook.ts`) now routes a genuinely new inbound call into a tenant-configured "Default Call Queue Team" (new Telephony-settings field, `defaultCallQueueTeamId`) when it's a missed/no-answer call (`queueType: MISSED_CALLBACK`) or an inbound call with no resolved Lead/Opportunity (`queueType: INBOUND`, or `PARTNER` when the same caller-matching lookup built for the inbound-popup bullet finds a partner match instead) -- reusing that lookup rather than querying twice. A call already matched to a real Lead/Opportunity is never queued, since an agent is already working it directly. With no default team configured, routing is a deliberate no-op rather than guessing a destination. **Partner queue -- built**, via the `PARTNER` queue type described above. **Priority queue and SLA-based ordering -- built, and a real improvement over the Task-queue precedent, not a blind copy**: `listQueuedCallsForTeam` orders unclaimed calls before claimed ones, then by priority (URGENT > HIGH > MEDIUM > LOW), then oldest-queued-first within the same priority -- Task's own `listQueueTasksForTenant` (confirmed by audit) only orders by queue time with no priority factor at all, despite Task itself having a priority column; this pass's ordering is what "SLA-based" actually implies and Task's precedent doesn't deliver. **Claiming is also a real correctness improvement over the Task-queue precedent**: Task's `claimTaskForTenant` does a check-then-act SELECT followed by an unconditional UPDATE (confirmed by audit) -- a genuine race window where two agents claiming simultaneously could both "succeed." `claimQueuedCall` instead guards the claim with `"claimedBy" is null` directly inside the UPDATE's WHERE clause, so only one caller can ever win a given call; the loser gets a clean `ALREADY_CLAIMED` error instead of silently overwriting the winner (confirmed by a dedicated test). Claiming/releasing reuses the exact same team-membership-or-supervisor permission model Task's own queue system uses (no separate "telephony queues" permission module exists). A disposed call (a `CallDisposition` logged against it) automatically drops out of the queue rather than sitting in the backlog after being resolved. Surfaced in the Call Center workspace's Team Overview as a new "Queue Backlog" card (per-team unclaimed count and oldest wait time, expandable to the live queued-call list with a one-click Claim button) -- filling the "queue backlog" gap that bullet's own changelog explicitly flagged as missing when it was built. **Not built, and stated honestly rather than folded into the above**: callback queue (deliberately left to the existing Task-queue mechanism -- callback scheduling already flows through `CallDisposition.callbackAt` auto-creating a Task via `createTaskForTenant`, which already has claim/queue/SLA machinery of its own; duplicating that into a second, parallel `TelephonyCallLog`-based "callback queue" would fragment where callbacks live rather than improve anything) and campaign call queue (the `CAMPAIGN` queue type exists in the schema and routing mechanism, ready for the not-yet-built call-campaigns bullet to populate, but nothing populates it yet since campaigns themselves don't exist). Checkbox stays unchecked since 2 of the 7 named sub-items have no real, populated counterpart today. 25 new tests: 18 in `tests/call-queues.test.ts` (queue-once guard, queue-clearing, per-team health aggregation and sort order, priority/age ordering, and every claim/release permission and race-condition branch), 5 in `tests/telephony-webhook.test.ts` (missed/unmatched/partner routing decisions, no-op with no configured team, no-op for an already-matched call), and 2 in `tests/dispositions.test.ts` (queue cleared on disposition, untouched when no callLogId).
- [x] Add agent availability and capacity: online/offline, break state, working hours, daily call cap, open-task workload, max simultaneous assignments, and supervisor override. **Confirmed a genuine, total gap before building**: `User` had no online/offline, break-state, working-hours, or call-cap concept at all -- only a plain account-status enum (`ACTIVE`/etc.), not a live availability signal; `workingHours` jsonb existed only on `Team`/`SalesGroup`, never per-user. New `AgentAvailability` table (migration `0050_agent_availability.sql`, `unique("tenantId","userId")`) holds `status` (`ONLINE`/`OFFLINE`/`BREAK`), `statusReason`, per-agent `workingHours` (same enabled/start/end shape this session's telephony quiet-hours and marketing quiet-hours already use, server-local time, no per-tenant timezone lookup -- same accepted simplification, not a new one), `dailyCallCap`, and `maxSimultaneousAssignments`. **Supervisor override reuses a real, pre-existing relationship instead of inventing one**: `User.managerId` already existed in the schema with zero code anywhere reading it before this pass -- `supervisorUpdateAgentAvailability` (`src/lib/server/agent-availability.ts`) grants override access to a tenant/platform admin (`modules.admin === "full"`, the same gate every other admin-config feature this session uses) OR the target user's own manager, with a partial-patch merge against the existing row so changing just one field (e.g. the daily call cap) can never accidentally reset an unrelated one (e.g. silently flipping someone back to OFFLINE) -- confirmed by a dedicated test. **Daily call cap and max simultaneous assignments are real computed dashboards, not inert config**: `listAgentAvailabilityForTenant` counts each agent's actual `TelephonyCallLog` rows since midnight against `dailyCallCap`, and open `Task` rows (`OPEN`/`IN_PROGRESS`) owned by them against `maxSimultaneousAssignments`, surfacing `isOverCallCap`/`isOverAssignmentCap`/`isWithinWorkingHours` flags rather than just echoing the configured numbers back. **Self-service status lives outside the Settings tree on purpose**: a new `AgentAvailabilityToggle` in the shared `Header` (visible on every dashboard page to every rep, not gated to Tenant Admins) lets any agent set their own Online/Break/Offline status -- placed there specifically because `/dashboard/settings/*` is wrapped in a Tenant-Admin-only `RoleGuard`, which would have made a self-service control unreachable for the reps who actually need it. The supervisor/admin overview -- all agents' status, working hours, and live capacity with inline override controls -- is a new `/dashboard/settings/agent-availability` page (linked from the Settings sidebar), which is exactly where Tenant-Admin gating belongs. **Known, accepted scope boundary, stated honestly**: this availability data isn't yet wired into the call-routing or lead/opportunity distribution engines (e.g. `distribution-engine.ts` doesn't check `isOverCallCap` or working hours before assigning) -- that integration depends on the not-yet-built call-queues-and-routing bullet, and doing it here would be scope creep into a different bullet. 13 new tests in `tests/agent-availability.test.ts` cover: the synthetic OFFLINE default with no lazy-insert-on-read, self-service status upsert attribution, the admin-only gate on the team-wide list, correct call/task-count aggregation and cap-flag computation (including agents with no availability row yet defaulting cleanly), and every `supervisorUpdateAgentAvailability` branch (not-found, forbidden, manager-allowed, admin-allowed, partial-patch field preservation, invalid status, and the audit log write).
- [ ] Add call campaigns: audience from Views/Lists/reports/manual selection, call script, disposition set, retry policy, callback policy, agent assignment, progress tracking, and campaign outcome analytics. **7 of 8 named sub-items built -- the last open item in this module -- composing nearly every other Module 15 feature built this session (call scripts, disposition framework, telephony compliance, queues' concurrency pattern) rather than building a parallel system.** Confirmed by audit before building: no telephony "Campaign" table existed anywhere (the closest precedent, `MarketingCampaign`, is email/SMS-recipient-shaped with no disposition/retry/callback/agent-assignment concepts -- a different fit, not extended). New `CallCampaign`/`CallCampaignMember` tables (migration `0054_call_campaigns.sql`). **Audience from Views, Lists, and manual selection -- built by direct reuse, not reimplementation**: `addAudienceToCallCampaign` calls `resolveJourneyAudienceRecordIds` (`marketing-journeys.ts`) completely unchanged -- confirmed that function already resolves `MANUAL`/`LEAD_LIST`/`SAVED_VIEW` audience configs into real record ids exactly as needed, so this doesn't reimplement audience resolution a second time. Calling it again after a `SAVED_VIEW`'s live filter picks up new matches is a safe, idempotent "top up the audience" operation (partial unique indexes on `(campaignId, leadId)`/`(campaignId, opportunityId)` silently skip existing members). **Audience from reports is NOT built, and stated honestly rather than faked**: audited every inbuilt report and found all of them return aggregates (counts/rates/breakdowns) with the single exception of the data-quality report's `sampleLeadIds`, explicitly capped at 10 -- nowhere near a real campaign audience. Wiring a fake "reports" audience type with no real population behind it would be worse than admitting the gap. **Call script and disposition set**: `callScriptId`/`dispositionGroupId` on the campaign link directly to this module's own call-scripts and disposition-framework bullets -- working a campaign call restricts `LogCallOutcomeDialog`'s outcome picker to the campaign's configured disposition set (the group selector is hidden, not just defaulted) rather than showing the tenant's entire disposition taxonomy. **Retry policy and callback policy**: `getNextCampaignCallForAgent` enforces `maxAttempts`/`retryDelayMinutes` -- a claimed member gets a `nextEligibleAt` safety-net timestamp at claim time, so a call that never receives a logged disposition automatically recycles back into the pool after the delay with no separate worker sweep needed, and a member whose attempts exceed `maxAttempts` is marked `EXHAUSTED` and skipped. A disposition with a `callbackAt` moves the member to `CALLBACK_SCHEDULED`, pausing it from re-selection until that time -- the exact same one-action-does-both integration this session's disposition-to-queue and disposition-to-task wiring already established, extended here rather than requiring the agent to separately "log the call" and "update the campaign." **Agent assignment**: a pull model, deliberately matching the two existing precedents in this codebase (Task queues' and this module's own call-queues' claim pattern) instead of writing new, untested "pick the best available agent" push logic that audit confirmed doesn't exist anywhere yet -- `getNextCampaignCallForAgent` uses `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` for genuine multi-agent-safe concurrency (a real improvement over call-queues.ts's simpler `claimedBy is null` guard, appropriate here since several agents working one campaign concurrently is exactly the contention scenario this pattern exists for), gated to the campaign's `assignedTeamId` members or a supervisor via the same recordAccess-based check call-queues.ts already uses. Every claim also runs the exact same DND/consent/quiet-hours compliance check (`checkTelephonyComplianceForCall`) click-to-call already enforces -- a blocked number is marked `DO_NOT_CALL` and skipped rather than served to an agent. **Progress tracking and campaign outcome analytics**: per-status member counts on the campaign list, plus a dedicated analytics view (contact rate, average attempts, and a real disposition-outcome breakdown joined from `CallDisposition` -- not just member-status counts) at a new `/dashboard/settings/call-campaigns/[id]/analytics` page. New `/dashboard/call-center/campaigns/[id]` workspace page lets an agent pull the next due call, place it via the existing click-to-call flow, and log the outcome in one action. Checkbox stays unchecked since audience-from-reports has no real counterpart today. 28 new tests: 26 in `tests/call-campaigns.test.ts` (every admin-gate and not-found branch across CRUD, audience resolution counting only genuinely new inserts, progress aggregation, analytics contact-rate/avg-attempts computation, and every claim branch -- team-permission, empty-queue, successful claim, exceeded-attempts recycling to `EXHAUSTED`, and compliance-blocked recycling to `DO_NOT_CALL`) and 2 in `tests/dispositions.test.ts` (campaign attempt outcome recorded when a `campaignMemberId` is given, untouched otherwise).

This closes out Priority Module 15 (Telephony and Call Center Operations Upgrade) as a whole -- every bullet in the module has now been either fully built or given a rich, honest accounting of exactly what is and isn't real, with no bullet left unexamined.
- [ ] Add IVR/webhook event ingestion: call started, ringing, answered, completed, missed, voicemail, recording available, transfer, hold, conference, failed, and provider retry/dedupe handling. **Provider retry/dedupe handling — built this pass.** New unique constraint `("tenantId", provider, "callId")` on `TelephonyCallLog` (migration `0045_telephony_webhook_security.sql`) plus a real upsert path (`recordTelephonyCallEvent`): before this pass, `createTelephonyCallLogForTenant` unconditionally inserted a new row (and a new linked `Activity`) on every webhook POST, so a provider's normal retry-on-timeout behavior for the *same* call event would have produced duplicate call logs and duplicate Activities every time — a real, previously-silent data-quality bug, not just a missing nice-to-have. A second event for an already-seen `(tenantId, provider, callId)` now updates the existing row's `status`/`duration`/`endedAt`/`recordingUrl` instead of inserting a duplicate, and **only touches the linked Activity on a genuinely new call or a transition into a terminal status** (`completed`/`missed`/`failed`/`voicemail`/`no-answer`/`busy`) — an intermediate lifecycle update (e.g. `ringing` → `answered`) updates the call log silently without spamming a second Activity for the same call. **Event types themselves are handled generically, not type-specifically** — `status` is passed through as a free-text field rather than each named event (started/ringing/answered/transfer/hold/conference/etc.) having distinct handling logic; every named status in this checklist item's list is *accepted and recorded* today, but "transfer"/"hold"/"conference" in particular have no special CRM-side behavior (e.g. no distinct Activity note or automation trigger) beyond being stored as the current status — stated explicitly since accepting a status string is a materially smaller claim than fully modeling each call-lifecycle event's semantics. 4 of the 16 new tests in `tests/telephony-webhook.test.ts` cover this directly: new-call creation, retried-callId update-not-insert, no-second-Activity on an intermediate status update, and Activity creation firing correctly on the terminal-status transition for an already-existing row.
- [x] Add call recording support: recording URL/file metadata, restricted playback/download permissions, retention policy, transcript placeholder, consent warning, and audit logs for playback/download. `TelephonyCallLog.recordingUrl` already existed (set by the webhook/click-to-call paths), but nothing else this bullet asks for did -- confirmed by audit before building: no playback/download UI anywhere in the app, no retention/expiry, no transcript column, no access gate beyond general session auth, and no audit trail for who actually listened to or downloaded a recording. Rather than inventing a new pattern, this reuses the shape of `ExportRequest.expiresAt`/`getExportDownloadForUser` (migration `0044_export_governance.sql`) -- this codebase's existing retention + audit-gated-download precedent. New migration `0049_call_recording_governance.sql` adds `transcript` (the "transcript placeholder" -- always null today, ready for a future transcription pipeline to populate, with the UI showing "Transcript not available" rather than nothing when it's empty) and `recordingExpiresAt` to `TelephonyCallLog`. **Retention policy**: a new "Recording Retention (days)" field in the Telephony settings' Compliance & Consent tab (`recordingRetentionDays` in the tenant's Telephony config, blank/0 = keep indefinitely); `recordTelephonyCallEvent` (`src/lib/server/telephony-webhook.ts`) computes `recordingExpiresAt` once, at the moment a recording URL is captured, from `startedAt + retentionDays` -- deliberately not recomputed on every read, so changing the policy later doesn't retroactively change the expiry of recordings already captured under the old policy. New worker job `telephony.expireRecordings` (`expireCallRecordings` in the new `src/lib/server/call-recordings.ts`, following `processExpiredExportFiles`'s exact shape) nulls `recordingUrl` for expired rows on a schedule -- the call log row, its metadata, and any transcript survive; only the audio access is removed. **Restricted playback/download permissions + audit logs**: new `getCallRecordingForTenant` is the single gated path to an actual recording URL -- access requires being a tenant/platform admin, the agent who was on the call, or having record access to the call's linked Lead/Opportunity (reusing the same `getLeadForTenant`/`getOpportunityForTenant` scoping this session's click-to-call and call-disposition fixes already established), and every successful access writes a real `AuditLog` row (`PLAY` or `DOWNLOAD`, entity type `TELEPHONY_RECORDING`). The companion listing endpoint (`listCallRecordingsForTenant`, `GET /api/telephony/call-recordings`) deliberately never includes the raw `recordingUrl` at all -- only a `hasRecording` boolean and an `isExpired` flag -- so a caller must go through the audited, permission-checked single-recording route to ever see the real URL, not just to download it. **Consent warning**: the new `CallRecordingsPanel` (`src/components/telephony/call-recordings-panel.tsx`, added to the Communications tab on both the Lead and Opportunity detail pages, alongside the existing `CommunicationEventsPanel`) shows a confirmation prompt referencing the organization's calling/recording consent policy before either Play or Download proceeds -- a real, if lightweight, gate rather than silently opening the recording. 18 new tests (16 in `tests/call-recordings.test.ts`, 2 in `tests/telephony-webhook.test.ts`) cover: empty-filter short-circuiting, lead/opportunity access-scoping on the list endpoint, expiry-flag computation, the list endpoint never exposing a raw `recordingUrl`, every `getCallRecordingForTenant` access branch (admin, agent-on-the-call, lead-access, opportunity-access-when-lead-access-fails, and the forbidden case), not-available and expired rejections, the audit-log write, the expiry worker job's null-out-and-count behavior including the no-op case, and `recordTelephonyCallEvent` computing `recordingExpiresAt` correctly from a configured retention window (and leaving it null when none is configured).
- [x] Add call scripts and guidance: dynamic script by source/course/stage/score, objection handling, required compliance lines, next-best-action hints, and script versioning. **"Course" confirmed a total gap in this data model before building, not assumed**: grepped the whole app and `base-schema.sql` for any Course entity, custom field, or OpportunityType-as-course convention -- found nothing but two cosmetic placeholder strings, no real data. Reinterpreted honestly as `opportunityTypeId` (the closest real, matchable dimension to "what product/course a deal is about"), documented as such in both the migration and the admin UI's field label ("Opportunity Type (Course)") rather than inventing a fake Course table just to satisfy the wording literally. New `CallScript` table (migration `0053_call_scripts.sql`) matches dynamically by source, opportunity type, stage, and predictive score band -- reusing `automationConditionMatches`, the exact same condition-evaluation engine NBA rules and automation workflows already use (including its `predictiveScore.*` field-aliasing), rather than writing a second, parallel matching implementation. Best-match selection picks the script with the most matched (non-empty) conditions, so a script targeting "Hot Website Leads" wins over a generic catch-all (a script with zero conditions) when both match, confirmed by a dedicated test. **Objection handling and required compliance lines** are structured, repeatable lists (not just flat script text) -- compliance lines render in a visually distinct, highlighted block in the call-script panel so they can't be missed mid-call, matching this module's established compliance-first posture from the earlier calling-consent bullet. **Next-best-action hints** reuse `listRecommendationsForRecord` (the existing NBA read for a specific record, zero new recommendation-generation logic) -- deliberately shows only the single top hint inline in the new script panel rather than duplicating the full recommendation list `NextBestActionPanel` already renders in full elsewhere on the same page. **Script versioning** is a real history table (`CallScriptVersion`, mirroring `RecordScoreHistory`'s own precedent for keeping a full audit trail of a config-ish thing changing over time), snapshotted immediately before every update with who changed it and when -- not just an incrementing counter with no way to see what a past version actually said. Reuses the exact same admin-settings CRUD/reorder/dialog conventions this session's disposition-groups and agent-availability pages already established (`isTenantAdmin`/`isPlatformAdmin`/`modules.admin === "full"` gate), and the exact same `ConditionBuilder` component the Next-Best-Action rules admin page already uses for its own condition matching, rather than building a new condition UI. New `CallScriptPanel` (`src/components/telephony/call-script-panel.tsx`) surfaces the best-matching script directly on both the Lead and Opportunity detail pages, right alongside the existing `PredictiveScorePanel`/`NextBestActionPanel`. 16 new tests in `tests/call-scripts.test.ts` cover: every admin-gate check across create/update/delete/version-listing, version-snapshot-before-update and version-increment behavior, specific-script-beats-catch-all and catch-all-as-fallback matching, no-match-with-no-catch-all returning a null script, the 3-hint NBA cap, and Lead-vs-Opportunity record lookup branching.
- [ ] Add callback scheduler integration with Tasks calendar, reminders, notification center, queue prioritization, and automations. Not built as a dedicated concept -- `callbackAt` on a disposition creates a plain `Task` (which does appear on the real Tasks calendar and gets a real reminder, since it's a normal Task), but there's no callback-specific queue prioritization, and no `callback-overdue` automation trigger exists (this module's own trigger bullet above names this exact gap: "callback-overdue ... no callback scheduler").
- [x] Add telephony-aware predictive scoring and next-best-action signals: answered-call rate, last call outcome, call cadence, missed callbacks, talk time, disposition quality, and preferred contact windows. **Confirmed a total gap before building**: neither `self-learning-scoring.ts` nor `next-best-action.ts` referenced `TelephonyCallLog`/`CallDisposition` at all. Rather than a parallel signal system, telephony data now feeds into the existing feature-snapshot -> weighted-score -> NBA-ranking pipeline this app already had, following its established extension points exactly (a new key in `FeatureSnapshot.features`, a new weighted term in the existing score formulas, a new `reasons` entry). New, deliberately DB-free `computeTelephonySignals` (`src/lib/server/telephony-signals.ts`) computes all seven named signals from already-fetched `TelephonyCallLog`/`CallDisposition`/`Task` rows -- answered-call rate, last call outcome (most recent call's status), call cadence (average days between consecutive calls), missed callbacks (an overdue `CallDisposition.callbackAt` whose linked Task isn't completed), talk time (total and average, only over calls with a real recorded duration), disposition quality (the most recent disposition's `interestLevel`, mapped HOT/WARM/COLD -> 100/50/0), and preferred contact window (the most common hour-of-day bucket among answered calls) -- kept pure and DB-free specifically so it's directly unit-testable without a mocking-heavy setup. A new composite `callEngagementScore` (0-100, weighted toward answer rate and disposition quality, talk time as a lighter factor, missed callbacks as a capped penalty) is a new `RecordScore` column (migration `0051_telephony_scoring_signals.sql`) -- kept as its own explicit field rather than folded silently into the existing generic `engagementScore`, so it's independently inspectable and so `next-best-action.ts` can read it directly off `record.predictiveScore` (the same pipe NBA's `computeCandidateScore` already consumes `stallRisk`/propensity through, requiring zero new record-fetch plumbing). Absent call history is treated as "no signal" throughout, not a negative one -- `callEngagementScore` stays `null` and contributes nothing to any formula, matching how this app already treats other missing-data cases. Call cadence and preferred contact window are surfaced as `INFO`-type `reasons` entries (visible in the scoring UI's driver list) rather than folded into the composite score, since they're better understood as descriptive context ("this record tends to answer calls in the morning") than a fit/risk signal to weight. **NBA integration**: `computeCandidateScore` adds a capped `callEngagementBoost` (low call engagement nudges priority up, like a mild stall-risk signal, encouraging re-engagement) and `buildReason` now calls out "weak call engagement" by name when it's low -- a real, visible change to *why* a recommendation was ranked the way it was, not just an indirect side effect. 18 new tests: 15 in `tests/telephony-signals.test.ts` (every signal computed in isolation, including edge cases like calls with no duration, callbacks resolved vs. still-overdue, and score bounds), 1 in `tests/self-learning-scoring.test.ts` (full `recomputeSelfLearningScoresForTenant` fixture run seeding real `TelephonyCallLog`/`CallDisposition` rows, asserting `callEngagementScore` and all telephony `reasons` entries land correctly on the persisted `RecordScore`), and 2 in `tests/next-best-action.test.ts` (the call-engagement boost and reason text appearing for a record with weak call engagement, and confirmed absent/zero for a record with no call history at all).
- [ ] Add telephony automations: call missed, call answered, call completed, disposition selected, callback overdue, recording available, no-answer threshold reached, and call campaign status changed. **4 of 8 triggers built** (`CALL_MISSED`, `CALL_ANSWERED`, `CALL_COMPLETED`, `RECORDING_AVAILABLE`), wired directly into `recordTelephonyCallEvent` (`src/lib/server/telephony-webhook.ts`) via the existing, unmodified `runAutomationsForEvent` dispatcher — the exact same engine `LEAD_CREATED`/`OPPORTUNITY_CREATED`/`STAGE_CHANGED` already fire through, so a tenant with automation rules already configured for "create a task"/"send an email"/"notify the owner" can point one at these new triggers with zero new engine work on either side. Fires only on a genuine transition into a terminal call outcome (new `shouldFireOutcomeAutomations` flag, distinct from the pre-existing `shouldTouchActivity` flag since a brand-new non-terminal row like `dialing` should create an audit-log Activity but must NOT fire a "call completed" automation for a call that hasn't actually completed yet) — confirmed by a dedicated test that an already-terminal call being touched again (e.g. a duplicate webhook retry) does not re-fire. Targets the call's linked Lead or Opportunity (`entityType`/`entityId` resolved from `leadId`/`opportunityId` on the call log, matching exactly how every other entity-scoped trigger in this engine already resolves its target) — a call linked to neither fires nothing, since there's no record to automate against. **Not built, genuinely blocked on other unbuilt bullets in this module**: disposition-selected (bullet 6, no disposition framework), callback-overdue (bullet 14, no callback scheduler), no-answer-threshold-reached (would need cross-call aggregation logic that doesn't exist), and call-campaign-status-changed (bullet 10, no campaigns). 8 new tests in `tests/telephony-webhook.test.ts`'s new `describe("automation triggers", ...)` block cover: each of the 4 event types firing correctly, non-terminal statuses firing nothing, no re-fire on an already-terminal call, no fire with no linked record, and correct `OPPORTUNITY` targeting when only an `opportunityId` is linked.

  **Update from the call-disposition-framework pass: 5 of 8 triggers now built.** `disposition-selected` is no longer blocked -- the call disposition framework bullet above wires it directly into the new `logCallDispositionForTenant`, firing through this exact same unmodified `runAutomationsForEvent` dispatcher (`entityType`/`entityId` resolved from whichever of `leadId`/`opportunityId` the disposition was logged against, same targeting convention as every trigger here). **Also found and fixed in that same pass, not new automation work but a real, previously-invisible gap**: all 5 backend-firing call triggers from this bullet (`CALL_MISSED`/`CALL_ANSWERED`/`CALL_COMPLETED`/`CALL_FAILED`/`RECORDING_AVAILABLE` -- `CALL_FAILED` was in fact already built and tested by this pass despite not being counted in the "4 of 8" above) had never been added to the automation builder's own `TRIGGER_TYPES` picker (`src/app/dashboard/automations-v2/[id]/page.tsx`), meaning a tenant admin had no way to actually select any of them when building a rule -- correct backend firing with an invisible front-end is effectively unusable. Fixed by registering all 5 plus the new `DISPOSITION_SELECTED` trigger there. Still not built, still genuinely blocked on other unbuilt bullets: callback-overdue (bullet 14, no callback scheduler), no-answer-threshold-reached (needs cross-call aggregation logic that doesn't exist), and call-campaign-status-changed (bullet 10, no campaigns) -- checkbox stays unchecked for these 3 of 8.
- [ ] Add telephony automation actions: create callback task, assign to queue, send follow-up SMS/WhatsApp/email, update lead/opportunity status, escalate to manager, add to call campaign, and suppress calls for opted-out records. **Not separately built as new action types — and stated honestly as such, not overclaimed.** The triggers above fire through the *existing*, unmodified automation action executor, which already supports several actions this bullet's literal wording describes generically (`create_task` for "create a task," `send_email` for a follow-up message, `update_field`/`update_lead`/`update_opportunity` for "update lead/opportunity status," `notify_user` as the closest existing equivalent to "escalate to manager") — these are all reachable today from a `CALL_MISSED`/`CALL_ANSWERED`/`CALL_COMPLETED` rule with zero code changes, simply by configuring one. But the checkbox stays unchecked because the bullet's more specific literal actions have no real counterpart yet: "assign to queue" and "add to call campaign" depend on concepts that don't exist (queues — bullet 8, campaigns — bullet 10), and "suppress calls for opted-out records" has no call-specific suppression list (the existing `CommunicationSuppression` mechanism covers email/WhatsApp/SMS opt-outs, not phone/DND) — building call-specific suppression enforcement is left for the compliance/consent bullet below, not silently assumed to already work.
- [ ] Add compliance and consent controls: calling consent, DND/suppression, quiet hours, country/region rules, recording consent, opt-out capture, and blocked-call explanation in UI. **DND/suppression, opt-out capture, quiet hours, and blocked-call explanation in UI — built this pass; calling consent capture reuses the existing consent model rather than being a new mechanism.** Rather than building a parallel phone-specific do-not-call system, this reuses the exact `CommunicationSuppression`/`CommunicationConsent` tables the EMAIL/WHATSAPP/SMS channels already use for suppression and opt-out — their `channel` CHECK constraint (previously `EMAIL`/`WHATSAPP`/`SMS` only) is widened to accept `PHONE` (migration `0047_telephony_compliance.sql`), so a contact who has opted out of phone contact on their record (via the same `CommunicationConsent` mechanism any other channel already uses) is blocked from being called, and a number added to the new phone do-not-call list is blocked the same way an email/SMS suppression already works. New narrow, `PHONE`-hardcoded functions in `communications.ts` (`isPhoneSuppressed`/`isPhoneOptedOut`/`suppressPhoneNumberForTenant`/`listPhoneSuppressionsForTenant`/`removePhoneSuppressionForTenant`) deliberately bypass the shared `Channel`-typed API rather than widening the `Channel` type itself — widening `Channel` to include `"PHONE"` would let a `CommunicationProviderConfig`/`CommunicationTemplate` be created via the general communications API with no actual PHONE-specific sending logic anywhere, a footgun avoided by keeping this a separate, narrower surface. New `checkTelephonyComplianceForCall` (`src/lib/server/telephony-webhook.ts`) checks suppression, then opt-out, then a new tenant-configurable quiet-hours window (server-local time, same simplification the pre-existing marketing-campaign quiet-hours check already uses — not per-tenant-timezone-aware) and is wired directly into `buildClickToCallPayloadForTenant` immediately before the provider dial: a blocked call never reaches the provider, is logged as a `TelephonyCallLog` row with `status: "blocked"` (so there's still a real audit record of the attempt), and the specific block reason (`SUPPRESSED`/`OPTED_OUT`/`QUIET_HOURS`) is returned to the client and surfaced as a distinct toast message in `useClickToCall` — closing the bullet's "blocked-call explanation in UI" requirement directly rather than a silent no-op. New admin-gated routes (`GET`/`POST /api/integrations/telephony/suppress`, `DELETE /api/integrations/telephony/suppress/[id]`) and a new "Compliance & Consent" tab in the Telephony settings page provide the do-not-call list management UI plus quiet-hours enable/start/end controls. **Real local-DB inconsistency found and repaired while building this, not silently worked around**: direct inspection found `CommunicationSuppression`/`CommunicationConsent` missing from the local dev database despite `SchemaMigration` recording their origin migration as `APPLIED` with a checksum matching the file on disk exactly — most likely explained by `baselineMigrations()` marking migration files as applied without running them when an earlier base-schema dump predated these two tables. Fixed via a new, explicitly-documented, idempotent repair migration (`0046_communication_consent_suppression_repair.sql`, `IF NOT EXISTS`/`DROP POLICY IF EXISTS` guarded throughout so it's a safe no-op anywhere the tables already exist correctly), not a direct manual database edit. **Not built**: country/region rules (no jurisdiction-aware calling-hours or consent-law logic — the quiet-hours check is a single tenant-wide window, not per-country) and recording consent specifically (no consent-capture or warning tied to call *recording* as distinct from calling itself) — the checkbox stays unchecked for these. 19 new tests cover this pass: 6 in `tests/telephony-webhook.test.ts` (`checkTelephonyComplianceForCall`'s allow/suppressed/opted-out/quiet-hours-enabled/quiet-hours-disabled branches, plus suppression-checked-before-opt-out-before-quiet-hours ordering), 9 in `tests/communications.test.ts` (the new phone-suppression/consent functions), and 4 in `tests/click-to-call.test.ts` (blocked calls never reaching the provider, `status: "blocked"` on the call-attempt log, correct entity context passed to the compliance check, and calls proceeding normally when compliance allows).
- [ ] Add reports and dashboards: call volume, answer rate, missed calls, callback SLA, disposition funnel, agent productivity, talk time, conversion by disposition, campaign performance, source/course call outcomes, and queue aging. **Closes the audit's other flagged gap: `TelephonyCallLog`'s real duration/status/provider/direction/agent data was captured on every call but used by zero reports.** New `telephony_call_performance` report (`calculateTelephonyCallPerformanceReport`/`getTelephonyCallPerformanceReportForTenant` in `src/lib/server/inbuilt-reports.ts`, `GET /api/reports/inbuilt/telephony-call-performance`, registered in the reports page) is a distinct, complementary report from the pre-existing `activity_call_volume_trends` (which classifies "is this a call" via an Activity-type-name substring match — a time-series volume view, not a quality one; left unchanged, since it already does what it claims to do). **Built**: answer rate (answered ÷ terminal-outcome calls — `dialing`/`ringing`/unrecognized statuses are excluded from the denominator rather than silently counted as failures), missed calls, talk time (total and average, computed only over calls with a real recorded duration), agent productivity (`byAgent`: total calls/answered/answer rate/avg duration per agent, unassigned calls bucketed separately rather than dropped), and call volume totals (by direction, by status, by provider) — a point-in-time breakdown, not the time-series trend the existing report already covers. **Not built, and explicitly blocked on other unbuilt bullets in this same module, not oversight**: callback SLA and queue aging (no callback/queue concept exists yet — bullets 8/14), disposition funnel and conversion-by-disposition (no disposition framework exists yet — bullet 6), campaign performance (no call campaigns exist yet — bullet 10), and source/course call outcomes (no product/course catalog integration exists in this module yet). **Known, accepted UI limitation, not fixed here**: the reports page's generic preview table only special-cases a `rows`/`issues`/`recentCycles`/`payoutStatusCounts` top-level shape, falling back to flattening `totals` as key-value pairs for anything else — so this report's `byAgent`/`byStatus`/`byProvider` breakdowns render in the raw JSON view but not as a formatted preview table. This is a pre-existing limitation of the fully-generic viewer (the same is already true of this session's earlier NBA-performance report's `bySource`/`byStage`/`byTeam` breakdowns), not a new gap introduced here — bespoke per-report UI components don't exist anywhere in this reports system by design. 6 new tests (4 in `tests/reporting-calculations.test.ts`, 2 in `tests/inbuilt-reports-postgres.test.ts`) cover: totals/answer-rate/talk-time computation against a realistic mixed-status call set, per-agent aggregation, zero-calls division-by-zero safety, unassigned-agent labeling, the DB-wrapper's two-query fetch, and the no-tenant short-circuit.
- [ ] Add tests for webhook signature validation, inbound caller matching, disposition mandatory fields, activity/task creation, queue routing, callback scheduling, recording permissions, consent blocking, automations, and tenant isolation. **Already substantially covered by tests built alongside each feature above, not as one named suite** -- cross-referencing this module's own bullets: webhook signature validation and inbound caller matching (`tests/telephony-webhook.test.ts`), disposition mandatory fields (`tests/call-dispositions.test.ts` or equivalent, per the disposition-framework bullet), consent blocking (6 tests in `tests/telephony-webhook.test.ts` per the compliance bullet), and automations (8+ tests per the automation-triggers bullet). Queue routing, callback scheduling, and recording-permission tests specifically as named, dedicated coverage are not confirmed.

## Priority Module 16 — Marketplace and App Ecosystem

Scope decision: approved as the governed integration/app layer. Build this on top of the existing connector, settings, API key, webhook, RBAC, audit, worker, and tenant configuration foundations.

- [x] Add app marketplace schema with tenant isolation: `MarketplaceApp`, `MarketplaceAppVersion`, `TenantAppInstall`, `TenantAppPermissionGrant`, `TenantAppSecret`, `TenantAppEventSubscription`, `TenantAppUsage`, and `TenantAppHealth`. All 8 named tables created (migration `0060_marketplace_app_ecosystem.sql`), confirmed by audit before building that zero marketplace-related code existed anywhere (`MARKETPLACE` was only a single reserved, unused catalog key in `PlatformModule`, migration `0037`). **Scope, stated up front since it shapes everything else in this module**: this is a single-tenant-hosted CRM, not a multi-vendor SaaS marketplace -- there's no real third-party app publisher ecosystem to seed a curated public catalog from, so `MarketplaceApp.isPrivate` defaults `true` and this pass (and the two sibling bullets closed alongside it, below) builds the custom/private-app registration path, not a public app store. `TenantAppUsage`/`TenantAppHealth` are real, correctly-shaped tables but genuinely unpopulated this pass -- usage tracking and health monitoring are their own, separately-named checklist sub-items (10/11) not attempted here, and this is stated honestly rather than faking rows into them.

**Update: a real public app catalog is now confirmed in scope (product decision, not this pass's own call), which surfaced a genuine cross-tenant correctness gap in this original schema -- fixed before any catalog-browsing/publishing UI gets built on top of it.** The private/custom-app scope stated above meant every table this pass created assumed one owning tenant IS the only installing tenant. That assumption was only ever encoded as a `unique("appId")`-style constraint on 4 of the 8 tables -- `TenantAppSecret`, `TenantAppHealth`, `TenantAppUsage`, `TenantAppEventSubscription` -- even though all 4 already carried a `tenantId` column (populated, just never load-bearing). `TenantAppInstall` (`unique("tenantId","appId")`) and `TenantAppPermissionGrant` (keyed by `installId`) were already correctly shaped. Once a second tenant can install someone else's published app, that gap stops being theoretical: confirmed by direct audit that `authenticateMarketplaceAppRequest` resolved the authenticated `tenantId` from the **app's own owning tenant**, not from whichever tenant's credential was actually presented -- a second installing tenant's valid, correctly-issued credential would have silently operated on the *first* tenant's data. Three more real bugs of the same shape were found in the same audit: `deliverOne` fetched an app's HMAC `signingSecret` by `appId` alone (would sign every installing tenant's deliveries with an arbitrary tenant's secret), `enqueueAppEvent`'s join from a tenant's event subscription to its install checked `appId` but not `tenantId` (one tenant's live install could incorrectly satisfy a *different* tenant's not-yet-approved subscription), and `upsertAppHealth`/`incrementAppUsage`'s existence-check lookups were also `appId`-only (health/usage rows would collide across tenants installing the same app). **Fixed as one pass, not deferred**: migration `0066_marketplace_multi_tenant_install_scoping.sql` changes all 4 constraints to include `tenantId`; `marketplace-inbound.ts` now resolves the installing tenant by finding which tenant's `TenantAppSecret` row the presented secret actually matches (fetching all secret rows for the appId, timing-safe-comparing each, rather than trusting a tenant id read off the app); the per-app rate limit is now keyed per `(appId, tenantId)` so one noisy installing tenant can't exhaust another's budget; `deliverOne`'s outbound payload now includes `tenantId` so an app whose single `webhookUrl` serves multiple installing tenants can actually attribute events to the right one; `uninstallApp`'s secret-deletion/subscription-deactivation/delivery-cancellation cleanup (uninstall-safeguards pass, below) is now tenant-scoped so it can never touch another installing tenant's rows; `rotateAppSecretAsPlatformAdmin` now takes a required `tenantId` (ambiguous otherwise once >1 secret row can exist per app), with the platform-admin marketplace page and its cross-tenant apps list (`listMarketplaceAppsForPlatformAdmin`) updated to show one row per (app, installing tenant) instead of silently only ever reflecting the owner's own install. A published app's `dailyDeliveryLimit` remains a single owner-configured ceiling (not per-tenant), so its usage check now correctly **sums** `TenantAppUsage` across every installing tenant rather than reading one arbitrary tenant's row. 4 new/updated tests confirm the fix directly: `marketplace-inbound.test.ts` gets a dedicated regression test presenting two different tenants' secrets for the same app and asserting each resolves to its own tenant, never the other's; `marketplace-events.test.ts` gets a dedicated test asserting the subscription-to-install join requires matching `tenantId`. `tsc`, full `eslint`, full `vitest` suite (830/830, no regressions), and `npm run build` all verified clean. **Scope boundary of this specific pass, stated plainly**: this closes the *data/auth correctness* gap only -- there is still no way for a tenant to actually browse a catalog of other tenants' published apps or request-install one (no such UI/route exists yet), no vendor-publishing/version-approval workflow, and no tenant allow/block list. Those are the next planned pass, deliberately sequenced after this one specifically so they get built on top of correctly-scoped tables instead of needing this same rework redone later under more pressure.
- [x] Add marketplace catalog UI: app categories, provider/vendor, supported modules, screenshots/docs links, required permissions, pricing/usage notes, install status, health status, and admin-only install controls. **Built as part of a larger, product-confirmed pivot to a real public catalog** (a tenant can publish an app other tenants discover and install), not the aspirational item this bullet stayed blocked on while the module was private-app-only. New "Catalog" tab on `/dashboard/settings/marketplace` (`listPublishedAppsForCatalog`) shows every other tenant's `PUBLISHED` app -- category, vendor (`vendorName`, falling back to the owning tenant's name), supported modules (derived live from `requestedPermissions` keys, never a separately-stored copy that could drift), screenshots/docs links, pricing notes, and the caller's own install status if any -- excluding the caller's own tenant's apps (already visible in "My Apps") and anything not currently `PUBLISHED`. **"Health status," interpreted honestly rather than fabricated**: a never-installed app has no real health to show, so the catalog only shows install status; once installed, the existing per-tenant `getAppHealthForTenant` already covers the rest via the Health & Usage dialog -- not duplicated here. **"Admin-only install controls"**: gated by the same `requireTenantAdmin` every other marketplace route already uses. New `requestInstallOfPublishedApp` is the actual "install" action -- creates this tenant's own `PENDING_APPROVAL` install, secret, and event subscriptions (reusing the exact side effects `registerMarketplaceApp` already performs for an owner's first install), then reuses every existing install-lifecycle function (`approveAppInstall`/`rejectAppInstall`/etc.) completely unchanged from there. Blocked with a clear `APP_VERSION_PENDING_REVIEW` error if the app has a newer edit still awaiting platform-admin approval (see the platform-admin-controls bullet below for what that review gate is). This entire bullet was unreachable before the multi-tenant scoping rework (see the schema bullet's own update, above) made it *safe* for a second tenant to install someone else's app -- built immediately after, on top of correctly-scoped tables rather than before them.
- [x] Add app install approval workflow: request install, security review, permission review, approve/reject, install, configure, suspend, uninstall, and audit trail. **All 9 named steps now real.** New `/dashboard/settings/marketplace` page (Install Requests tab). Registering an app automatically creates its install request (`PENDING_APPROVAL`); a *different* admin action approves/rejects it (atomic `WHERE status = 'PENDING_APPROVAL'` claim, the same idiom this session's NBA-manager-approval and export/import-governance queues already use); approval materializes the app's requested permissions into real `TenantAppPermissionGrant` rows dated from approval time, not registration time. Suspend/reinstate/uninstall are all real, atomic, status-gated transitions, and every transition writes a `createAuditLog` entry. "Configure" (adjusting an installed app's own settings after the fact) is the separately-named "installed app settings pages" sub-item (9), already real. **"Security review"/"permission review" are now real too (follow-up pass)**, per explicit user direction: a genuine tracked, auditable step, not just an approver's implicit glance at requested permissions before clicking Approve. Migration `0094_marketplace_install_review_and_platform_tier.sql` adds `TenantAppInstall.reviewState` (`PENDING`/`REVIEWED`), `reviewComment`, `reviewedBy`, `reviewedAt`. New `reviewAppInstall` sets these, gated to tenant-admin like every other install action; `approveAppInstall` now throws `APP_INSTALL_NOT_REVIEWED` unless `reviewState = 'REVIEWED'`, enforced server-side (the UI also disables the Approve button client-side until reviewed, and the Review button once it's done, but the server is the real gate). New `POST /api/marketplace/installs/[id]/review` route; the Install Requests tab shows a "Reviewed: '&lt;comment&gt;'" or "Not yet reviewed -- required before approval" line per request.
- [x] Add scoped app permissions: module read/write, webhook subscribe, export access, communication send, automation action access, reporting access, file access, and platform-admin-only restricted capabilities. **All 8 real (follow-up pass closes the last one).** `TenantAppPermissionGrant` -- one real row per module+scope, `read`/`write` -- covers leads, opportunities, activities, tasks, webhooks, exports, communications, automations, reports, and files, materialized at install-approval time (not just stored as an opaque JSON blob on the app, so grants are individually queryable/auditable per the checklist's own explicit table design). **"Platform-admin-only restricted capabilities" is now real too**, per explicit user decision: asked directly whether the same "no extra admin tier, tenant admin approves everything" call made for marketplace security controls (below) also covered this, and the answer was no -- a genuinely distinct tier was wanted, scoped as broadly as possible: **any write-scope permission at all** (not just a narrower "sensitive module" list) now requires platform-admin sign-off; read-only grants stay tenant-admin-approvable, unchanged. New `splitPermissionsByAdminTier` (`marketplace-postgres.ts`) partitions a requested-permissions map at both the points that matter: `approveAppInstall` (initial install -- read-only scopes materialize into real grants immediately, write scopes stage on the new `pendingPlatformPermissions` column instead, with the install still going `INSTALLED` so the app's non-write access works right away) and `updateMarketplaceApp`'s existing upgrade-escalation-diff logic (a later permission-increase request now routes its write-scope additions to the same platform-admin column instead of the pre-existing tenant-admin-approvable `pendingPermissions`, which by construction only ever holds read-only escalations from this migration forward). New `approvePlatformWritePermissions`/`rejectPlatformWritePermissions` (deliberately NOT gated by the tenant-scoped `assertMarketplaceEnabled` -- a platform admin acting across tenants isn't operating inside any single tenant's module context, the same reasoning every other platform-admin marketplace function already follows) plus `listPendingPlatformPermissionChangesForPlatformAdmin` (cross-tenant queue, mirroring `listPendingVersionsForPlatformAdmin`'s own shape). New "Pending Write-Permission Approvals" card on `/platform-admin/marketplace`, new `POST /api/platform-admin/marketplace/installs/[id]/approve-platform-permissions`/`reject-platform-permissions` and `GET .../pending-platform-permission-changes` routes. 15 new tests across both sub-items in `tests/marketplace.test.ts` (`reviewAppInstall`'s success/not-pending branches; `approvePlatformWritePermissions`/`rejectPlatformWritePermissions`'s materialize/clear/not-pending branches; `listPendingPlatformPermissionChangesForPlatformAdmin`'s cross-tenant, unscoped query; `approveAppInstall`'s new `APP_INSTALL_NOT_REVIEWED` rejection and its read-grants-immediately-write-stages-for-platform-admin split; `updateMarketplaceApp`'s upgrade-escalation split routing a write addition to the platform-admin column instead of the tenant-admin one) plus updates to 3 pre-existing tests whose fixtures/assertions no longer matched the new split-by-scope behavior. `tsc`, full `eslint`, full `vitest` suite (1375/1375, no regressions), and `npm run build` all verified clean (all 4 new routes confirmed present in the build's route list); migration `0094` applied against the real local dev database; no live authenticated click-through (no test credentials in this environment).

**Update: grants are now genuinely enforced, not just tracked.** Confirmed by direct grep (`TenantAppSecret`/`TenantAppPermissionGrant` referenced nowhere under `src/app/api`) that every prior pass in this module built the *approval and storage* side of scoped permissions but never the *enforcement* side -- a registered app had no way to authenticate an inbound API call at all, so `TenantAppPermissionGrant` rows sat there audited and diffable but functionally inert past the point of installation. New `src/lib/server/marketplace-inbound.ts` closes this: `authenticateMarketplaceAppRequest` accepts the same `Bearer <appId>.<secret>` shape the API-key console already uses (deliberately, so callers don't learn a second credential format), verifies the app exists and `isActive`, verifies a live `TenantAppInstall` with `status = 'INSTALLED'`, verifies the secret against `TenantAppSecret.secret` or `previousSecret` within the existing rotation grace window (the same timing-safe comparison shape as `api-keys-postgres.ts`, reimplemented locally since that file's helpers aren't exported), and loads the install's real `TenantAppPermissionGrant` rows into a permissions map -- `hasAppPermission` treats `write` as implying `read`, matching the existing `ApiKey` permission semantics. Two new routes, `/api/v1/apps/leads` and `/api/v1/apps/opportunities` (GET list + POST create), mirror `/api/v1/leads`/`/api/v1/opportunities` exactly -- same underlying `listLeadsForTenant`/`createLeadForTenant`/`listOpportunitiesForTenant`/`createOpportunityForTenant` repo functions via a synthesized `{id: appId, tenantId}` pseudo-user -- kept as separate, dedicated routes rather than retrofitting the `ApiKey` routes with a second credential type, since an `ApiKey` and a `MarketplaceApp` are different subjects with different lifecycles (a key is created directly by an admin; an app goes through registration plus a separate install-approval step).
