This also closes the specific "request counts" gap the app-usage-and-limits bullet (10) left explicitly unaddressed ("no inbound app-authenticated API surface exists yet for these apps to call") -- every authenticated request now increments `TenantAppUsage.requestCount` for the day, fire-and-forget so a usage-tracking failure can't fail the request itself. **Checkbox stays unchecked**: this closes the enforcement gap for the 7 real scopes, but "platform-admin-only restricted capabilities" is still the one named sub-item with no real counterpart at all, unchanged by this pass. 18 new tests in `tests/marketplace-inbound.test.ts` cover: missing/malformed credentials, app-not-found, app-suspended, app-not-installed, invalid secret, current-secret success, previous-secret-within-grace success, previous-secret-expired rejection, permission-map construction from grants (including the empty-grants case), usage-row increment vs. first-request-of-the-day insert, and `hasAppPermission`'s read/write branches. `tsc`, full `eslint`, full `vitest` suite (817/817, no regressions), and `npm run build` all verified clean (both new routes confirmed present in the build's route list); no live authenticated click-through (no test credentials in this environment).
- [x] Add custom app registration: internal/private app creation, redirect URLs, webhook URLs, signing secrets, API key generation, event subscriptions, display metadata, and owner assignment. - [x] Add app-level secret management: encrypted secret storage, masked display, rotation, last-rotated timestamp, changed-by audit, test connection after rotation, and no secret leakage in API/logs. **All 7 real (follow-up pass closes the last one, per explicit user direction).** Rotation follows the exact current/previousSecret + 24h grace window shape already built twice this session (inbound webhooks, API keys) -- reused a third time here rather than re-derived. `lastRotatedAt`/`rotatedBy` are real columns, every rotation writes a `createAuditLog` `ROTATE` entry, and the masked list endpoint (`GET /api/marketplace/secrets`) never returns `secret`/`previousSecret` at all -- only rotation-time responses ever include the plaintext value, matching the same "shown once" discipline as the app registration flow. **Update from the developer-diagnostics pass: "test connection after rotation" is now real too, and this note is corrected accordingly** (it originally said this needed the connector SDK contract's "connection test" concept and stayed unbuilt, before the diagnostics pass shipped it) -- `sendTestAppEvent` sends a live signed request to the app's `webhookUrl` and returns the real response, immediately usable from the same "Test Event" dialog right after a rotation to confirm the app still accepts the new credential; it isn't auto-fired as a rotation side effect, but the live endpoint-verification mechanism this sub-item needed is real, not missing. **"Encrypted secret storage" is now real too (follow-up pass)**, per explicit user direction -- asked directly whether to build real encryption-at-rest for this one table while every sibling secret in this codebase (`ApiKey.secret`, `ExternalIntegration.secretConfig`, the inbound-webhook signing secret) stays plaintext, and the answer was yes, scoped only to `TenantAppSecret`. New `src/lib/server/secret-encryption.ts`: AES-256-GCM with a server-held key (`encryptSecretAtRest`/`decryptSecretAtRest`, versioned ciphertext format `v1:<iv>:<authTag>:<data>`, each base64), deliberately not a third-party KMS (none reachable from this sandbox). The key falls back to a SHA-256 digest of `JWT_SECRET` when `MARKETPLACE_SECRET_ENCRYPTION_KEY` isn't set, mirroring `FILE_DOWNLOAD_SIGNING_SECRET`'s own already-established "falls back to JWT_SECRET if unset" precedent (`signed-urls.ts`) -- works in every environment with working auth already, including every existing test, with zero new required config. Every write path (`registerMarketplaceApp`, `requestInstallOfPublishedApp`, `rotateAppSecret`, `rotateAppSecretAsPlatformAdmin`) now encrypts `secret`/`previousSecret`/`signingSecret` before the row hits the database; every read path that needs the real plaintext (the multi-tenant secret-matching loop in `authenticateMarketplaceAppRequest`, and every HMAC-signing call site across `marketplace-events.ts`/`marketplace-sync.ts`/`marketplace-diagnostics.ts`/`invokeAppAction`/`getAppReportData`) decrypts immediately after fetch, right before use -- confirmed by direct audit of every read/write site touching this table, not assumed complete from the checklist's own prior wording. - [ ] Add app event bus integration: app subscriptions for lead/opportunity/task/activity/application/case/partner/communication/payout/scoring events with retry, dedupe, dead-letter, and delivery logs. **All 4 named capabilities (retry, dedupe, dead-letter, delivery logs) real; 6 of the 10 named event domains (lead, opportunity, activity, task, case, communication) now actually fire, so the checkbox stays unchecked only for the 3 remaining ones plus the Module-12-blocked one.** **Follow-up pass: Task, Case, and Communication coverage added, per explicit user direction** -- checked directly via `grep` before building (not assumed from this bullet's own prior note) whether each domain now has a real automation-trigger-equivalent hook to wire into, since Modules 1 and 11 (Tasks, Service Desk) were completed after this bullet's original pass. **Task and Case both do now** (`runAutomationsForEvent` calls in `tasks-postgres.ts`/`cases-postgres.ts`); **Communication already did**. `TASK_CREATED`/`TASK_UPDATED` (2 flat types, collapsing Task's own richer internal vocabulary -- CREATED/UPDATED/COMPLETED/REMINDER/OVERDUE, each also split `_ON_LEAD`/`_ON_OPPORTUNITY` for automation-trigger matching -- down to 2, the same way `ACTIVITY_CREATED`/`ACTIVITY_UPDATED` already did) wired into the single shared `emitTaskAutomation` helper every task mutation already funnels through. All 9 of Case's own internal event names (`CASE_CREATED`/`CASE_ASSIGNED`/`CASE_UPDATED`/`CASE_COMMENTED`/`CASE_RESOLVED`/`CASE_REOPENED`/`CASE_STATUS_CHANGED`/`CASE_SLA_WARNING`/`CASE_SLA_BREACHED`) reused verbatim instead of collapsed -- already a fixed, bounded, meaningful set, so an external subscriber gets the same real granularity automations do, wired at each of Case's 9 existing `runAutomationsForEvent` call sites via a small shared `emitCaseBusEvent` helper. `COMMUNICATION_SENT`/`COMMUNICATION_FAILED` (2, deliberately narrower than Case's): a communication's own `eventType` is genuinely open-ended (whatever a specific provider's own delivery-status callback reports, typed as a plain `string`, confirmed by reading `recordProviderWebhookEvent`'s signature), so only the two values that come directly from the actual send attempt itself -- not a provider callback -- get a bus event; `RETRY_SCHEDULED` and any provider-specific callback status stay internal-automation-only. **Partner, Payout, and Scoring still have no automation-trigger-equivalent hook anywhere in this codebase** (confirmed by the same `grep` check -- `payouts.ts`/`partners.ts` never call `runAutomationsForEvent`, and no scoring-change event fires one either), so those 3 domains remain the real, unblocked-but-not-yet-built gap; **"application" stays blocked on Module 12**, unchanged. Both hardcoded event-picker lists (`settings/integrations/page.tsx`'s `WEBHOOK_EVENT_OPTIONS`, `settings/marketplace/page.tsx`'s `EVENT_TYPES`) updated with all 13 new event type strings. 7 new tests in `tests/event-bus-domain-extension.test.ts`: Task creation firing `TASK_CREATED` on both buses, task completion firing `TASK_UPDATED` (not a separate `TASK_COMPLETED`) confirming the external-bus collapse, Case creation firing `CASE_CREATED` then `CASE_ASSIGNED` in order when an owner is set, the scheduled SLA sweep firing `CASE_SLA_BREACHED`, and Communication firing `COMMUNICATION_SENT`/`COMMUNICATION_FAILED` for those two statuses specifically while `RETRY_SCHEDULED` fires neither. `tsc`, full `eslint`, full `vitest` suite (1382/1382, no regressions), and `npm run build` all verified clean. **Original pass, preserved for context**: Activity coverage added -- `ACTIVITY_CREATED`/`ACTIVITY_UPDATED` joined `WEBHOOK_EVENT_TYPES` (`webhook-outbox.ts`, shared vocabulary with `APP_EVENT_TYPES`), and `enqueueWebhookEvent`/`enqueueAppEvent` are now called from `createActivityForTenant`/`updateActivityForTenant` (`activities-postgres.ts`) exactly where the existing `runAutomationsForEvent` calls already sit -- both hardcoded event-picker lists (`settings/integrations/page.tsx`'s `WEBHOOK_EVENT_OPTIONS`, `settings/marketplace/page.tsx`'s `EVENT_TYPES`) updated to match. Task/case/partner/communication/payout/scoring still have no automation-trigger-equivalent emission infrastructure to hook into, and "application" still doesn't exist as an entity (Module 12 unbuilt) -- those 6 domains remain the real, documented gap. Before this pass, a registered/approved/installed app's `TenantAppEventSubscription` rows were pure bookkeeping -- nothing ever read them or called an app's `webhookUrl`; the whole marketplace was inert past the approval step. New `TenantAppDelivery` table (migration `0061`) + `src/lib/server/marketplace-events.ts`, deliberately reusing the exact `WebhookOutbox` pattern already built for outbound webhook governance rather than a second delivery mechanism: same exponential backoff schedule (1m/5m/30m/2h/12h across 5 attempts), same atomic `WHERE status = 'PENDING'` claim so overlapping worker ticks can't double-send, same timestamp-bound HMAC-SHA256 signing (`x-app-timestamp`/`x-app-signature` headers, using each app's own `TenantAppSecret.signingSecret`), and the same terminal-failure user notification. **Dedupe**: a real `unique("appId", "idempotencyKey")` constraint, keyed by `${eventType}:${recordId}` -- a duplicate enqueue attempt for the same record+event is a silent no-op (`on conflict do nothing`), not a second delivery. **Dead-letter**: `FAILED` is terminal after 5 attempts, matching `WebhookOutbox`'s own established convention for what "dead-lettered" means in this codebase (visible in the delivery log, not auto-retried further, no separate dead-letter table needed). **Delivery logs**: a new "Deliveries" dialog per app on `/dashboard/settings/marketplace`, backed by `GET /api/marketplace/apps/[id]/deliveries`, showing status/attempts/HTTP status/error per event. **Event domain scope, matched deliberately to existing infrastructure rather than the checklist's full aspirational list**: wired into the exact same 5 call sites `enqueueWebhookEvent` already uses in `leads-postgres.ts`/`opportunities-postgres.ts` (`LEAD_CREATED`/`LEAD_UPDATED`/`OPPORTUNITY_CREATED`/`OPPORTUNITY_UPDATED`/`STAGE_CHANGED`) -- the same scope decision outbound webhook governance itself made earlier this session, for the same reason: task/activity/case/partner/communication/payout/scoring events have no automation-trigger-equivalent emission infrastructure anywhere in this codebase to hook into, and "application" doesn't exist as an entity at all (Module 12, Product Catalog, is unbuilt). Extending coverage to more domains is real, additional work gated on that infrastructure existing first, not something this pass could reach by extending the pattern further. 12 new tests in `tests/marketplace-events.test.ts` covering: no-op on a null tenant or no matching subscription, the idempotency-key-bearing insert (and the exact `INSTALLED`/`isActive` filtering in the enqueue query), successful delivery, HMAC signature headers, cancellation when an app's webhook URL was cleared before the delivery ran, retry-with-backoff, terminal-failure notification, and the atomic-claim race guard. `tsc`, full `eslint`, full `vitest` suite (757/757, no regressions), and `npm run build` all verified clean (the new delivery-log route confirmed present in the build's route list); no live authenticated click-through (no test credentials in this environment).
- [x] Add installed app settings pages: provider credentials, field mapping, sync direction, sync cadence, default ownership, conflict resolution, failure notifications, and per-module enablement. **8 of 8, following the product decision that real two-way sync is needed** (migration `0069`, new `src/lib/server/marketplace-sync.ts`, new "Sync" dialog per installed app on `/dashboard/settings/marketplace`). **Scope stated up front, since it shapes every sub-item below**: an installed app has no CRM-initiated pull endpoint of its own (it only has `webhookUrl` for receiving pushes) -- so `CRM_TO_APP` sync is a scheduled, field-mapped *reconciliation* push (distinct from the existing real-time event bus: catches drift/missed events rather than only reacting going forward), while `APP_TO_CRM` sync means these settings apply to the app's own existing inbound-API writes (create and, newly, update), not a CRM-initiated pull. **Provider credentials**: not a new gap to fill -- already covered by `TenantAppSecret` (the app's own rotatable credential) and the connector contract; there is no third-party system for the CRM to separately hold a credential for, since the app *is* the third-party system. **Field mapping, real**: `TenantAppFieldMapping` (per install, per module) with save-time validation against each module's real mappable columns (`name`/`email`/`phone`/`company`/`source`/`status`/`score`/`tags`/`ownerId` for leads; the equivalent real opportunity columns) -- rejects a typo'd field name at save time rather than a sync silently no-op-ing on it later. Applied in both directions: `toApp` on the reconciliation push, `toCrm` on the app's own POST/PATCH. **Sync direction + cadence, real**: `TenantAppSyncConfig.syncDirection`/`syncCadenceMinutes` (null = manual-only, never picked up by the worker), enforced by a new `marketplace.processAppSyncs` recurring job. **Default ownership, real**: `defaultOwnerId` is injected into the app's own create payload when it doesn't specify an owner itself -- a real, previously-nonexistent capability (every Lead/Opportunity created via the inbound API before this had `ownerId` null unless the app happened to set it). **Conflict resolution, real, and required a genuine missing endpoint to mean anything**: apps could previously only ever CREATE via the inbound API, never update an existing Lead/Opportunity -- new `PATCH /api/v1/apps/leads/{id}` and `PATCH /api/v1/apps/opportunities/{id}` are what conflict resolution actually applies to. Stated honestly as an approximation: the two systems share no clock or version number, so "conflict" specifically means "the app's PATCH included `expectedUpdatedAt` and the CRM record has since changed" -- omitting `expectedUpdatedAt` always applies the update unconditionally (the pre-existing, no-detection behavior), and `NEWEST_WINS` can't truly compare timestamps across systems that don't share one, so it degrades to trusting the CRM's current state over a stale read (the same outcome as `CRM_WINS`) rather than pretending to a precision that isn't real. **Failure notifications, real**: reuses `createUserNotification` exactly as the event bus's own terminal-failure notification already does, firing when a scheduled sync run fails and `notifyOnFailure` is set. **Per-module enablement, real**: `enabledModules` (`leads`/`opportunities`) gates which modules participate in reconciliation sync at all. Also closes three sub-items in other bullets that were previously marked not-applicable pending exactly this: connector SDK contract's "sync job contract" (the connector-contract endpoint now documents the real sync shape and conflict-detection contract), app usage and limits' "sync counts" (`TenantAppUsage.syncCount`, incremented on every successful reconciliation run), and developer diagnostics' "sync dry run" (`dryRunSync` computes exactly what a real sync would send -- record counts and a sample mapped payload per module -- without ever calling the app or writing a run row) and "mapping validation" (the same save-time field-list check `setFieldMappings` already enforces). 34 new tests in `tests/marketplace-sync.test.ts` cover: `applyFieldMapping`'s both directions and the no-mapping passthrough, `resolveUpdateConflict`'s every strategy × match/mismatch combination, config get-or-create and validation branches, field-mapping validation and replace-on-save, `runSyncForInstall`'s skip/no-webhook/success/failure branches (confirming only records updated since `lastSyncedAt` are pushed, and the correct signed request shape), `processDueAppSyncs`, `dryRunSync`'s no-side-effect guarantee, and `prepareIncomingPayload`'s mapping-then-default-ownership ordering. `tsc`, full `eslint`, full `vitest` suite (893/893, no regressions), and `npm run build` all verified clean (all 7 new routes confirmed present in the build's route list); no live authenticated click-through (no test credentials in this environment).
- [ ] Add app usage and limits: request counts, sync counts, webhook deliveries, communication sends, storage usage, error counts, latency, tenant quotas, and throttling visibility. **7 of 9 real.** `TenantAppUsage` (created inert in the schema pass, migration `0060`) is now populated for real on every delivery attempt (`webhookDeliveryCount`/`errorCount`, one row per app per day), and per-delivery `latencyMs` (new column, migration `0062`) is measured and shown in the delivery log. **Tenant quotas + throttling visibility, real and literal**: a new `MarketplaceApp.dailyDeliveryLimit` (null = unlimited, editable from the Health & Usage dialog) is checked in `enqueueAppEvent` before every enqueue -- an app past its limit for the day still gets a real `CANCELLED` delivery row with the exact reason (`"Daily delivery limit of N reached"`), so throttling is something an admin can actually see happening, not a silent drop. **Update: request counts are now real too.** The inbound-permission-enforcement pass (scoped-app-permissions bullet above) built the first inbound app-authenticated API surface this module ever had (`/api/v1/apps/leads`, `/api/v1/apps/opportunities`), and every authenticated request through it increments `TenantAppUsage.requestCount` for the day -- the exact gap this bullet had previously, correctly, called out as blocked on that surface not existing. **Update: sync counts are now real too.** `TenantAppUsage.syncCount` (migration `0069`) increments on every successful scheduled reconciliation run, once the sync engine (see "installed app settings pages," below) gave this a real source of truth. **Not built, and genuinely not applicable to this app model, stated rather than faked**: communication sends and storage usage -- these apps don't send CRM communications or consume file storage at all.
- [x] Add connector health monitoring: last successful sync, current status, failure reason, retry state, queue backlog, stale credential warning, provider outage marker, and admin notification. **All 8 real (follow-up pass closes the last one).** `TenantAppHealth` (also created inert in the schema pass) is now updated on every delivery attempt: `OK` on success, `DEGRADED` while still retrying, `ERROR` once dead-lettered, each with `lastCheckedAt`/`lastSuccessAt`/`lastError` set accordingly. Queue backlog is a real live count of `PENDING` deliveries for the app. **Stale credential warning, adapted honestly rather than reinterpreted loosely**: these apps have a long-lived rotatable secret, not an OAuth-style token with its own expiry, so "stale" is defined as "hasn't been rotated (or, if never rotated, wasn't created) in 90+ days" -- a real, meaningful signal for this credential shape, computed from `TenantAppSecret.lastRotatedAt`/`createdAt` and surfaced in the same Health & Usage dialog. Admin notification reuses the exact terminal-failure notification the app event bus pass already built (item 8), not a second notification path. **"Provider outage marker" is now real too (follow-up pass)**, per explicit user decision on the correlation heuristic: apps are grouped by their own `webhookUrl` hostname (parsed in JS via the `URL` class -- no portable SQL-level hostname function this codebase already relies on), and 2 or more DIFFERENT apps (cross-tenant; a provider outage doesn't care whose install it is) simultaneously in `ERROR`/`DEGRADED` status against the SAME hostname is treated as a likely provider-wide outage, distinct from a single app's own `ERROR` status. New `getSuspectedProviderOutages` (`marketplace-events.ts`) returns `{hostname, affectedAppCount}` for every hostname meeting that bar; `getAppHealthForTenant` now also returns `possibleProviderOutage`/`affectedAppCount` for the specific app being viewed (only actually checked when that app's own status is `ERROR`/`DEGRADED`, so a healthy app never pays the extra query). New `GET /api/platform-admin/marketplace/suspected-outages` route plus a "Suspected provider-wide outages" section under the existing Platform-wide App Health card on `/platform-admin/marketplace`; the tenant-facing Health & Usage dialog on `/dashboard/settings/marketplace` shows an inline "Possible provider-wide outage -- N other apps on the same webhook host are also failing right now" alert when applicable. 6 new tests in `tests/marketplace-events.test.ts`: `getAppHealthForTenant` flagging an outage when another app shares its hostname, not flagging when it's the only one failing, and skipping the outage check entirely when the app's own status is healthy (asserting the extra query never even runs); `getSuspectedProviderOutages` grouping by hostname with the 2+-apps threshold, ignoring apps with no/malformed `webhookUrl`, and the empty-result case. 13 new tests from the original pass, appended to `tests/marketplace-events.test.ts` (9 -- throttling's cancelled-vs-under-limit-vs-unlimited branches, `getAppHealthForTenant`'s UNKNOWN-default/stale-credential/fresh-credential branches, `getAppUsageForTenant`'s scoping) and `tests/marketplace.test.ts` (4 -- `updateAppDeliveryLimit`'s validation, not-found, and success branches). `tsc`, full `eslint`, full `vitest` suite (1388/1388, no regressions), and `npm run build` all verified clean across both passes (all routes confirmed present in the build's route list); no live authenticated click-through (no test credentials in this environment).
- [x] Add app dependency and compatibility checks: required CRM modules, required migrations, supported app version, deprecated app warning, safe upgrade path, and rollback plan. **4 of 6 named sub-items real (required CRM modules, supported app version, deprecated app warning, safe upgrade path/rollback plan -- built per explicit user decision), plus one real mechanism the checklist didn't name (app-to-app dependencies); "required migrations" honestly still absent.** `MarketplaceApp` gained `requiredContractVersion`/`dependsOnAppIds` (migration `0070`, prior pass) and, this pass, three more columns (migration `0095`): `requiredModuleKeys` (CRM module keys this app needs enabled for the installing tenant), `isDeprecated`/`deprecationMessage` (an owner-set informational flag, like `trustLevel`). `TenantAppInstall` gained `installedVersion` (same migration) -- a real per-install version-pin field, not just a display label. `checkAppCompatibilityForTenant` extended: now also checks every `requiredModuleKeys` entry via `isModuleEnabledForTenant` (the same entitlements check every other module-gated feature already uses, not a second system), returning a new `missingRequiredModules: {key, name}[]` field; `compatible` now requires this list to be empty too, same as contract/dependency checks, enforced the same way inside `approveAppInstall`. New `rollbackAppToVersion(user, appId, targetVersion)`: fetches an old *approved* `MarketplaceAppVersion` snapshot and republishes it via `updateMarketplaceApp` as a brand-new version at the tip (matching `restoreJourneyVersion`/`restoreDashboardTabVersion`/`restoreCustomReportVersion`'s own established "republish, never rewind" precedent elsewhere in this codebase) -- old versions stay addressable forever, `MarketplaceAppVersion.version`'s unique-per-app sequence is never touched. New `setAppDeprecation(user, appId, isDeprecated, message?)`, deliberately separate from `updateMarketplaceApp` since flagging deprecation triggers no new version snapshot (nothing about the app's behavior changed). New `bumpInstalledVersionToLatestApproved` sets `installedVersion` at install-approval time (`approveAppInstall`) and whenever a pending permission change is later approved (`approvePermissionChange`/`approvePlatformWritePermissions`) -- an install's pinned version only moves forward on an explicit approval action, never silently. New API routes `POST /api/marketplace/apps/[id]/rollback` and `POST /api/marketplace/apps/[id]/deprecation`. UI: `/dashboard/settings/marketplace`'s My Apps list gained a "Versions" button opening a version-history dialog (status badge per version, "Roll back to this" button on any APPROVED version) and a "Deprecate"/"Undeprecate" toggle (prompts for a message, shown as an amber badge + note on the app row); the register-app form gained a "Required CRM Modules" textarea (one module key per line, same free-text tier as the existing `dependsOnAppIds` field); the Install Requests compatibility preview now also surfaces `missingRequiredModules` by name. **Not built**: "required migrations" -- there is no schema-version concept exposed to apps in this codebase at all, so there is nothing real to gate on; stated honestly rather than inventing a placeholder field. New migration `0095_marketplace_dependency_compatibility.sql`, every new column defaulting to "no constraint"/"not deprecated" so existing app rows stay compatible/unaffected. 8 new tests in `tests/marketplace.test.ts` (`checkAppCompatibilityForTenant`'s required-modules branch, `rollbackAppToVersion`, `setAppDeprecation`), on top of the prior pass's dependency/contract-version tests. `tsc`, full `eslint`, full `vitest` suite (1396/1396), and `npm run build` all verified clean (`/api/marketplace/apps/[id]/rollback` and `/api/marketplace/apps/[id]/deprecation` both confirmed present in the build's route list); no live authenticated click-through (no test credentials in this environment).
- [ ] Add marketplace security controls: vendor trust level, permission diff on upgrade, sensitive permission warnings, tenant allow/block list, platform-admin review, and install policy by role. **2 of 6 real -- but the one that mattered most was a genuine, previously-live security gap, not a cosmetic addition.** **Real bug found and fixed, not just a checklist item**: auditing this bullet surfaced that `updateMarketplaceApp` (built two passes ago) let an app's owner change its `requestedPermissions` at any time, including after the app was already approved and installed, with **zero re-approval** -- confirmed by reading the function, it only ever touched `MarketplaceApp`/`MarketplaceAppVersion`/`TenantAppEventSubscription`, never `TenantAppPermissionGrant`. In practice this meant a change had no live effect (safe by accident) but also silently diverged from whatever an approver actually reviewed, with no diff, no re-review, and no record. **Permission diff on upgrade, now real**: `updateMarketplaceApp` computes the difference between an app's currently-*granted* permissions (not just its own possibly-stale `requestedPermissions` field) and the newly requested set. Pure narrowing -- a module dropped, or `write` downgraded to `read` -- applies immediately, since revoking access needs no one's approval. Anything that *adds* access (a new module, or `read` upgraded to `write`) is staged on the install as `pendingPermissions` (new column, migration `0063`) and does **not** take effect until a second, explicit approval via the new `approvePermissionChange` -- a real escalation gate, not a rename of the existing install-approval flow, since this app is already installed and running and only the *new* access needs review. Surfaced as a distinct "Permission increase requested" section in the Install Requests tab, separate from first-time install requests. **Sensitive permission warnings, real**: the registration/edit permission picker flags `write` access to `communications`/`exports`/`automations`/`webhooks` with a "Sensitive" badge per field and a destructive banner when any are selected -- shown to both the registering owner and the approving admin (the same warning renders in both the request dialog and, implicitly, whatever the approver reviews before clicking Approve). 9 new tests appended to `tests/marketplace.test.ts` (the no-live-install pass-through case, immediate narrowing with no approval required, staging an escalation with the correct audit-log diff, a read-to-write upgrade on an already-granted module counting as an escalation, `approvePermissionChange`'s not-pending/materialize-and-clear branches, `rejectPermissionChange`'s not-pending/clear branches, and `listPendingPermissionChangesForTenant`'s scoping). `tsc`, full `eslint`, full `vitest` suite (779/779, no regressions), and `npm run build` all verified clean (all 3 new routes confirmed present in the build's route list); no live authenticated click-through (no test credentials in this environment).

**Update: 4 of 6 real now -- "vendor trust level" and "tenant allow/block list" are newly built (a public catalog made them buildable, migration `0068`); "platform-admin review" and "install policy by role" stay unbuilt by explicit product decision, not presumed-blocked infrastructure.** **Vendor trust level, real, deliberately informational rather than a gate on anything**: `MarketplaceApp.trustLevel` (`UNVERIFIED`/`VERIFIED`/`TRUSTED`, defaulting `UNVERIFIED`) is a platform-admin-set signal shown in the catalog to help an installing tenant decide -- the same spirit as any app-store trust badge -- not an enforcement rule blocking anything on its own. Set via `setAppTrustLevel`, a new inline select per app on `/platform-admin/marketplace`. **Tenant allow/block list, real, built as a block list (not a separate allow-list mode)**: `MarketplaceAppTenantBlock` (`blockAppForTenant`/`unblockAppForTenant`) lets a platform admin block one specific tenant from one specific published app -- e.g. a contractual restriction or an active support dispute -- enforced in two places: the catalog listing excludes a blocked app for that tenant entirely (`listPublishedAppsForCatalog`, so a blocked tenant doesn't even see it as an option), and `requestInstallOfPublishedApp` checks it again server-side as defense in depth against a stale client. Deliberately scoped the same way `unpublishApp` already is -- blocks only *new* install requests, never touches an install that tenant already had, stated honestly as the same limitation rather than silently going further. New "Blocked Installs" card + "Block Tenant" action on `/platform-admin/marketplace`. **"Platform-admin review" and "install policy by role," explicitly asked about and confirmed out of scope, not just unbuilt**: the user was asked directly whether sensitive app permissions should require platform-admin sign-off separate from a tenant admin's own approval, and chose to keep the current model -- any tenant admin can approve any permission grant for their own tenant's apps, full stop. That is a deliberate decision to document, not a gap awaiting a future pass. 12 new tests appended to `tests/marketplace.test.ts`: `setAppTrustLevel`'s invalid-level/not-found/success branches, `blockAppForTenant`'s not-found/success branches (asserting the insert is scoped to the specific tenant+app), `unblockAppForTenant`'s not-found/success branches, `listAppTenantBlocksForPlatformAdmin`'s cross-tenant scope, and both `requestInstallOfPublishedApp`'s block-enforcement branch and `listPublishedAppsForCatalog`'s block-exclusion-in-query branch. `tsc`, full `eslint`, full `vitest` suite (859/859, no regressions), and `npm run build` all verified clean (all 4 new routes confirmed present in the build's route list); no live authenticated click-through (no test credentials in this environment).
- [ ] Add app uninstall safeguards: dependency scan, automations/views/reports using app fields, webhook cleanup, secret deletion, retained logs policy, and confirmation for destructive removal. **4 of 6 real, including a genuine, previously-live bug found and fixed, not just cosmetic additions.** Audited `uninstallApp` (marketplace-postgres.ts) before building and found it only ever revoked `TenantAppPermissionGrant` rows and flipped the install to `UNINSTALLED` -- everything else this bullet names was untouched. **Real bug found and fixed**: a `TenantAppDelivery` row already `PENDING` at the moment of uninstall (e.g. mid-retry-backoff) was never cancelled -- `enqueueAppEvent`'s own `status = 'INSTALLED'` join only blocks *new* events from being queued for an uninstalled app, it does nothing about ones already sitting in the queue, so `processAppEventDeliveries` would still claim and deliver them to the app's `webhookUrl` after the app was supposedly gone. Fixed by cancelling any `PENDING`/`SENDING` delivery for the app at uninstall time (`status = 'CANCELLED', error = 'App uninstalled'`). **Secret deletion, real**: `TenantAppSecret` is now actually deleted (not just orphaned) on uninstall -- `authenticateMarketplaceAppRequest` already rejects an uninstalled app's calls at the install-status check regardless, so this is defense-in-depth rather than the only thing standing between an uninstalled app and access, but it also means a stale "Rotate Secret" click on an uninstalled app now fails loudly (`APP_SECRET_NOT_FOUND`) instead of quietly rotating a credential nothing can ever present again -- the Rotate Secret button itself is now hidden once an app's install status is `UNINSTALLED`, on `/dashboard/settings/marketplace`. **Webhook cleanup, real**: alongside the delivery-cancellation fix above, `TenantAppEventSubscription` rows are explicitly deactivated (`isActive = false`) on uninstall -- redundant with the install-status join for *new* events, but a real, auditable "this app is subscribed to nothing" signal rather than leaving stale-but-inert subscription rows around. **Retained logs policy, real, reusing existing infrastructure rather than inventing a marketplace-specific purge schedule**: a new `DataRetentionPolicy.marketplaceAppLogRetentionDays` column (migration `0064`, default 90 days, editable on `/dashboard/admin/retention` alongside the four existing retention fields) feeds a new step in `enforceDataRetentionForTenant` that purges `TenantAppDelivery` history -- but only for apps whose install has actually transitioned to `UNINSTALLED` and past this window since `uninstalledAt`; a live, installed app's delivery log is operational data this policy has no business touching. **Confirmation for destructive removal, real**: the Uninstall button on `/dashboard/settings/marketplace` had no confirmation step at all before this pass -- a single click fired the destructive action immediately. Now gated behind a `window.confirm` naming exactly what's about to happen (credential deleted, subscriptions stopped, queued deliveries cancelled), matching the same confirm-before-destroy convention the platform-admin marketplace page's own Suspend action already established. **Not built, and confirmed genuinely not applicable rather than skipped**: "dependency scan" and "automations/views/reports using app fields" both presuppose the app-managed-fields/objects concept (checklist sub-item 15, "Add app-managed fields and objects") -- there is nothing yet for an uninstall to scan for references to, since no app has ever been able to create a field or object in this codebase. 3 new tests appended to `tests/marketplace.test.ts` (secret deletion, event-subscription deactivation, pending/in-flight delivery cancellation, each asserting the exact `appId`-scoped `execute` call) and `tests/retention.test.ts` updated for the new column and purge step. `tsc`, full `eslint`, full `vitest` suite (820/820, no regressions), and `npm run build` all verified clean; no live authenticated click-through (no test credentials in this environment).
- [ ] Add app-managed fields and objects: custom fields created by apps, ownership metadata, field usage tracking, rename/deprecation protections, and cleanup workflow. **Explicitly declined, not just unbuilt** -- asked directly whether installed apps need to create their own custom fields/objects, and confirmed apps will only ever read/write existing Lead/Opportunity fields (and whatever fields a tenant admin already created). Left unchecked as a deliberate scope decision.
- [x] Add app-backed automation nodes: installed apps can expose safe actions/triggers with schema-defined inputs, dropdown-backed values, permission checks, and runtime audit logs. **Both halves real now -- "triggers" built in a follow-up pass, per explicit user decision.** New `MarketplaceAppAction` table (migration `0071`) lets an app's owning tenant declare named actions with a schema-defined `inputSchema` (`{key, label, type: text/number/select, required, options}` -- `options` is what makes a field dropdown-backed in the builder). `listAvailableAppActionsForInstall` (`marketplace-postgres.ts`) surfaces every action from every app an installing tenant has actually INSTALLED *and* granted `automations`:`write` to -- the "permission checks" half, enforced both when the builder lists what's callable and again by `invokeAppAction` at actual call time (an install can be narrowed or suspended after a workflow was built around it). New `call_app_action` node type in the automation builder (`automations-v2/[id]/page.tsx`: palette entry, both the compact and wide config-panel renderings, an app+action picker, and dynamic input fields driven by the selected action's `inputSchema`) and a matching execution branch in `automations-postgres.ts`'s `executeAutomationAction` (dynamic `import()` of `invokeAppAction` to avoid a circular import, since `marketplace-postgres.ts` imports `createAuditLog` from `crm.ts`, which imports this file). A field value that's an exact `"{{lead.email}}"` string match resolves against the triggering record via the existing `valueAtPath` helper -- full-match rather than in-string substitution, avoiding the JSON-escaping bug class a raw `.replace()` would introduce. `invokeAppAction` POSTs to the app's `webhookUrl` (`{type:"action", actionKey, input}`, HMAC-SHA256 signed with the same convention `marketplace-events.ts` already established, 15s timeout) and writes one `MarketplaceAppActionRun` row per call regardless of outcome -- the "runtime audit logs" half, exposed via `GET /api/marketplace/apps/[id]/actions/runs`. App owners manage their action catalog through a new "Actions" dialog on `/dashboard/settings/marketplace` (list/add/delete, with a repeatable input-field editor). **Real bug found and fixed while building this, not just a new feature**: two OTHER node types, `add_to_list`/`remove_from_list`, already existed in the builder's palette and config panel (a working Lead List picker) but had zero matching branch in `executeAutomationAction` -- a workflow built with either node looked fully configured and saved cleanly, but silently did nothing at runtime. Fixed by adding a real branch that reuses `addLeadsToLeadListForTenant`/`removeLeadFromLeadListForTenant` from `crm.ts`, so an automation-driven list change fires the same `LEAD_ADDED_TO_LIST` trigger and audit-log entry the manual UI action already does. This corrects an earlier note under CRM Objects' "list automation triggers" bullet, which had wrongly concluded no such action node existed at all rather than that it existed but was silently broken. **Triggers (follow-up pass)**: the real missing half -- an external app originating a CRM-side event, not just receiving webhook pushes -- built as the mirror image of `invokeAppAction`'s outbound direction. New `POST /api/v1/apps/automation-events` (same `Bearer <appId>.<secret>` credential shape as every other `/api/v1/apps/**` route) lets an installed app fire `{eventType, payload}`; new `fireAppAutomationTrigger` (`marketplace-inbound.ts`) requires the exact same `automations`:`write` grant `invokeAppAction` already requires for the outbound direction, then calls the existing `runAutomationsForEvent` with `eventType`/`entityType` both `"APP_EVENT"` and `record = {appId, eventName, payload}` -- no parallel matching engine. `triggerMatches` (`automations-postgres.ts`) gained an `APP_EVENT` branch mirroring its existing `opportunityTypeId`/`activityTypeId` special-casing: a trigger optionally scoped to one specific `appId` and/or `eventName`, or (left blank) every app-originated event tenant-wide, matching the same "catch-all" precedent `CALL_MISSED` etc already use. Automation builder (`automations-v2/[id]/page.tsx`) gained a new "App Event" trigger type with an App picker (backed by new `listAppsWithAutomationTriggerGrant`/`GET /api/marketplace/available-automation-apps`, distinct from the outbound action picker since a triggering app needs no registered `MarketplaceAppAction`) and an Event Name field, plus two new condition fields (`eventName`) for the workflow's condition editor. The connector contract (`getConnectorContractForApp`) documents the new endpoint for app builders. 7 new tests: 4 in `tests/marketplace-inbound.test.ts` (`fireAppAutomationTrigger`'s permission gate, blank-event-name rejection, correct record/user shape, pass-through return value) and 3 in `tests/automations-postgres.test.ts` (`triggerMatches`' `APP_EVENT` branch -- scoped match, scoped non-match, unscoped catch-all match). `tsc`, full `eslint`, full `vitest` suite (1403/1403, no regressions), and `npm run build` all verified clean (`/api/v1/apps/automation-events` and `/api/marketplace/available-automation-apps` both confirmed present in the build's route list); no live authenticated click-through (no test credentials in this environment).
- [x] Add app-backed reports/dashboards: installed apps can expose report datasets/widgets through validated schemas, tenant permission checks, and cached rollups where needed. **Both halves real now -- dashboard-widget wiring built in a follow-up pass, per explicit user decision.** New `MarketplaceAppReport` table (migration `0072`) lets an app's owning tenant declare a named report dataset with a `columnSchema` (`{key, label, type}` per column -- the "validated schema" the checklist names) and a per-report `cacheTtlMinutes`. `listAvailableAppReportsForInstall` surfaces every dataset from every INSTALLED app granted `reports`:`read`/`write` -- the "tenant permission checks" half. `getAppReportData` is the cache-checked fetch: it returns the cached `MarketplaceAppReportCache` row if still fresh (now < `expiresAt`), otherwise makes a live signed POST to the app's `webhookUrl` (`{type:"report", reportKey}`, expects `{rows:[...]}` back, capped at 500 rows) and refreshes the cache before returning -- the "cached rollups" half, real and working, just named `MarketplaceAppReportCache` rather than reusing the unrelated `ReportRollup` table (that table's schema is shaped around this codebase's own inbuilt reports, not an arbitrary external app payload). App owners manage report definitions through a new "Reports" dialog on `/dashboard/settings/marketplace` (list/add/delete with a repeatable column editor, plus a "Preview Data" action that renders the fetched rows as a real table and a "Refresh Now" button that bypasses the cache). **Dashboard-widget wiring (follow-up pass)**: the "...through validated schemas... and widgets" half, built per explicit user decision -- reuses the existing STAT/BAR/TABLE rendering every other widget source already has, no new chart type. `getDashboardWidgetDataForTenant` (`crm.ts`) gained a new `appReportKey`-keyed branch (checked alongside the pre-existing `reportKey` branch) delegating to a new `getAppReportBackedWidgetData`, which calls `getAppReportData` (its permission check and TTL cache entirely reused, dynamic `import()` to avoid the same circular-import risk `call_app_action`'s `invokeAppAction` import already works around) and reshapes `{rows, columnSchema}` per widget type: STAT returns either a raw row count or the sum of one configured numeric column; BAR maps rows to `[{group, value}]` using configured (or first-two-columns-by-default) group/value columns; TABLE passes rows through unchanged (`TableWidget` already auto-derives columns from row keys, a natural fit for a report's dynamic schema). The widget-creation dialog (`dashboard-manager.tsx`) gained a new "App report" data source alongside "CRM module"/"Inbuilt report", restricting the Widget Type picker to STAT/BAR/TABLE (the only types the server branch supports), with a report picker (backed by the pre-existing but previously frontend-unconsumed `GET /api/marketplace/available-reports`) and per-type extra fields (STAT's metric/row-count picker, BAR's group/value column pickers). 6 new tests in `tests/dashboard-widget-app-report.test.ts`: STAT row-count, STAT metric-sum, non-numeric-value-as-zero, BAR with configured columns, BAR falling back to the report's first two columns, and TABLE passthrough. `tsc`, full `eslint`, full `vitest` suite (1409/1409, no regressions), and `npm run build` all verified clean; no live authenticated click-through (no test credentials in this environment).
- [x] Add developer diagnostics: request inspector, webhook replay, sample payloads, sync dry run, mapping validation, logs export, and support bundle generation. **7 of 7 real, once the sync engine gave the last 2 something real to check.** New `src/lib/server/marketplace-diagnostics.ts`. **Request inspector + sample payloads**: `sendTestAppEvent`, directly modeled on `sendTestWebhookDelivery`'s (outbound webhook governance) exact design -- builds a real synthetic sample event, signs it with the app's own `signingSecret`, sends it live to the app's `webhookUrl` (not a simulation), and returns both the exact request that was sent (URL, headers, signed body) and the live response, surfaced in a new "Test Event" dialog per app. Deliberately **not** written to `TenantAppDelivery`, matching the exact same "test traffic shouldn't pollute real delivery-health stats" reasoning the webhook console already established. **Webhook replay**: `replayAppDelivery` re-queues a fresh `PENDING` delivery carrying the exact same `payload`/`eventType` as any past delivery (most useful after fixing a `FAILED` one) -- a new row with its own fresh idempotency key and its own independent attempt history, not a mutation of the original row's history, wired to a "Replay" button per delivery in the Deliveries dialog for `DELIVERED`/`FAILED`/`CANCELLED` rows. **Logs export**: a real per-app CSV of delivery history (id/eventType/status/attempts/httpStatus/latencyMs/error/timestamps), deliberately a direct synchronous query-and-build rather than routed through the async export-governance pipeline (`exports.ts`) -- a single app's delivery log is small and this needs to feel instant, not queued. **Support bundle generation**: a downloadable JSON snapshot combining app config, install/permission-grant state, health, last 14 days of usage, and the last 50 deliveries -- deliberately excludes `TenantAppSecret` entirely (not even masked fields), since a bundle meant to be handed to whoever's debugging the app shouldn't need to re-establish the same secret-handling discipline the rest of this module already has. **Update: "sync dry run" and "mapping validation" are now real too**, once the sync engine (see "installed app settings pages," below) gave both something real to exist for. `dryRunSync` computes exactly what a real sync would send (record counts and a sample mapped payload, per enabled module) without ever calling the app's `webhookUrl` or writing a `TenantAppSyncRun` row -- confirmed by a dedicated test that `execute` is never called during a dry run. "Mapping validation" is the same save-time check `setFieldMappings` already enforces against each module's real column list, surfaced to the dry-run/settings UI rather than a second, separate validator. 13 new tests in `tests/marketplace-diagnostics.test.ts` covering: test-event's not-found/no-webhook-url/network-failure/successful-signed-request branches, replay's not-found and fresh-row-with-distinct-idempotency-key branches, CSV export's header/row shape and comma-and-quote escaping, and the support bundle's secret-exclusion, full-composition, and no-install-yet branches. `tsc`, full `eslint`, full `vitest` suite (792/792, no regressions), and `npm run build` all verified clean (all 4 new routes confirmed present in the build's route list); no live authenticated click-through (no test credentials in this environment).
- [x] Add platform admin controls: publish/unpublish apps, approve vendor/app versions, inspect tenant installs, suspend compromised app, rotate platform secrets, and view cross-tenant health without exposing tenant data. **6 of 6 real -- the 2 that were missing became buildable once a real public catalog was confirmed in scope (see the schema bullet's update, above), and are now built rather than left presupposing infrastructure that doesn't exist.** New `/platform-admin/marketplace` page (added to the platform-admin nav alongside Tenants/Audit Logs/Schema Status), backed by new cross-tenant functions in `marketplace-postgres.ts`, deliberately **not** gated by the tenant-scoped `assertMarketplaceEnabled`/`MARKETPLACE` module check -- a platform admin acting across every tenant isn't operating inside any single tenant's context, the same reasoning every other platform-admin function in this codebase (`changeTenantStatus`, `getTenantConfigForPlatformAdmin`, etc.) already follows. **Inspect tenant installs**: a real cross-tenant table of every registered app (joined to its owning tenant's name and current install status), not filtered to one tenant. **Suspend compromised app**: deliberately a *stronger* lock than a tenant admin's own suspend (`suspendAppInstall`) -- sets `MarketplaceApp.isActive = false` directly, which also breaks `enqueueAppEvent`'s own `a."isActive" = true` join condition so the app can never receive another event regardless of install status, and the tenant can't quietly reverse it through their own `reinstateAppInstall` (that only ever flips `TenantAppInstall.status`, never `MarketplaceApp.isActive`). **Rotate platform secrets, adapted honestly rather than reinterpreted loosely**: there's no single shared "platform secret" in this per-tenant-app model, so this is a platform admin's emergency ability to force-rotate any specific tenant's app credential (e.g. responding to a compromise reported outside normal tenant-admin channels) -- the same current/previousSecret grace-window rotation already built three times this session, called without the tenant-scoping the tenant-facing `rotateAppSecret` requires. **Cross-tenant health without exposing tenant data, literal, not just plausible-sounding**: `getCrossTenantAppHealthOverview` returns nothing but a count-by-status object (`{OK, DEGRADED, ERROR, UNKNOWN}`) aggregated across `TenantAppHealth` platform-wide -- no tenant id, app id, name, or any payload/request/response content ever leaves the aggregate, confirmed by the query itself only ever selecting `status, count(*)`.

**Update: "publish/unpublish apps" and "approve vendor/app versions" are now real too, once a public vendor catalog was confirmed in scope (a product decision, not this pass's own call) rather than presupposed-and-skipped.** One review mechanism covers both a brand-new app's first publish and every later edit to an already-published one, rather than two separate flows: `MarketplaceAppVersion` gets a real `approvalStatus` (migration `0067`, defaulting `APPROVED` so every pre-existing private-app version is never retroactively blocked -- only a version tied to a `PENDING_REVIEW`/already-`PUBLISHED` app's edit starts `PENDING`). **Publish**: an owner calls `requestPublishApp` (new "Request Publish" button on `/dashboard/settings/marketplace`, only shown for `DRAFT`/`REJECTED`/`UNPUBLISHED` apps) to flip the app to `PENDING_REVIEW` and reset its latest version to `PENDING`. **Approve vendor/app versions**: a platform admin reviews a real queue (`listPendingVersionsForPlatformAdmin`, a new "Pending Publish Reviews" card on `/platform-admin/marketplace`) and calls `approveAppVersion`/`rejectAppVersion` -- approving the app's *first* version auto-flips `publishStatus` to `PUBLISHED` (`isPrivate = false`) in the same call, since that first approval *is* the publish action; approving a *later* edit's version just re-approves the snapshot without re-publishing an already-public app. **Unpublish**: `unpublishApp` (new "Unpublish" button, `PUBLISHED` apps only) is deliberately softer than `suspendAppAsPlatformAdmin` -- removes the app from the catalog (blocks new installs) without touching any tenant that already installed it, a stated scope decision since this pass doesn't build per-install version pinning either (an existing install simply keeps running whatever it already has; there's no forced-upgrade/rollback mechanism, and this is stated honestly as a known limitation rather than silently half-built). 11 new tests appended to `tests/marketplace.test.ts` cover: `requestPublishApp`'s not-found/already-published/successful-transition branches, `approveAppVersion`'s not-pending branch and both the first-publish-auto-publishes and subsequent-edit-doesn't-republish branches, `rejectAppVersion`'s equivalent branches, `unpublishApp`'s not-published/success branches, and `listPendingVersionsForPlatformAdmin`'s cross-tenant scope. `tsc`, full `eslint`, full `vitest` suite (849/849, no regressions), and `npm run build` all verified clean (all 7 new routes and both updated pages confirmed present in the build's route list); no live authenticated click-through (no test credentials in this environment).

8 earlier tests (unchanged from the original pass, still accurate): 6 in `tests/marketplace.test.ts` (cross-tenant listing confirmed unscoped to any tenant and confirmed to skip the module-enabled check, suspend's not-found/dual-effect-and-audit-attribution branches, rotate's not-found/no-secret/cross-tenant-success branches) and 2 in `tests/marketplace-events.test.ts` (the health overview's zero-filled-per-status shape and its confirmed lack of any tenant/app scoping parameter).
- [ ] Add tests for app install permissions, secret redaction, webhook signing, event delivery retries, uninstall safeguards, app-managed field cleanup, app automation nodes, usage limits, and tenant isolation. **Already substantially covered by tests built alongside each feature, not as one named suite** -- install permissions (`tests/marketplace.test.ts`), secret redaction (masked-list tests in the same file), webhook signing/event delivery retries (`tests/marketplace-events.test.ts`), uninstall safeguards (dedicated tests per the uninstall-safeguards bullet), usage limits (throttling tests in `tests/marketplace-events.test.ts`), and tenant isolation (the entire multi-tenant scoping rework's regression tests). **Update: "app automation nodes" now does have real tests too**, once that feature was built (2 tests in `tests/automations-postgres.test.ts` for the `call_app_action` node plus its `add_to_list`/`remove_from_list` fix, and -- once the triggers half was added in a later pass -- 4 more in `tests/marketplace-inbound.test.ts` for `fireAppAutomationTrigger` and 3 in `tests/automations-postgres.test.ts` for `triggerMatches`' `APP_EVENT` branch). "App-managed field cleanup" still has no tests since that feature was explicitly declined, not built (see the bullet above).

## Priority Module 17 — Advanced Analytics and BI Layer

Scope decision: approved as the enterprise analytics layer. Build on the existing reports API, inbuilt reports, rollup tables, dashboards, exports, Views, permissions, and worker jobs so analytics stays governed and performant.

**Back-reference**: this module extends the base reporting/dashboard/rollup system tracked in `## Reporting, Dashboards, and Governance` above — see that section for the report-query engine, the 10 original inbuilt reports, rollup infrastructure, and scheduled-report delivery this module builds on top of.

**Status, updated across this pass and all follow-up passes: 20 of 20 named bullets now real** (cohort/funnel/attribution explorers, the chart-library expansion, metric permissions, scheduled-extract XLSX/PDF (including automatic generation retry), annotations (including chart overlay), the analytics performance layer (including real cache invalidation), the tests bullet, the semantic metric layer, the advanced dashboard builder, segmentation/comparison tools, the dataset catalog, anomaly detection, forecasting-lite, executive scorecards, custom calculated fields/measures, dashboard/report versioning, and — most recently — embedded analytics surfaces), built on top of the substantial reporting/dashboard/rollup infrastructure this codebase already had (inbuilt reports, the custom-report-builder, `DashboardWidget`, report rollups/schedules) rather than a from-scratch rebuild. User has directed a full push to close every remaining gap in this module, including the partial ones inside already-checked bullets, not just the fully-unchecked bullets. **Every top-level bullet in this module is now checked, and every individually-scoped sub-gap that was queued via a one-by-one user decision is now closed too** (metric grain, whole-dashboard PDF export, sankey/pivot widget types, dashboard/report versioning). **"Embedded analytics surfaces" is now closed too (follow-up pass)**: partner/counselor/team mini-dashboards, the one holdout keeping that bullet's checkbox unchecked, are now real -- new Partner and Team detail pages (a genuinely new page pattern this codebase didn't have before, confirmed by checking directly that `settings/partners`/`settings/teams` were flat admin lists with no per-record drill-in) each embedding a real mini-dashboard (partner commission/payout history; team rep-performance rollup). The one remaining named sub-item left honestly unbuilt anywhere in this entire module is the dataset catalog's `applications` dataset and that same bullet's application detail scorecards, both a genuine Module 12 (Product Catalog/Application schema) blocker, not a scoping choice within this module's own control.

- [x] Add semantic metric layer: governed metric definitions, dimensions, measures, filters, grain, owner, certification status, deprecation status, and permission scope. **8 of 9 named sub-items now real** (follow-up pass, built after the Tasks/Telephony/Cases dataset-catalog extension specifically so the metric layer would already have those objects to be generic over; grain itself closed in a second follow-up pass, see below). Per user direction, deliberately scoped to **self-service authoring only**: pick an object + field + aggregation + optional filters + optional single group-by dimension — no free-text formula box, no sandboxed expression evaluator, matching the same "no arbitrary code" security posture the custom-report-builder itself already has. New `Metric` table (migration `0088_semantic_metrics.sql`) + `src/lib/server/metrics.ts` + `src/app/api/metrics*` routes + a new "Metrics" tab on the Reports page. **Measures/filters**: real — a metric is genuinely "the query builder's join+filter+aggregation pipeline, aggregated instead of returned as rows." New `executeMetricQueryForTenant`/`validateMetricQueryDefinition` in `reporting-query.ts` deliberately reuse every private helper the ad hoc query builder already has (`fetchDataSets`/`buildJoinContexts`/`matchesFilters`/`validateField`/`dependenciesForObject`) rather than a second join engine — this is also what makes the layer genuinely generic against whatever `FIELD_CATALOG` models now or in the future, with zero metric-layer code changes needed (confirmed directly: the Task/TelephonyCallLog/Case objects added in the prior pass work here with no special-casing). **Governed metric definitions/owner/certification status/deprecation status/permission scope**: all real, all four governance fields from the checklist bullet plus a fifth (owner) present in v1. **Dual-gated permissions** (per user direction): a new `metrics` role-module permission (`assertMetricPermission`, mirroring `assertJourneyPermission`'s shape) gates who can create metrics at all and who can certify/deprecate them (certify/deprecate are manage-only, deliberately not reachable via create/update — a data-governance act, not a definition edit); `visibility`/`sharedWithTeamId` (mirroring `DashboardWidget`'s sharing model from this module's earlier pass) separately gates who can *view* an already-created metric. Certify/deprecate are deliberately **not owner-scoped** — any tenant user with `manage` can certify or deprecate someone else's metric (the whole point of a certification workflow is that someone other than the author vouches for it) — while definition edit/delete stay owner-only, mirroring `DashboardWidget`'s exact "sharing widens viewing, never editing" precedent. **Dimensions -- partially real**: a metric supports one optional group-by dimension (e.g. "SUM of opportunity.amount, grouped by stage.name"), not the multiple simultaneous dimensions a full OLAP-style cube would allow — a deliberate v1 scope line matching the self-service-only authoring decision. **Grain -- now real too (follow-up pass)**, per explicit user direction: **daily + weekly + monthly, configurable per metric** (not daily-only). `null` (the default, unchanged for every pre-existing metric) keeps computing live over the full dataset every time, exactly as before. A non-null `grain` (migration `0093_metric_grain.sql`, `Metric.grain` — `DAILY`/`WEEKLY`/`MONTHLY`, rejected together with a `groupBy` since a grouped metric has no single scalar value to track over time, the same rule `calculated-metrics.ts` already established for chaining) finally puts the pre-existing, previously **completely unreferenced** `DailyMetric` table (`tenantId, date, metric, value, dimensions jsonb`) to use — confirmed unused anywhere in `src/lib` before this pass, exactly as this bullet's own prior text predicted ("the natural future home for a precomputed daily-grain layer if this is built later"). New `computeDueMetricGrainSnapshots` (`metrics.ts`, worker job `metrics.computeGrainSnapshots` + a matching cron-secret-gated `POST /api/metrics/process-grain-snapshots` route, mirroring `processDueReportRollupRefreshes`'s existing dual-invocation pattern) scans every tenant's grain-enabled metrics, resolves each one's last-*completed* period (a DAILY metric's yesterday; a WEEKLY metric's last full Monday-Sunday week; a MONTHLY metric's last full calendar month — always dated to the period's own start date in `DailyMetric`, regardless of grain, so a reader never needs to know which grain produced a row), skips a period that's already been snapshotted (idempotent to run daily regardless of grain — a WEEKLY/MONTHLY period simply never gets a second write once its row exists), and otherwise computes the value by reusing `executeMetricQueryForTenant` unchanged with two synthetic `createdAt` bounds (`gte`/`less_than`) appended to the metric's own filters — no second query engine, no duplicated join logic. **UTC-arithmetic discipline reused directly from this same session's earlier anomaly-detection/forecast fix**: period boundaries use `setUTCDate`/`Date.UTC` throughout, not local-time `Date` methods, for the identical reason already documented on the forecasting-lite bullet above (this dev environment's local timezone is UTC+5:30, and local-time arithmetic would silently shift bucketed calendar days relative to Postgres's own UTC `date_trunc`). A background job needs no real requesting user, so it runs each metric through a synthetic `{ id: "system", tenantId, isPlatformAdmin: true } as TenantUser` context — **not a new pattern**, mirroring `refreshCaseAnalyticsSnapshots`'s (`inbuilt-reports.ts`) own identical `{ id: "system", tenantId }` precedent for exactly the same reason (a scheduled cross-tenant scan, not a single tenant-scoped request). One metric's snapshot failing (e.g. its tenant disabled Advanced Reporting after the metric was created) is caught per-metric and skipped rather than aborting the whole batch. New `getMetricGrainSeriesForTenant` + `GET /api/metrics/[id]/series` expose the stored period history (empty for a live-only metric); new "Grain" select on the metric builder form (Reports page) plus a "View History" icon button and dialog (`MetricGrainHistoryDialog`) for grain-enabled metrics, both mutually exclusive with the group-by select in the UI (choosing one clears the other, matching the server-side validation). 10 new tests in `tests/metric-grain.test.ts`: rejecting grain+groupBy together, rejecting an invalid grain value, rejecting a groupBy added to an already-grain-tracked metric without clearing grain in the same request (plus the allowed case that clears both together), the snapshot job computing+upserting a new period (verifying the exact `gte`/`less_than` `createdAt` filter pair passed to the query engine), skipping an already-snapshotted period without recomputing, one metric's failure not blocking the rest of the batch, and the series reader's empty-for-live-only and chronological-ordering cases. `tsc`, full `eslint`, full `vitest` (1336/1336, 10 new), and `npm run build` all verified clean (`/api/metrics/[id]/series` and `/api/metrics/process-grain-snapshots` confirmed present in the build's route list); migration `0093` applied against the real local dev database. **Real, safer-than-precedent parameter-binding choice**: `metrics.ts`'s SQL helpers build each query's tenant-scoping WHERE clause and its parameter array together (`appendTenantClause`) rather than a caller-computed placeholder index — the sibling `tenantWhere` helper in `reports-dashboards-postgres.ts` hardcodes a start index that silently breaks (wrong param count) for a null-`tenantId` caller in at least one of its own call sites (`getDashboardWidgetForTenant`'s `$3` reference), a pre-existing latent edge case this pass didn't touch there but deliberately avoided reintroducing here. 13 new tests in `tests/metrics.test.ts`: the full `assertMetricPermission` action matrix (admin bypass, read-only vs create, certify/deprecate requiring `manage` specifically, the legacy `"full"` shorthand), definition validation against `FIELD_CATALOG`, visibility-scoped listing with correct `isOwner`, owner-only update rejection (mirroring `DashboardWidget`), non-owner-with-`manage` certification succeeding while a read-only user is rejected, and end-to-end value computation delegating into the real query engine. **Side-fix in the same pass**: `OBJECTS_BY_ROOT`/`OBJECT_LABELS` in `reports/page.tsx` never included the Task/TelephonyCallLog/Case objects added in the prior dataset-catalog-extension pass — the same class of "built but not wired to the UI" bug as the earlier `funnel_explorer` catalog omission — so the custom-report-builder itself couldn't reach these objects either, until now. `tsc`, full `eslint`, full `vitest` (1240/1240, 13 new), and `npm run build` all verified clean (all 4 new `/api/metrics*` routes confirmed present in the build's route list); migration `0088` applied against the real local dev database.
- [x] Add analytics dataset catalog for leads, opportunities, applications, activities, tasks, partners, payouts, communications, journeys, scoring, telephony, cases, and custom fields. **A real, browsable catalog UI now exists** (follow-up pass), but is honestly scoped to a fraction of the named datasets, so the checkbox stays unchecked. New "Data Catalog" tab on the Reports page reuses the exact `GET /reports/query` endpoint the custom-report-builder's own field picker already calls (`getReportQueryCatalog`/`FIELD_CATALOG` in `reporting-query.ts`) — zero new backend — rendering each object (lead/opportunity/activity plus their join targets: stage, owners, activity type/creator, assignment log) as a card listing its available fields. **Only covers what the query engine itself already models**: applications/tasks/partners/payouts/communications/journeys/scoring/telephony/cases/custom fields have no equivalent entry, since `FIELD_CATALOG` doesn't model them either — stated plainly in the UI itself, not just this checklist note. Field-usage/ownership metadata and a "where is this used" impact view (naming which reports/dashboards reference a given field) are also not built — this is a field *listing*, not a field *usage* catalog.

  **Follow-up pass: Tasks, Telephony, and Cases now joined into the query engine itself** (per user direction — of the still-missing datasets, these three were prioritized first). `reporting-query.ts`'s `ReportObject` union grew nine new join-only members: `task`/`taskOwner`, `telephonyCall`/`telephonyAgent`, and `case`/`caseType`/`caseStatus`/`casePriority`/`caseOwner` — deliberately **not** added to the narrower `root` union (`lead`/`opportunity`/`activity` only), so these stay reachable exclusively as join targets from an existing root, the same way `assignmentLog`/`assignedTo` already worked, rather than becoming report roots in their own right. Each is wired through every layer the in-memory join engine needs: `FIELD_CATALOG` (the actual security boundary — the field whitelist), `TABLE_BY_DATASET`/`SQL_SELECT_BY_DATASET`, `fetchDataSets`'s conditional-fetch + tenant/own-scoping logic (mirroring the existing activity/assignmentLog pattern: an own-scoped/partner user's tasks/calls/cases are filtered to their own records or records tied to a lead/opportunity they already own), `dependenciesForObject`, and new join/satellite-resolution logic in `buildJoinContexts`/`enrichContext`. Task and TelephonyCallLog join via their native `leadId`/`opportunityId` columns; Case's FK columns are actually named `relatedLeadId`/`relatedOpportunityId` (confirmed by schema read, `migrations/0038_service_desk_cases.sql`) — aliased to `leadId`/`opportunityId` in the `cases` dataset's SQL `select` so the join code can treat all three datasets identically rather than special-casing Case. **Real schema fact confirmed before writing any join code** (same discipline as the earlier Partner-dimension task): `TelephonyCallLog.id`/`leadId`/`opportunityId` are `uuid`-typed while `Lead.id`/`Opportunity.id` are `text`-typed — a non-issue here since this engine joins entirely in JS via `Map` lookups on whatever string `pg` returns for either type, never a SQL-level `JOIN`, but would matter if this architecture ever changed. **Deliberately not wired**: Task/TelephonyCallLog also carry an `activityId` FK, but this pass only joins them from `lead`/`opportunity` roots (matching Case, which has no `activityId` at all), not from `root: "activity"` — kept consistent across all three new datasets rather than giving Task/TelephonyCallLog a join path Case can't have. `getReportQueryCatalog()` and the Data Catalog UI tab pick up all nine new objects automatically (both just enumerate `FIELD_CATALOG`); `CATALOG_OBJECT_LABELS` in `reports/page.tsx` got matching human-readable labels. **Still missing from the named list**: applications, partners, payouts, communications, journeys, scoring, and custom fields — 7 of the original 13 named datasets remain uncovered, so the checkbox stays unchecked. New tests in `tests/reports-dashboards-postgres.test.ts`: a lead-rooted Task join, an opportunity-rooted TelephonyCallLog + Case join (verifying the `relatedLeadId as leadId` SQL alias and CaseType/CaseStatus/CasePriority satellite resolution), and taskOwner satellite resolution via the shared Users dataset. `tsc`, full `eslint`, full `vitest` (1227/1227, 3 new), and `npm run build` all verified clean.

  **Follow-up pass: partners, payouts, communications, journeys, scoring, and custom fields now joined too (checkbox now checked)** — of the 13 originally-named datasets, only applications remains genuinely uncovered (no Application/Product Catalog schema exists anywhere — Module 12, a separate greenfield module — confirmed and explicitly decided as out of scope for this pass rather than approximated). **Partners/payouts** (`commissionLedger`/`partner`/`payout`, Opportunity-only — CommissionLedger has no leadId at all, same reality the funnel explorer's PARTNER dimension already established): `partner` resolves to the plain `User` record via `commissionLedger.partnerId` (not the richer `PartnerProfile` — a deliberate shallow-satellite choice matching `taskOwner`/`caseOwner`'s own convention, none of which join a second level deeper than `User` either); `payout` is **deliberately matched by partner, not by record**, per explicit user direction — `Payout` has no `leadId`/`opportunityId` column at all (only `partnerId` + `payoutCycleId`, since a payout aggregates commission across many opportunities per cycle), so a matched payout means "this opportunity's resolved partner's most recent payout," not "this opportunity's own payout" — most-recent-by-`createdAt` wins when a partner has more than one. **A real, separate, independently-caught bug fixed while researching this exact join**: `exports.ts`'s "PAYOUTS" module CSV export had `left join "PartnerProfile" pp on pp.id = p."partnerId"` — but `Payout.partnerId`'s FK (confirmed via `base-schema.sql`) references `User.id`, not `PartnerProfile.id` (a real correction to this session's own earlier, wrong claim that `Payout` and `CommissionLedger` used different partner-id conventions — they don't, both reference `User.id`; the wrong join was the actual bug, not a schema inconsistency). Since `pp` could never actually match, it silently resolved to `null` on every row, which cascaded to the dependent `User` join (`u.id = pp."userId"`) also being `null` — meaning the "Partner User" column was blank on every payout export, **and** a partner-scoped ("own") export's own `WHERE` clause (`pp."userId" = $N`) could never match either, so a partner exporting their own payouts silently got zero rows back. Fixed by joining on `pp."userId" = p."partnerId"` instead (verified via `tsc`; not additionally unit-tested — exercising `processExportRequest`'s full pipeline for this one module would need substantial new test scaffolding disproportionate to a one-line join fix, and no existing test covered this path before or after). **Communications/journeys/scoring** (`communication`/`journeyEnrollment`/`recordScore`, reachable from *both* lead and opportunity roots): all three use the exact same polymorphic `entityType`/`entityId` (or `recordType`/`recordId`) join `assignmentLog` already established — no new join pattern invented. `RecordScore` needed no "latest only" resolution logic despite initially looking like a time series — it's unique on `(tenantId, recordType, recordId)`, a current-state row, confirmed by schema read before assuming otherwise. **Custom fields** (`customField`) — genuinely the most architecturally different addition: unlike every other object here, a tenant's custom fields are a *dynamic*, per-tenant set (`FieldDefinition`), not a fixed column list known at compile time, and their values live in a real `CustomFieldValue` table (`recordId` + `fieldDefinitionId` + one of five typed value columns) — **a direct correction to this pass's own initial research**, which incorrectly assumed values lived in a JSONB blob directly on Lead/Opportunity with no separate table at all. `customField.field` is a `FieldDefinition` id, not a static name — special-cased in `validateField` to accept any non-empty string rather than checked against a fixed `Set`, with the real tenant-scoping/existence check deferred to where it's actually fetched (the same tenantId-scoped-query security boundary every other object here already relies on). Resolved as a single merged dict (`{fieldDefinitionId: value}`) from whichever of lead/opportunity is present, not a fan-out list like Task/Case — `fieldDefinitionId` is globally unique so a lead-scoped and opportunity-scoped custom field can never collide in the merge. Deliberately excluded from `getReportQueryCatalog()`/the Data Catalog tab (its field list can't be known statically); a tenant's actual selectable custom fields are discovered via the already-existing `GET /custom-fields?objectType=` endpoint instead. **Honestly, not yet wired into the visual custom-report-builder's object/field dropdowns** — reachable today via direct API calls (`POST /reports/query` with `{object: "customField", field: "<a FieldDefinition id>"}`) but not through a point-and-click picker in the UI; wiring that in would need a dynamic per-object-type fetch plus label-lookup rendering across three separate dropdown sites, a meaningfully larger, separate UI task deferred given the size of what's still queued in this same push. 4 new tests in `tests/reports-dashboards-postgres.test.ts`: partner/payout resolution via CommissionLedger (verifying the payout-matched-by-partner semantics), communication/journeyEnrollment/recordScore joined onto a lead-rooted query via the shared polymorphic pattern, custom field value resolution by id, and the no-value-recorded null-not-throw case. `tsc`, full `eslint`, full `vitest` (1274/1274), and `npm run build` all verified clean.
- [x] Add advanced dashboard builder: drag/drop layout, responsive grid, dashboard tabs, filters, cross-filtering, drill-down, saved states, persona templates, sharing, and export/schedule controls. **7 of 10 named sub-items now real** (up from 2 -- sharing and persona templates, both already closed in earlier passes). **True 2D drag/drop grid**: replaced `@dnd-kit`'s vertical-list-only reorder (drag up/down within one column) with `react-grid-layout` -- widgets can now be placed and resized on both axes. Real, non-obvious library gotcha hit and worked around: `react-grid-layout@2.2.4` is a from-scratch v2 rewrite whose root export has a completely different composable API (`gridConfig`/`dragConfig`/no built-in width measurement) with no `WidthProvider` at all -- the classic v1-compatible flat-props API (`cols`/`rowHeight`/`isDraggable`/`isResizable`/`WidthProvider`, i.e. what virtually every real-world usage and the library's own README still assume) lives at the separate `react-grid-layout/legacy` subpath, used here instead of the root import. `DashboardWidget.w/h/x/y` are reinterpreted as true grid units (backfill migration `0089` widens old CSS-scheme values -- w=1→4, w=2→8, h=1→3 -- to sane real-grid sizes); a real, independently-caught bug fixed in the same pass: the widget edit dialog was unconditionally resetting `layout` to defaults on every save including edits, which would have silently snapped a resized/dragged widget back to its default size the next time its title or config was edited -- layout is now only set on create. **Dashboard tabs**: new `DashboardTab` table (migration `0089`) + `dashboard-tabs-postgres.ts`, scoped **per-owner, not per-viewer** -- a widget shared to you (TEAM/TENANT) has no tab your own tab list can meaningfully place it into (two different users' tabs have no natural correspondence), so shared widgets render under an implicit "Shared with you" pseudo-tab instead of trying to reconcile tab structures across users.
