Deleting a tab reassigns its widgets to another of the owner's tabs (preferring the default) rather than orphaning them; the very last tab can't be deleted. **Saved dashboard states**: new `DashboardLayoutSnapshot` table, deliberately narrow -- a snapshot captures tab assignment + grid position for every widget the saving user owns, never a widget's data-source config; restoring skips any referenced widget that's since been deleted rather than failing the whole restore. **Cross-filtering/drill-down -- cross-filtering only, drill-down explicitly not this**: clicking a bar in a module-backed BAR widget (LEADS or OPPORTUNITIES only -- the two modules with one unambiguous filterable field, `status`/`source` or `stage` name) sets a per-dashboard active filter that narrows every other STAT/BAR widget on the same module, shown as a "Filtering by: X ×" chip; clicking the same bar again clears it. **Deliberately excludes** ACTIVITIES and all 19 report-backed widget shapes (each has its own grouping semantics -- a truly generic filter mechanism across all of them would need per-report filtering logic, not one mechanism) and excludes navigating to a filtered list view (drill-down proper), which is a distinct, unbuilt concept. New `DashboardCrossFilter` type threaded through `getDashboardWidgetDataForTenant` (`crm.ts`) and the `/api/dashboard-widgets/[id]/data` route's query params. **Export for a whole dashboard is now real too (follow-up pass)** -- per explicit user direction, **one combined PDF, deliberately not a scheduled digest email** (a scheduled per-report digest already exists, Module 17's own scheduled-extracts bullet; this is the missing per-*dashboard* ad hoc export). A "dashboard" here is a `DashboardTab` (see the versioning bullet's own note on this same equivalence). New `exportDashboardTabPdfForTenant` (`crm.ts`) resolves exactly the widgets that tab-membership rule already governs client-side (`(widget.tabId ?? defaultTabId) === tabId`, owned widgets only -- never another user's shared widgets, since a viewer has no standing to export someone else's dashboard) and renders each through the *same* `getDashboardWidgetDataForTenant` every widget's own live data already comes from -- no second data-fetching path. New `renderDashboardTabPdf` (`report-pdf.tsx`), reusing the exact `@react-pdf/renderer` + raw-PDF-fallback pattern `renderReportTablePdf` already established one page per widget in one combined document: a scalar (STAT) widget renders as "Value: X", an array-shaped widget (FUNNEL/TREND/BAR/report-backed) renders as a small table with the same styling, and an empty dashboard still produces a valid one-page PDF rather than erroring. New `GET /api/dashboard-tabs/[id]/export/pdf` (a direct synchronous download, not the heavier governance-gated async export-request pipeline every other export in this module uses -- deliberately: a dashboard PDF is a visual summary of what a widget already renders for its own viewer, not raw sensitive tabular data pulled straight from an entity that needs sensitive-field masking) plus a new "Export PDF" item on each dashboard tab's dropdown menu (`dashboard-manager.tsx`), opened in a new tab exactly like the pre-existing partner-invoice PDF download link. 5 new tests: `tests/export-formats.test.ts` (2 -- real, unmocked `@react-pdf/renderer` execution producing a genuine `%PDF-` buffer for a mixed scalar+tabular widget set and for zero widgets) and `tests/dashboard-tab-pdf-export.test.ts` (3 -- tab-not-found rejection, and the actual tab-membership resolution: an explicitly-tagged widget and an untagged widget that falls back to the default tab are both included, while a widget on a different tab and a widget merely shared to this viewer are both correctly excluded). **Still genuinely missing**: breakpoint-based responsive column layout (this grid is fluid-width via `WidthProvider`, not multi-device breakpoint-aware like `ResponsiveGridLayout`), a general dashboard-level filter bar (date range, etc. -- only the click-driven cross-filter exists), and drill-down-to-list-view. 21 new tests in the original pass (`tests/dashboard-tabs.test.ts`, 13, and `tests/dashboard-widget-cross-filter.test.ts`, 8) covering tab CRUD, snapshot save/restore, and cross-filter narrowing -- see prior history below. `tsc`, full `eslint`, full `vitest` (1341/1341), and `npm run build` all verified clean (`/api/dashboard-tabs/[id]/export/pdf` confirmed present in the build's route list, alongside all 6 original `/api/dashboard-tabs*`/`/api/dashboard-layouts*` routes); migration `0089` applied against the real local dev database. A live dev-server smoke test confirmed `/dashboard` compiles and serves without a server error, but there was **no live authenticated click-through** (no test credentials in this environment, and no headless-browser tooling available without a new install) -- the actual drag/resize/tab-switch/cross-filter-click/export interactions were not exercised in a real browser.
- [x] Add chart library beyond current stat/bar/trend/funnel widgets: line, stacked bar, grouped bar, area, pie/donut, table, pivot, heatmap, cohort matrix, funnel, sankey-style journey, and score distribution. **All 11 named types now real** (line/trend already existed): new `AREA`/`PIE`/`STACKED_BAR`/`SCORE_DISTRIBUTION`/`HEATMAP`/`TABLE` `DashboardWidget` types (`widget-library.tsx`), plus a genuinely real `FUNNEL` (recharts' own `Funnel`/`FunnelChart` shape, replacing the horizontal-bar-chart stand-in that was there before). "Cohort matrix" and "grouped bar" reuse the new `HEATMAP` and `STACKED_BAR` components respectively rather than being separate widget types, since they're the same rendering shape with different data. Wired to real new data sources, not placeholder chrome: `marketing_attribution_summary`→PIE, `sender_reputation`→STACKED_BAR, `funnel_explorer`→FUNNEL/TABLE, `campaign_roi`→TABLE, `cohort_funnel_progression`→HEATMAP, all added to `getReportBackedWidgetData` (`crm.ts`) and the widget-creation dialog's report catalog. **Sankey and pivot table are now real too (follow-up pass)**, per explicit user direction ("yes, build it" for both). **A direct correction to this bullet's own prior claim**: "recharts has no Sankey component, a real one needs a dedicated library like `d3-sankey`" was factually wrong -- recharts (already a real, pre-existing dependency) ships its own `Sankey` component built on the same `{nodes, links}` shape `d3-sankey` itself uses, confirmed by reading its actual type definitions before adding anything; **no new dependency was added**. New `SANKEY` widget type visualizes the opportunity pipeline's own lead-source-to-stage flow -- computed in `getDashboardWidgetDataForTenant` (`crm.ts`) by reusing `listOpportunitiesForTenant`'s already-decorated rows (`opportunity.lead`/`opportunity.stage` are joined in-memory there already, the same source this widget's BAR/FUNNEL siblings already read from), grouped into `(source, stage)` flow counts and rendered via recharts' `Sankey`. **Pivot table**: deliberately reuses the semantic metric layer's own object/field/aggregation model rather than a second query-definition UI -- a `PIVOT` widget references an existing `Metric` (its own `groupBy` becomes the pivot's row dimension) plus one additional column dimension picked at widget-creation time, the smallest real extension of the already-built self-service authoring model. New `executePivotQueryForTenant` (`reporting-query.ts`) reuses `fetchDataSets`/`buildJoinContexts`/`matchesFilters` unchanged -- the exact same private helpers `executeMetricQueryForTenant` already reuses -- extended to group by two dimensions (row + column) at once instead of one, producing `{rowLabels, columnLabels, cells}`; new `PivotTableWidget` (`widget-library.tsx`) renders it as a real HTML cross-tab. Widget-creation UI (`dashboard-manager.tsx`): SANKEY needs no configuration (fixed to the pipeline's own flow, like FUNNEL); PIVOT gets a metric picker (filtered to metrics that already have a group-by dimension set) plus an object+field picker for the column dimension, reusing the exact `GET /reports/query` catalog fetch `MetricsSection`/`CustomReportBuilder` already call on the Reports page. 10 new tests: `tests/pivot-query.test.ts` (5 -- COUNT and SUM aggregation into a real row×column grid, plus the same root/aggregateField/FIELD_CATALOG validation `executeMetricQueryForTenant` already enforces) and `tests/dashboard-widget-sankey-pivot.test.ts` (5 -- the sankey node/link graph construction including the Unknown/Unassigned fallback, the pivot widget resolving its referenced metric's groupBy as the row dimension and delegating to `executePivotQueryForTenant` with the exact expected definition, and the two empty-pivot short-circuits for a group-by-less metric or incomplete widget config). `tsc`, full `eslint`, full `vitest` (1351/1351, 10 new), and `npm run build` all verified clean.
- [x] Add cohort explorer: cohort by created date, source, campaign, course, university, partner, owner/team, score band, and application stage, with retention/progression metrics. Was previously a single fixed dimension (created-date, week/month grain only) — `calculateCohortReport`/`getCohortReportForTenant` (`inbuilt-reports.ts`) gained a `CohortDimension` parameter (`CREATED_DATE`/`SOURCE`/`CAMPAIGN`/`SCORE_BAND`/`OWNER`) that reuses the exact same stage-progression precomputation, just switching the bucketing key from a date period to a dimension value (source/campaign via the existing `getLeadCampaignLookup`, score band via a direct `RecordScore` query, owner via `listTenantUsers`). Non-date cohorts sort by lead count descending (most useful for comparing segments) rather than the chronological order date cohorts keep. Retention/progression metrics (stage-reach rate, avg days to reach a stage) were already real from earlier work and are unchanged. **Course/university/partner/application-stage dimensions are not built** — there is no course/university/application schema anywhere in this codebase (Module 12's Product Catalog is unbuilt), so those dimensions have no data to cohort by; partner-dimension cohorting would need a lead/opportunity-to-partner link this pass didn't add. Tests: `tests/reporting-calculations.test.ts` (a non-date SOURCE cohort, sorted by lead count, with null `cohortStart`/`cohortEnd`).
- [x] Add funnel explorer: lead-to-opportunity-to-application-to-enrollment funnels, stage conversion, stage aging, drop-off reasons, re-entry handling, and segment comparison. New `calculateFunnelExplorerReport`/`getFunnelExplorerForTenant` (`inbuilt-reports.ts`, new `funnel_explorer` report key) adds, on top of the existing flat stage-conversion table: **stage aging** (avg/max days a currently-open Opportunity has sat in its stage, computed from `OpportunityStageHistory` with a fallback to `createdAt` when no history exists); **drop-off** (closed-lost Opportunities bucketed by the stage they were in at closure, with up to 5 top reasons per stage pulled from `CallDisposition.reasonLost` — the only place a "lost reason" exists anywhere in this schema, confirmed via Module 15's own telephony work; **a real, honest limitation, not a gap**: reasons are only populated when a call disposition was logged, not a formal reason taxonomy on the Opportunity itself); **re-entry handling** (a stage reappearing in an Opportunity's own ordered stage-history means it moved backward and re-entered a stage already passed through); and **segment comparison** (won-rate by `SOURCE`/`OWNER`/`OPPORTUNITY_TYPE`, `?segment=` on the new report route). **"Lead-to-opportunity-to-application-to-enrollment" as 4 distinct stages is NOT built** — there is no Application/Enrollment schema anywhere in this codebase (Module 12's Product Catalog is unbuilt), so this stays scoped to the Opportunity pipeline's own stages, same limitation the checklist's own funnel-report note already flagged elsewhere. Tests: `tests/reporting-calculations.test.ts` (aging falls back to `createdAt` with no history vs. the most recent re-entry into a stage; drop-off reason bucketing; re-entry detection; segment win-rate comparison).
- [x] Add attribution explorer: first/last/linear/U-shaped/W-shaped/time-decay attribution, source/campaign/partner touch paths, ROI, assisted conversions, and revenue/application influence. New `getAttributionExplorerForTenant` (`marketing-journeys.ts`, new `attribution_explorer` report key/route) adds real per-record touch-path assembly (ordered source/campaign/channel/timestamp per converting record, capped at 200 for payload size), assisted-conversion detection (more than one touch contributed credit), and revenue influence (won-Opportunity amount, weighted per the selected model's credit split) on top of the flat per-source credit table Module 8 already built. **ROI, a genuinely new join**: `MarketingCostEntry` (`scopeType='JOURNEY'`) joined via `MarketingAttributionTouch.journeyId` — the one real FK a touch carries; **a real bug caught and fixed during testing, not shipped**: an earlier version of this tried to join cost by touch `channel`, but a touch's `channel` is its capture mechanism (`WEBSITE_VISIT`/`FORM_SUBMISSION`/etc.), a completely different vocabulary from `MarketingCostEntry.channel` (`EMAIL`/`WHATSAPP`/`SMS`) — the two never overlap, so that join would have silently produced zero ROI rows in production; caught by the existing touch-channel type union rejecting the test's `EMAIL`/`WHATSAPP` fixture values at compile time, not a runtime discovery. **"Partner touch paths" is now real too (follow-up pass)** — the one remaining named sub-item. There's still no touch-level partner attribute (a touch only ever carries source/campaign/channel) -- partner is resolved as a whole-RECORD fact instead, the exact same way the funnel explorer's PARTNER segment dimension already does: via `CommissionLedger` (populated only once commission is actually earned on a won deal). Every touch in a converting Opportunity's path is labeled with that record's resolved partner (`touchPaths[].partner`), and a new `byPartner` breakdown (conversions + revenue per partner) is added alongside `bySource`/`byJourney` -- deliberately **not** weighted by the selected attribution model like source/campaign credit is: partner credit is a plain per-record rollup (one partner per record, never several to split credit between), since the model's whole purpose is splitting credit across multiple touches, and there's only ever one partner to attribute regardless of how many touches a record has. LEAD-type touches get `partner: null` -- partner association only exists at the Opportunity level, same limitation as revenue. **Real, separate UI bug caught and fixed while adding this** -- the same class of "built but never wired to the UI" gap as the funnel explorer's own bug from an earlier pass: `attribution_explorer` (this report/API, tested since Module 8) was never added to the Reports page catalog at all, reachable only by calling the API directly; a catalog entry, attribution-model dropdown (reusing the existing one already built for `marketing_attribution_summary`), and a `bySource`-as-primary-table preview mapping were all added. Tests: `tests/marketing-journeys.test.ts` (2-touch assisted conversion on a won deal vs. a 1-touch non-assisted conversion on a lost one, LINEAR credit/revenue split, journey-scoped ROI, and a new test covering partner resolution via CommissionLedger including the no-commission-entry "No Partner" fallback and the per-partner revenue rollup).
- [x] Add segmentation and comparison tools: compare users, teams, sales groups, partners, universities, courses, sources, campaigns, time periods, cohorts, and score bands. **Not built as its own dedicated, generic comparison tool** — a user picking any two arbitrary segments and diffing them side by side doesn't exist. **Real, meaningful overlap now exists piecemeal**, worth noting rather than treating as a full duplicate gap: the cohort explorer (this module's own item above) can bucket by source/campaign/score-band/owner; the funnel explorer's segment comparison can compare win rates by source/owner/opportunity-type; the attribution explorer breaks credit down by source. **"Time periods" is now closed too** (follow-up pass): new `getPeriodComparisonReportForTenant`/`calculatePeriodComparisonReport` (`inbuilt-reports.ts`, new `period_comparison` report key) compares leads created, opportunities created/won, won value, and win rate for the current vs. previous week/month/quarter (a preset dropdown, matching the existing attribution-model dropdown pattern on this same Reports page — not free-form date pickers, kept deliberately simple), with a null (not fabricated/infinite) percent change whenever the baseline period was 0. Same 1000-row cap every other report in this file uses, with the same honest caveat: a tenant with more than 1000 leads/opportunities total could under-count a comparison against a period older than the most recent 1000 rows. **"Sales groups" is now closed too** (second follow-up pass): the cohort explorer gained a `SALES_GROUP` dimension, bucketing leads by their owner's `SalesGroup` (via `SalesGroupMember`) — a user can belong to more than one group (the unique constraint is `groupId`+`userId`, not `userId` alone), so this picks the first membership found per user, a documented simplification rather than fabricating a "primary group" concept that doesn't exist anywhere else in this codebase. **Real, separate UI bug caught and fixed while adding this**: the cohort explorer's `SOURCE`/`CAMPAIGN`/`SCORE_BAND`/`OWNER` dimensions (added earlier in this module's own work) had no dimension picker anywhere in the Reports page UI at all — they were only ever reachable by calling the API directly with a `?dimension=` param, never from a click. A new dimension `<Select>` (matching the existing attribution-model/period-preset dropdown pattern on this same page) now makes all 6 dimensions, including the new one, actually usable from the UI for the first time. **"Partners" is now closed too** (third follow-up pass): the funnel explorer gained a `PARTNER` segment dimension. **A real schema discovery made along the way, not assumed**: there is no direct `partnerId` on Opportunity at all — partner association is a derived fact from `CommissionLedger` (populated only when commission is actually earned on a won deal), so this dimension only resolves a partner for opportunities that have already generated a commission entry; everything else buckets into "No Partner" rather than a fabricated association. **A second, more subtle schema fact that would have silently broken this if assumed instead of verified**: `CommissionLedger.partnerId` references `User.id` directly (confirmed via its FK constraint), a *different* convention from `Payout.partnerId`, which references `PartnerProfile.id` instead — getting this wrong would have resolved every partner name to "Unknown" with no visible error. Cohort explorer stayed the right home for the sales-groups dimension (Lead-owner-based) while partner naturally landed on the funnel explorer instead, since partner association only exists at the Opportunity level, not Lead. **A second real, separate UI bug caught and fixed while adding this**: `funnel_explorer` itself — the report/API this whole sub-item and item 12 above are about — was never added to the Reports page catalog at all; it had a real, tested backend since this module's original pass but was completely unreachable from the UI until now. Both a catalog entry and a segment-dimension `<Select>` (source/owner/opportunity-type/partner) were added. **Still not built**: universities/courses comparison (no schema exists for either — that's Module 12's Product Catalog, a separate large greenfield module) and a generic "pick any two arbitrary segments" tool that would replace this piecemeal set of comparisons with one unified UI. Tests: `tests/reporting-calculations.test.ts` (period-comparison percent-change math) and `tests/inbuilt-reports-postgres.test.ts` (SALES_GROUP bucketing including the no-owner fallback; PARTNER resolution via the CommissionLedger join, including the no-commission-entry "No Partner" fallback). **"Teams" is now closed too** (fourth follow-up pass): the cohort explorer gained a `TEAM` dimension, same shape as `SALES_GROUP`/`OWNER` -- buckets a lead by its owner's `Team` (via `User.teamId`), falling back to "No Team". **The bullet's own core gap is now closed too (fifth follow-up pass, checkbox now checked)**: a genuine "pick any two arbitrary segments and diff them side by side" tool, per explicit user direction allowing comparison **across mismatched levels/dimensions** (e.g. a LEAD Source segment vs. an OPPORTUNITY Partner segment), not just within one dimension type. New `getSegmentComparisonReportForTenant` (`inbuilt-reports.ts`, new `segment_comparison` report key/route) resolves each of the two segments independently (LEAD-level: SOURCE/CAMPAIGN/SCORE_BAND/OWNER, reusing the cohort explorer's exact dimension-resolution logic; OPPORTUNITY-level: SOURCE/OWNER/OPPORTUNITY_TYPE/PARTNER/STAGE, reusing the funnel explorer's) and computes one common, level-agnostic metric set for each -- record count, won count/rate, and average deal value -- since that's what stays meaningfully comparable regardless of which level or dimension a segment came from, unlike each level's own internal fields. A LEAD "wins" if it has produced at least one won Opportunity (Lead itself has no won concept). New "Compare" tab on the Reports page: two segment builders (level → dimension → free-text value, since values are open-ended live data like actual owner names or stage names, not from a fixed picklist in this v1) and a side-by-side results table. **Deliberately not built** (matching this exact bullet's already-decided scope, same reasoning as the cohort/funnel explorers' own equivalent gaps above): universities/courses as a comparable dimension at all -- no course/university/application schema exists anywhere in this codebase (Module 12's Product Catalog, a separate large greenfield module), so there is nothing to segment by for either. Tests: `tests/inbuilt-reports-postgres.test.ts` (3 new -- a cross-level/cross-dimension comparison end to end, the null-not-divide-by-zero path when a segment matches zero records, and rejecting a dimension that isn't valid for its given level).
- [x] Add anomaly detection for key metrics: lead volume spikes/drops, conversion drops, SLA breach spikes, payout anomalies, campaign performance changes, scoring drift, and telephony/case backlog anomalies. **All 7 named domains built at once**, per explicit user direction, sharing one rolling-average ± standard-deviation baseline/threshold method (also per user direction) rather than a bespoke heuristic per domain. New `getAnomalyDetectionReportForTenant`/`detectAnomalyFromDailySeries`/`fillDailySeries` (`inbuilt-reports.ts`, new `anomaly_detection` report key/route) reduce each domain to one daily count (or daily average, for scoring drift) over a 15-day trailing window (14 baseline days + the evaluated day), flagging the latest day anomalous when it falls ≥2 standard deviations outside the baseline's mean in either direction. **Domain data sources**: lead volume (`Lead.createdAt`), conversions (`OpportunityStageHistory` rows landing on a won `StageDefinition`), SLA breaches (`Activity` where `slaStatus='BREACHED'`, bucketed by `dueAt`), payout amounts (daily `CommissionLedger.commissionAmount` sum, `entryType='EARNED'`), campaign performance (daily `MarketingAttributionTouch` conversions), scoring drift (daily average `RecordScore.conversionProbability`), and service backlog (daily `Case` created minus resolved — a real backlog-growth proxy, not a point-in-time open-case snapshot, since reconstructing true historical open counts would need full status-transition history that doesn't exist as its own table). **"Telephony/case backlog," the checklist's own combined naming, is honored by scope, not by literally blending two signals**: `TelephonyCallLog` logs completed calls, not a pending queue, so it has no real backlog concept of its own to combine with case backlog — this domain covers the one service-desk surface (cases) that actually has a backlog concept in this schema. **A real, zero-variance edge case caught and fixed while writing this function's own tests, not shipped as a silent gap**: a perfectly flat baseline (stddev = 0) followed by any real change previously reported "not an anomaly" outright (avoiding a division by zero swallowed the actual signal too) — fixed so a zero-variance baseline still flags any departure from it as anomalous, just without a stddev-based deviation figure (there's no meaningful number to report when the baseline has no spread at all). **A second real, environment-dependent bug caught the same way**: the day-bucketing math originally used local-server-time `Date` methods (`setHours`/`setDate`) while reading results back via `toISOString()` (always UTC) — harmless on a UTC server, but this dev/test environment runs in `Asia/Calcutta` (UTC+5:30), where that combination shifted every bucketed day by up to 24 hours; fixed to use UTC methods throughout (`setUTCHours`/`setUTCDate`), matching Postgres's own `date_trunc('day', ...)` calls (which run in the DB session's UTC timezone here) so both sides bucket days identically — not tenant-timezone-aware, the same documented limitation every other date-bucketed report in this file already carries. New "Anomaly Detection" catalog entry on the Reports page, with a dedicated preview-row mapping (one summary row per domain — latest value, baseline mean/stddev, deviation, spike/drop/none — the full daily series stays in the raw export only, matching the same "one primary table" convention attribution explorer's `bySource` preview already established). 7 new tests in `tests/reporting-calculations.test.ts` (spike/drop detection against a stable baseline, no false positive on normal fluctuation, insufficient-history and zero-variance edge cases, and `fillDailySeries`'s dense 0-fill behavior) plus 2 in `tests/inbuilt-reports-postgres.test.ts` (a full 7-domain wiring test and the no-tenant empty-report path). `tsc`, full `eslint`, full `vitest` (1284/1284, 9 new), and `npm run build` all verified clean.
- [x] Add forecasting-lite dashboards where relevant: expected applications, expected enrollments, projected fee collection, campaign volume, task backlog, SLA breach risk, and partner payout projection. **All 4 buildable domains built at once**, per explicit user direction, using a real linear-regression trend projection (not a simple moving average, also per user direction) — genuinely new capability, not a display layer on existing numbers. **3 named sub-items stay unbuilt, unchanged**: expected applications/enrollments/projected fee collection have no underlying schema to project from at all (Module 12's Product Catalog/Application model is unbuilt) — forecasting those would mean forecasting data that doesn't exist. New `getForecastReportForTenant`/`projectLinearTrend` (`inbuilt-reports.ts`, new `forecast` report key/route) fit an ordinary-least-squares trend line over a 30-day history for each domain, then project 7 days forward, clamped to ≥0 (none of these 4 counts can be meaningfully negative, and an unclamped downward trend would otherwise happily project a negative count). Reuses `fillDailySeries` from the anomaly-detection pass directly — same dense-daily-series shape, just a longer window and a trend fit instead of a baseline-deviation check. **Domain data sources**: campaign volume (daily `MarketingAttributionTouch` rows with `touchType='TOUCH'`), task backlog (daily `Task` created minus completed — the same "growth, not snapshot" proxy anomaly detection's `SERVICE_BACKLOG` domain already established for `Case`, for the identical reason: no full status-transition history table exists to reconstruct a true point-in-time open count), SLA breach risk (daily `Activity` `slaStatus='BREACHED'` count, the same series anomaly detection's `SLA_BREACHES` domain uses, just over a longer history window here), and partner payout projection (daily `CommissionLedger.commissionAmount` sum, `entryType='EARNED'` — again the same series anomaly detection's `PAYOUT_AMOUNT` domain uses). New "Forecast" catalog entry on the Reports page, with a dedicated preview-row mapping (one summary row per domain — latest actual value, next forecasted value/date, per-day trend slope — the full day-by-day history/forecast arrays stay in the raw export only, matching the same "one primary table" convention every other multi-domain report added this pass already follows). 4 new tests in `tests/reporting-calculations.test.ts` (a rising trend projected forward correctly, a falling trend's projection clamped to 0 rather than going negative, a flat history projecting flat, and the fewer-than-2-points no-forecast case) plus 2 in `tests/inbuilt-reports-postgres.test.ts` (a full 4-domain wiring test and the no-tenant empty-report path). `tsc`, full `eslint`, full `vitest` (1290/1290, 6 new), and `npm run build` all verified clean.
- [x] Add metric permissions and row/field-level enforcement in analytics: restrict sensitive metrics, export approval for protected fields, dashboard sharing scope, and no raw ids in chart/table output. **Restrict sensitive metrics**: new `assertSensitiveReportAccess` (`inbuilt-reports.ts`) gates `campaign_roi` and `lead_source_roi` (real $ marketing-spend/ROI figures) to tenant admins/platform admins, throwing `SENSITIVE_REPORT_ACCESS_DENIED` (mapped to a 403 in both routes). **Deliberately excludes `commission_payout_summary`** from this new gate — that report already has real, correct row-level scoping for partner self-service access (`isPartnerScopedUser`/`partnerScoped` in `listCommissionPayoutSummaryInputs`), and adding a blanket admin-only gate would have broken a partner's legitimate access to their own commission summary; confirmed by reading the function before gating anything, not assumed. **Export approval for protected fields**: already real from earlier work (the `ExportRequest.status = 'PENDING_APPROVAL'` sensitive-column gate in `exports.ts`, reused unchanged by CSV/XLSX/PDF report exports) — not new, but confirmed still the enforcement point. **Dashboard sharing scope**: this module's own dashboard-builder-sharing sub-item above (`DashboardWidget.visibility`). **No raw ids in chart/table output**: audited rather than newly built — the existing STAT/TREND/BAR/FUNNEL/NBA widget types were confirmed to never render a raw entity id as a visible chart label/value (the NBA widget's `id`/`recordId` fields are click-through-navigation metadata, not displayed values); the new PIE/STACKED_BAR/TABLE/HEATMAP/SCORE_DISTRIBUTION types follow the same convention (grouped by human-readable labels — source/channel/stage names — never a raw id). Tests: `tests/inbuilt-reports-postgres.test.ts` (non-admin rejected before any query runs, tenant admin and platform admin both pass).
- [x] Add data freshness controls: rollup freshness badges, last updated timestamps, manual refresh, scheduled refresh, stale-data warnings, failed refresh alerts, and per-widget refresh policy. **All 7 named sub-items now real, built across two genuinely different surfaces this codebase already had (the `ReportRollup` cache and `DashboardWidget`), rather than inventing a third.** Confirmed by audit before building: `ReportRollup`/`ReportRefreshState`/`ReportRefreshJob` (migration `0008`) already had a complete state machine (`FRESH/STALE/REFRESHING/ERROR`), a `refreshIntervalMinutes` column, and a `ReportRefreshState_due_idx` index built for exactly a "what's due" scan -- but nothing ever read any of it. `requestReportRollupRefresh`/`refreshReportRollupForTenant` (manual admin-triggered refresh) existed and worked, but were the *only* way a refresh ever happened, and both tables were write-only: no route anywhere ever `select`ed a `ReportRefreshState` row back out to a UI. Separately, `DashboardWidget` (the real drag/drop dashboard builder from earlier report-infrastructure work this session) had zero freshness concept at all -- it recomputes live on every request with no timestamp, no auto-refresh, and no stale/error indicator ever shown. **Rollup freshness badges + last updated timestamps**: new `GET /api/reports/rollups/status` (admin-gated) exposes `ReportRefreshState` for the first time; the Reports page's Inbuilt Reports panel now shows a real `RollupFreshnessBadge` (status-colored, `formatWorkspaceRelativeTime` for "last refreshed," the actual error message on `ERROR`) next to the pre-existing Refresh Rollup button, which previously wrote a rollup row nobody could ever see the state of. **Manual refresh**: already real for rollups; added a per-widget manual refresh button (spinning `RefreshCw` icon) to `DashboardWidget` (`widget-library.tsx`). **Scheduled refresh**: the actual missing half -- new `processDueReportRollupRefreshes` scans `ReportRefreshState` for rows past their own `refreshIntervalMinutes` (or never successfully refreshed) with no job already in flight, and enqueues a real `SCHEDULED` `ReportRefreshJob`, consumed by the exact same `processPendingReportRefreshJobs` worker that already existed -- not a second refresh pipeline. Wired into `scripts/worker.ts` as `reports.processRollupSchedule`, plus a mirrored cron-secret-gated `POST /api/reports/rollups/process-schedule` route matching every other worker job's existing dual (persistent-worker + serverless-cron) invocation pattern in this codebase. For dashboard widgets, "scheduled" is a per-widget client-driven auto-refresh interval (see per-widget policy below) rather than a server rollup, since widgets were never rollup-backed to begin with. **Stale-data warnings**: the rollup badge itself doubles as this (`STALE`/`ERROR` render in amber/red); widgets now keep showing their last-known-good data on a failed refresh instead of blanking the card, with an inline "Refresh failed -- showing data from X ago" warning rather than silently going stale with no indication. **Failed refresh alerts**: new `createUserNotification` call in `processPendingReportRefreshJobs`'s failure branch, reusing the exact same in-app-notification primitive `webhook-outbox.ts` and the scoring worker already use for "background job failed" -- a `MANUAL` job notifies its actual requester, a `SCHEDULED` job (no requester) falls back to the tenant's earliest-created user, mirroring `webhook-outbox.ts`'s own established "no specific owner" precedent exactly. Deliberately **not** extended to per-widget fetch failures -- a dashboard can hold a dozen auto-refreshing widgets, and a notification-inbox entry per failed poll would be spammy rather than useful; the inline stale-data warning on the widget itself is the proportionate treatment there, consistent with how no other polling UI in this codebase (e.g. the SSE notification stream itself) generates a notification for its own transient failures. **Per-widget refresh policy**: `DashboardWidget.config.refreshPolicy.autoRefreshMinutes`, added with zero migration since `config` is already this table's established free-form settings bag (same place `reportKey`/`groupBy`/`metric` already live) -- editable via a new "Auto-refresh every (minutes)" field in the widget add/edit dialog (blank = manual-only, unchanged default behavior), driving a real per-widget `setInterval` in `widget-library.tsx`. Report-rollup scope also got a real per-report policy control: `refreshIntervalMinutes` (already a column) is now editable from the Reports page via a new `PATCH /api/reports/rollups/policy` route. 12 new tests in `tests/report-rollups.test.ts` covering the state list/policy-update validation (missing reportKey, non-positive interval, existing-vs-new state row), the due-scan's enqueue/no-op branches, and both failure-notification branches (requester-attributed and fallback-to-earliest-user, including the "no user exists at all" no-op case).
- [x] Add scheduled extracts and subscriptions: CSV/XLSX exports, dashboard PDFs where supported, email subscriptions, secure download links, expiry, delivery audit, and retry/failure tracking. **All 7 named sub-items now genuinely real** — CSV/email/links/expiry/audit were already built in an earlier pass (see below for that history); **XLSX and PDF, the 2 sub-items previously left unchecked, are now real too (Module 17 gap-closing work)**. **XLSX**: new `toXlsxBuffer` (`exports.ts`) using `exceljs` — **a genuinely new dependency**, added specifically for this since hand-rolling a correct OOXML/SpreadsheetML writer would be a much larger, more error-prone undertaking than using an established library for what is, at its core, a flat-table export. **PDF**: new `renderReportTablePdf` (`report-pdf.tsx`), reusing the exact `@react-pdf/renderer` + raw-PDF-fallback pattern `invoice-pdf.tsx` already established for partner invoices. **Direct correction to this bullet's own prior claim**: "no PDF library exists anywhere in this codebase" was factually wrong — `@react-pdf/renderer` already existed as a real dependency, just never wired to reports/dashboards; the actual gap was narrower than originally stated. Both formats are wired through the same `fetchExportContent`/`processExportRequest` pipeline CSV already used (`ExportRequest.exportType` — the column already had the right CHECK constraint allowing `XLSX`/`PDF`, just always hardcoded to `'CSV'` at insert time until now), scoped narrowly to inbuilt-report exports only (every other export module stays CSV-only, unchanged). Migration `0087_analytics_export_formats.sql` widens `ReportSchedule`/`ReportEmailDelivery`'s `format` CHECK constraints to allow `XLSX` alongside the existing `LINK`/`CSV`/`PDF`. UI: the Reports page's schedule-creation form and the ad hoc `QueueExportButton` (shown next to any inbuilt report) both gained an XLSX/PDF option. **Retry/failure tracking -- the one remaining named sub-item, now also real (follow-up pass)**: real for the email-delivery leg from day one (via the reused outbox's retry-with-backoff); *report generation* retry (the actual remaining gap) is now real too. Retry state (`retryCount`/`nextRetryAt`, migration `0090_report_schedule_retry.sql`) lives on `ReportSchedule` itself, deliberately separate from `lastRunAt`/`nextRunAt` -- the schedule's normal cadence keeps advancing regardless of a failure; retry is a sooner-than-cadence extra attempt layered on top, not a replacement for the normal run. New `retryFailedReportSchedules` (worker job `reports.retryFailedSchedules` + a matching cron-secret-gated `POST /api/reports/schedules/retry-failed` route, mirroring `processDueReportSchedules`'s existing dual invocation pattern) scans schedules with pending retry state and re-attempts generation, using the exact same backoff ladder `webhook-outbox.ts` already established (1m/5m/30m/2h/12h) rather than inventing a second one. **Not every failure is retried**: a governance gate (`EXPORT_PENDING_APPROVAL`, needs a human, not a retry), a stale/renamed report key (`UNKNOWN_REPORT_KEY`), or an unsupported format (`*_EXPORT_NOT_AVAILABLE`) are permanent failures that retrying can't fix, so they're excluded from the retry ladder entirely rather than burning through 5 attempts pointlessly. After exhausting the ladder, a schedule's retry state resets and it simply waits for its own next natural cadence, same as before this pass. Tests: `tests/export-formats.test.ts` (real, unmocked `exceljs`/`@react-pdf/renderer` execution — round-trips a generated XLSX buffer back through `exceljs` to verify headers/values, and confirms a real `%PDF-` buffer for both populated and zero-row reports), `tests/report-schedules.test.ts` (real PDF/XLSX success paths, plus 5 new: retryable vs non-retryable failure classification, successful retry resetting state without touching `nextRunAt`, ladder exhaustion, and the disabled-tenant skip), and `tests/exports-audit-logging.test.ts` (new `exportType` column position in the insert).

  **Original build history (CSV/email/links/expiry/audit), preserved for context**: `ReportSchedule`/`ReportEmailDelivery` and the `processDueReportSchedules` worker job already existed and correctly computed cadence/recipients, but `createDelivery`'s own inline comment admitted the real gap outright — "Mail transport is not configured; this row is ready for a future email adapter." No email was ever sent, no file was ever rendered, and no download link was ever generated; every delivery stayed `PENDING` forever, the same class of "looks built but is an inert stub" gap as `WebhookOutbox` elsewhere in this project. CSV rendering reuses the existing export-governance pipeline (`createExportRequestForUser`/`processExportRequest`) completely unchanged, reusing its `PENDING_APPROVAL` sensitive-field gate, `FileObject` storage, and `expiresAt` handling; migration `0055_report_schedule_real_delivery.sql` added `ReportEmailDelivery.exportRequestId` to link a delivery to its generated export. Real emails are queued per recipient through the existing `queueCommunicationForTenant`/`CommunicationOutbox` pipeline, so suppression/consent checks apply and retry-with-backoff comes from `processCommunicationOutbox` unchanged. Secure download links are a public, unauthenticated `GET /api/public/report-deliveries/[deliveryId]/download`, identified solely by the unguessable `ReportEmailDelivery` row id, joining through to the linked `ExportRequest`/`FileObject`, verifying completion and non-expiry, streaming via `readPrivateFile`, and writing an audit-log `DOWNLOAD` entry attributed to the schedule's owning user. Expiry is inherited from the export pipeline's own `ExportRequest.expiresAt`. Delivery audit is each `ReportEmailDelivery` row's real status/error trail plus the download-time `AuditLog` entry.
- [x] Add executive scorecards: admissions summary, marketing ROI, counselor productivity, partner performance, payout exposure, scoring quality, service/case SLA, telephony performance, and data quality. **One single combined scorecard page built (not per-persona surfaces), per explicit user direction** — the underlying data for all 8 buildable areas already existed as separate, real inbuilt reports; this pass adds the scorecard concept itself: new `getExecutiveScorecardReportForTenant` (`inbuilt-reports.ts`, new `executive_scorecard` report key/route) calls all 6 underlying report functions (`campaign_roi`, `rep_performance`, `commission_payout_summary`, `telephony_call_performance`, `case_analytics`, `data_quality`) plus `listScoringModelVersionsForTenant` (dynamically imported from `self-learning-scoring.ts`, matching this function's own `getScopeCostSummary`/`marketing-cost.ts` dynamic-import precedent right above it) in parallel, extracting a handful of key metrics from each into one glanceable 8-section shape rather than 8 separate report-catalog entries a user has to click into individually. **"Admissions summary" (the 9th named area) stays excluded** — no Application schema exists to summarize admissions from (Module 12 again). **Gated the same way `campaign_roi` already is** (`assertSensitiveReportAccess`, tenant-admin/platform-admin only) rather than inventing a second sensitivity gate — a reasoned choice, not an afterthought: an executive scorecard is inherently leadership-level data (marketing spend, payout exposure, partner commission) by nature. **A real bug caught before it shipped, not assumed**: the marketing-ROI section initially read a `journey.revenue` field that doesn't exist on `getScopeCostSummary`'s return shape (the real field is `attributedRevenue`) — would have silently reported $0 attributed revenue for every tenant with real revenue, caught by reading the actual return-shape source before trusting a guessed field name, not by the type checker (the aggregation code uses `any` for these cross-report values). New "Executive Scorecard" catalog entry on the Reports page, with a dedicated preview-row mapping (one row per section+metric pair — the simplest honest flat rendering of "8 sections, each with a few key metrics," rather than inventing a bespoke multi-panel scorecard layout for this pass). 3 new tests in `tests/inbuilt-reports-postgres.test.ts`: all 8 sections present with real (non-fabricated) zero values for an all-empty tenant, a non-admin correctly rejected, and the no-tenant empty-report path. `tsc`, full `eslint`, full `vitest` (1293/1293, 3 new), and `npm run build` all verified clean.
- [x] Add analytics annotations: mark campaigns, events, outages, policy changes, intake deadlines, fee deadlines, and launch dates on charts for context. New `ReportAnnotation` table (migration `0086`: `label`/`description`/`category` — `CAMPAIGN`/`EVENT`/`OUTAGE`/`POLICY_CHANGE`/`INTAKE_DEADLINE`/`FEE_DEADLINE`/`LAUNCH`/`OTHER`, matching every named sub-item exactly — and a date-only `occurredAt`, since an annotation marks a day on a chart, not a precise instant), CRUD via `src/lib/server/report-annotations.ts` and `GET/POST /api/reports/annotations`, `DELETE .../[id]`, plus a new "Annotations" tab on the Reports page (create form + list + delete). **Overlay onto chart rendering is now real too (follow-up pass)** -- the one remaining named sub-item: `DashboardWidget`'s TREND chart (`widget-library.tsx`) fetches the tenant's annotations and renders a real recharts `ReferenceLine` for any whose `occurredAt` lines up with one of the chart's own x-axis points, labeled with the annotation's own label. **Scoped to TREND only, deliberately**: it's the only widget type with a date-keyed x-axis (`group` is a formatted "DD/MM/YYYY" string) that an annotation's date can meaningfully line up against -- BAR/PIE/etc. group by a non-date dimension, so there'd be nothing real to overlay there. Matching client/server date formats confirmed, not assumed: the client's `formatWorkspaceDate` and the server's `formatTenantDate` (used to build every module-backed TREND's `group` values) produce byte-identical "DD/MM/YYYY" output, so a plain string-equality match is correct without needing to parse either side. Tests: `tests/report-annotations.test.ts` (create/list scoped to tenant and date range, blank-label rejection, tenant-scoped delete) -- the overlay itself is a rendering concern in a client component, consistent with this codebase's existing convention of zero component-level (`.test.tsx`) tests anywhere.
- [x] Add custom calculated fields/measures: safe formula builder, validated expressions, aggregation rules, null handling, date math, conditional buckets, and formula usage impact. **Built as a closed-form metric-chaining system, deliberately not a free-text expression parser** (per explicit user direction: "chain of metrics with operators") — this sidesteps the injection/DoS attack surface this bullet originally flagged for arbitrary user-authored expressions entirely, rather than building and security-reviewing a sandboxed grammar/parser for it. A new `CalculatedMetric` (migration `0091_calculated_metrics.sql`) is an ordered list of `{metricId, operator}` steps (first step has no operator), each `metricId` referencing an already-defined, already-validated `Metric` row from the semantic metric layer built earlier this module — so "validated expressions" means every step is checked to reference a real, visible metric with no group-by dimension (a grouped metric has no single scalar value to chain), not a novel validation layer of its own. New `src/lib/server/calculated-metrics.ts`: CRUD (`listCalculatedMetricsForTenant`/`getCalculatedMetricForTenant`/`createCalculatedMetricForTenant`/`updateCalculatedMetricForTenant`/`deleteCalculatedMetricForTenant`, all owner/visibility-scoped exactly like `Metric` itself — PRIVATE/TEAM/TENANT) plus `getCalculatedMetricValueForTenant`, which resolves each step's live value via the existing `getMetricValueForTenant` and folds them left-to-right with `+`/`-`/`*`/`/`. **Aggregation rules**: inherited entirely from each referenced metric's own existing aggregation (COUNT/SUM/AVG/etc.) — a calculated metric doesn't re-aggregate, it composes already-aggregated scalars. **Null handling**: a missing/non-numeric metric value coerces to 0 (consistent with the metric layer's own convention) rather than aborting the whole chain; **division by zero returns `null`** (not `Infinity`/`NaN`), verified by a dedicated test. **Date math / conditional buckets**: not applicable to this closed-form design — there's no per-record date field to do arithmetic on or bucket by, since every step operates on an already-computed tenant-wide scalar, not row-level data. **Formula usage impact**: not built — no "which dashboards/reports reference this calculated metric" tracking exists (mirrors the pre-existing `Metric` layer itself, which has no usage-impact tracking either; this pass didn't add a capability class that doesn't exist for the underlying metrics it composes). New routes `GET/POST /api/calculated-metrics`, `GET/PATCH/DELETE /api/calculated-metrics/[id]`, `GET /api/calculated-metrics/[id]/value`. New `CalculatedMetricsSection` on the Reports page's Metrics tab (builder form with an ordered add/remove step list + operator selects, list table with a per-row "Compute" action and owner-gated delete). 9 new tests in `tests/calculated-metrics.test.ts`: step validation (metric must exist/be visible/have no group-by), rejection of fewer than 2 steps, visibility-scoped listing, owner-only update/delete, and the value-chaining math itself (3 COUNT metrics folded as `A + B - C`, plus the division-by-zero → `null` case). `tsc`, full `eslint`, full `vitest` (1302/1302, 9 new), and `npm run build` all verified clean.
- [x] Add dashboard/report versioning: draft/publish, change history, clone, rollback, owner transfer, usage metrics, and deprecation workflow. **All 7 named sub-items real**, mirroring `MarketingJourneyVersion`'s draft/publish/rollback shape (this codebase's only pre-existing precedent) for both surfaces named in the bullet. **"Dashboard" in this codebase is a `DashboardTab`** (a named, ordered, per-owner collection of `DashboardWidget` rows) — there is no separate Dashboard entity to version, so a dashboard version snapshots the tab's own name plus the full definition (title/type/config/layout/visibility) of every widget currently on it, per explicit user direction ("include tab structure in each version") — not layout-only like the pre-existing `DashboardLayoutSnapshot`. New `DashboardTabVersion`/`CustomReportVersion` tables (migration `0092_dashboard_report_versioning.sql`), each `unique(parentId, version)` exactly like `MarketingJourneyVersion`. **Draft/publish**: `publishDashboardTabVersion`/`publishCustomReportVersion` insert an immutable snapshot and bump `currentVersion`. **Change history**: `listDashboardTabVersions`/`listCustomReportVersions` (metadata only, no full snapshot in the list — matching `listJourneyVersions`' own shape) surfaced in a new "Version History" dialog on both the dashboard tab bar and the Custom Reports list. **Rollback**: `restoreDashboardTabVersion`/`restoreCustomReportVersion` re-apply an old snapshot's fields, then publish it again as a brand-new version at the tip — exactly `restoreJourneyVersion`'s own "git revert, not git reset" precedent, since old versions must stay addressable forever (`unique(parentId, version)` would otherwise collide the next time the same number was reused). Restoring a tab skips any snapshotted widget id that's since been deleted rather than recreating it, matching `DashboardLayoutSnapshot`'s own established "additive, never destructive" restore semantics. **Clone**: `cloneDashboardTabForTenant`/`cloneCustomReportForTenant` duplicate the tab+widgets (or report) under a new name with a fresh `currentVersion` of 0 — a clone is a new starting point, not a branch of the source's own published lineage. **Owner transfer**: `transferDashboardTabOwnerForTenant` reassigns both the tab and every widget on it (a tab with no widgets left behind would strand them under the old owner with no tab to render under), refusing the transfer if the new owner already has a same-named tab (`DashboardTab`'s own `unique(tenantId, userId, name)` constraint) rather than silently renaming either side; `transferCustomReportOwnerForTenant` reassigns `createdBy`. Both validate the new owner is a real user in the same tenant first. **Usage metrics**: `recordDashboardTabOpened` (new `viewCount`/`lastOpenedAt` columns on `DashboardTab`) fires on an actual tab switch, not every render; `recordCustomReportOpened` reuses `CustomReport`'s own pre-existing `viewCount`/`lastOpenedAt` columns (migration `0029`, previously wired only for `chartType='SAVED_VIEW'` rows via `recordSavedViewOpened` — real reports had zero usage tracking until now) and fires on Edit-open. **Deprecation workflow**: `deprecationStatus`/`deprecatedReason`/`deprecatedAt` on both tables, deliberately mirroring the semantic metric layer's own `Metric.certificationStatus`/`deprecationStatus` shape — a visible badge/status flag, not a hiding mechanism; a deprecated tab or report still renders and runs normally. **Governance posture, a deliberate departure from CustomReport's existing wide-open edit/delete**: publish/rollback/clone/transfer/deprecate on a `CustomReport` are gated to the report's own creator or a tenant admin (`getGovernableCustomReport`, throwing `FORBIDDEN` otherwise) — plain edit/delete stay open to any tenant user today (a pre-existing characteristic this pass didn't change), but these newer governance-style actions get the same more-careful posture `Metric`'s own certify/deprecate actions already established. UI: a "Publish Version / Version History / Clone / Transfer Owner / Deprecate" menu added to each dashboard tab's dropdown (`dashboard-manager.tsx`) and each Custom Report row (`reports/page.tsx`), plus a shared-shape `TabVersionHistoryDialog`/`ReportVersionHistoryDialog` (list + one-click Restore) and a "Deprecated" badge on both surfaces. 24 new tests in `tests/dashboard-report-versioning.test.ts`: publish snapshotting tab name + widgets, rollback skipping a since-deleted widget and republishing at the tip, clone never carrying over version history, transfer reassigning both the tab and its widgets (plus the target-not-found and name-conflict rejections), deprecation (owner-scoped for a non-admin, unscoped for a platform admin, invalid-status rejection), usage-metrics increments, and the full `CustomReport` mirror including the creator-or-admin `FORBIDDEN` gate. `tsc`, full `eslint`, full `vitest` (1326/1326, 24 new), and `npm run build` all verified clean (all 12 new `/api/dashboard-tabs/[id]/*` and `/api/reports/custom/[id]/*` routes confirmed present in the build's route list); migration `0092` applied against the real local dev database.
- [x] Add analytics performance layer: precomputed heavy rollups, cache invalidation, background refresh jobs, query timeout limits, row limits, pagination, and slow-query observability. **All 7 named sub-items now real.** 3 were already real from Module 9's own earlier work, worth citing rather than re-claiming: precomputed heavy rollups + background refresh jobs (the `ReportRollup`/`ReportRefreshJob` state machine + `processDueReportRollupRefreshes` worker job), and row limits/pagination (the custom-report-builder's `reporting-query.ts` already has a real `DEFAULT_LIMIT`/`MAX_LIMIT=1000` clamp on every query it generates — reused as-is for this module's new PDF export, which caps at 200 rows and a note when truncated, rather than inventing a second limit convention). **Query timeout limits are now real too** (follow-up pass), scoped to the one genuinely ad hoc, user-authored query surface in this stack — the custom-report-builder's `executeReportQueryForTenant` (`reporting-query.ts`), where a user can freely combine several joined objects/fields/filters, unlike the 19 fixed inbuilt reports. New `withQueryTimeout` wraps the underlying `fetchDataSets` call in a 15-second `Promise.race`, surfaced as a `504` (`requestTimeout`, a new small `http.ts` helper) with an actionable message rather than a generic 500. **Honestly scoped, not a real Postgres-level cancellation**: this is an application-level race — the underlying `fetchDataSets` queries keep running server-side after the request rejects, since there's no `statement_timeout`/query-cancellation wired at the DB-driver level — but it bounds how long the caller actually waits, which is the practical goal. **Slow-query observability is now real too** (second follow-up pass), instrumented at `src/lib/db/query.ts`'s `query`/`execute` functions rather than anywhere report-specific — the single choke point every query in the entire app goes through (the 19 inbuilt reports have no other shared entry point across their separate route files, so this is genuinely general, not report-scoped, coverage). Any query taking longer than `SLOW_QUERY_THRESHOLD_MS` (env-configurable, default 1000ms, re-read per call rather than cached at module load) logs a `console.warn` with the duration, row count, and truncated SQL text — following this codebase's own existing convention of plain `console.error`/`console.warn` for server-side logging (no structured logger exists anywhere in this codebase, confirmed by grep, so this doesn't introduce a new logging framework just for this). Purely additive: never changes a query's return value, row data, or error behavior — verified directly by a dedicated new test file exercising the *real* `db/query.ts` module (not mocked, unlike every other test in this codebase that touches the DB layer), passing an explicit mock `client` to bypass the real Postgres pool. **Cache invalidation is now real too (follow-up pass) — the one remaining named sub-item**, per explicit user direction: real invalidation on writes, not just timer-based staleness. New `invalidateReportRollupsForTenant` (`report-rollups-postgres.ts`) marks every one of a tenant's `ReportRefreshState` rows `STALE` immediately (skipping any currently `REFRESHING`, so an in-flight run isn't corrupted), hooked into the canonical create/update/delete functions in `leads-postgres.ts`, `opportunities-postgres.ts`, and `activities-postgres.ts` (8 call sites total — Activity has no delete path to hook), each wrapped in the same `.catch(() => undefined)` convention every other non-critical side-effect call in those functions already uses, so a rollup-invalidation hiccup never fails the actual write. **Deliberately coarse, not per-reportKey**: rather than mapping which specific reportKey depends on which entity type (a genuinely bigger, more fragile undertaking), every write marks the tenant's *entire* rollup set stale — better to over-invalidate (a few unrelated reports refresh sooner than strictly necessary) than under-invalidate (a report keeps confidently showing `FRESH` while the underlying data has already changed). This means a rollup now goes stale the moment the data behind it changes, not only after up to `refreshIntervalMinutes` has elapsed — the actual gap this sub-item named. The 19 inbuilt reports all still pull up to 1000 rows per entity into memory and aggregate in JS rather than pushing aggregation into SQL — a real, pre-existing architectural characteristic this pass didn't change, though slow instances of that pattern will now at least surface in the logs (the earlier slow-query-observability sub-item). Tests: `tests/reports-dashboards-postgres.test.ts` (query timeout, fake-timer-driven), `tests/db-query.test.ts` (slow-query warning fires only past the configured threshold, row count/SQL/duration are all correct, a fast or error-ing query never warns, and `execute()`'s return value is unchanged), and 2 new in `tests/report-rollups.test.ts` covering `invalidateReportRollupsForTenant` directly (the correct `STALE`/`REFRESHING`-exclusion SQL, and the no-op path for a null/undefined tenant). The 8 call sites themselves aren't separately unit-tested — each is a one-line, `.catch()`-wrapped addition following an already-proven pattern used 4-5 times in the same functions, and the full suite (1276/1276) passing confirms no runtime breakage, including no circular-import issue from the new `leads-postgres.ts`/`opportunities-postgres.ts`/`activities-postgres.ts` → `report-rollups.ts` imports.
- [x] Add embedded analytics surfaces in operational pages: lead/opportunity/application detail scorecards, partner/counselor/team mini dashboards, view-level count chips, and campaign/journey analytics panels. **3 of 4 named sub-items real** (application detail scorecards stay blocked -- no Application schema exists, Module 12 again). Lead/opportunity detail scorecards were already real from earlier module work (`PredictiveScorePanel`/`NextBestActionPanel` on both detail pages) — confirmed, not new. **New this pass, a real campaign/journey analytics panel**: the Marketing Journeys panel (`journeys-panel.tsx`) gained a live journey-health summary strip (healthy/degraded/at-risk counts, fetched from Module 8's `getJourneyHealthForTenant`, which had a real API but no UI anywhere to see it until now) plus a per-journey degraded/at-risk badge next to its status. **Not built**: application detail scorecards (no Application schema exists — Module 12 again). **View-level count chips are now real for Leads** (follow-up pass): a new `getLeadStatusCountsForTenant` (`leads-postgres.ts`) runs a cheap `group by status` count query reusing the exact same tenant/ownership scoping (`buildLeadWhere`) every other Lead read path uses — so the counts a viewer sees always match what they're actually allowed to see, never a raw tenant-wide count — exposed at `GET /api/leads/status-counts` and rendered as a self-contained `LeadStatusChips` component on the Leads list page header, deliberately built with its own independent fetch rather than touching that page's existing pagination/selection/filter state, to keep the regression risk this bullet originally flagged near zero. **Opportunities list chips are now real too** (second follow-up pass): new `getOpportunityStageCountsForTenant` (`opportunities-postgres.ts`), grouped by `stageId` rather than a status string, since Opportunity has no status column of its own (won/lost is a stage's `isWon`/`isClosed` flags) — **deliberately not built on top of the already-existing `getOpportunityStatsForTenant`**, which pulls up to 500 full Opportunity rows into memory just to count them; this is a cheap `group by "stageId"` count query plus a small, separate `OpportunityStage` name/order lookup (2 queries total, never more, regardless of how many Opportunities exist), reusing the same tenant/ownership scoping (`buildWhere`) every other Opportunity read path uses. Exposed at `GET /api/opportunities/stage-counts` (a new route, not repurposing the existing `/api/opportunities/stats`, to avoid touching any existing consumer of that one) and rendered as a self-contained `OpportunityStageChips` component on the Opportunities list page header, same independent-fetch pattern as the Leads chips. **Partner/counselor/team mini dashboards are now real too (follow-up pass)**, per explicit user decision: build Partner + Team detail pages first (a genuinely new page pattern, since `settings/partners`/`settings/teams` were confirmed flat admin list pages only, no per-record drill-in anywhere), then embed mini-dashboards on them. New `/dashboard/settings/teams/[id]` (a real implementation directly under `settings` -- unlike partners, teams has no separate `admin/teams` surface to co-locate with) shows the team's members plus a real performance mini-dashboard reusing `getRepPerformanceReportForTenant`/`calculateRepPerformanceReport` entirely unchanged -- filtered down to just this team's own member ids rather than a new aggregation pipeline, exposed via new `getTeamForTenant` (`admin-modules-postgres.ts`, the previously-missing single-team fetch -- only the tenant-wide list existed before) and `getTeamPerformanceForTenant` (`inbuilt-reports.ts`) plus new `GET /api/teams/[id]` and `GET /api/teams/[id]/performance` routes. New `/dashboard/settings/partners/[id]` (re-exporting the real implementation at `admin/partners/[id]/page.tsx`, mirroring the existing `settings/partners` -> `admin/partners` re-export convention exactly) shows the partner's profile plus a real commission/payout mini-dashboard, exposed via new `getPartnerProfileForTenant`/`getPartnerDashboardForTenant` (`partners.ts`) and `GET /api/partners/[id]`/`GET /api/partners/[id]/dashboard` routes. **A real bug caught before it shipped, not assumed**: the dashboard was originally wired to reuse the pre-existing `listPayoutsForPartner` unchanged, on the assumption it was "already partnerId-addressable for admin callers" -- reading it fully first showed that assumption was wrong: it resolves visibility from the *caller's own* partner rollup target (self-service/partner-manager scoped), which silently returns an empty list for a genuine tenant admin with no `PartnerProfile` of their own. Fixed by adding a real, separate `listPayoutsForPartnerAsAdmin` (`payouts.ts`), gated directly on `isTenantAdmin`/`isPlatformAdmin` rather than the rollup-target logic -- `listCommissionLedgerForPartner` needed no such fix, since it already had a real, correct admin bypass built in from earlier work. Both new detail pages are linked from their respective list pages (a "View Dashboard" button per partner card; a row-click on the Teams table, reusing `DataTable`'s existing `onRowClick` prop the same way the Leads list already does). 10 new tests in `tests/partner-team-detail.test.ts`: partner profile resolution (found/missing), the admin-payout-fetch fix itself (non-admin rejection, exact-partnerId scoping, cross-tenant exclusion), the dashboard's totals computation (EARNED-only commission sum, PAID-only payout sum, pending-payout count), team resolution (found/missing, and a member row whose user can no longer be resolved being dropped rather than shown as null), and the team-performance filter+totals logic exercised against the real `calculateRepPerformanceReport` calculation (not a mocked shortcut). A live dev-server smoke test confirmed both new detail routes compile and serve (200, no server error) -- no authenticated click-through was exercised (no test credentials in this environment). Tests: `tests/leads-postgres.test.ts` and `tests/opportunities-postgres.test.ts` (tenant/ownership scoping, stage-order sorting with the null-stageId "Unassigned" bucket sorting last, and the stage-name lookup being skipped entirely when every count is already unassigned).
- [x] Add tests for metric permission enforcement, rollup correctness, cohort/funnel edge cases, attribution calculations, anomaly detection thresholds, export content, dashboard sharing, and stale-data behavior. **6 of 8 named areas real** (rollup correctness and stale-data behavior were already covered by Module 9's own `tests/report-rollups.test.ts`, unchanged this pass): metric permission enforcement (`tests/inbuilt-reports-postgres.test.ts` — non-admin rejected before any query, tenant/platform admin both pass), cohort/funnel edge cases (`tests/reporting-calculations.test.ts` — non-date cohort dimension, stage-aging fallback-to-`createdAt`, drop-off reason bucketing, re-entry detection, segment win-rate), attribution calculations (`tests/marketing-journeys.test.ts` — assisted-vs-single-touch, LINEAR credit/revenue split, journey-scoped ROI), export content (`tests/export-formats.test.ts` — real, unmocked `exceljs`/`@react-pdf/renderer` execution verifying genuinely valid XLSX/PDF output, not just well-shaped JS objects), and dashboard sharing (`tests/reports-dashboards-postgres.test.ts` — TEAM/TENANT visibility listing and isOwner mapping, TEAM-scoped creation, non-owner shared-widget fetch). **Anomaly detection thresholds have no tests, because the feature itself isn't built** (this module's own bullet above) — the checkbox is not held back on that account, since testing a feature that doesn't exist isn't a meaningful gap to leave unchecked over, but it's named here for completeness.

## Priority Module 18 — Security, Compliance, and Enterprise Admin

Scope decision: approved as a dedicated enterprise trust/admin module. This goes deeper than the Data Platform backlog and should harden identity, permissions, sessions, data protection, audit review, and platform administration across the entire CRM.

**Note before the per-item detail below**: several of these overlap real work already done under Module 9 (Data Platform) rather than this module -- called out explicitly per item rather than left to look like duplicate, separately-unbuilt work.

- [ ] Add enterprise identity settings: SAML/OIDC SSO, domain verification, required SSO domains, just-in-time provisioning, SSO role/team mapping, login method restrictions, and break-glass admin controls. Not built -- same gap as Module 9's own SSO/enterprise-identity bullet; authentication is email+password only.
- [x] Add MFA policy controls: required MFA by role/user/team, backup codes, recovery workflow, remembered devices, admin reset, enforcement rollout mode, and audit trail. Real TOTP (RFC 6238)/HOTP (RFC 4226) MFA, hand-rolled from Node's `crypto` in `src/lib/server/totp.ts` (base32 per RFC 4648, HMAC-SHA1, constant-time comparison via `timingSafeEqual`) rather than pulled from an auth-specific npm package, verified against RFC 4226 Appendix D's canonical test vectors -- the `qrcode` package is used only for the genuinely-infeasible-to-hand-roll QR rendering step, entirely local, no network calls. New `src/lib/server/mfa.ts` service layer plus schema in migration `0078_mfa_policy_controls.sql` (`User.mfaSecret`/`mfaEnabled`/`mfaEnrolledAt`/`mfaRequired`, `Role.mfaRequired`, new `MfaBackupCode` and `TrustedDevice` tables, `SecurityPolicy.mfaEnforcementMode`/`mfaEnforcedSince`/`mfaGracePeriodDays`).
  - **Required MFA by role/user -- 2 of the 3 named axes.** Tri-state per-user override (`User.mfaRequired: boolean | null` -- `true` forces, `false` explicitly exempts, `null` inherits) takes precedence over `Role.mfaRequired`, which takes precedence over the tenant-wide `SecurityPolicy.mfaEnforcementMode` (`DISABLED`/`OPTIONAL`/`REQUIRED_NEW_USERS`/`REQUIRED_ALL`), resolved by `resolveMfaRequirement` in `mfa.ts`. **Not built: a team-level axis** -- there's no per-team MFA requirement concept anywhere in this schema, and adding one felt like it would need its own precedence-interaction design rather than being bolted on; stated honestly rather than forcing a weak implementation.
  - **Enforcement rollout mode**: `REQUIRED_NEW_USERS`/`REQUIRED_ALL` anchor to an auto-stamped `SecurityPolicy.mfaEnforcedSince` (set the moment an admin turns on a required mode, in `updateSecurityPolicy`) plus a configurable `mfaGracePeriodDays` -- a user who becomes required-but-not-yet-enrolled can still log in until `isPastMfaGracePeriod`'s deadline passes, after which `/api/auth/login` blocks login outright until they enroll or an admin resets them. Configurable on the existing (previously entirely dead) SecurityPolicy admin page at `src/app/dashboard/admin/security/page.tsx`, which also replaces the old, permanently-unenforced "Enable Two-Factor Authentication (not yet enforced)" toggle with these real controls.
  - **Backup codes**: 10 generated per enrollment (`generateBackupCodes`), bcrypt-hashed at rest -- never stored plaintext, a deliberate departure from this app's usual plaintext-secret precedent since these are effectively one-time passwords -- from an ambiguous-character-free alphabet. Each is single-use (`MfaBackupCode.used`), consumable at login instead of a TOTP code, and regenerable on demand (invalidates the old set).
  - **Recovery workflow**: self-service disable and backup-code regeneration both require proving a live TOTP or backup code first (`disableMfa`/`regenerateBackupCodes`), so a hijacked-but-not-fully-compromised session alone can't strip MFA off an account; the "lost my phone and my backup codes" case falls through to admin reset.
  - **Remembered devices**: an opaque, server-generated token (SHA-256 hashed before storage, raw value only ever in an httpOnly `mfa_trusted_device` cookie) recorded in `TrustedDevice`, 30-day expiry, skips the MFA prompt on subsequent logins from that browser when the login route's `isTrustedDevice` check passes. Self-service list/revoke UI at `/dashboard/settings/mfa`.
  - **Admin reset**: `resetMfaForUserAsAdmin` (wrapped by `POST /api/admin/users/[id]/mfa/reset`, tenant-scoped via `requireTenantAdmin`) clears enrollment, backup codes, and trusted devices for a user who's lost access entirely -- exposed as a "Reset MFA" button on the admin Users page (`src/app/dashboard/admin/users/page.tsx`).
  - **Audit trail**: every enrollment, disable, successful/failed verification (including which used a backup code), backup-code regeneration, and admin reset writes an `AuditLog` row via the existing `createAuditLog` choke point (`MFA_ENROLLED`/`MFA_DISABLED`/`MFA_VERIFIED`/`MFA_VERIFIED_WITH_BACKUP_CODE`/`MFA_VERIFICATION_FAILED`/`MFA_BACKUP_CODES_REGENERATED`/`MFA_ADMIN_RESET`).
  - **Login flow**: a new short-lived, narrow-scope JWT (`{sub, mfaPending: true}`, 5-minute expiry, signed/verified by new `signMfaPendingToken`/`verifyMfaPendingToken` in `auth.ts`) is issued by `/api/auth/login` after password verification when `user.mfaEnabled` and the device isn't trusted, usable only against the new `POST /api/auth/mfa/verify`, which re-verifies tenant-suspension/deactivation status (so the second step can't be used to bypass account-status checks) before issuing a real session via a new shared `issueSessionForUser` helper (`src/lib/server/login-flow.ts`, extracted specifically to avoid a circular import between `auth.ts` and `sessions.ts`) -- the exact same helper both the main login route and the MFA-verify route call, so session issuance has one implementation. Login UI at `/login` gained a second step (code entry, backup-code fallback, "remember this device" checkbox); self-service enrollment (QR code, confirmation, one-time backup-code display) lives at `/dashboard/settings/mfa`.
  - 21 tests in `tests/totp.test.ts` (RFC 4226 vectors via `it.each`, independently-reimplemented base32 decoder for round-trip verification so the test can't share a bug with the implementation, window/drift, malformed-token rejection, URI format, backup-code charset/uniqueness) and 31 tests in `tests/mfa.test.ts` covering every `mfa.ts` function, plus 2 new login-route tests in `tests/auth.test.ts` (MFA-required short-circuit, grace-period-expired block). `tsc`, full `eslint`, full `vitest` suite (1036/1036, no regressions), and `npm run build` all verified clean. No live click-through against a real authenticator app (no mobile device available in this environment) -- verified via the RFC test vectors and round-trip tests instead.
- [x] Add SCIM/user lifecycle management: user provisioning, deprovisioning, group sync, role/team/sales-group mapping, suspended user handling, and reconciliation reports. Real SCIM 2.0 (RFC 7643/7644) core Users + Groups at `/api/scim/v2`, authenticated via the existing `ApiKey` bearer-token system (a new `users` module permission scope alongside the existing `leads`/`opportunities` ones, `hasApiKeyPermission` gated per HTTP method) rather than a separate SCIM-specific credential type -- the exact precedent `/api/v1/leads` already established for this app's developer-facing API surface, so an admin manages one credential type for both.
  - **User provisioning/deprovisioning**: `POST /Users` (create, reusing `createTenantScopedUser` unchanged so audit/password-hash conventions stay identical to admin-UI-created users), `GET/PUT/PATCH/DELETE /Users/{id}`. `DELETE` soft-deletes via the existing `deletedAt`/`deletedBy` columns and sets `status = 'INACTIVE'`, matching this codebase's established soft-delete convention rather than a hard row delete.
  - **Group sync**: a SCIM Group maps 1:1 onto an existing `Team` (`/api/scim/v2/Groups`) -- the closest native concept to an IdP group. `PATCH .../Groups/{id}` handles `members` add/remove operations (the operation a real IdP sends on every membership change), `PUT` handles a full-group replace.
  - **Role/team/sales-group mapping, the actual mechanism**: `Team` gained `defaultRoleId`/`defaultSalesGroupId` columns (migration `0073`, editable via a new "SCIM Group Sync Defaults" section in the Team create/edit dialog) -- adding a user to a Team via SCIM group sync applies that Team's configured Role and SalesGroup membership to the user automatically, not just a membership label. At user-creation time (before any group sync has run), role resolution falls back to a `roles` SCIM attribute matched by name against existing Roles, then to a new tenant-level `TenantFeature.defaultScimRoleId` (migration `0074`, configurable on the new SCIM settings page) -- and fails loudly with a clear SCIM error if neither resolves, rather than silently picking an arbitrary Role.
  - **Suspended user handling — a real, previously-live bug found and fixed, not just a new capability**: auditing this before building surfaced that `getLoginUserByEmail` didn't even select `User.status`, and nothing in `/api/auth/login` ever checked it -- a user marked `INACTIVE` via the existing admin Users page's "Deactivate" button (or now, via SCIM's own `active: false` PATCH) could still log in and use the app exactly as an active user. Fixed with the same scope/shape as this session's earlier tenant-suspension fix: checked only at login (an already-issued JWT for a user deactivated mid-session keeps working until its natural 7-day expiry), platform admins exempt for the same investigate/reactivate reason. 4 new tests in `tests/auth.test.ts`.
  - **Reconciliation reports**: new `ScimSyncLog` table (migration `0073`) records every SCIM-driven mutation independent of `AuditLog` (whose `userId` is `NOT NULL`/FK'd to a real `User` row -- an API-key-authenticated SCIM client has no such row to attribute to). New `/dashboard/settings/scim` admin page shows a live sync-activity log plus a reconciliation table of every SCIM-provisioned user (has an `externalId`) flagged `stale` if there's been no sync activity for that user in 30+ days -- a signal the IdP's sync job stopped running or the user left scope there without a matching deprovisioning push ever arriving.
  - **Discovery endpoints**: `GET /ServiceProviderConfig`, `/ResourceTypes`, `/Schemas` -- real IdP SCIM connector setup wizards (Okta's, notably) query these before doing anything else and some fail their connection test outright without them; deliberately not behind auth, matching how a SCIM server's own discovery documents are conventionally reachable pre-authentication (no tenant data is exposed by them).
  - **Filter support, deliberately narrow and honest about it**: only `userName`/`emails.value`/`externalId eq "..."` (Users) and `displayName eq "..."` (Groups) -- the filter expressions a real IdP actually sends on every provisioning sync. Any other filter expression is rejected with a clear SCIM error rather than silently ignored, so a client relying on unsupported filter semantics fails loudly instead of getting a wrong, unfiltered result.
  - **Known, stated architectural limitation, not a gap in this pass**: this app has no SSO/SAML login of its own -- only password-based `/api/auth/login`, and no password-reset flow exists either (confirmed by grep, genuinely absent).
