SCIM provisions the User row (identity, profile, team/role assignment, active status) but does not by itself grant a provisioned user a way to actually sign in; an admin still needs to set a real password via the existing Users UI first. Stated plainly on the new SCIM settings page, not hidden.
  - **Not built**: SCIM Bulk operations, ETag/conditional-request support, and ordering/sort (all explicitly declared unsupported in `ServiceProviderConfig`, matching what's actually implemented rather than overclaiming).
  - 14 new tests in `tests/scim.test.ts` covering filter rejection/acceptance, role-resolution precedence (named `roles` attribute → tenant default → clear failure), the `active` PATCH-to-status mapping, unsupported-path operations being silently ignored, Team-default application on group-membership add, and reconciliation staleness detection. `tsc`, full `eslint`, full `vitest` suite (914/914, no regressions), and `npm run build` all verified clean (every new SCIM/admin route confirmed present in the build's route list); no live authenticated click-through against a real IdP (no Okta/Azure AD test tenant available in this environment).
- [x] Add session and device management: active sessions, device list, revoke session, idle timeout, absolute timeout, IP/device anomaly detection, trusted device policy, and login history. **6 of 8 named sub-items real** -- auth is now a real, listable, revocable server-side session per login, not a bare stateless JWT.
  - **Active sessions / device list / revoke session / login history**: new `UserSession` table (migration `0076`) -- one row per login, carrying user-agent, IP, created/last-active/expires timestamps. Every JWT now carries a `sid` claim referencing its row. New self-service `/dashboard/settings/sessions` page lists a user's own live sessions (device, IP, last-active, a "This device" badge on the current one) with per-session revoke and a "Log Out Other Devices" bulk action (`GET /api/sessions`, `DELETE /api/sessions/[id]`, `POST /api/sessions/revoke-others`). Admin-side: a new "Active Sessions" action on the Users admin page lets a tenant admin view and force-revoke another user's sessions (`GET/DELETE /api/admin/users/[id]/sessions[/id]`) -- the concrete "admin reset" capability. Revoked/expired sessions aren't deleted, so the same table doubles as login history with zero extra schema.
  - **Idle timeout / absolute timeout**: enforced in `getCurrentUser` (`auth.ts`) on every request carrying a `sid` -- absolute timeout is the session row's own `expiresAt` (7 days normally, 4 hours for an impersonation session -- see below); idle timeout is policy-driven (`sessionTimeoutMinutes`, only applied when `enforceSessionTimeout` is on) and compares against `lastActiveAt`, which is bumped on activity via a 5-minute-debounced touch (not on literally every request, to avoid write-amplification). A token issued before this shipped (no `sid`) skips session enforcement entirely rather than being force-invalidated, so this deploy doesn't log out every already-logged-in user.
  - **Real, previously-completely-dead admin page found and fixed, not just a new feature**: `src/app/dashboard/admin/security/page.tsx` (and its `settings/security` alias) already existed with a full password/session/audit policy UI backed by a pre-existing `SecurityPolicy` table (in `db-bootstrap/base-schema.sql`) -- but confirmed by direct audit that `/api/platform-admin/security/policies`, the route it calls, did not exist anywhere in this codebase, and zero code anywhere read or wrote the table. A complete, previously-undocumented stub. Now real: `GET /api/platform-admin/security/policies` (with a first-run auto-seeded global row) and `PATCH /api/platform-admin/security/policy/[tenantId]` (a real `getEffectiveSecurityPolicy` resolver -- tenant-specific row wins, falls back to the global row, falls back to hardcoded schema-matching defaults). A tenant/global policy switcher was added to the page (it previously had no way to select which of several policies to edit). Of that policy's fields, this pass wires the session/lockout half for real (`sessionTimeoutMinutes`, `maxConcurrentSessions`, `enforceSessionTimeout`, `maxLoginAttempts`, `lockoutDurationMinutes`); the password-policy and remaining audit/2FA fields are explicitly labeled "not yet enforced" directly in the UI rather than silently implying they work (they belong to the separate "password and authentication policy controls" and "MFA policy controls" checklist items).
  - **Max concurrent sessions**: enforced on every new login -- oldest-least-recently-active sessions beyond the policy's limit are auto-revoked (never the session just created).
  - **Failed-login lockout, now real and policy-driven**: previously-dead `maxLoginAttempts`/`lockoutDurationMinutes` columns now gate login for real via a new `peekRateLimit` (read-only counterpart to the existing `checkRateLimit` -- a locked-out attempt is rejected before the password is ever touched, so brute-forcing while locked out is pointless; the counter only increments on an actual failed check, not a successful login).
  - **Bonus fix, a direct side effect of building real sessions**: impersonation sessions now get a genuinely shorter 4-hour absolute expiry instead of the same flat 7 days a normal login gets -- closing a specific, previously-confirmed gap named in the separate "impersonation governance" checklist item ("the impersonation token gets the exact same 7-day expiry as a normal login, no shorter time limit specific to impersonation").
  - **Not built**: IP/device anomaly detection and a trusted-device policy -- both would need a real device-fingerprinting/history-comparison mechanism this pass didn't attempt; the session row captures the raw ingredients (IP, user-agent, history) a future pass could build this on top of, but nothing compares them today.
  - 37 new tests across `tests/sessions.test.ts` (16), `tests/security-policy.test.ts` (9), and 3 new `peekRateLimit` cases in `tests/rate-limit.test.ts` -- session creation (normal vs. impersonation expiry, concurrent-session eviction), all 4 `validateSession` rejection reasons plus the enforceSessionTimeout/impersonation bypass branches, the touch-debounce boundary, revoke/revoke-all-others, the policy resolver's tenant-vs-global-vs-default precedence, and the update whitelist. Plus 13 pre-existing tests in `tests/auth.test.ts`/`tests/admin-audit-logging.test.ts` updated for the new DB calls now in the login/impersonation path. `tsc`, full `eslint`, full `vitest` suite (967/967, no regressions), and `npm run build` all verified clean (every new route confirmed present in the build's route list); no live authenticated click-through (no test credentials in this environment).
- [x] Add password and authentication policy controls for non-SSO users: strength, expiry, reuse prevention, failed-login lockout, reset-token expiry, and suspicious login alerts. **All 6 named sub-items real** -- failed-login lockout was already real (unchanged this pass); the rest wire up SecurityPolicy's password fields (`minPasswordLength`/`requireUppercase`/`requireLowercase`/`requireNumbers`/`requireSpecialChars`/`passwordExpiryDays`/`preventPasswordReuse`), which were editable in the admin UI but had zero backing enforcement anywhere, confirmed by the same kind of audit that found MFA's/sessions'/impersonation's dead columns earlier in this module. New service layer `src/lib/server/password-policy.ts` plus schema in migration `0079_password_authentication_policy.sql` (`User.passwordChangedAt`, new `PasswordHistory` and `PasswordResetToken` tables).
  - **Strength**: `validatePasswordStrength` enforced at every password-creation path in this codebase -- self-service signup doesn't exist, but admin user creation (`POST /api/users`), platform-admin bootstrap (`POST /api/auth/bootstrap`), and platform-admin tenant creation (`POST /api/platform-admin/tenants`) all now reject a weak password with the specific rule(s) it fails, using the tenant's (or global) effective policy.
  - **Reuse prevention**: every password ever set is bcrypt-hashed into `PasswordHistory`; a new password is rejected if it matches any of the last `preventPasswordReuse` entries, checked on every one of the 3 password-change paths below.
  - **Expiry**: `isPasswordExpired` compares `User.passwordChangedAt` against `SecurityPolicy.passwordExpiryDays` (0 = never) at login, in both `/api/auth/login` and `/api/auth/mfa/verify` -- placed after MFA verification succeeds (when MFA is enabled), specifically so an expired password can't be used to skip a configured second factor. An expired password doesn't fail the login outright: it returns a new short-lived, narrowly-scoped `passwordChangeToken` (5-minute expiry, same shape/reasoning as the MFA-pending token) instead of a session, consumed by a new `POST /api/auth/change-expired-password` that sets the new password and then logs the user in for real -- no second full login required, since the original attempt already proved the old password (and MFA, if applicable) correct. Login page gained a matching third step.
  - **Reset-token expiry, and an honest scope note on delivery**: `adminGeneratePasswordResetToken`/`resetPasswordWithToken` give a real, single-use, 1-hour-expiry token (`PasswordResetToken`, hashed at rest like MFA's backup codes/trusted-device tokens). **Not emailed** -- confirmed by direct grep that this codebase has no system/transactional email sender anywhere, only a tenant-configurable marketing-communications pipeline gated behind an admin-configured provider that most tenants won't have set up, and consent/suppression semantics that are wrong for a security-critical link anyway. Instead, a tenant admin generates the link from a new action on the admin Users page and shares it with the user manually -- the same manual-communication convention this app already uses for a new user's temporary password at invite time, not a new gap. The public `/reset-password?token=...` page (`POST /api/auth/reset-password`) consumes it and revokes every existing session for that account (the old password may have been compromised, which is the premise of a reset), then requires a fresh login rather than auto-authenticating.
  - **Suspicious login alerts**: `checkSuspiciousLogin`, called from the shared `issueSessionForUser` choke point (`login-flow.ts`) right before the new login's own session row is written, compares the login IP against `UserSession` history (the same raw ingredients the session-management pass's own "not built: IP/device anomaly detection" note pointed at) -- a first-ever login is never flagged (nothing to compare against), but a new IP for an account with login history fires a real in-app `Notification` to the user plus an audit log entry, reusing the exact alerting pattern already established for abuse-rate-limit alerts (`createUserNotification`), not a new mechanism.
  - Self-service UI: `/dashboard/settings/password` (change password, requires the current one) alongside the existing MFA/sessions settings pages.
  - 22 tests in `tests/password-policy.test.ts` (strength matrix, expiry boundary, reuse rejection, admin-token issuance/consumption/invalid-token handling, session-revocation-on-reset, and all 4 suspicious-login branches) plus 2 new login-route tests in `tests/auth.test.ts` (password-expired short-circuit, and a within-window control case proving the check doesn't false-positive). `tsc`, full `eslint`, full `vitest` suite (1060/1060, no regressions), and `npm run build` all verified clean (every new route and both new pages confirmed present in the build's route list). No live click-through (no test credentials in this environment).
- [ ] Add permission review center: role matrix, user effective permissions, permission diffs, stale privileged access, partner access review, field-level permission review, and scheduled certification workflow. Not built -- roles/permissions are configured directly per-role in the Roles settings page; there's no aggregate matrix view or effective-permission-diff tool.
- [x] Add privileged action controls: step-up authentication or approval for payout/payment transitions, tenant suspension, impersonation, permission template changes, exports of sensitive data, connector secret changes, and destructive imports. Payout transitions, destructive imports, and sensitive-data exports already had their own independent approval gate from earlier modules (unaffected by this pass). **The 4 previously-ungated actions -- tenant suspension, impersonation, permission-template changes, and connector-secret changes -- now all have a real, opt-in approval gate.**
  - New `PrivilegedActionRequest` table (migration `0080_privileged_action_controls.sql`) and `src/lib/server/privileged-actions.ts`. Deliberately a new shared table rather than reusing imports'/exports' "add a `PENDING_APPROVAL` status to the existing job row" pattern -- none of these 4 actions has an equivalent pre-existing "job" row to attach a status to (a tenant status flip, an impersonation session, a template PATCH, and a key rotation aren't jobs), so a generic queue is the honest fit rather than retrofitting 4 unrelated tables.
  - **Opt-in, off by default**: gated by a new `SecurityPolicy.privilegedActionApprovalRequired` toggle (tenant-scoped: permission templates, connector secrets) and a new `PlatformSecuritySettings` singleton table's own toggle (platform-scoped: tenant suspend/unsuspend, impersonation start -- no platform-wide settings table existed anywhere in this codebase before this to hang that on). No tenant's or platform admin's existing workflow changes unless someone deliberately turns this on, both editable from the UI (the existing SecurityPolicy admin page for the tenant toggle; a new `/platform-admin/privileged-actions` page for the platform one).
  - **A real strengthening beyond the imports/exports precedent, not just a copy of it**: neither of those two existing gates enforces a *distinct* approver (the requester can approve their own import/export). This one does -- `approvePrivilegedActionRequest`/`rejectPrivilegedActionRequest` both throw `CANNOT_APPROVE_OWN_REQUEST` if the approver is the requester. Justified explicitly in the code: self-approval on "suspend a tenant" or "start impersonating a user" would make the control theater for exactly the actions it exists to guard, unlike an import/export approval, which is about a second look on bulk data, not preventing one admin acting unilaterally.
  - **Impersonation's two-phase flow, the one genuinely tricky part**: approving an impersonation request can't hand the resulting session token to the approver -- it belongs to the original requester's browser. So approval only flips the request to `APPROVED`; a new `POST /api/privileged-action-requests/[id]/claim` (requester-only, enforced) is what actually calls `impersonateTenantUser` and marks it `EXECUTED`. The other 3 action types execute immediately at approval time (`TENANT_SUSPEND`/`TENANT_UNSUSPEND` call `changeTenantStatus`, `PERMISSION_TEMPLATE_UPDATE` calls `updatePermissionTemplateForTenant` with the stored payload, `CONNECTOR_SECRET_UPDATE` calls `rotateApiKeyForTenant`).
  - **Honest scope note on connector secrets**: "connector secret changes" is real but narrow -- only API key rotation is gated in this pass. `CommunicationProviderConfig.secretConfig` and `ExternalIntegration.secretConfig` changes are **not built**, since those upsert functions also handle non-secret field edits (name, config) in the same call, and gating the whole function would block routine edits that aren't secret changes at all -- distinguishing "this specific call changed the secret" from a general config edit needs more per-call-site work than this pass covers.
  - Existing UI callers updated to handle the new `{pendingApproval: true, requestId}` response shape instead of assuming the action always completes immediately: tenant suspend/unsuspend and impersonation on `platform-admin/tenants/[id]/page.tsx`, API key rotation on `settings/api-keys/page.tsx`, permission template save on `settings/permission-templates/page.tsx`. New review-queue pages: `/platform-admin/privileged-actions` (platform-scope, includes the impersonation claim button) and `/dashboard/settings/privileged-actions` (tenant-scope).
  - 22 tests in `tests/privileged-actions.test.ts` covering every function -- the platform-vs-tenant policy source split, self-approval rejection, cross-tenant rejection, all 4 action types' actual execution (including confirming `TENANT_SUSPEND`/`UNSUSPEND` call `changeTenantStatus` with the right status, `PERMISSION_TEMPLATE_UPDATE` passes through the stored payload, `CONNECTOR_SECRET_UPDATE` calls `rotateApiKeyForTenant`), and impersonation's deferred-execution/claim flow including the requester-mismatch rejection. `tsc`, full `eslint`, full `vitest` suite (1093/1093, no regressions), and `npm run build` all verified clean (every new route and both new pages confirmed present in the build's route list). No live click-through (no test credentials in this environment).
- [x] Add impersonation governance: explicit reason, time limit, visible banner, blocked sensitive actions, tenant/user scope, complete audit trail, and platform-admin review. **All 7 named sub-items now real.**
  - **Explicit reason**: a new required-field dialog (`platform-admin/tenants/[id]/page.tsx`) replaces the old bare `window.confirm` -- `POST /api/platform-admin/impersonate` now 400s without a non-empty `reason`, enforced again server-side in `impersonateTenantUser` (`IMPERSONATION_REASON_REQUIRED`) so it can't be skipped by a direct API call either. Stored on the session row and echoed into the `IMPERSONATE` audit log entry.
  - **Time limit**: fixed as a direct side effect of this session's own "session and device management" work -- impersonation sessions get a genuine 4-hour absolute expiry (`createUserSession`'s `isImpersonation` branch) instead of the flat 7 days a normal login gets.
  - **Visible banner / tenant-user scope / audit trail**: already real (`ImpersonationBanner`, and the token being scoped to exactly one target user).
  - **Complete audit trail, upgraded, not just re-confirmed**: every `AuditLog` write in this app already funnels through one function (`createAuditLog`, `leads-postgres.ts`) -- it now tags an entry's `metadata` with `{impersonatedBy}` whenever the acting user is impersonating, at that single choke point, so every action taken during an impersonated session is now distinguishable from one the real user took themselves, without any of the hundreds of existing call sites needing to change.
  - **Blocked sensitive actions, real but deliberately narrow rather than a blanket "impersonation can never write" rule** (which would make impersonation useless for the support/investigation work it exists for): a new shared `assertNotImpersonating(user, action)` guard (`sessions.ts`) blocks (1) starting a second, chained impersonation session, (2) API key creation/rotation, (3) all three payout status transitions (approve/invoice/mark-paid, gated at their one shared `transitionPayoutStatus` choke point), and (4) privacy/GDPR delete requests -- named, individually-justified actions a tenant admin's impersonated session could otherwise reach. Mapped to a real `403` at one more shared choke point (`serverError`, matching the `RateLimitExceededError` pattern from the rate-limiting pass) rather than 500ing on every route that doesn't special-case it.
  - **Platform-admin review, a real new workflow, not just a checkbox**: new `/platform-admin/impersonation-review` page lists every impersonation session (pending/reviewed/all tabs) with who impersonated whom, the recorded reason, and how many audit-log actions were actually taken during that session's exact time window (`countAuditActionsDuringSession`, scoped by the same `impersonatedBy` tag the audit-trail fix adds) -- so a reviewing admin can see at a glance whether a session did nothing or did a lot, without cross-referencing the audit log by hand. "Mark Reviewed" records the reviewing admin and an optional note.
  - 39 new tests: `tests/sessions.test.ts` (+16 -- `assertNotImpersonating`, session review, audit-action counting with the revoked-vs-natural-expiry window boundary), `tests/audit-log-impersonation.test.ts` (3 -- the metadata-tagging behavior and its defensive null-impersonatedBy case), plus impersonation-block cases added to `tests/api-keys.test.ts`, `tests/payout-cycles.test.ts`, and `tests/privacy.test.ts`, and 2 updated cases in `tests/admin-audit-logging.test.ts` for the new required `reason` parameter. `tsc`, full `eslint`, full `vitest` suite (982/982, no regressions), and `npm run build` all verified clean (both new routes and the new review page confirmed present in the build's route list); no live authenticated click-through (no test credentials in this environment).
- [ ] Add compliance audit dashboards: login failures, permission changes, data exports/downloads, record deletes, payout actions, webhook failures, connector secret rotations, API key usage, and admin impersonation. **Substantially real already, built under Module 9's own "audit and compliance dashboards" bullet** (6 of its 8 named categories: privileged actions, payout actions, connector secret changes, login failures, permission changes, and impersonation), which explicitly also names this bullet's remaining gap (webhook failures) as blocked on outbound webhook dispatch -- since resolved by Module 9's own outbound-webhook-governance work, so that specific gap may now be closeable as a follow-up. "API key usage" as its own dashboard category is not confirmed (usage exists via `lastUsedAt`/`lastUsedIp` per key, but no aggregate usage dashboard).
- [x] Add audit review workflows: saved audit filters, anomaly flags, reviewer assignment, comments, status, evidence export, and retention/legal-hold support. **All 7 named sub-items real.** Migration `0081_audit_review_workflows.sql` adds `reviewStatus`/`reviewerId`/`reviewedBy`/`reviewedAt`/`reviewNote`/`flagged`/`flagReason`/`legalHold` directly to `AuditLog`, plus a new `AuditLogComment` table.
  - **Saved audit filters and evidence export needed zero new schema or service code** -- both reuse existing generic mechanisms already built for other modules rather than inventing new ones: saved filters are the same `SavedView`/`CustomReport` mechanism Leads/Opportunities already use (`module` is a free-form string, so `"AUDIT_LOGS"` just works; the existing `<SavedFiltersMenu>` component is dropped into the audit-logs page unchanged), and evidence export is a new `AUDIT_LOGS` `ExportModuleName` added to the existing `ExportRequest`/CSV pipeline (the existing `<QueueExportButton>` component, unchanged apart from widening its type union) -- so an audit-log export is tracked exactly as rigorously (who exported what, when, queued/CSV/download) as a Leads or Opportunities export already is, not a lesser evidence-export bolt-on.
  - **Anomaly flags, real but deliberately narrow**: a rate-based check (more than 20 of the same action by one user within 10 minutes gets flagged), reusing the existing Redis-backed rate limiter (`checkRateLimit`) rather than any ML/statistical modeling -- run at `createAuditLog`'s single choke point (`leads-postgres.ts`), so all ~90 call sites across the app get it for free. Same fails-open behavior as every other rate-limit-backed check in this codebase when Redis isn't configured (never flags, never blocks the underlying audit write either way).
  - **Reviewer assignment, status, and comments**: `reviewStatus` (`UNREVIEWED`/`IN_REVIEW`/`RESOLVED`), an assignable `reviewerId` (`updateAuditLogReview`, `src/lib/server/audit-review.ts`) with `reviewedBy`/`reviewedAt` auto-stamped only on the transition into `RESOLVED`, a free-text review note, and a full `AuditLogComment` thread per entry (mirrors `CaseComment`'s existing shape). New `PATCH /api/governance/audit-logs/[id]/review`, `GET/POST .../comments`.
  - **Retention/legal hold**: a `legalHold` boolean, settable via `PATCH /api/governance/audit-logs/[id]/legal-hold` (tenant-admin gated), checked directly in `enforceDataRetentionForTenant`'s purge query (`retention-postgres.ts`) -- a held row survives the retention job regardless of age, closing the exact gap an earlier module's own privacy/retention bullet named as not built ("no per-record 'exclude from retention' flag").
  - Audit-logs page (`dashboard/settings/governance/audit-logs`) rewired with all of the above: review-status/flagged/legal-hold filters and quick-toggle buttons, a date-range filter (didn't exist before this pass either), status/flag/hold badges per row, and a detail dialog with review-status control, "Assign to me," a review note, a legal-hold toggle, and the comment thread.
  - 19 new tests (12 in `tests/audit-review.test.ts` covering every service function including the RESOLVED-only reviewedBy/reviewedAt stamping and the empty-patch no-op guard; 4 new anomaly-flagging tests added to `tests/audit-log-impersonation.test.ts`, including confirming a rate-limiter failure never breaks the underlying audit write; 1 new export-module-acceptance test; 1 new retention-purge legal-hold-exclusion assertion added to the existing retention test). `tsc`, full `eslint`, full `vitest` suite (1110/1110, no regressions), and `npm run build` all verified clean (every new route confirmed present in the build's route list). No live click-through (no test credentials in this environment).
- [ ] Add data retention policies: module-specific retention, soft-delete lifecycle, purge approval, retention exceptions, legal hold, feature snapshot retention, communication event retention, and export file expiry. **Substantially real already, built under Module 9's consent-and-privacy-governance bullet**: `DataRetentionPolicy` (per-tenant, per-module retention days for Lead/Opportunity/Activity/AuditLog/deleted-records, plus marketplace app logs from this session's own Module 16 work), real anonymize-vs-hard-delete enforcement, and export-file expiry (Module 9's export-governance bullet) are all real. **Not built**: purge approval (enforcement runs unattended, no admin sign-off step), retention exceptions/legal hold (no per-record "exclude from retention" flag), and feature-snapshot/communication-event-specific retention beyond the general policy.
- [ ] Add data subject/privacy workflows: search subject data, export subject package, redact/anonymize, delete request, consent history, suppression enforcement, approval chain, and audit evidence. **Substantially real already, built under Module 9's consent-and-privacy-governance bullet**: `PrivacyRequest` covers search-by-email, full export bundle, cascading delete with a `PARTIAL`-status honest failure mode, consent history (`CommunicationConsentHistory`), and suppression enforcement -- all real. **Not built**: a formal approval chain before a privacy request executes (today it runs as soon as an authorized admin submits it, no second-approver step).
- [ ] Add encryption and key management backlog: application-level secret encryption, key rotation plan, envelope encryption support, field-level encryption candidates, and secret access audit. **Not built, and deliberately so, stated consistently throughout this entire document**: every integration/API-key/app secret in this codebase (`ApiKey.secret`, `TenantAppSecret`, `ExternalIntegration.secretConfig`, `CommunicationProviderConfig.secretConfig`, inbound-webhook secrets) is stored in plaintext by explicit, repeated design decision -- request-signing/HMAC verification needs the raw secret bytes, which a one-way hash would make impossible. Rotation (a real, repeatedly-reused current/previousSecret grace-window pattern) exists as the substitute risk control instead of encryption-at-rest.
- [ ] Add sensitive-field classification: PII, financial, education, identity, communication consent, partner tax/bank data, and custom field classification with masking/export rules. **Confirmed real but narrow, exactly as Module 9's own privacy bullet already states**: `maskFieldsForUser` masks fields, but purely by role-permission configuration ("hidden"/"readonly" per role), not by any PII/sensitivity classification -- there's no tagging concept for "this field is financial" or "this field is education data" independent of who's allowed to see it.
- [ ] Add IP allowlist and network policy: tenant admin allowlist, API key allowlist, webhook allowlist where feasible, platform-admin bypass controls, and lockout safeguards. **API-key-level IP allowlisting is real** (Module 9's API-management-console bullet: an optional per-key allowlist, checked on every authenticated request, unrestricted when unconfigured). **Not built**: a tenant-admin-level login IP allowlist, a webhook-specific allowlist, or any platform-admin bypass/lockout-safeguard concept beyond what already exists for the API-key case.
- [x] Add rate limiting and abuse controls: per-user/API-key/tenant limits, login throttling, webhook throttling, export throttling, automation/job throttling, and abuse alerting. **All 6 named sub-items now real**, every one reusing the same Redis-backed `checkRateLimit` (`rate-limit.ts`) rather than a second rate-limiting mechanism -- per-API-key, per-marketplace-app, and login throttling were already real from earlier sessions; this pass closes the rest.
  - **General per-user/per-tenant limit, independent of a specific credential**: new `assertGeneralRateLimit`, wired into `requireCurrentUser` (`auth.ts`) -- the single choke point nearly every session-authenticated API route already calls (`requireInternalUser`/`requireTenantAdmin`/`requirePlatformAdmin` all call it internally, so they inherit this automatically). Deliberately generous (300/min per user, 1000/min per tenant) so no realistic UI usage trips it -- an abuse ceiling, not a normal-usage budget. Placed in `requireCurrentUser`, not `getCurrentUser`, since the latter is also called for non-throwing page-level auth checks that fire far more often than real actions and would be hard to calibrate without false positives.
  - **HTTP-status wiring, a real architectural problem solved cleanly rather than by touching every route**: throwing a new error type from `requireCurrentUser` risked surfacing as a generic 500 on ~340 existing routes, each with its own independent `if (error.message === "X")` catch block (confirmed by grep -- no shared session-route error-mapping helper exists in this codebase, unlike the marketplace/API-key surfaces which do have one). Solved at the other equally-universal choke point instead: `serverError` (`http.ts`), which nearly every route already calls as its final catch-all, now special-cases a new `RateLimitExceededError` and returns a real `429` + `Retry-After` -- one change, not ~340.
  - **Webhook throttling**: `WebhookSubscription` gained a `rateLimitPerMinute` column (migration `0075`, default 60, editable on the Add Webhook dialog and shown as a badge per subscription), enforced in `webhook-outbox.ts`'s `deliverOne` before each delivery attempt. Throttled deliveries are re-queued for a short retry (15s) rather than cancelled or counted against the real `MAX_ATTEMPTS` failure budget -- a transient per-minute cap, unlike the marketplace app's daily-limit precedent where waiting doesn't help until the next day.
  - **Export throttling**: 10 exports/hour per user, enforced in `createExportRequestForUser` (`exports.ts`) -- per-user rather than per-tenant, since bulk-data-exfiltration abuse is an individual-actor concern.
  - **Automation/job throttling**: a new tenant-wide ceiling (1000 triggering events/minute) in `runAutomationsForEvent` (`automations-postgres.ts`), distinct from the pre-existing per-automation `maxExecutionsPerRecord` cap (a workflow cycle-safety guard, not a throughput limit) -- generous enough that a real bulk import doesn't trip it, but catches a runaway loop or scripted abuse. Same fire-and-forget philosophy as the existing `automationEnabled` feature-flag check right above it: every caller already wraps this in `.catch(() => undefined)`, so a throttled batch is silently skipped, not an error.
  - **Abuse alerting, built once and reused everywhere else**: new `checkRateLimitWithAlert` wraps `checkRateLimit` with a separate Redis-tracked violation counter (independent of the rate-limit window itself, so "5 violations in 10 minutes" survives across several separate windows); on the 5th violation it fires exactly one notification via the new `alertAbuseThresholdCrossed` (`abuse-alerts.ts`) to the tenant's earliest-created user -- the same "no specific owner, fall back to earliest-created user" convention already established for webhook/marketplace terminal-failure notifications -- and won't re-fire again until the violation window resets. All four throttled surfaces above (general, webhook, export, automation) opt into this through the same one function.
  - 9 new tests in `tests/rate-limit.test.ts` (window counting, independent key scoping, the exact 5th-violation alert-firing threshold with no re-fire, no-tenant no-alert, and both branches of `assertGeneralRateLimit`) against a real in-memory fake Redis (not a live one), plus 1 existing test in `tests/webhook-subscription-crud.test.ts` updated for the new column. `tsc`, full `eslint`, full `vitest` suite (923/923, no regressions), and `npm run build` all verified clean (every new/changed route confirmed present in the build's route list); no live authenticated click-through (no test credentials in this environment).
- [ ] Add platform admin tenant controls: suspend/unsuspend tenant, lock login, maintenance banner, tenant health view, storage/worker/database usage, feature flags, and migration readiness without exposing tenant data unnecessarily. **Substantially real already, built under Module 9's tenant-environment-controls bullet**: suspend/unsuspend (with real login/session enforcement, not just a cosmetic flag), maintenance banner, feature flags (`TenantFeature`, 8 real flags), and migration readiness (`/platform-admin/schema-status`) are all real. **Not built**: "lock login" as a distinct concept from full suspension, and a tenant-level storage/worker/database usage view (the `/dashboard/admin/usage` page is a confirmed stub, per the Phase 0 correction above).
- [x] Add secure file/download controls: expiring signed URLs, permission recheck at download time, download audit, virus-scan hook placeholder, blocked public file paths, and safe content-disposition names. **All 6 named sub-items now real.** Download audit was already real (export-governance); this pass closes the rest.
  - **True expiring signed URLs**: new `src/lib/server/signed-urls.ts` -- `generateSignedDownloadToken(resource, resourceId, ttlSeconds)`/`verifySignedDownloadToken(...)`, HMAC-SHA256 over `resource:resourceId:expiresAt` with a constant-time signature comparison (`timingSafeEqual`, guarding against a byte-by-byte timing attack a naive `===` would allow). A cryptographically time-limited link now genuinely exists, independent of how long the underlying resource itself stays valid -- distinct from the pre-existing server-side status/timestamp checks (e.g. `ExportRequest.expiresAt`), which are still real but were never cryptographic. Applied to two concrete, complete flows: `POST /api/exports/[id]/signed-url` / `GET /api/public/exports/[id]/download` and `POST /api/partner-invoices/[id]/signed-url` / `GET /api/public/partner-invoices/[id]/download` -- both additive (the existing session-authenticated download routes are unchanged), so an admin can now mint a link (e.g. to paste into an email to a partner) that works standalone for exactly as long as it says, without sharing a live session.
  - **Permission recheck at download time, as distinct from request-creation time**: new `assertAccountActiveForDownload` (`file-download-guards.ts`), called from `getExportDownloadForUser`, `getPrivacyRequestDownload`, and both new token-mint functions -- a fresh, direct DB check of the CURRENT `User.status`/`Tenant.status` at the moment of download, not carried over from create-time or from the standard per-request JWT check (which deliberately does not re-verify `User.status` on every request -- see the login-status bug fixed earlier this session; an already-issued session token keeps working until it naturally expires). Downloading is the one action in this app that hands raw data out, so it gets this extra recheck specifically, rather than folding it into the general request path.
  - **Virus-scan hook, a genuine placeholder, not a fake one**: new `scanFileForThreats` in `file-storage.ts`, called from `writePrivateFile` itself -- the single choke point every file write in this app already goes through -- so it's real infrastructure, not a decorative function sitting unused. Audited first and confirmed honestly: there is no end-user file-upload surface anywhere in this codebase today (every file this app ever writes -- CSV exports, PDF invoices, GDPR JSON dumps, rendered report CSVs -- is app-generated, not user-uploaded), so nothing malicious can reach it yet; it always reports clean. Structured so a future upload feature gets real scanning for free by routing through `writePrivateFile`, without any caller needing to change.
  - **Blocked public file paths -- confirmed already real, not newly built**: `storage/` lives entirely outside `public/` (verified directly -- `public/` contains only the default Next.js SVG assets), so private files were never reachable via Next's static file server in the first place; combined with `resolveLocalStoragePath`'s pre-existing path-traversal guard, every private file was already only reachable through an authenticated or token-verified API route, never a static path. Verified and documented, no code change needed.
  - **Safe content-disposition names, closing a real gap in 3 of the routes that already had *a* protection, just not a complete one**: `exports/[id]/download`, `public/report-deliveries/[deliveryId]/download`, and `governance/gdpr/requests/[id]/download` previously only stripped `"` from a DB-stored filename before interpolating it into the `Content-Disposition` header -- real, but not CRLF-safe, so a filename containing a newline could theoretically inject additional response headers on an unpatched HTTP stack. New shared `safeContentDispositionFilename` (`http.ts`) whitelists to a safe character set instead of trying to enumerate what's unsafe, matching the pattern the Payouts statement/invoice routes had already independently arrived at (their own `.replace(/[^a-zA-Z0-9._-]/g, "-")`) -- now the one shared implementation all download routes use.
  - 17 new tests: `tests/signed-urls.test.ts` (8 -- valid/tampered/wrong-resource/wrong-id/expired/malformed/null tokens, TTL accuracy), `tests/file-download-guards.test.ts` (5 -- active/deactivated/suspended-tenant/platform-admin-exempt/no-row-found), and 2 new cases each in `tests/exports-audit-logging.test.ts`/`tests/privacy.test.ts` (the new deactivated-account rejection), plus 1 new case in `tests/file-storage.test.ts` confirming the scan hook sits in the real write path, not just standalone. 5 pre-existing tests updated for the new account-status check consuming an extra mocked query. `tsc`, full `eslint`, full `vitest` suite (939/939, no regressions), and `npm run build` all verified clean (all 4 new routes confirmed present in the build's route list); no live authenticated click-through (no test credentials in this environment).
- [x] Add security headers and browser protections: CSP, frame policy, referrer policy, secure cookies, CSRF protection for mutating routes, and safe CORS defaults for API/webhooks. **All 6 named sub-items real.** Confirmed by direct audit before starting that `next.config.ts` had zero security headers and no `middleware`/`proxy` file existed at all.
  - **CSP, frame policy, referrer policy**: `next.config.ts`'s `headers()` now sets a real Content-Security-Policy (`default-src 'self'`, `frame-ancestors 'none'`, `object-src 'none'`, etc. -- `script-src`/`style-src` include `'unsafe-inline'` rather than a nonce-based policy, a deliberate, stated tradeoff: `app/layout.tsx` has one genuine inline `<script>` for the no-flash color-theme setter, and a nonce-based CSP would need new per-request proxy plumbing this pass didn't attempt), `X-Frame-Options: DENY`, `Referrer-Policy: strict-origin-when-cross-origin`, `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`, a restrictive `Permissions-Policy`, and HSTS in production. **Real exception, not an oversight**: the public form pages (`/f/[slug]`, `/public-form/[id]`) get a second, deliberately relaxed ruleset with no frame restriction at all -- confirmed these are a genuine, shipped feature (`EmbedCodeDialog.tsx` hands tenants a ready-made `<iframe>` snippet to embed those exact pages on their own external websites), and a blanket DENY would have broken it. Getting this right took real verification, not just writing the config: Next.js only overrides a header *value* when a later rule sets the *same key* for an overlapping path (confirmed against Next's own docs and empirically), so a naive "general rule, then a relaxed override after it" approach left `X-Frame-Options: DENY` still applying to the embed pages (the override rule didn't mention that key, so nothing cleared it) even though the CSP's `frame-ancestors` correctly relaxed. Fixed with a negative-lookahead `source` pattern so the strict rule never matches those two path prefixes in the first place, then confirmed live via `curl` against a running build across `/`, `/login`, `/dashboard`, `/api/auth/me`, `/f/test-slug`, `/public-form/test-id`, and adversarial near-misses (`/public-formish`, bare `/f`) -- exactly the right pages get exactly the right headers.
  - **Secure cookies**: audited every `.cookies.set(` call site (5 total, all in the login/MFA-verify/password-reset flows) -- all already had `secure: NODE_ENV==='production'` and `sameSite: 'lax'`; the one MFA trusted-device cookie is also `httpOnly`. Nothing needed fixing here; the audit confirmed a real prior finding: the primary `token` cookie is deliberately non-httpOnly (read by `apiFetch` and sent as a `Bearer` header, not relied on ambiently), which is also *why* CSRF turned out to already be a narrow risk rather than a demonstrated one -- see below.
  - **CSRF protection for mutating routes**: new `src/proxy.ts` (Next.js 16 renamed `middleware.ts` to `proxy.ts` mid-migration; this app was already on Next 16, so it's built directly as `proxy.ts`/`export function proxy`, not the deprecated name). Audited first rather than assumed: every one of the ~227 call sites that gate a mutating route already calls `requireCurrentUser(request)`/`getCurrentUser(request)`, which requires a real `Authorization: Bearer` header a cross-site page can't attach -- classic ambient-cookie CSRF was already structurally blocked. This adds a real, explicit defense-in-depth layer anyway, using the Fetch Metadata `Sec-Fetch-Site` header (sent by every modern browser, harder to spoof/omit than Origin/Referer): any POST/PUT/PATCH/DELETE to `/api/*` with `Sec-Fetch-Site: cross-site` is rejected with 403, except a named, justified exemption list (inbound webhook receivers, SCIM, the public form submission endpoint -- which is unavoidably cross-site when called from an embedded form on a tenant's own site -- the other `/api/public/*` endpoints, and the API-key-authenticated `/api/v1/*`). Absent-header requests (non-browser/server-to-server clients) pass through untouched, since there's no victim browser in that threat model. Adding this file required its own real fix: it triggered Next.js to also build an edge-runtime bundle of `src/instrumentation.ts`, which broke the production build (`pg`'s Node-only `fs`/`path` requires got pulled into the edge compilation) -- fixed by rewriting the file's `NEXT_RUNTIME` guard from an early-return-on-negative-check to the positive-check shape Next's build tooling specifically recognizes and tree-shakes per target.
  - **Safe CORS defaults**: confirmed by grep -- no `Access-Control-Allow-Origin` or CORS handling exists anywhere in this app, which is already the safe state (no cross-origin browser access is granted to anything); every inbound external caller (webhooks, SCIM, the public form submit endpoint, `/api/v1`) already authenticates independently of cookies/CORS, so no explicit CORS policy was needed or added.
  - **Two real, unrelated pre-existing bugs found and fixed while getting a server running to verify this at all**: `next start`/`next dev`/the standalone server crashed at boot with "You cannot use different slug names for the same dynamic path" -- confirmed as a genuine, long-standing structural defect (not something this pass introduced): `/api/call-queues/[callLogId]/...` and `/api/call-queues/[teamId]/...` were sibling dynamic routes with different parameter names at the same tree position, which Next.js doesn't allow regardless of whether their sub-paths differ; same problem for `/api/disposition-groups/[id]` vs `/api/disposition-groups/[groupId]`. Fixed the first by moving the less-used branch under a new static `team/` prefix (`/api/call-queues/team/[teamId]/calls`, with the one frontend caller in `call-center/page.tsx` updated to match) and the second by simply renaming the param (`[id]` → `[groupId]`) with zero URL/frontend change needed. Confirmed via a written sibling-dynamic-segment scanner that no other such conflicts exist anywhere in `src/app`. This app could not previously be started at all outside the test suite -- a significant, unrelated finding surfaced only because this pass needed a real running server to verify header behavior.
  - Verification: 11 new tests in `tests/proxy.test.ts` (GET/non-API pass-through, cross-site block, no-header/same-origin allow, every named exemption, and the one adversarial near-miss -- `webhook-secret` management correctly NOT exempted despite sharing a prefix with the real webhook receiver). `tsc`, full `eslint`, full `vitest` suite (1071/1071, no regressions), and `npm run build` all verified clean. Beyond that, this is the most live-verified module of this entire session: built the production standalone server, ran it, and used `curl` to confirm the exact header values on 8+ real paths plus 3 real cross-site/same-origin/exempt CSRF scenarios against the running server -- not just code review. **Not done**: a full browser console check for CSP violations (no Playwright/Chromium available in this environment within a reasonable startup time) -- mitigated by the CSP's deliberately permissive `'unsafe-inline'` script/style policy and confirmed absence (by grep) of any external script/style/connect sources, which structurally rules out the most common way a CSP silently breaks a page's own rendering.
- [ ] Add tests for tenant isolation, RBAC matrix, SSO/MFA policy behavior, session revocation, privileged action guards, impersonation restrictions, export/download permission checks, retention purge, and audit integrity. **Partially covered already, not as one named suite**: tenant isolation has dedicated tests (`tests/tenant-isolation.test.ts`) plus per-feature isolation tests throughout this session's work; retention purge (`tests/retention.test.ts`) and export/download permission checks (export-governance tests) are real. SSO/MFA/session-revocation/impersonation-restriction tests don't exist because those features don't exist; a cross-cutting RBAC-matrix test (checking every route against every role systematically) is not confirmed as its own suite.

## Priority Module 19 — Quality Management and Supervisor Coaching

Scope decision: approved as the performance-quality layer for sales, counseling, telephony, service, and partner operations. Build this on top of activities, telephony call logs, counseling sessions, tasks, communications, automations, scoring, reports, and permissions.

**Status: 0 of 19, confirmed genuinely greenfield** -- no `QualityScorecard`/`QualityReview`/`CoachingPlan`/equivalent table exists anywhere. **One real, relevant adjacent piece already exists**: bullet 6 ("call/session playback and evidence handling") names recording metadata/restricted playback/retention policy -- `TelephonyCallLog.recordingUrl`/`recordingExpiresAt` plus `call-recordings.ts` (Module 15's own call-recording-governance work) already provide exactly that for calls specifically, just with no QA-review workflow attached to it yet.

- [ ] Add quality management schema with tenant isolation: `QualityScorecard`, `QualityScorecardSection`, `QualityScorecardQuestion`, `QualityReview`, `QualityReviewAnswer`, `QualityCalibrationSession`, `CoachingPlan`, `CoachingTask`, `PerformanceImprovementPlan`, and `QualityDispute`.
- [ ] Add QA scorecard builder: weighted sections, question types, pass/fail rules, critical failure questions, comments, evidence links, scoring bands, role/module targeting, and versioned publishing.
- [ ] Add review queue workspace for calls, activities, counseling sessions, cases, partner interactions, and communication threads with filters by user/team/sales group, source, outcome, date, score band, and risk flags.
- [ ] Add automatic sampling rules: random sample, low-score/high-risk records, missed SLA, failed disposition, high-value opportunity, new user ramp-up, complaint/unsubscribe, and supervisor-defined sampling quota.
- [ ] Add manual review flow: assign reviewer, open interaction context, listen/view transcript where available, score against scorecard, add comments, request correction, publish feedback, and audit review changes.
- [ ] Add call/session playback and evidence handling: recording metadata, transcript attachment, timeline references, restricted playback permission, download audit, and review evidence retention policy.
- [ ] Add calibration workflow: multiple reviewers score the same sample, compare variance, resolve disagreement, capture calibration notes, track reviewer consistency, and publish calibration outcomes.
- [ ] Add coaching plan workflow: create plan from QA review, assign coach/manager, define goals, tasks, due dates, training links, check-ins, completion criteria, and progress status.
- [ ] Add performance improvement plan workflow for repeated quality gaps: trigger conditions, approval, milestones, manager comments, employee acknowledgements, status tracking, and audit trail.
- [ ] Add agent/counselor feedback experience: review summary, score breakdown, comments, examples, assigned coaching tasks, acknowledgement, dispute/appeal flow, and improvement history.
- [ ] Add quality dispute workflow: user disputes a score, reviewer/manager response, evidence review, score adjustment, final decision, and immutable audit log.
- [ ] Add quality analytics: average QA score, critical failure rate, trend by user/team/queue/source, scorecard section weakness, reviewer calibration variance, coaching completion, and conversion/SLA impact.
- [ ] Add supervisor dashboards: team quality heatmap, coaching backlog, users needing review, recurring issues, scorecard drift, recent disputes, and quality-to-outcome correlation.
- [ ] Add automation triggers/actions: QA review created/published, critical failure found, score below threshold, coaching plan assigned/completed, dispute opened/resolved, calibration variance high, and PIP started/completed.
- [ ] Add quality signals to predictive scoring and next-best action: recurring objections, poor disposition quality, missed script steps, low QA trend, coaching completion, and quality-risk flags.
- [ ] Add role permissions: manage scorecards, assign reviews, conduct reviews, view own reviews, view team reviews, edit published score, manage coaching plans, manage PIPs, playback/download recordings, and resolve disputes.
- [ ] Add notifications: review assigned, feedback published, coaching task due, dispute response, calibration session due, critical failure escalation, and supervisor weekly quality digest.
- [ ] Add export/report governance: restrict QA comments, sensitive recordings/transcripts, employee performance data, dispute details, and PIP exports behind privileged permissions/approvals.
- [ ] Add tests for scorecard versioning, weighted score calculations, critical failure behavior, sampling rules, review permissions, dispute flow, coaching task creation, analytics aggregates, recording permission checks, and tenant isolation.

## Priority Module 20 — Testing, Release Management, and DevOps Operations

Scope decision: approved as the delivery and operations layer for VPS readiness and long-term enterprise reliability. Build this around the existing direct Postgres migration plan, local/VPS runbooks, worker stack, API smoke tests, lint/type/build checks, seeded demo data, and deployment scripts.

**Status: mostly 0 of 17, but several items have real, relevant overlap with work done elsewhere in this document -- called out per item below rather than left to look like duplicate gaps.**

- [ ] Add CI pipeline for every pull/merge: install, lint with zero warnings, `npx tsc --noEmit`, unit tests, API smoke tests, build, migration syntax checks, and artifact generation. Not built as an actual CI config (no `.github/workflows` or equivalent exists) -- but every one of these checks (`lint`, `tsc --noEmit`, `vitest run`, `build`) is real, working, and has been run manually after every change this entire session; only the automation-on-push wiring is missing, not the checks themselves.
- [ ] Add migration safety checks: ordered migration validation, duplicate migration number detection, destructive SQL detection, schema drift check against `SCHEMA.md`, local apply test, rollback notes, and production approval gate. **Substantially real already, built under Module 9's schema/version-governance bullet**: unapplied-migration detection, `SCHEMA.md` freshness checking, a migration advisory lock, and a pre-deploy compatibility script (`npm run db:check-migrations`) are all real. **Not built**: duplicate migration-number detection, destructive-SQL static detection, and a formal production-approval gate (the check script can fail a deploy pipeline, but nothing requires a human sign-off before running migrations).
- [ ] Add seeded data governance: deterministic local seed, demo tenant seed, reset-safe seed mode, large demo data generation, idempotency, fixture versioning, and clear labels for synthetic data. Not built as a governed system -- seed scripts exist ad hoc in this repo but have no idempotency guarantee, versioning, or synthetic-data labeling convention.
- [ ] Add staging/demo environment workflow: separate database, separate Redis, separate file storage, isolated provider sandbox secrets, predictable demo credentials, and demo refresh runbook. Not built -- this is an infrastructure/hosting-topology concern (how many environments exist and how they're isolated), not something the application code itself defines.
- [ ] Add release checklist automation: pending migrations, env var diff, worker compatibility, database backup confirmation, smoke test results, build artifact hash, release notes, and rollback command preview. Not built as an automated checklist -- the individual signals it would aggregate (pending migrations, smoke/build results) exist as separate manual checks, not a combined release gate.
- [ ] Add deployment pipeline for VPS: build image/artifact, transfer/deploy, run migrations, restart frontend/backend/worker/ml service, health checks, warmup, and post-deploy smoke test. Not built -- no deployment automation exists in this repo; `deploy/vps/` (referenced by Module 9's runbook-updates bullet) was never touched this session.
- [ ] Add rollback runbook and tooling: previous release artifact, DB migration rollback strategy, feature flag fallback, worker pause/drain, emergency maintenance banner, and restore decision tree. **One real building block**: the maintenance banner (Module 9's tenant-environment-controls work) is real and could serve the "emergency maintenance banner" role. Everything else -- artifact retention, a migration rollback strategy, worker pause/drain, and a documented restore decision tree -- is not built.
- [ ] Add service health monitoring: frontend, backend/API, worker, Redis, Postgres, ML service, file storage, SMTP, WhatsApp, SMS, telephony, webhooks, and scheduled jobs. **Substantially real already, built under Module 9's connector-health-dashboard bullet**: database, Redis, worker-queue depth, ML service, and local file storage all have real live checks; SMTP/WhatsApp/SMS/telephony/webhooks report configured-and-active status (a deliberate, documented trade-off against expensive live-probing, per that bullet's own note). **Not built**: frontend health itself and scheduled-job health as distinct monitored signals.
- [ ] Add error tracking: server exceptions, client runtime errors, API failures, worker job failures, webhook failures, ML service failures, and release correlation. **Partially real**: `serverError` logs every 500 server-side (the Phase 0 fix), and failed webhook/marketplace-app/report-schedule deliveries are tracked in their own dedicated tables with real status/error fields. **Not built**: client-side runtime error capture, a unified cross-source error-tracking view, and release correlation (no release/version identifier is attached to any logged error).
- [ ] Add performance monitoring: API latency, slow queries, page load timing, bundle size, worker queue depth, export processing duration, report query duration, and database connection pool health. **Worker queue depth is real** (the connector-health dashboard's BullMQ `getJobCounts` check). Nothing else on this list -- API latency, slow-query logging, page load timing, bundle-size tracking, or connection-pool health -- is built.
- [ ] Add uptime and synthetic checks: login, dashboard load, lead list, opportunity update, task create, export queue, worker export completion, report load, communication enqueue, and scoring service health. Not built -- no synthetic/canary check of any real user flow exists; `/api/health` (referenced by the connector-health dashboard) is a bare liveness check, not a flow-level synthetic test.
- [ ] Add log management: structured JSON logs, request ids, tenant/user context where safe, secret redaction, log retention, search/runbook examples, and separate app/worker/ml logs. Not built -- logging today is plain `console.error`/`console.log` calls (e.g. the Phase 0 `serverError` fix), with no structured format, request-id correlation, or retention policy.
- [ ] Add worker operations: queue dashboard, pause/resume queues, retry failed jobs, dead-letter inspection, concurrency config, graceful shutdown, stuck job detection, and scheduled job registry. **Partially real**: `scripts/worker.ts`'s `recurringJobs` array is a real, single job list (a scheduled-job registry, though without the richer metadata Phase 0's own note on this already covers), and several individual subsystems have their own dead-letter/retry visibility (webhook outbox, marketplace app deliveries, import/export governance). **Not built**: any operator-facing dashboard, pause/resume controls, or stuck-job detection across the worker system as a whole.
- [ ] Add database operations runbook: backup schedule, restore rehearsal, vacuum/analyze guidance, index review, connection limits, slow query review, storage growth, and data retention jobs. **Data retention jobs are real** (`retention.enforce`, Module 9). Everything else -- backup schedule, restore rehearsal, vacuum/index/connection-limit guidance -- is not documented anywhere in this repo, echoing the Phase 0 backup/restore-runbook gap.
- [ ] Add release approvals: owner approval, security approval for sensitive changes, migration approval, demo acceptance, production deploy approval, and audit trail. Not built -- no approval-gate concept exists for releases specifically (distinct from the in-app approval workflows this session built for imports/exports/payouts/permission changes, which gate application actions, not code releases).
- [ ] Add feature flag system: tenant/user scoped flags, rollout percentage, kill switch, dependency checks, audit log, and safe default behavior when flag config is missing. **Real, but tenant-scoped only, not user-scoped or percentage-based**: `TenantFeature` (8 real, enforced flags) is a genuine working feature-flag system with a safe default (new tenants get sensible defaults, confirmed in the Gamification & Payouts migration note) -- it just operates at the tenant level, not per-user, and has no rollout-percentage or dependency-check concept. `PlatformModule`/`TenantModuleEntitlement` (Module 21) is a second, real, adjacent flagging system for whole modules rather than individual features.
- [ ] Add environment/config validation: required env vars, secret length checks, production unsafe value detection, provider connector readiness, Redis/Postgres reachability, and ML service compatibility. **Provider/Redis/Postgres/ML-service reachability is real** (the same connector-health dashboard as above). **Not built**: startup-time required-env-var validation, secret-length/strength checks, or production-unsafe-value detection (e.g. a debug flag left on) -- nothing validates configuration at boot, only live connectivity on demand.
- [ ] Add browser verification suite: Playwright login, navigation, critical CRUD flows, automation builder, reports, exports, marketing, scoring, payouts, partner portal, and responsive viewport checks.
- [ ] Add security/regression suite: auth negative cases, RBAC matrix, tenant isolation, CSRF/session behavior, webhook signature failures, export/download permissions, payout transition guards, and platform-admin flows.
- [ ] Add release documentation templates: changelog, known risks, migrations applied, env var changes, manual verification, rollback plan, and customer/demo notes.

## Priority Module 21 — Tenant Module Entitlements and Modular Platform

Scope decision: approved as a platform requirement. Most major capabilities must be modular, selectable while creating a tenant, and enable/disable-able later by platform admins without code changes or unsafe hidden routes.

**Overall scope note, stated up front**: this 25-item list describes a genuine multi-month platform initiative (module manifests, dependency graphs, lifecycle hooks, usage telemetry, billing metadata, retention policies, health badges, contract tests). This pass builds the real, working core (catalog schema, per-tenant entitlement, platform-admin management UI, audit trail) and wires actual backend + UI enforcement into the two modules added earlier in this same session that had zero tenant-level kill switch until now (Next-Best Action, Marketing Journey Orchestration) — closing a concrete, self-inflicted gap rather than only adding scaffolding nobody calls. The other ~26 catalog entries (Payouts, Gamification, Opportunities, etc., which already have their own separate ad-hoc `TenantFeature` gate from earlier sessions) were **not** retrofitted onto this new system — that's a real, large, separate migration, not attempted here. 6 of 25 items below are checked, all with real but narrow delivery; 19 are explicitly not built.

- [x] Add module catalog schema with tenant isolation/platform scope — **3 of 6 tables**: `PlatformModule` (platform-wide catalog, no RLS, same convention as `Tenant` itself), `TenantModuleEntitlement` (per-tenant status, RLS-enabled), `TenantModuleAuditLog` (RLS-enabled). Migration `0037_module_entitlements.sql`. **Not built**: `PlatformModuleDependency`, `TenantModuleSetting`, `TenantModuleUsage` — dependency rules, per-module settings, and usage telemetry are each their own real sub-feature, deferred below.
- [x] Add first-class module keys for all major capabilities — all 28 keys from the checklist's list seeded into `PlatformModule` (`DASHBOARD`, `LEADS`, ... `DEVOPS_OPS`), each tagged with a category and `isCore` flag (core modules like Leads/Dashboard/Activities/Tasks/Views/Security-Admin can never be disabled, enforced server-side in `setTenantModuleStatus`). Most of these keys are placeholders for modules that don't exist yet (Telephony, Service Desk, Product Catalog, etc.) — the catalog row exists so the system is ready the moment those modules are built, but only `NEXT_BEST_ACTION` and `JOURNEY_ORCHESTRATION` have any real entitlement behavior wired to them today (see below).
- [ ] Add tenant creation wizard with module selection, default bundle templates, dependency warnings, user/partner limits, storage/export limits, enabled channels, default personas, and seeded-demo toggle. **Not built** — tenant creation (`create-tenant-dialog.tsx`) was not touched.
- [x] Add platform-admin module management UI to enable, disable, suspend, trial-enable, or retire modules per tenant with reason and audit trail — new "Modules" card on the platform-admin tenant detail page (`/platform-admin/tenants/[id]`, alongside the pre-existing Feature Flags card), a status dropdown (Enabled/Trial/Suspended/Disabled) per module with a reason prompt on disable/suspend, backed by `PATCH /api/platform-admin/tenants/[id]/modules/[moduleKey]`. **Not built**: "retire" as a distinct terminal status (the schema's `status` check constraint doesn't include it), effective-date scheduling (changes apply immediately), and dependency validation (no dependencies exist to validate, see below).
- [ ] Add module dependency rules: modules can require other modules, recommend optional modules, block incompatible modules, and explain why an action is unavailable. **Not built** — no `PlatformModuleDependency` table, no validation logic.
- [ ] Add backend entitlement guard for every module route/API/action/worker job, not just navigation hiding. **Built for 7 of ~28 modules across six passes, not "every"**: `assertModuleEnabled`/`isModuleEnabledForTenant` (`src/lib/server/module-entitlements.ts`) are wired into every mutating entry point of Next-Best-Action (`generateRecommendationsForRecord`, rule/strategy CRUD, `respondToRecommendation`), Marketing Journeys (`createJourneyForTenant`, `transitionJourneyStatus`, `enrollAudienceIntoJourney`), Distribution Engine (`distributeRecord`, assignment-rule CRUD/reorder), Service Desk (`Case` CRUD/comments/assignment, all settings CRUD), Predictive Scoring (`recomputeSelfLearningScoresForTenant` and all scoring-settings/feature-catalog/model-promotion/manual-override mutations), Marketing Communications (`upsertMarketingCampaignForTenant`, `updateMarketingCampaignStatusForTenant`, `launchMarketingCampaignForTenant`, `sendMarketingCampaignTestForTenant`), and Partners (`createPartnerForTenant`, `createPartnerLoginForTenant`, `updatePartnerProfileForTenant` — reads like `listPartnerProfilesForTenant` and self-service `/partners/me` stay ungated; confirmed no overlap with the already-gated `payoutsEnabled`/`gamificationEnabled` partner-*visibility* logic, which lives in separate files and doesn't call into `partners.ts`'s mutations at all). Distribution Engine, Predictive Scoring, Marketing Communications, and Partners were each found the same way — fully built, zero gating of any kind (neither old `TenantFeature` flag nor the new system) — confirmed by direct grep before fixing, discovered by systematically checking each already-built module's catalog key for a real guard rather than assuming one exists. Each throws `MODULE_DISABLED:<key>`, surfaced by the relevant API routes as a 403/400 per this session's established `error.message.startsWith("MODULE_DISABLED")` pattern. The other ~21 catalog modules have no backend guard from this new system (several already have their own separate `TenantFeature`-based guard from earlier work, which was left as-is rather than migrated — see the scope note above; the rest correspond to not-yet-built modules, so there's nothing to gate yet). **Follow-up audit completed**: the six modules called out above as "some gate already via `TenantFeature`, not individually re-confirmed" (Automations, Forms, Opportunities, Reports, Gamification, Payouts) were each checked directly against their real mutation entry points. Result: 2 of 6 (Forms, Automations) had the exact same "zero gating" pattern as Distribution/Predictive Scoring/Marketing/Partners despite already having a real, seeded `TenantFeature` flag; the other 4 had a real but *partial* gate (some mutations covered, others not). All fixed this pass using the older `assertFeatureEnabled`/`FEATURE_DISABLED` system (matching each module's pre-existing flag, not the newer `PlatformModule` catalog, since these all predate it):
  - **Forms** (`formBuilderEnabled`, `forms-postgres.ts`): `createFormForTenant`, `updateFormForTenant`, and — highest severity — `submitPublicForm` had zero check of their own flag (the module had a *different*, narrower `opportunityEnabled` sub-check for opportunity-mapped fields specifically, documented above; that's unrelated and was left as-is). `submitPublicForm` is reachable via the fully unauthenticated `POST /api/public/forms/[identifier]/submit` route, so a tenant with Form Builder disabled could still accept live public submissions. Fixed by gating all three plus their 3 API routes.
  - **Opportunities** (`opportunityEnabled`, `opportunities-postgres.ts`): `createOpportunityForTenant`, `updateOpportunityForTenant`, `deleteOpportunityForTenant` had zero check — fixed, plus their 2 API routes.
  - **Automations** (`automationEnabled`, `automations-postgres.ts`): `createAutomationForTenant`, `updateAutomationForTenant`, `testAutomationForTenant`, `enrollRecordsInAutomation` had zero check — fixed, plus their 4 API routes. `runAutomationsForEvent` (the actual trigger-matching engine, called fire-and-forget from every other module's own mutations — leads, opportunities, forms, distribution, cases, marketing, partners) now no-ops gracefully (returns `[]`) rather than throwing, matching `distributeRecord`'s established convention for the same reason: every caller already wraps it in `.catch(() => undefined)`, so a thrown error would be indistinguishable from a genuine failure instead of the deliberate "nothing to do" outcome a disabled tenant should get. The `automation.processDue` worker job now cancels (rather than executes) a due queue job whose tenant has since disabled Automations.
  - **Gamification** (`gamificationEnabled`, `src/lib/server/badges.ts`): `createBadgeForTenant`, `updateBadgeForTenant`, `deleteBadgeForTenant` had zero check (the module's points/award engine was already correctly gated from earlier work — this was specifically the Badges CRUD, a separate file) — fixed, plus their 2 API routes. Confirmed `evaluateBadgesForEvent` and an `award_points`/`evaluate_badges` automation-node integration are not actually wired into `executeAutomationWorkflow` today (dormant code, not a live gap) — worth re-checking once that wiring is eventually built.
  - **Payouts** (`payoutsEnabled`, `src/lib/server/commission.ts` + `partner-invoices.ts`): `createCommissionRuleForTenant`, `updateCommissionRuleForTenant`, `deleteCommissionRuleForTenant` had zero check — fixed, plus their 2 API routes. `calculateAndRecordCommissionForOpportunity` also gated defensively (graceful no-op, same reasoning as `runAutomationsForEvent` above) even though it's confirmed not yet wired into any live automation trigger. Separately, found and fixed a real ordering bug in `cancelAndReissuePartnerInvoice`: it cancelled the existing invoice (a real, audit-logged write) *before* ever reaching a feature-gate check — the only check in the whole function was buried two calls deep inside its own reissue step. A disabled tenant could trigger a real invoice cancellation with no replacement before that inner gate fired. Fixed by adding an explicit `assertFeatureEnabled` check as the very first line of the function, before any write happens.
  - **Reports** (`advancedReporting`, `reports-dashboards-postgres.ts` + `report-schedules-postgres.ts` + `reporting-query.ts`): this module had the "Reports" nav item already correctly gated (`NavigationDrawer.tsx`, pre-existing) but zero backend enforcement anywhere — `advancedReporting` wasn't even in the `EntitlementFeature` union type, so it was impossible to gate via this system until now. Extended the type, then gated `createCustomReportForTenant`/`update`/`delete`, `createDashboardWidgetForTenant`/`update`/`delete`, `createReportScheduleForTenant`/`update`/`delete`, and `executeReportQueryForTenant` (the actual report-run engine used by both the ad-hoc query builder and every custom report/schedule), plus all 7 corresponding API routes. The `reports.processSchedules` worker job now deactivates (rather than executes) a due schedule whose tenant has since disabled Advanced Reporting.

  All six confirmed to have zero frontend-nav work needed — every one already had its settings-sidebar/main-nav entry correctly gated from earlier sessions (`sidebar-nav.tsx`, `NavigationDrawer.tsx`); this pass was purely closing backend enforcement gaps. New regression tests added across `tests/forms-postgres.test.ts`, `tests/opportunities-postgres.test.ts`, `tests/automations-postgres.test.ts`, `tests/badges.test.ts`, `tests/commission-ledger.test.ts`, `tests/partner-invoices.test.ts`, `tests/reports-dashboards-postgres.test.ts`, and `tests/report-infra-postgres.test.ts` (each covering: rejects when disabled, platform-admin bypass, and — where applicable — the graceful-no-op/worker-skip behavior). Running total: backend entitlement guards now built for **13 of ~28** catalog modules across this session's two audit passes. `tsc`, full `eslint`, full `vitest` suite (262/262, 29 new), and `npm run build` all verified clean.
- [ ] Add frontend navigation gating: sidebar, global create, command palette, settings tabs, dashboard widgets, automations nodes, reports datasets, filters, and smart view modules. **Built for the same 7 modules.** The `useModuleEnabled(moduleKey)` hook (`src/components/auth/feature-gate.tsx`) now gates: the "Next-Best-Action" settings-sidebar entry + `NextBestActionPanel`; the "Journeys" tab on the Marketing page; the "Cases" main-nav entry + "Service Desk" settings-sidebar entry (both added alongside that module); a new "Assignment Rules" gate on the Distribution Engine's rule CRUD routes (backend only — the settings-sidebar entry for it predates the module-entitlement system and wasn't re-tagged); the "Lead Scoring" settings-sidebar entry plus `PredictiveScoreBadge`/`PredictiveScorePanel` (gated once, inside the shared component, covering all 4 render call sites on the Leads/Opportunities list and detail pages); the "Marketing" main-nav entry (kept visible if *either* `MARKETING` or `JOURNEY_ORCHESTRATION` is enabled, since Journeys lives as an independent tab on the same page) plus the Marketing page's four Marketing-specific tabs; and, this pass, the "Partners" settings-sidebar entry plus the admin Partners page's "Add Partner"/"Add Login" buttons and per-login role/parent/status/payout-access dropdowns (existing partner data stays readable and visible when disabled, matching the "reads stay open" convention — only creation/edit actions are hidden/disabled). Automation trigger pickers for Distribution and Service Desk events are also gated (`useModuleEnabled` filters in the trigger-type picker, alongside the pre-existing `opportunityEnabled` filter). Command palette, global create, dashboard widgets, reports datasets, and smart view modules remain untouched for this new system.
- [x] Add module-aware RBAC composition: a user needs both tenant module entitlement and role permission; tenant users cannot self-enable modules. **True today by construction, not by a dedicated composition utility**: `assertModuleEnabled` is layered as an additional check alongside each function's existing role/permission checks (never replacing them), so acting on an NBA recommendation or a journey already requires both to pass. Only `requirePlatformAdmin`-gated routes can change a `TenantModuleEntitlement` row — no tenant-admin-facing self-service enable/disable exists anywhere, satisfying "tenant users cannot self-enable" by simply not building that surface.
- [ ] Add module-aware migrations and seeders. **Not built** — no seed data of any kind is conditioned on module entitlement in this pass.
- [ ] Add module lifecycle hooks: on enable create defaults, on disable pause workers/automations, on suspend block actions but preserve reads, on re-enable restore safe state. **Not built** — disabling `NEXT_BEST_ACTION`/`JOURNEY_ORCHESTRATION` blocks new mutating actions (via the guards above) but does not proactively pause in-flight enrollments/recommendations or otherwise reach into already-running state.
- [ ] Add module usage telemetry. **Not built.**
- [ ] Add module billing/readiness metadata: plan tier, trial end, usage limit, soft/hard limit block. **Not built** — `TRIAL` exists as a selectable status with no trial-end-date, limits, or enforcement behind it beyond "counts as enabled."
- [ ] Add module visibility in tenant settings: show enabled/disabled modules, dependency explanation, health, usage, contact/request state. **Not built as a tenant-facing view** — module status is only visible to platform admins today, not surfaced anywhere in tenant Settings for a tenant admin to see their own enabled/disabled modules.
- [ ] Add module-aware demo tenant generation. **Not built.**
- [ ] Add module-aware exports/imports/reports/views: disabled-module datasets/fields must not appear in builders/filters/exports/analytics. **Not built** for this new system (Payouts already has this from earlier work via `isSmartViewModuleEnabled`, unrelated to this catalog).
- [ ] Add module-aware worker registry: every recurring processor declares required module keys, can be paused per tenant, records skipped jobs without noisy retries. **Not built as a declared manifest** — in practice, `nba.processScheduledRefresh` and `journeys.processEnrollmentRefresh` already behave reasonably when a tenant's module is disabled (the underlying `generateRecommendationsForRecord`/`enrollAudienceIntoJourney` calls throw `MODULE_DISABLED` and the worker's per-tenant `.catch(() => undefined)` swallows it silently, so there's no noisy retry) — but this is incidental to how those functions were already written defensively, not a real declared-manifest registry that could report "N tenants skipped due to module X being disabled."
- [x] Add module-aware API smoke tests — **narrow, not the full described matrix**: new `tests/module-entitlements.test.ts` (7 tests) covers default-enabled-with-no-row, explicit disable then blocked, audit log written on every status change, platform-admin bypass, refusing to disable a core module, and rejecting an unknown module key. **Not covered**: dependency failures (no dependencies exist), tenant-creation-bundle tests (no wizard exists), and navigation-visibility/role-permission-intersection tests (covered manually via `tsc`/build/dev-server smoke, not dedicated unit tests). Full suite verified at 196/196 passing (7 new).
- [ ] Add migration/runbook updates for VPS: default module catalog seed, platform-admin enablement workflow, tenant bootstrap steps, emergency module suspension procedure. **Not built** — `deploy/vps/` was not touched; the catalog seed lives only in migration `0037` itself.
