- [ ] Add module packaging rules: each module declares routes, nav items, settings sections, permissions, custom fields, automations, reports, dashboard widgets, worker jobs, seeders, and cleanup hooks in one discoverable manifest. **Not built** — this is a substantial, standalone architectural undertaking (effectively: refactor every existing module to register itself against a manifest) and was not attempted.
- [ ] Add module setup checklist framework. **Not built.**
- [ ] Add module data retention defaults and overrides for disabled modules. **Not built** — disabling a module in this pass never touches its data at all (preserve-by-default, but not a deliberate configurable choice).
- [x] Add tenant-module audit views: who enabled/disabled a module, what changed, and when. **Built the "who/what/when/reason" core**: `TenantModuleAuditLog` records every status change with `moduleKey`, `action`, `reason`, `performedBy`, `performedAt` (queryable via `listTenantModuleAuditLog`, though not yet surfaced in a dedicated UI beyond the Modules card itself reflecting current state). **Not built**: dependency-change tracking (no dependencies exist), "what defaults were created" (no lifecycle hooks create defaults), and "which workers were paused/resumed" / "users/roles gained or lost access" (no lifecycle hooks exist to log).
- [ ] Add platform-safe module disable flow: pre-disable impact scan for active campaigns, automations, views, dashboards, reports, exports, payouts, tasks, pending jobs, and external webhooks. **Not built** — disabling a module in the platform-admin UI is immediate, with no impact-scan confirmation step.
- [ ] Add module health badges: healthy, setup incomplete, connector failing, worker backlog, stale data, disabled by dependency, suspended, or trial expired. **Not built** — the Modules card shows raw status only (Enabled/Trial/Suspended/Disabled), no derived health state.
- [ ] Add module contract tests requiring every module to expose permission metadata, route guards, seed defaults, API smoke paths, and disabled-module behavior before it can be marked enterprise-ready. **Not built** — no such contract-enforcement mechanism exists; this would need the packaging-manifest system above to exist first.
- [x] **Post-implementation adversarial code review of this module, and fix.** An independent review pass found `setTenantModuleStatus` used a select-then-insert-or-update pattern against `TenantModuleEntitlement`, which has a real `unique("tenantId", "moduleKey")` constraint — two concurrent platform-admin actions toggling the same tenant/module could both see "no existing row" and both attempt an insert, one failing on the constraint violation instead of cleanly updating. Fixed by replacing it with a single atomic `INSERT ... ON CONFLICT ("tenantId", "moduleKey") DO UPDATE SET ...`. `tests/module-entitlements.test.ts`'s mock updated to match (7/7 passing); `tsc`, lint, full `vitest` suite, and build verified.

## Priority Module 22 — MCP Server: AI Agent Interface

Scope decision: approved as a new, first-class integration surface, added per explicit request. This is a genuinely new subsystem with no existing code today (confirmed: no MCP server, route, or dependency exists anywhere in this repo) — every item below is `[ ]`. Designed in-session via the brainstorming process before any code was written; nothing here is built yet.

**Architecture note, stated up front**: the MCP layer must be a thin protocol translation over this CRM's *existing* service-layer functions (`src/lib/server/*.ts` — `createLeadForTenant`, `listOpportunitiesForTenant`, the inbuilt reports, etc.), never a new parallel path to the database. A new `src/mcp/` module holds all tool/resource/prompt code and is only ever allowed to import from `src/lib/server/`, not from `src/lib/repositories/` or `src/lib/db/` directly — enforced by an ESLint `no-restricted-imports` rule scoped to that directory. This is what makes every MCP action automatically inherit tenant isolation (RLS/`enterTenantContext`), OWN/TEAM/ALL record-scope, field-permission masking, atomic audit+outbox writes, and rate limiting for free, with zero duplicated business logic — and means every future permission/security fix to the underlying functions protects MCP too, without anyone remembering to update a second implementation.

- [ ] Add the MCP protocol transport layer at `src/app/api/mcp/*`, implementing the current MCP spec's Streamable HTTP transport (JSON-RPC 2.0 over HTTPS, no persistent socket required — fits Next.js API routes) with correct capability negotiation (`initialize`/`initialized` handshake advertising exactly which of tools/resources/prompts this server supports), protocol version negotiation, and structured JSON-RPC error responses (not raw 500s) for every failure mode (auth, permission-denied, validation, not-found, rate-limited).
- [ ] Add the `McpConnection` table and its migration: `id, tenantId, connectionType (USER_DELEGATED | APP_CONNECTOR), name, secretHash, ownerUserId (for USER_DELEGATED), scopeOverride (jsonb, optional narrowing), recordAccess/ownerUserId/fieldPermissions (for APP_CONNECTOR, reusing the exact TenantAppInstall pattern from WP04), agentWritePolicy (jsonb: per-module/per-action write allowlist), isActive, createdAt, lastUsedAt, revokedAt`. RLS-enabled like every other tenant table.
- [ ] Add `USER_DELEGATED` personal tokens: a staff member generates a token from a new `/dashboard/settings/mcp` page (matching the existing ApiKey settings-page UX pattern) — the agent acts as that user by default (their real role/OWN-TEAM-ALL scope/field permissions apply automatically), with an optional self-service narrowing step at creation time (e.g. read-only, or a module allowlist) that can only restrict, never expand, the user's own real permissions. Tokens are listable, renameable, and revocable; revoking takes effect on the connection's next call (no cached-permission window).
- [ ] Add `APP_CONNECTOR` registered connectors: a tenant admin registers a named connector (id + secret shown once, matching `MarketplaceApp`/`TenantAppSecret`'s existing rotation-with-grace-window convention) with its own `recordAccess`/`ownerUserId`/`fieldPermissions` (reusing WP04's mechanism directly, not a reimplementation) plus a new **agent write policy**: an explicit per-module/per-action allowlist (e.g. "may create Tasks and update Lead status; may not delete or touch Payouts") gating *write tool calls specifically*, layered on top of (never instead of) `recordAccess`/`hasAppPermission`-style module grants.
- [ ] Add the write-policy enforcement point: a single shared `assertAgentWriteAllowed(connection, module, action)` check called at the top of every write tool's handler (mirroring how `assertModuleEnabled`/`assertFeatureEnabled` are already layered onto existing mutations elsewhere in this codebase) — denies with a structured JSON-RPC error naming exactly which policy blocked it, never a silent no-op.
- [ ] Add the module-contract-driven tool generator: each existing domain module (Leads, Opportunities, Tasks, Activities, Cases, Telephony, Marketing Journeys, Predictive Scoring, Marketplace, Gamification & Payouts, Product Catalog/Admissions, Partner Portal, Automations, Distribution, Forms, Reports/Dashboards, and every other module in this checklist) registers one small descriptor (`{entity, listFn, getFn, createFn?, updateFn?, filterColumns, writeActions[]}` pointing at its already-existing service functions) in a central `src/mcp/module-registry.ts`. A generator turns each descriptor into a `list_<entity>`/`get_<entity>` MCP tool (always) and `create_<entity>`/`update_<entity>` tools (only where the module defines them and the connection's write policy allows them) — new modules opt in by adding one descriptor, not a bespoke integration.
- [ ] Generate correct MCP tool JSON Schemas from each module's existing filter-column/field metadata (`FilterColumnEntry`, `fieldPermissionMap`) rather than hand-writing a second, driftable schema per tool — so a tool's declared input shape can never silently diverge from what the underlying function actually accepts.
- [ ] Add list-tool pagination following MCP's cursor convention (not a raw page/limit pair) for every generated `list_<entity>` tool, backed by this codebase's existing pagination (extended to genuine keyset pagination where a list already supports it, offset-based otherwise as an honest interim).
- [ ] Add the knowledge-layer Resources: `crm://modules` (every module's purpose + its available tools, generated from the module registry, not hand-maintained prose that can go stale), `crm://schema/<entity>` (fields, types, and meanings per entity, generated from the same filter-column/field-permission metadata the tools already use), and `crm://glossary` (CRM-specific concepts an agent needs to reason correctly: OWN/TEAM/ALL scope, lead vs. opportunity vs. application, pipeline stage semantics, tenant/team boundaries, what "predictive score" fields mean).
- [ ] Add a live-record Resource template (`crm://lead/<id>`, `crm://opportunity/<id>`, etc.) so an agent can be handed a direct, permission-checked link to one record instead of only ever discovering it via a list tool.
- [ ] Add guided Prompts for common agentic workflows: "triage my overdue leads," "summarize this opportunity's full history," "draft a follow-up for a stalled deal," "find leads matching X and Y" — each pre-assembling the right sequence of tool calls so an agent doesn't rediscover the pattern from scratch every session.
- [ ] Add distinct audit attribution: every MCP tool call writes to the existing `AuditLog`/outbox pipeline with a connection-type actor marker (`MCP_USER_DELEGATED:<userId>` / `MCP_APP_CONNECTOR:<connectionId>`) so "what did an AI agent actually do, and as whom" is always separately visible from ordinary human/API-key/marketplace-app activity — surfaced on a new platform-admin "MCP Activity" view reusing the usage-stats pattern already built this session (`getPlatformUsageOverview`/`getPlatformAutomationStats`).
- [ ] Add MCP-specific rate limiting reusing the existing atomic Redis `MULTI`+`EXPIRE NX` primitive (`rate-limit.ts`), keyed per connection (both `USER_DELEGATED` and `APP_CONNECTOR`), with its own capacity-derived budget separate from the human-session/API-key/marketplace-app budgets so a runaway agent loop can't exhaust another surface's limit or vice versa.
- [ ] Add platform-admin oversight: list every `McpConnection` across all tenants, force-revoke any connection, and a platform-wide kill switch (a single flag that rejects all MCP protocol requests immediately, for incident response) independent of any one tenant's or connection's own status.
- [ ] Add client configurability so this is genuinely plug-and-play in a coding agent's own UI/CLI, not just a spec-compliant endpoint nobody can easily reach: the token-creation step in `/dashboard/settings/mcp` (and the connector-registration step in its platform/tenant-admin equivalent) generates ready-to-use, copy-paste connection instructions with the real bearer token already filled in — a `claude mcp add --transport http <name> <tenant-scoped-url>` command line for Claude Code, and the equivalent remote-server JSON block for Claude Desktop/other MCP-config-file clients — rather than making the user hand-assemble a config from generic docs.
- [ ] Add a documented, versioned base URL convention per tenant (e.g. `https://<host>/api/mcp/<tenant-slug>` or an equivalent tenant-scoped path) so a generated client config never silently points at the wrong tenant, plus a discovery/`initialize`-time response that clearly states the connected tenant's name back to the agent (so a human watching the agent's first tool call can immediately confirm it landed on the right workspace).
- [ ] Add a thin, npm-installable stdio bridge CLI (e.g. `npx @crm/mcp-bridge --token=<token>`) for MCP clients that don't yet support the remote Streamable HTTP transport — translates local stdio JSON-RPC to an authenticated call against the same `/api/mcp/*` HTTP endpoint, so no client is left unable to connect purely for transport reasons.
- [ ] Add tests: protocol-level (capability negotiation, JSON-RPC error shapes, pagination cursors), auth (`USER_DELEGATED` scope-narrowing never expands real permissions, `APP_CONNECTOR` write-policy denial, revoked-token rejection, cross-tenant isolation identical to the rest of the app), and per-generated-tool tests confirming a tool's actual DB effect matches what the connecting identity's real permissions should allow — reusing this session's own real-Postgres verification convention for anything permission-shaped, not mocks alone.
- [ ] Suggested build order (even though the design above covers every module up front): (1) the protocol layer, `McpConnection` schema, and write-policy engine in isolation; (2) the module-registry generator applied to just the core entities (Leads, Opportunities, Tasks, Activities, Reports) as the proving ground for the whole pattern, plus the knowledge-layer Resources and client-configurability step so it's genuinely usable end-to-end at that point; (3) the same descriptor pattern applied to every remaining module in this checklist as a mechanical rollout, not a redesign each time.

## Enterprise Upgrade Audit — Existing Functionality Depth

This section tracks cross-cutting improvements to current modules that already exist but still feel basic, fragmented, static, or not enterprise-grade. Mark items complete only after the relevant UI, API, permissions, worker behavior, tests, and demo data are verified.

**How this section was audited, stated once up front rather than repeated 130 times**: unlike the numbered Priority Modules above, every item here describes *polish on top of something that already exists* (its own intro says so: "current modules that already exist but still feel basic"), not a feature to build from zero. None of these 16 subsections' items are checked, and that's accurate -- but "unchecked" here means "the underlying capability is real, this specific cross-cutting refinement of it is not," which is a different, narrower gap than the greenfield modules elsewhere in this document. Rather than write a near-identical "not built" line 130 times, each subsection below gets one status note calling out the specific real overlaps this session's work already created (so a future pass doesn't rediscover them from scratch) and confirming the rest is genuinely unbuilt polish.

### Enterprise Architecture Backbone

**Status: 0 of 8, genuinely unbuilt as unified cross-cutting infrastructure.** One real, relevant partial: "standardize all list APIs on one pagination/filter/sort contract" is *closer* to true than the others -- Leads and Opportunities both already use the same `{data, meta: {total, page, last_page, limit}}` shape (confirmed in Module 9's performance-hardening note), though not every list endpoint in the app follows it and there's no shared contract/type enforcing it. The rest (module manifests, bounded-service folders, a shared domain-event contract, shared lookup hydration, unified data-access-policy helpers, a mutation-transaction helper) describe real architectural refactors this codebase doesn't have -- each individual module instead re-implements its own version of these patterns inline, which is exactly what this bullet group is naming as a maturity gap, not a functional one.

- [ ] Consolidate module manifests, route definitions, sidebar metadata, permission keys, worker job declarations, report datasets, export datasets, and setting sections so each module is discoverable and independently gateable.
- [ ] Introduce bounded-service folders for load-bearing domains (`leads`, `opportunities`, `tasks`, `views`, `automations`, `reports`, `marketing`, `partners`, `payouts`, `gamification`, `scoring`) with clear server/client/shared boundaries.
- [ ] Create a shared domain-event contract for create/update/delete/status-change events, including event id, tenant id, actor id, module, record reference, friendly label, changed fields, idempotency key, and emitted worker jobs.
- [ ] Standardize all list APIs on one pagination/filter/sort contract: page, page size, total, selected ids, filters, search, sort, visible columns, export context, and server-side count semantics.
- [ ] Standardize all detail APIs on one include/expand pattern so detail pages do not create N+1 fetch storms for owner/stage/type/custom fields/tasks/activities/audit/scoring/communications.
- [ ] Add a shared lookup hydration layer that resolves users, teams, sales groups, roles, partners, partner orgs, stages, opportunity types, activity types, lists, views, campaigns, courses, applications, and payouts.
- [ ] Add data-access policy helpers that combine tenant, role, team/sales-group hierarchy, partner organization hierarchy, module entitlement, field permissions, and record sharing in one reusable check.
- [ ] Add a mutation transaction helper with audit log, domain event emission, idempotency, notification enqueue, and worker job enqueue as a consistent post-commit pattern.

### Data Model and Schema Governance

**Status: 0 of 6 as formal governance, but the underlying conventions are largely followed in practice already.** Every migration this session wrote does follow the `tenantId`/`createdAt`/`updatedAt`/`createdBy` + status-check + tenant-index convention consistently (confirmed across dozens of migrations this session alone) -- it's just never been written down or enforced by tooling, so "normalize naming conventions" is really "document and lint what's already the de facto standard," not build it from scratch. Schema-drift protection between migrations and `SCHEMA.md` is real (Module 9's schema-governance bullet). **Genuinely not built**: database-level state-machine constraints (status transitions are enforced in application code, not DB constraints, for most tables), a systematic missing-index review, a formal data-migration/backfill checklist, and custom-field lifecycle governance (rename/deprecate/merge/impact-scan -- `FieldDefinition` supports soft-delete only, per Module 18's data-catalog note).

- [ ] Normalize naming and relationship conventions for all new tables: `tenantId`, `createdAt`, `updatedAt`, `createdBy`, status enums/checks, FK indexes, tenant indexes, audit references, and soft-delete/archive strategy.
- [ ] Add database-level constraints for state machines where feasible, especially payout, invoice, application, campaign, journey, case, scoring model, and module entitlement transitions.
- [ ] Add missing indexes for common dashboard/report/filter paths: owner, status, stage, type, source, campaign, due date, created date, updated date, tenant+module, tenant+record, and tenant+status.
- [ ] Add schema drift protection between migrations, `SCHEMA.md`, local Postgres, VPS Postgres, and production snapshots.
- [ ] Add data migration/backfill checklist for every schema change: default values, existing tenant data, old records, demo data, rollback safety, and verification query.
- [ ] Add custom-field lifecycle governance: create, rename label, deprecate, archive, delete blocked by usage, picklist value merge, dependency tracking, and reporting/automation/view impact scan.

### Permission, Privacy, and Tenant Isolation

**Status: 0 of 6 as the specific systematic tooling described, but real individual coverage exists for several.** Sensitive-field masking (`maskFieldsForUser`) and export/download approval rules (Module 9's export-governance sensitive-field gate) are both real, though narrower than described here (role-permission-driven, not a PII-classification system -- same honest caveat as Module 18's sensitive-field-classification note). Destructive-action approvals exist for several of the named actions individually (payouts, destructive imports, sensitive exports -- Module 18's privileged-action-controls note has the full breakdown) but not as one maker-checker framework. Tenant isolation is enforced throughout via RLS + explicit `tenantId` scoping, with real dedicated regression tests for some route families (`tests/tenant-isolation.test.ts`) but not "every route family" systematically. **Genuinely not built**: an automated RBAC-matrix test generator, and a distinct "external-user security model" as its own abstraction (partner/public-form access is gated ad hoc per route today, e.g. `requireInternalUser`, not through one shared external-user-safe pattern).

- [ ] Build an automated RBAC matrix test generator that checks every route/action against Admin, Manager, Rep, Partner primary, Partner member, Finance, Platform Admin, and unauthenticated users.
- [ ] Add tenant-isolation regression tests for every route family, including list counts, detail fetches, exports, downloads, worker-created records, webhook-created records, and report rollups.
- [ ] Add sensitive-field masking policy for phone, email, parent details, education data, tax/bank details, payout details, recordings/transcripts, notes, and scoring feature snapshots.
- [ ] Add export/download approval rules for sensitive fields and high-volume exports, with request history, approver comments, expiry, and download audit.
- [ ] Add destructive/sensitive action approvals with maker-checker where needed: payout paid, payout held/released, invoice generated, campaign launched, automation published, tenant suspended, model promoted, data purge, and permission changes.
- [ ] Add external-user security model for partner/applicant/public-form contexts, so external users never rely on internal role assumptions.

### API, Workers, Performance, and Reliability

**Status: 0 of 8 as the systematic guarantees named here, but real individual coverage is extensive across this whole session's work.** Expensive actions already run on workers broadly (exports, imports, report rollups, scoring recompute, automation processing, SLA timers, reminders, telephony-adjacent jobs, marketplace sync -- confirmed by `scripts/worker.ts`'s real `recurringJobs` registry, cross-referenced in Module 20 above). Idempotency/retry/dead-letter handling is real for several specific pipelines (webhook outbox, marketplace deliveries, import/export governance) via the same reused atomic-claim pattern throughout. **Genuinely not built as systematic, cross-cutting guarantees**: full API action verification across every route family as one audit (Module 9/18's own security-hardening notes make the same point), a formal N+1/pagination/index performance audit, auto-generated API contract documentation, a unified worker request-history model, and production performance budgets/resilience tests for infrastructure failure (Postgres/Redis/ML-service down) as a tested scenario rather than an assumed graceful-degradation behavior.

- [ ] Add full API action verification for GET/POST/PATCH/DELETE across every route family, including negative auth/RBAC/tenant tests and structured user-friendly errors.
- [ ] Move all expensive actions to workers: exports, report rollups, campaign sends, scoring retraining/recompute, automation due/wait processing, SLA timers, reminders, notifications, telephony post-processing, and analytics rollups.
- [ ] Add performance audits for N+1 queries, missing pagination, missing indexes, overfetching, client-side heavy joins, bundle size, repeated Fast Refresh triggers, and slow dashboard/report endpoints.
- [ ] Add reliability safeguards: idempotency for mutating actions, retry/dead-letter handling, concurrency locks, transaction boundaries for payout/stage/approval flows, and graceful degradation when external services fail.
- [ ] Add API contract documentation generated from route metadata: method, auth, module entitlement, permission key, request schema, response schema, errors, rate limits, and example payload.
- [ ] Add worker request-history model for long-running user-visible actions: export, import, report refresh, score recompute, campaign launch, automation bulk enrollment, payout generation, and invoice generation.
- [ ] Add production performance budgets: max page load, max list API latency, max dashboard widget latency, max report preview time, max worker queue age, and max export generation time by record count.
- [ ] Add resilience tests for Postgres unavailable, Redis unavailable, ML service unavailable, provider connector failing, storage unavailable, and worker crash/retry behavior.

### Import, Export, and Data Operations

**Status: substantially real already -- this subsection is close to satisfied by Module 9's import/export-governance and dedupe/merge-center work, confirmed accurate in this same audit pass.** "Unified import center" (templates, staged validation preview, duplicate strategy, `PENDING_APPROVAL` gate, worker processing with progress polling, partial-failure reporting) is real -- only true rollback-of-a-completed-import is missing, an explicitly named and honestly-flagged gap in Module 9's own note. "Unified export framework" (selected-vs-filtered scope, templates, sensitive-field approval, request history, expiry, download audit) is also real and used from the one shared `QueueExportButton` component across the whole app. Duplicate merge (leads/opportunities, not users/partners) is real via the dedupe/merge center. **Genuinely not built**: bulk field-update-with-preview as a distinct correction tool (bulk actions exist per-module, but not a generic "preview then apply" correction UI), owner-reassignment/status-correction as bulk tools specifically, and a consolidated data-lineage view (the underlying data mostly exists scattered across `AuditLog`/`ImportJob`/webhook and API-key `lastUsedAt`, just never joined into one "where did this record come from" view).

- [ ] Add a unified import center with templates, validation staging, duplicate strategy, approval, worker processing, progress, partial-failure downloads, rollback, and import-to-automation trigger controls.
- [ ] Add a unified export framework available from each module's current list/view/filter context, with selected rows vs all filtered records, columns, format, approval, request history, expiry, and download audit.
- [ ] Add data correction tools: bulk field update with preview, duplicate merge, owner reassignment, status/stage correction, source/UTM cleanup, and undo/rollback where feasible.
- [ ] Add data lineage visibility: source of record creation/update, import job, form, API key, webhook, automation, campaign, partner submission, and manual user action.
- [ ] Add data operation tests for large imports, large exports, selected vs filtered counts, tenant timezone/currency formatting, idempotent retries, rollback, and permission-denied downloads.

### Connector and External-System Operations

**Status: substantially real already, spread across several already-audited pieces of this same document.** Connector readiness/monitoring exists via the connector-health dashboard (Module 9: SMTP/WhatsApp/SMS/telephony/webhooks/storage/ML-service/Redis/Postgres). Connector test consoles with secret redaction and response diagnostics are real for both webhooks (`sendTestWebhookDelivery`) and marketplace apps (`sendTestAppEvent`, Module 16). External-event idempotency and replay are real in multiple places: inbound webhook dedupe keys, outbound webhook/marketplace-delivery idempotency keys, and a real per-delivery Replay action (marketplace diagnostics). **Genuinely not built**: sandbox/live mode as a formal, UI-badged separation per provider (providers are configured once, with no distinct test-vs-live credential slots), and connector-specific *alerting* (the health dashboard is pull/on-demand today, not a push alert when a connector degrades -- distinct from the per-app/per-webhook failure notifications that do exist for delivery failures specifically).

- [ ] Add connector readiness checklist for SMTP, WhatsApp, SMS, telephony, storage, ML service, webhooks, and future marketplace apps.
- [ ] Add connector test consoles with safe sample payloads, secret redaction, response diagnostics, last successful request, failure reason, and retry advice.
- [ ] Add connector-specific monitoring alerts for expired credentials, webhook signature failures, provider rate limits, delivery failures, stale syncs, queue backlog, and repeated retries.
- [ ] Add sandbox/live mode separation for every external provider, including separate credentials, test sends, fake payment/call/message events where applicable, and clear UI badges.
- [ ] Add external event idempotency and replay tooling: dedupe keys, replay by event id, dead-letter repair, payload audit, and safe reprocessing permissions.

## Automations

- [x] Add automation-level exit conditions. Builder exposes workflow-level exit rules with AND/OR matching and dropdown-backed values where field options exist; runtime stops matching records before node execution.
- [x] Add loop protection and per-record execution caps. Builder exposes max runs per record and max steps per run; runtime records skipped executions once a record reaches its cap and stops long runs at the step cap.
- [x] Make builder context-aware by trigger. Add-step popup now filters available nodes based on lead, opportunity, activity, or task trigger scope, and condition defaults use the current trigger module instead of always defaulting to lead fields.
- [x] Fix Multi If/Else branch defaults. New Multi If/Else nodes now create only `If 1` and `Else`; else-if branches are added only when configured, and stale empty else-if branch nodes are cleaned up on save.
- [x] Add task actions to automation builder/runtime. Automations can create, update, assign, reschedule, and complete tasks where trigger context supports tasks.
- [x] Add real messaging queue actions when email/SMS/WhatsApp integrations are configured. Automation Send Message nodes now enqueue Email, WhatsApp, or SMS into `CommunicationOutbox` for worker delivery instead of being UI-only.
- [x] Add bulk enrollment and bulk update execution tracking. Confirmed no enrollment mechanism existed at all (not even a manual single-record one) -- this is genuinely new, modeled on `ImportJob`'s row-lifecycle (this codebase's real "batch job with tracking" precedent: insert `PROCESSING` up front, per-item try/catch accumulates counts/errors, update to a final status), since `AutomationExecution` already exists per individual run but had no concept of a parent batch. New `AutomationEnrollmentJob` table (migration `0032_automation_enrollment.sql`) and `enrollRecordsInAutomation` (`automations-postgres.ts`) run the target automation's own workflow steps directly for a hand-picked batch of existing Lead/Opportunity records, bypassing normal trigger matching entirely (enrollment IS the trigger) -- capped at 500 records per call, synchronous like this codebase's other bulk actions (e.g. `bulkUpdateTasksForTenant`) rather than a new background-worker job. New `POST /api/automation-v2/:id/enroll` and `GET .../enrollment-jobs`, plus a real UI entry point: an "Enroll" button on the automation builder opens `EnrollRecordsDialog` (multi-select records, run, see per-job success/failure history) -- modeled on the existing `TestWorkflowDialog`'s record-fetching pattern.
- [x] Add sub-automation action. New `run_automation` action node lets one automation's workflow directly invoke another automation's workflow for the same record. **Confirmed and closed the one real risk**: no recursion/cycle guard existed anywhere in the execution engine (the existing `maxExecutionsPerRecord`/`maxStepsPerRun` caps only bound a single automation's own node count, not cross-automation depth) -- a naive implementation calling the existing `executeAutomationWorkflow` primitive directly would let an A→B→A chain recurse until the call stack failed. Fixed with a `visitedAutomationIds: Set<string>` threaded through `executeAutomationWorkflow`'s `options` (seeded with the top-level automation's own id) and `executeAutomationAction`'s new last parameter: a repeat visit to an automation already running in the current chain is silently skipped, matching this file's existing convention of quietly no-op'ing an action whose target can't be resolved rather than throwing and failing the whole execution. The sub-automation's run gets its own real `AutomationExecution` row (tagged `context: {subAutomation: true}`), so it shows up correctly in the Automation Performance report above. Builder UI: a plain automation picker (Select), gated only to active automations.
- [x] Enhance split test with percentage allocation and performance analytics. Percentage allocation and variant branch creation were already implemented; confirmed the taken branch was already being recorded in `AutomationExecution.executionLog.steps[].result` (stamped by the existing `automationBranchLabelForNode` at run time) -- so "performance analytics" was a pure new report over already-existing data, no new instrumentation needed. New "Split Test Performance" inbuilt report groups executions by `(automation, split node, variant)`, computing each variant's share of that node's traffic and its completion rate (whether the overall execution reached `COMPLETED`) as the performance signal -- not a full statistical-significance A/B test, a real but intentionally bounded metric.
- [x] Enhance wait nodes with exact date/time, timezone, day/time windows, timeout continue/exit, and max wait limits. The remaining piece (timezone-aware calendar semantics) is done: `automationDelayDate`'s day/time window check previously compared against the **server process's local time** via native `Date.getHours()`/`getMinutes()` -- no tenant timezone was involved at all, confirmed via direct read. Now wired to the tenant's real configured timezone (`getTenantTimeZone`, the same helper already used for report/export date formatting elsewhere) via two new small helpers (`zonedParts`/`zonedPartsToUtc`) that correctly read and construct wall-clock times in an arbitrary IANA zone using the standard offset-correction technique (`Intl.DateTimeFormat` + a guess-and-correct UTC round-trip), rather than native `Date` methods which are always server-local. `automationDelayDate` is now async (its one call site updated); wait-node behavior for tenants outside the server's own timezone is now actually correct instead of silently using the wrong clock.
- [x] Add explicit Triggered Activity vs Lead Activity condition source. Confirmed this wasn't "ambiguous" as originally worded -- for a Lead-triggered automation, there was no way to reference any Activity field at all (not a competing/wrong meaning, a total gap); `activity.<field>` for Activity-triggered automations unambiguously means the specific Activity that fired the trigger. Added the missing second source: a new "Lead's Latest Activity" condition field group (`leadActivity.<field>`) available for Lead/Opportunity/Task-scoped triggers, not just Activity-scoped ones. Runtime-wise, **deliberately did not make the (deeply, synchronously, recursively used) condition evaluator async** -- that would have meant threading `await` through every branch/condition/exit-check call site in the whole engine, a much larger and riskier change than this item warranted. Instead, `attachLatestLeadActivity` runs once per workflow execution (one extra query, at the top of `executeAutomationWorkflow`) and merges the lead's most recent Activity onto a copy of `record` as `record.leadActivity`, which the existing generic-object-path resolution in `valueAtPath` already handles with zero changes to the evaluator itself.
- [x] Add opportunity share/stop-share actions after sharing model exists. The sharing model (`RecordShare`) now exists (built as part of the "Reporting, Dashboards, and Governance" section's record-sharing item) -- two new action nodes, `share_opportunity` (additive: merges into whatever's already shared, so a recurring automation can't wipe out a manually-added share) and `stop_share_opportunity` (removes just the specified targets, or clears all sharing if none are specified), calling straight into the just-built `RecordShare` upsert logic. Builder UI: a Users/Teams multi-select picker matching the Save View / Record Share dialog's existing target-menu convention.

**Enterprise-polish follow-up** (checklist consolidation: merged in from a former separate "### Automations" Enterprise-Upgrade-Audit subsection; re-verified against the delivered work above rather than left as a stale "all open" list — two of its five items were directly contradicted by what's actually built, so this isn't a fresh audit, it's a correction):

- [ ] Upgrade automation builder into a polished context-aware visual builder: node popup configuration, auto layout, no broken side panel, clean multi-if/else, branch labels, validation warnings, and read-only execution preview. **Partially real**: multi-if/else branch defaults are fixed (see above) and the builder is already context-aware by trigger (filters available nodes/condition defaults by trigger scope, see above); branch labels exist for reporting purposes (`automationBranchLabelForNode`, stamped into `AutomationExecution.executionLog` and used by the Split Test Performance report below) though not necessarily as an on-canvas visual label. **Still genuinely open**: auto layout, a general validation-warnings pass, and a read-only execution preview.
- [ ] Add enterprise execution controls: bulk enrollment, re-enrollment rules, dedupe, idempotency keys, sub-automations, transactional step groups, error branches, retry policy, pause/resume, and dry-run simulation. **Directly contradicted on 2 of 10 sub-items by the delivered work above — both are now real**: bulk enrollment (`AutomationEnrollmentJob`, see above) and sub-automations (`run_automation` action node with cross-automation cycle protection, see above). **Still genuinely open**: re-enrollment rules, dedupe, idempotency keys, transactional step groups, error branches, a distinct retry policy, pause/resume, and dry-run simulation as a bulk concept (`TestWorkflowDialog` and the in-builder "simulate" patterns used elsewhere in this document only test a single synthetic record, not a bulk dry-run of an enrollment batch).
- [ ] Add condition/value selector consistency: all dropdown/picklist/lookup fields support multi-select where valid, values are fetched dynamically, and unavailable values explain module/permission causes. Genuinely still open — not contradicted by anything delivered above.
- [ ] Add automation performance analytics: enrollment count, success/failure, step latency, branch conversion, skipped reasons, downstream impact, and worker queue health. **Partially real, via two different reports built elsewhere**: the "Automation Performance" inbuilt report (`## Reporting, Dashboards, and Governance` section) already covers per-automation run/enrollment counts, success rate, and average execution time; the Split Test Performance report above covers branch conversion for split-test nodes specifically (not branch conversion generally). **Still genuinely open**: step-level latency, a skipped-reasons breakdown, downstream impact, and worker queue health specifically for automation processing.
- [ ] Add automation safety: module entitlement guards, role permissions, destructive action approval, loop/cycle detection, rate limits, audit logs, and tenant-isolation tests. **Directly contradicted on most sub-items by delivered work, both here and in other modules — re-verified rather than left as "all open"**: module entitlement guards and role permissions are real (Module 21's Automations entitlement-gap fix); loop/cycle detection is real (per-record execution caps and max-steps-per-run above, plus the sub-automation cross-automation cycle guard above); a tenant-wide automation/job rate limit (1000 triggering events/minute) is real (Module 18's rate-limiting-and-abuse-controls bullet); audit logging is real via the standard `AuditLog` conventions every mutation in this app already goes through. **Still genuinely open**: a destructive-action-specific approval gate for automation-triggered mutations (the general privileged-action-controls mechanism, Module 18, doesn't specifically cover automation-node-triggered actions), and a dedicated tenant-isolation test suite for the automation engine specifically.

## CRM Objects

- [x] Add core/system activities. Implemented as lazy, idempotent-per-tenant `ActivityType` provisioning rather than a fixed seeded list: `ensureSystemActivityType(user, name, icon, color)` (`src/lib/server/crm.ts:407`) looks up an existing `ActivityType` row by name/tenant and creates it on first use if missing. Called for "Call" (telephony call logging, both the outbound-call flow at `crm.ts:1777` and the inbound telephony webhook at `telephony-webhook.ts:272`) and "Page Visit" (website tracking ingestion below) — a tenant only gets the system activity types its actual usage triggers, not a fixed upfront set.
- [x] Add website tracking script and web activity ingestion. `GET /api/tracking/script` serves a small tenant-scoped JS snippet (cached 5 minutes) that reads `leadId`/`email`/UTM params from the page URL and beacons page views via `fetch(..., {keepalive:true})` to `POST /api/tracking/page-visit`, which calls `ingestWebsiteVisitForTenant`. That function matches the visit to an existing Lead by `leadId` or email (silently no-ops with `{tracked:false, reason:"NO_MATCHING_LEAD"}` for unmatched anonymous traffic — there's no anonymous-visitor identity/cookie model), then logs a real "Page Visit" activity (via `ensureSystemActivityType`) with the URL/title/referrer in the notes. No visit deduplication or session grouping — every matched page load creates its own activity row.
- [x] Add static lists and smart lists. `LeadList.type` is `STATIC` or `SMART` (`src/lib/repositories/lead-lists-postgres.ts`). STATIC lists store explicit membership rows in `LeadListMember` (add/remove via `addLeadsToLeadListForTenant`/`removeLeadFromLeadListForTenant`); SMART lists store a `filters` JSON array reusing the same lead-filter condition shape as saved views, and membership/count is computed live at read time by re-running `listLeadsForTenant` with those filters (`countLeadsForTenant`, `getLeadListForTenant`) — there's no cached/materialized smart-list membership, so a large tenant's smart list count is recomputed on every list-index and list-detail fetch.
- [x] Add list automation triggers and add/remove list actions. Adding a lead to a static list fires a real automation trigger — `runAutomationsForEvent(user, "LEAD_ADDED_TO_LIST", "LEAD", leadId, {id, leadId, listId})` (`crm.ts:1212`) — so "when added to list X" automations work. **Asymmetric, confirmed by direct code read**: removing a lead from a list only writes an audit log (`crm.ts:1217-1221`) with no corresponding `LEAD_REMOVED_FROM_LIST` trigger (left as a documented gap per an explicit decision — not built). **Correction to an earlier pass of this audit, and a real bug fixed, not just documented**: this note originally claimed no `add_to_list`/`remove_from_list` automation *action* node type existed at all — that was wrong. Both node types have real entries in the automation builder's palette and a working config panel (a Lead List picker, `automations-v2/[id]/page.tsx`), but `automations-postgres.ts`'s execution engine had zero matching branch for either — a workflow built with this node looked fully configured and saved cleanly, but silently did nothing at runtime, with no error surfaced anywhere. Fixed by adding a real `add_to_list`/`remove_from_list` branch to `executeAutomationAction` that reuses `addLeadsToLeadListForTenant`/`removeLeadFromLeadListForTenant` from `crm.ts` (dynamic `import()` to avoid a circular import, since `crm.ts` itself imports this file) — so an automation-driven add fires the exact same `LEAD_ADDED_TO_LIST` trigger and audit-log entry the manual "Add to List" UI action already does, not a second divergent code path. 2 new tests in `tests/automations-postgres.test.ts` covering both directions, including resolving the lead via `record.leadId` when the triggering entity is an Opportunity. Smart lists, being filter-computed rather than membership-based, still have no add/remove events to trigger on or action to perform — that part remains genuinely not applicable.

## Reporting, Dashboards, and Governance

**Checklist consolidation note**: this section merges two former separate top-level sections ("Reporting and Admin" and "Reporting & Dashboards") that had drifted into tracking the same evolving reporting subsystem from two angles, plus one exact duplicate bullet and one dead pointer bullet that have been removed rather than kept twice (see the two inline notes below marking exactly what was merged/removed). It also folds in, at the end, the still-open items from the former "### Reports and Dashboards" Enterprise-Upgrade-Audit subsection, re-annotated against what Module 17 — Advanced Analytics and BI Layer actually closed since that subsection was last written (Module 17 itself builds directly on top of everything in this section — see its own back-reference note). The governance-only bullets just below (field-level audit trail, tenant-wide audit log, field-level permissions, record sharing) aren't pure reporting features, but are kept in this section rather than split out, since that's where they were originally tracked and splitting them into a third section would just recreate this same fragmentation problem.

**Governance: audit trail, field permissions, and record sharing**

- [x] Add field-level audit trail for leads, opportunities, and activities. Each repo's `createAuditLog` call is paired with a local `fieldDiff`/`diff` helper (`leads-postgres.ts:307`, `opportunities-postgres.ts:360`, `activities-postgres.ts:277`) that compares an explicit per-entity allow-list of fields (e.g. Lead: `name/email/phone/company/source/status/ownerId`) between the before/after record and writes only the changed keys into `AuditLog.diff` as `{field: {before, after}}` — not a full before/after blob diffed generically. `getGovernanceHistoryForTenant`/`GET /api/governance/history/[entityType]/[entityId]` reads this back with stage/type/activity-type IDs resolved to human labels (via `valueLabels`) for real display, powering the `RecordHistory` component on Lead and Opportunity detail pages.
- [x] Show activity modification history with changed values in activity timeline. Not a separate history tab — the shared `Timeline` component (`src/components/timeline/timeline.tsx`) renders each activity's audit `diff` inline and expandable per-row (`expandedActivityIds`), with field-specific value formatting (`formatAuditActivityValue` resolves `typeId`/`stageId`/`opportunityTypeId` to real labels via the same `valueLabels` map, not raw IDs) — falls back to a raw before/after key comparison (`activityChangedFields`) if `diff` wasn't populated (older rows / non-UPDATE actions).
- [x] Add tenant-wide audit log endpoint for all modules. `listAuditLogsForTenant`/`GET /api/audit-logs` (also mirrored at `/api/governance/audit-logs`) queries `AuditLog` tenant-wide with optional `entityType`/`entityTypes[]`/`entityId`/`action` filters, capped at 200 most-recent rows, with actor names/emails resolved — not scoped to a single entity like `getGovernanceHistoryForTenant` above, this is the cross-module admin view.
- [x] Add field-level permissions. `PermissionTemplate.permissions.fieldPermissions` maps a scope (`lead` / `opportunity` / `opportunity:<typeId>` / `activity` / `activity:<typeId>`) to a per-field `visible`/`readonly`/`hidden` access level; `fieldPermissionMap` (`src/lib/server/crm.ts:180`) merges a user's assigned templates (base scope first, then type-specific overrides layered on top) into one effective map. Enforced on both directions: `maskFieldsForUser` nulls out `hidden` fields on read (and flags `${field}Hidden: true` so the UI can render a lock icon instead of a blank), and `editablePayloadForUser` strips `hidden`/`readonly` fields from incoming update payloads server-side before they reach the repository layer — a client can't bypass the restriction by sending the field anyway. Configured via a real admin UI/API at `/api/permission-templates`.
- [x] Add record sharing and opportunity sharing. Confirmed Lead/Opportunity visibility was previously governed *only* by `ownerId` + `Role.permissions.recordAccess` (OWN/TEAM/ALL) — no per-record explicit grant mechanism existed, and neither table had a jsonb column to piggyback a Saved-View-style `sharedUserIds` array onto without a migration either way. Migration `0031_record_sharing.sql` adds a single `RecordShare` table keyed by `(tenantId, recordType, recordId)` — one row per record, covering both Leads and Opportunities via a `recordType` discriminator (`LEAD`/`OPPORTUNITY`) rather than two near-duplicate per-module tables, the same `(recordType, recordId)` keying convention `RecordScore` already established for cross-object concerns. **Scoped to Users + Teams only** (not sales groups/roles, unlike Saved View sharing's four target types) — covers the real "share with a person or their team" case without adding two more target types on day one; noted explicitly as a scope decision, not an oversight. Enforcement sits directly in `buildLeadWhere`/`buildWhere` (`leads-postgres.ts`/`opportunities-postgres.ts`) as an `or`-clause alongside the existing `ownerId = $N` check for OWN-scoped users — a record shared with you (directly, or via your team) becomes visible through the exact same list/get/update/delete code paths every other query already goes through, not a separate access layer bolted on afterward. New `RecordShareDialog` component (modeled visually on Saved View's target-menu sharing UI, reused across both entities) plus `PUT/GET /api/leads/:id/share` and `/api/opportunities/:id/share`, gated to the record's own owner or a TEAM/ALL-scoped user — not every user who merely has read access via an existing share, which would let a shared user re-share further. `tsc`, lint, full test suite, and `npm run build` all verified clean for all three of these items; the new endpoints and UI were also smoke-tested against a live dev server.

**Report query engine and rollup infrastructure**

- [x] Add safe cross-object report query backend and custom report builder with joins **(merged, checklist consolidation)** — this document previously tracked the same `/api/reports/query` endpoint under two separate bullets ("Add custom report builder with joins" and "Add safe cross-object report query backend"); one is kept here, the other removed as an exact duplicate. `src/lib/server/reporting-query.ts` backs `/api/reports/query`, a backend-safe structured JSON query endpoint using a structured JSON definition validated against a hardcoded object/field catalog, executed via a Supabase query builder plus in-memory joins — never raw user SQL. Supports Lead/Opportunity/Activity roots plus owner, stage/type, activity, and assignment-log joins (later widened further by Module 17's dataset-catalog-extension work to also join Task/TelephonyCallLog/Case/Partner/Payout/Communication/Journey-enrollment/RecordScore/custom fields — see that module for the full current join list).
- [x] Add reporting rollup schema migration (`migrations/0008_reporting_rollups.sql`) for `ReportDefinition`, `ReportRollup`, `ReportRefreshState`, and `ReportRefreshJob`, with tenant RLS, refresh state, manual refresh job tracking, and null-scope uniqueness.
- [x] Audit current dashboard/reporting implementation: widgets support `STAT`, `TREND`, `BAR`, `FUNNEL`; existing reports were leads/opportunities/activities summary plus metadata-only custom reports; existing custom report model had no join execution.
- [x] Ship all 10 inbuilt report endpoints, each `tsc` + build verified and checked against real `tenant_demo` data where seeded data exists:
  - `/api/reports/inbuilt/funnel-by-stage`
  - `/api/reports/inbuilt/funnel-by-source-campaign`
  - `/api/reports/inbuilt/rep-performance`
  - `/api/reports/inbuilt/sla-response-breaches`
  - `/api/reports/inbuilt/lead-source-roi`
  - `/api/reports/inbuilt/reassignment-impact`
  - `/api/reports/inbuilt/activity-call-volume-trends`
  - `/api/reports/inbuilt/commission-payout-summary`
  - `/api/reports/inbuilt/cohort-funnel-progression`
  - `/api/reports/inbuilt/data-quality`
- [x] Add persona dashboard presets for Admin, Manager, Rep, and Partner as ordinary customizable `DashboardWidget` rows via `/api/dashboard-widgets/presets`; dashboard manager now seeds the server-chosen persona preset instead of hard-coded client defaults.
- [x] Extend dashboard widget data mapping to support report-backed `STAT`, `BAR`, and `TREND` widgets while preserving the existing widget-library/dashboard-manager components.
- [x] Validate permission-scoped internal dashboard/report behavior against seeded Admin/Manager/Rep users. Partner preset/report filtering is implemented via partner role detection and `partnerId = user.id`, but the current seed data has no partner user/profile, so live partner-scope verification still needs a partner fixture.
- [x] Add drill-down links from aggregate report/dashboard values to filtered record lists — inbuilt report previews now link stage/source/owner/activity-period/partner/cohort/data-quality rows to the relevant filtered records where a safe destination exists.
- [x] Add rollup/report calculation unit tests for edge cases (empty date ranges, leads with no activity, partial periods): `tests/reporting-calculations.test.ts` covers activity buckets, reassignment/no-activity breaches, cohort partial periods, and data-quality stale/missing/duplicate cases.
- [x] Wire rollup refresh execution jobs to compute and persist heavy report snapshots — `/api/reports/rollups/refresh` supports manual queue/run-now, `/api/reports/rollups/process-jobs` processes pending jobs, `ReportRollup` stores computed payloads, and `ReportRefreshState` tracks freshness/error state.

**Scheduled reports**

- [x] Add scheduled report email subscriptions: `ReportSchedule` + `ReportEmailDelivery` migration (`0009_report_schedules.sql`), CRUD APIs under `/api/reports/schedules`, and cron-style processor at `/api/reports/schedules/process-due`. Current app has no SMTP/mail adapter, so processor records PENDING delivery/outbox rows with rendered report payloads for a future sender. **(Checklist consolidation: this document also had a second, separate "Add scheduled report subscriptions" line under the former "Reporting and Admin" heading, whose own text already said it was "the same feature as, and fully covered by" this bullet — that duplicate pointer line has been removed rather than kept twice.)** Later closed further by Module 17's scheduled-extracts bullet (real XLSX/PDF formats and generation retry, on top of the CSV/email/links/expiry/audit this bullet already delivered).

**Later additions (new inbuilt reports)**

- [x] Add automation performance reports. New "Automation Performance" inbuilt report (`getAutomationPerformanceReportForTenant`/`calculateAutomationPerformanceReport` in `inbuilt-reports.ts`, flat-fetch + JS bucketing per this codebase's established reporting convention) shows per-automation run counts, success rate, and average execution time (`completedAt - startedAt`, mirroring `RepPerformanceReport`'s `avgFirstResponseMinutes` calculation shape). Automations that have never fired are seeded into the report too (not just ones with execution history) — a configured-but-dead automation is itself a useful signal, not something that should silently disappear. Registered at `/api/reports/inbuilt/automation-performance` and in the Reports page's `INBUILT_REPORT_OPTIONS` (that array's length is now used directly for the "N reports" badge instead of a hardcoded number, so it can't drift again).
- [x] Add form drop-off analytics by step/tab/field. Confirmed first that no data source for this existed at all: `FormSubmission` only ever records a completed submission, and the renderer's only server call was the final submit. This required genuinely new instrumentation, not just a new report: migration `0030_form_progress_events.sql` adds `FormProgressEvent` (tenantId, formId, sessionId, tabId, tabIndex, createdAt); `public-form-renderer.tsx` now beacons to a new `POST /api/public/forms/:identifier/progress` (via `navigator.sendBeacon`, falling back to a `keepalive` fetch) on mount and on every tab change, using a per-page-load random session id with no identity attached. The beacon endpoint always returns success even on a malformed body, so a lost/partial beacon (common with `sendBeacon` firing right as a tab closes) never surfaces as a console error to a real visitor. New "Form Drop-off Analytics" inbuilt report (`getFormDropOffReportForTenant`/`calculateFormDropOffReport`) computes, per form and tab, how many distinct sessions reached that tab and the drop-off count/rate versus the previous tab. **Scoped deliberately to only the public renderer** (`/f/[slug]`) — not `contextual-forms-panel.tsx` (the internal-user-fills-a-form-on-a-Lead/Opportunity-page variant), since drop-off/abandonment analytics is a real-visitor-traffic concept that doesn't meaningfully apply to an internal rep filling out a form in-context. **Also scoped tenant-wide rather than per-form**: the Reports page's generic inbuilt-report runner has no per-report parameter UI (every existing inbuilt report takes zero or one fixed default param), so this returns one row per (form, tab) across every form rather than requiring a new form-picker UI surface — still genuinely useful, and rows naturally group by form when sorted. **Cross-referenced from "Forms and CRM Placement"/the "### Forms" audit note**, which previously (inaccurately) said this data was collected but never read back out as analytics.
- [x] Add activity SLA reports. Folded into the "SLA & Response Breaches" inbuilt report (`/api/reports/inbuilt/sla-response-breaches`, `inbuilt-reports.ts`) rather than a standalone report: each owner row tracks `responseBreaches` (first-response SLA) and a separate `activitySlaBreaches` count (`activity.slaStatus === "BREACHED"`, or an overdue `slaTarget` with no `completedAt`) side by side, and rows sort by response breaches first, activity breaches second — one report surfaces both SLA dimensions together rather than requiring two separate report runs.

**Still open (checklist consolidation: folded in from the former "### Reports and Dashboards" Enterprise-Upgrade-Audit subsection, re-annotated against what Module 17 — Advanced Analytics and BI Layer has since closed, rather than left as a stale "0 of 7" list)**

- [ ] Upgrade custom report builder UI for complex joins, view/report sources, grouping, summaries, formulas, charts, filters, drill-down, scheduled delivery, and export governance without long-page clutter. The underlying joins/formulas/charts/scheduled-delivery/export-governance capability is real (this section, plus Module 17's semantic metric layer and chart-library-expansion bullets) — this item is specifically about the builder's own UI/UX (long-page clutter, view/report-source consolidation), which is still genuinely unbuilt.
- [ ] Add dashboard builder depth: sections/tabs, dashboard-level filters, cross-filtering, widget permissions, refresh/freshness status, persona defaults, and per-user layouts. **Mostly done via Module 17's own "advanced dashboard builder" bullet** (7 of 10 named sub-items there: true 2D drag/drop grid, dashboard tabs, saved states, cross-filtering, sharing, persona templates, and whole-dashboard PDF export). **Still genuinely open**: breakpoint-based responsive column layout (today's grid is fluid-width, not multi-device-breakpoint-aware), a general dashboard-level filter bar (only the click-driven cross-filter exists), and drill-down-to-list-view.
- [ ] Add inbuilt report discoverability in UI: categorized gallery, descriptions, owner/persona scope, last updated, sample preview, favorite/pin, and open/drill/export actions. Still genuinely open — not addressed by Module 17 or elsewhere.
- [ ] Add report reliability checks: correct counts for filtered/selected exports, tenant timezone formatting, no raw ids, query timeout handling, pagination, and large-report worker processing. **Mostly done already, scattered across this document rather than as one audited pass**: tenant timezone formatting is real and pervasive (`date-format.ts`, Phase 0), pagination and worker-backed processing are real (rollups above, plus Module 9's export/import governance), and query timeout handling is real for the custom-report-builder's ad hoc query path (Module 17's analytics-performance-layer bullet, a 15s `Promise.race`). **Still genuinely open, and not verified as one coordinated check**: a dedicated audit specifically confirming correct row counts for filtered-vs-selected exports, and a full "no raw ids anywhere in a report/export" sweep (the friendly-name convention is followed in practice but not verified as literally zero exceptions).
- [ ] Add metric definition governance: certified vs draft metrics, metric owner, formula versioning, data freshness, permitted dimensions, and deprecation warnings. **Cross-reference, checkbox deliberately left unchecked here**: Module 17's own semantic-metric-layer bullet now has a real, substantial version of most of this (certified-vs-uncertified status, metric owner, permitted dimensions via the same `FIELD_CATALOG` the report builder uses, deprecation status/warnings) — but "formula versioning" doesn't apply to what was built (metrics are object+field+aggregation, deliberately no free-text formulas at all, so there's no formula to version) and "data freshness" isn't tracked per metric (a metric computes live, same as every other report in this codebase, except where Module 17's later per-metric grain feature is explicitly configured). Not checking this box off the back of that work since this bullet's framing (draft-vs-certified, formula versioning) implies a different, formula-based metric model than what actually got built.
- [ ] Add report testing fixtures for every inbuilt report and all critical demo reports, including empty ranges, partial periods, duplicate records, missing owners, partner scoping, and disabled-module datasets. Still genuinely open as one dedicated fixture suite — individual reports have their own edge-case tests (the rollup/report-calculation unit tests above, and Module 17's own per-report tests), but not a systematic fixture sweep across every inbuilt report.
- [ ] Add dashboard performance safeguards: widget timeout, fallback cached data, stale badge, refresh queue, per-widget query budget, and slow-widget admin diagnostics. **Partially done via Module 17's data-freshness-controls bullet**: a stale-data badge and fallback-to-last-known-good-data on a failed widget refresh are both real. **Still genuinely open**: a per-widget request timeout, a generalized refresh queue (today's scheduled refresh queue is rollup-specific, not per-dashboard-widget), a per-widget query budget, and slow-widget admin diagnostics.

## Design System Consolidation

- [x] Audit every MUI-importing file (128 found) and confirm the color-token source of truth (`src/theme.ts`'s M3 `md3Colors`, ported byte-for-byte).
- [x] Migrate M3 color tokens to Tailwind v4 CSS variables in `src/app/globals.css` (`:root`/`.dark` blocks + `@theme inline` mapping), preserving every ported hex value exactly; verified against compiled CSS output.
- [x] Migrate all 128 MUI-importing files to shadcn/Tailwind — zero `@mui/*`/`@emotion/*` imports remain anywhere in `src/`.
- [x] Build a TanStack-Table-based `DataTable` primitive (`src/components/ui/data-table.tsx`) as the shadcn-idiomatic replacement for MUI's `x-data-grid`/`StandardDataGrid`, migrate every consumer, then delete `standard-data-grid.tsx` and remove `@mui/x-data-grid`.
- [x] Build new shadcn primitives as gaps were found, following existing `src/components/ui/` conventions: `skeleton`, `tooltip`, `avatar`, `badge`, `alert`, `accordion`, `radio-group` (all Radix-backed, no new MUI usage introduced at any point).
- [x] Delete `src/theme.ts` and `src/types/mui-theme.d.ts`; strip `ThemeRegistry.tsx` down to just the `next-themes` provider (no more `AppRouterCacheProvider`/`ThemeProvider`/`CssBaseline`/`LocalizationProvider`); remove `@mui/*` and `@emotion/*` from `package.json`.
- [x] Theme registry + selector — 4 named M3-structured color themes (Forest [default/original green], Ocean, Sunset, Grape), each with light+dark variants, selected via a `data-color-theme` attribute on `<html>` (`src/app/globals.css`), persisted to `localStorage` + a `next-themes`-style no-flash inline script in `layout.tsx`, with a live-preview swatch picker plus a light/dark/system mode toggle under Settings → General → Appearance (`src/components/settings/color-theme-picker.tsx`, `mode-toggle.tsx`). Root-mounted via `ColorThemeProvider`.
- [x] Baseline UX pass: compact/comfortable density toggle (built into `DataTable`, persisted per-table via `localStorage`); empty/loading/error states standardized on `EmptyState`/`ErrorState`/`TableSkeleton`/`PageSkeleton`; action-label-vs-toast-text audit (spot-checked across gamification/payouts, automations builder, and settings/integrations — no wording drift found, since toast text is business logic and was preserved verbatim throughout the migration); keyboard focus-visible audit found and fixed ~30 plain `<button>` elements across the app (drag handles, color/icon swatches, floating toolbars, tab strips, node-canvas controls) that were missing a visible focus ring — all now have `focus-visible:ring-2 focus-visible:ring-ring` (or an inverse-surface-appropriate ring color on dark floating pills).
- [x] Full WCAG contrast audit of the 3 new color themes (Ocean/Sunset/Grape) — computed actual WCAG relative-luminance contrast ratios (not just eyeballed) for every foreground/background role pair (`background`/`foreground`, `primary`/`primary-foreground`, `secondary`/`secondary-foreground`, `tertiary`/`tertiary-foreground`, each `*-container`/`on-*-container` pair, `muted`/`muted-foreground`, `surface-container-lowest`/`foreground`, `inverse-surface`/`inverse-foreground`) across all 4 themes (Forest/Ocean/Sunset/Grape) × light+dark = 8 palettes, 12 pairs each. **Every single pair passes WCAG AA (4.5:1 for normal text)** with comfortable margin — worst case observed was 5.42:1 (Ocean dark, `muted`/`muted-foreground`), everything else is 6:1 or higher. `destructive`/`error-container` are theme-independent by design (only defined once, on Forest, and shared across Ocean/Sunset/Grape per the existing `globals.css` comment) so they didn't need re-checking per theme. Non-text UI-component contrast (borders, inputs, focus rings — the lower 3:1 WCAG 1.4.11 bar) was not separately audited in this pass since those tokens are inherited from the same M3 role system already shipped with Forest, not new to the 3 new themes.
- [x] Public-facing embedded form renderer cross-origin CSS conflict concern — **verified this is a non-issue by construction, not something requiring a live third-party test page.** Read `EmbedCodeDialog.tsx` in full: all three embed methods it generates (raw iframe snippet, the JavaScript snippet, and the WordPress shortcode) exclusively wrap the public form page in an `<iframe src="{formUrl}">` — there is no inline/shadow-DOM/direct-injection embed option anywhere in this codebase. Iframe documents have their own completely separate CSSOM; no CSS on the host page (however aggressive its reset) can cross an iframe boundary in either direction, cross-origin or same-origin — this is a fundamental, unconditional browser guarantee enforced at the rendering-engine level, not an implementation detail that could vary or regress. The checklist's original concern would only apply to a script-based inline-DOM-injection embed method, which this app deliberately doesn't offer.

## Navigation, UI, and Design Quality

**Checklist consolidation note**: this section merges a former "### Navigation, UI, and Design Quality" Enterprise-Upgrade-Audit subsection into a combined narrative alongside `## UX Information Architecture` and `## Design System Consolidation` above, since that subsection's own status note already cited both of those sections as "largely real" and several of its 7 bullets turned out, on direct line-by-line comparison, to be substantially covered by that same work rather than fully open. All 7 are kept below (none deleted), each re-marked with an honest partial-completion note against what UX Information Architecture and Design System Consolidation actually delivered.

- [ ] Do a full UI pass for clutter, long-scroll pages, nested cards, inconsistent borders, mobile overflow, button/icon consistency, empty/error/loading states, and enterprise-density layouts. **Partially done**: long-scroll declutter is real for several major pages (Reports, Settings, Payout Cycles, Gamification Settings, Forms builder, Automation builder — see UX Information Architecture above); empty/error/loading states are standardized (`EmptyState`/`ErrorState`/`TableSkeleton`, Design System Consolidation above); a compact/comfortable density toggle is real and built into `DataTable`. **Still genuinely open**: a full app-wide sweep for nested cards/inconsistent borders/mobile overflow, and a dedicated button/icon consistency audit (as distinct from the focus-visible fix below, which addressed keyboard accessibility, not visual consistency).
- [ ] Ensure every visible lookup value renders a friendly name instead of a raw id across tables, cards, exports, filters, smart views, reports, audit logs, notifications, and detail pages. Still genuinely open as a verified, zero-exception guarantee — friendly-name-instead-of-raw-id is a consistently *followed convention* throughout this session's own work (confirmed repeatedly across this document), but that's a practice, not an audited guarantee; no dedicated sweep has confirmed literally zero exceptions app-wide.
- [ ] Standardize date/time and currency display from tenant settings across UI and exports, including tenant timezone conversion, `dd/MM/yyyy, hh:mm AM/PM`, and selected tenant currency. **Date/time half is done**: tenant-aware date/time formatting is real and pervasive (`date-format.ts`, used throughout this app, per Phase 0's own note). **Currency half is still open**: Phase 0's own shared-tenant-formatting-service bullet already confirms there is no equivalent shared, tenant-aware currency/number formatting service — only generic, non-tenant-aware helpers in `utils.ts`.
- [ ] Add no-polling realtime UX where required: notifications, export status, worker job status, campaign delivery progress, task reminders, and automation execution status. **Notifications and task reminders are done** (Postgres LISTEN/NOTIFY → SSE pipeline, real-time, confirmed in Module 1 and Module 10's own notification-center work). **Still genuinely open**: export status, worker job status, campaign delivery progress, and automation execution status still require a page refresh or manual/client-side poll in most places (e.g. the Call Center workspace's own 20-second poll, stated honestly there as "not true real-time push").
- [ ] Add cross-app design QA checklist: one table style, one page header style, one filter drawer pattern, one modal/drawer sizing pattern, one empty-state pattern, one bulk-action pattern, and one request-history pattern. **Mostly done, via work spread across Design System Consolidation and Module 10, not a checklist that was ever written down as such**: one table style (`DataTable`, Design System Consolidation above), one empty-state pattern (`EmptyState`/`ErrorState`/`TableSkeleton`, Design System Consolidation above), one filter drawer pattern (`AdvancedFilterDrawer`, unified across Leads/Lists/Opportunities/Activities, Module 10), and one bulk-action pattern (`BulkActionsToolbar`, rolled out to 8 of 10 named modules, Module 10). **Still genuinely open, not verified as unified**: one page header style, one modal/drawer sizing pattern, and one request-history pattern specifically (several per-domain "request history" UIs exist — imports, exports, privileged actions — but haven't been audited against each other for consistency).
- [ ] Add mobile-first acceptance for every module page: no inaccessible horizontal controls, sticky primary actions where appropriate, readable cards for dense tables, and touch-friendly action menus. **Substantially audited already, not "not built"**: Module 10's own mobile-responsive-pass bullet did a direct code-level audit of 11 named high-use flows (Leads, Opportunities, Tasks, Activities, Views, Reports, Marketing, Automations, Settings, Payouts, Dashboards), found and fixed 2 genuine bugs (Leads' unused mobile card list, the dashboard widget grid's fixed column count), and confirmed the rest already satisfy the requirement by construction (self-scrolling tables, existing responsive grid breakpoints). **Still genuinely open**: this was a code-level audit, not a live-viewport visual verification (no browser/viewport testing tooling in that environment), and it covered 11 flows, not literally every module page in the app.
- [ ] Add accessibility acceptance: focus trap, focus visible, keyboard navigation, screen-reader labels for icon buttons, color contrast, reduced motion, and semantic table/list status text. **Substantially more done than "not built" implies, across Module 10's own accessibility-pass bullet and Design System Consolidation above** — re-verified against both rather than assumed: focus trap is real (the mobile navigation drawer's manual trap, Module 10); focus-visible is real (Design System Consolidation's audit fixed ~30 plain-button instances missing a visible focus ring); keyboard navigation's 2 confirmed real gaps were fixed (Module 10); color contrast is real and passing (Design System Consolidation's full WCAG AA audit across all 4 themes × light/dark, worst case 5.42:1); reduced motion is real app-wide (`MotionConfig` + a global CSS media-query override, Module 10); semantic status text is real for the 3 shared components nearly every list page uses (`ErrorState`/`EmptyState`/`TableSkeleton`, Module 10). **Still genuinely open**: screen-reader ARIA labels for icon buttons are only partially fixed (Module 10's own audit found 178 icon-only buttons app-wide, ~101 still missing a label, concentrated in Settings pages) — a full sweep is explicitly deferred, not silently dropped.
